## I / 一

===Original===
> “If good the news, O bird, alight and welcome;
 

 If bad, draw up thy claws and hie away!”

===Chinese===
> “鸟儿啊，若是佳音，请落下来，受我欢迎；
> 若是凶讯，就收起利爪，快快飞去！”

===Original===
At the corner of a lofty housetop overlooking a great part of Cairo, a woman stood with arms uplifted and solemnly addressed a crow which seemed about to settle. The bird, as if the meaning of the chant had reached him, turned in the air with clumsy flapping, and withdrew, rising to join the hundreds of his kind which circled high above the city bathed in early sunlight. The woman shook her fist at his receding shape, glass bracelets tinkling on her strong brown arm. She sighed, “The curse of God on thy religion, O thou faithless messenger!” then, with a laugh, turned round to join the group of slave-girls, her companions, sent up to lay out herbs to dry upon the roof. These had watched her invocation of the crow with knowing grins. But one, a young Circassian, who sat watching while the others worked, betrayed surprise and asked the meaning of the little ceremony.

At that there was much giggling.

“Knowest thou not, O flower? It is the woman’s secret!”

“Secret of secrets, all unknown of men!”

“By Allah, men know nothing of it. Inshallah, they will be astonished some day!”

“O Hind, relate the story! Our honey, our gazelle, Gulbeyzah, has not heard it.”

===Chinese===
在开罗一座高楼屋顶的一角——从此处可以俯瞰城中大片地方——一个女人高举双臂站着，郑重其事地对着一只仿佛正要落下来的乌鸦念诵祝词。那鸟儿像是听懂了祝词的意思，在空中笨拙地扑棱着翅膀掉转身子，抽身飞走，节节升高，汇入千百只高高盘旋在晨曦初沐的城市上空的同类之中。女人冲着它远去的影子挥了挥拳头，她那健壮的褐色手臂上，玻璃手镯叮当作响。她叹道：“愿真主诅咒你的教门，你这无信的使者！”随即又笑了起来，转过身，回到那群与她一同被派上屋顶摊晒草药的女奴同伴中间。方才她们瞧着她召唤乌鸦，个个会意地咧嘴而笑。唯有一个年轻的切尔克斯女子（Circassian）坐在一旁只看不干，这时面露讶色，问这小小的仪式究竟是什么讲究。

这一问，惹得众人一阵咯咯的娇笑。

“你竟不知道吗，我的花儿？这可是女人的秘密！”

“秘密中的秘密，男人们全然不知！”

“以安拉起誓，男人对此一无所知。若安拉意欲（Inshallah），总有一天他们会大吃一惊！”

“噢，欣德（Hind），快讲讲这故事吧！咱们的蜜糖、咱们的小羚羊古尔贝雅扎（Gulbeyzah）还没听过呢。”

===Original===
Thus urged, the one who had adjured the crow, a free servant of the house, obsequious towards the slaves, its pampered children, explained as she knelt down again to work:

“In the name of Allah, thus it is related: Know, O my sweet, that, in the days of our lord Noah (may God bless him), after the flood, the men and women were in equal numbers and on equal terms. What then? Why, naturally they began disputing which should have the right to choose in marriage and, as the race increased, enjoy more mates than one. The men gave judgment on their own behalf, as usual; and when the women made polite objection, turned and beat them. What was to be done? The case was thus: the men were stronger than the women, but there exists One stronger than the men﻿—Allah Most High. The women sought recourse to Allah’s judgment; but﻿—O calamity!﻿—by ill advice they made the crow their messenger. The crow flew off towards Heaven, carrying their dear petition in his claws, and from that day to this he brings no answer. But God is everliving and most merciful; a thousand years with Him seem but an hour. Perhaps He does but hold our favour over, as might a son of Adam, till the evening for reflection, to grant it at the last. Inshallah!”

“Inshallah!” came the chorus of a dozen voices; followed by a general laugh when Gulbeyzah, the Circassian, yawned and sighed, “Four goodly husbands all my own! O Lord, give quickly!”

“That is the reason,” Hind concluded, “why good women have a word to say to crows who seek to settle. Any one of them may be the bearer of the blessed edict. The reason as related﻿—Allah knows!”

“Good news and hopeful, by my maidenhood!﻿—the best I ever heard!” chuckled Gulbeyzah, reposing with her back against the parapet. She then remained a long while silent, lost in daydreams.

===Chinese===
经众人这般撺掇，那召唤过乌鸦的女人——她是宅中一名自由身的仆妇，对这些奴隶、这宅子娇宠的心肝宝贝们向来曲意奉承——便重新跪下干活，一面解释道：

“奉安拉之名，传说是这样讲的：你要知道，我的心肝，在我们主努哈（Noah，愿真主赐福于他）的年代，大洪水过后，男人和女人数目相等，地位也平等。结果怎样呢？喏，他们自然而然就争执起来：婚配之事该由谁做主挑选；而且随着人丁滋繁，还争该由谁来享有不止一个的配偶。男人们照例自判自断、偏向自己；女人们客客气气地提出异议，他们便回身把她们揍了一顿。这可怎么好？情由便是如此：男人比女人强壮，可世上还存在一位比男人更强壮的——至尊的安拉。女人们便去求安拉公断；可是——唉，造孽！——她们误听馊主意，竟派了乌鸦做信使。乌鸦爪子里攥着她们宝贵的请愿书，一路朝天飞去，从那天直到今天，再没带回半句回音。然而真主永生永在、至仁至慈；千年在祂看来不过一个时辰。或许祂只是把我们的祈愿暂且搁置，就像亚当的子孙办事那样，留到晚间思量思量，最后总会恩准的。若安拉意欲！”

“若安拉意欲！”十来个声音齐声应和。接着，切尔克斯女子古尔贝雅扎打着哈欠叹道：“四个像样的丈夫全归我一人！主啊，快快赐下吧！”引得众人一阵大笑。

“缘故就在这里，”欣德总结道，“所以贤良的女子见了想要落下来的乌鸦，总要对它念叨几句。它们当中的任何一只，都可能是那道吉福敕令的投递者。缘故么，传说是如此——安拉知道！”

“好消息，有盼头，凭我这女儿身起誓——这是我平生听过的最好的消息！”古尔贝雅扎咯咯笑着说，背靠着女儿墙歇息。此后她久久无言，沉浸在白日梦里。

===Original===
The hour was after sunrise of a spring morning in the twelve hundred and eightieth year of the Hegirah, the second of the reign of Ismaîl. The house was that of Muhammad Pasha Sâlih, a Turk by origin but born and bred in Egypt, who held a high position in the government. The girls, their task accomplished, sat down on their heels, each with her tray of basketwork before her, and sniffed the breeze, in no haste to return indoors.

“Praise to Allah,” one exclaimed with fervour, “we escape for an hour from that Gehennum there below. Never have I seen the lady Fitnah so enraged. Her wrath is not so much because her son desires the English governess, as because the Pasha sees no hindrance to the match. I tremble every time I have to go to her, lest in her fury she should damage my desirability.”

“Praise be to Allah, I am not her property,” replied another, “but that of her durrah, the great lady. Yet I know her for a good and pious creature, not likely to be so enraged without rare cause. They say this foreign teacher has bewitched the young man. He is mad. He flung himself before her in the passage as she came from driving. She spurned him, and they bore him, senseless, to his chamber, where for two days he weeps and moans, refusing nourishment. It is enchantment, evidently, for the girl is ugly.”

“Nay, by Allah, she is white and nicely rounded. But shameless! But an infidel!”

===Chinese===
时值希吉来历（伊斯兰教历）一二八〇年春季的一个清晨，日出刚过；这一年是伊斯梅尔（Ismaîl）在位第二年。这座宅邸属于穆罕默德·帕夏·萨利赫（Muhammad Pasha Sâlih）——他祖上是土耳其人，自己却生在埃及、长在埃及，在政府中身居高位。姑娘们的活儿干完了，各自蹲坐在脚跟上，面前摆着自己的柳编托盘，嗅着微风，不急于回屋。

“感赞安拉，”一个姑娘热切地喊道，“咱们总算逃出来一个钟头，躲开底下那座火狱。我从没见过菲特娜夫人（the lady Fitnah）发这么大的火。她的恼怒，倒不全是因着她儿子恋上了那个英国家庭女教师，而是因着帕夏竟看不出这门亲事有何障碍。我每回得上她那儿去都浑身发抖，唯恐她一怒之下损了我的姿色。”

“感谢安拉，我可不是她的财产，”另一个答道，“我是她家杜拉（durrah）——那位大夫人——的财产。不过我知道菲特娜是个善良虔诚的人，若非天大的缘故，不至动这样大的肝火。听说这洋教师把少爷迷住了。他疯了。那天她乘车回来，他在过道里一头扑倒在她跟前。她把他踢开，众人把他抬回房中时他已不省人事；两天来他只是哭泣呻吟，不肯进食。这分明是中了邪术，因为那姑娘长得丑。”

“不，以安拉起誓，她皮肤白皙，身段也圆润可人。可是不要脸！可是个异教徒！”

===Original===
“She can change her faith.”

“As easily as dung can change its odour!”

“Gulbeyzah here is whiter and more appetizing.”

“Well, God alone knows what she is or is not. This is sure: I have no itching to go down into the house while Fitnah Khânum rages.”

“Nor I!” “Nor I!” exclaimed the rest with feeling.

===Chinese===
“她可以改宗嘛。”

“就像粪土改得了自己的臭味一样容易！”

“咱们这位古尔贝雅扎，可比她更白、更开胃。”

“唉，她到底是怎样的人，只有真主知道。只有一件事是定的：菲特娜·哈努姆（Fitnah Khânum）还在大发雷霆，我可一点儿也不想下楼进那屋子去。”

“我也不想！”“我也不想！”其余的人深有同感地喊道。

===Original===
The morning clamour of the city came up to them as a soothing murmur. Minarets dreamed round them in the sun-haze which was rosy at its heart but in the distance pearly with a tinge of brown. On one hand open country might be seen, green fields and palm trees crowding to the desert wave on which three pyramids stood out, minute as ciphers; on the other, ending the long ridge of the Mucattam Hill, arose the Citadel in smoky shadow, its Turkish dome and minarets, its towers and ramparts, appearing like a city of the sky. Here and there among the housetops a small cloud of doves went up, fluttered a moment and subsided peacefully. Kites hovered, crows were circling, in the upper air. Gulbeyzah watched their evolutions dreamily.

“Allah defend us from the liberty of Frankish women!” she remarked at length. “I could not bear it. To meet the stare of all men were too dreadful. My maidenhood would flush my brain and kill me. O pure shame! And yet they choose what men they like, the fact is known. Inshallah, the great favour, when the crow does bring it, will not destroy our blessed privacy.”

“Inshallah, truly!” answered Hind, with vehemence. “Fear nothing, O beloved; God is greatest! Their freedom is from Satan, their liege lord﻿—the curse of Allah on him! It is a travesty of God’s work, like all he does. Is it not known when Allah made the cow, he tried his best to do the same, but got no farther than the water-buffalo? All Heaven mocked him. Our charter, when it comes, will be perfection.”

===Chinese===
城中清晨的喧嚣传上屋顶，到她们耳边已成一片抚慰人心的低吟。四周的宣礼塔在日霭里如梦似幻——霭气近处泛着玫瑰色，远处则是一片珍珠灰，微微透着褐意。一侧望得见郊野，青绿的田畴和棕榈树一路挤到沙漠的沙浪边上，三座金字塔矗立其上，小得像三个零；另一侧，穆盖塔姆山（Mucattam Hill）长长的山脊到了尽头，城堡在烟色的阴影中拔地而起，它的土耳其式穹顶与宣礼塔、它的碉楼与壁垒，望去宛若一座天上的城。屋顶之间，这里那里有一小团鸽群腾起，扑腾片刻，又安然落定。高空里，鸢在悬停，鸦在盘旋。古尔贝雅扎恍恍惚惚地望着它们翻飞。

“安拉保佑我们，千万别沾法兰克女人（Frankish women，指西方女子）的那种自由！”她终于开口说道。“我可受不了。要受所有男人的逼视，太可怕了。我的女儿身会羞得我血往脑门上涌，要了我的命。噢，真是羞煞人！可话说回来，她们看中哪个男人就要哪个男人，这是众所周知的。若安拉意欲，那桩天大的恩典，等乌鸦真把它衔来的时候，可千万别毁了咱们这宝贵的深闺清静。”

“若安拉意欲，一点不假！”欣德激烈地应道，“心爱的，什么也别怕；真主最伟大！她们那种自由来自撒旦，她们的魔王——愿安拉诅咒他！那是对真主造化的拙劣戏仿，他干的事无一不是如此。难道没听人说过：当初安拉造出母牛，撒旦也使出浑身解数要照样造一头，结果至多造出一头水牛？满天神明都取笑他。咱们的那份特许状到来之时，必是十全十美。”

===Original===
“Talking of foreign women makes me curious to know how things are going, down below. Has the governess consented to give life to Yûsuf? Has the Pasha quieted the lady Fitnah?”

“Nothing could quiet her, unless it were the quick expulsion of the Englishwoman. Why did she ever have her children taught the lore of infidels? The fault is hers! She hoped to keep the Bey from honourable marriage, chaining his fancy with some slave-girl, her own creature.”

“With me, say plainly!” laughed Gulbeyzah, with a yawn. “I was brought into the house with that intention. Yet not her creature, for Murjânah Khânum is my mistress, and she would have seen to it that I was well respected. If the governess has pity on him﻿—which I think not likely﻿—as soon would the wild serpent wed the dove﻿—my lady must provide me with a proper husband. I have no mind to wither as a fruit untasted.” She yawned again. “Will no one go into the house and bring me news?”

===Chinese===
“说起外国女人，我倒好奇底下的事进展如何了。那位女教师答应救优素福（Yûsuf）一命了吗？帕夏把菲特娜夫人安抚下来了吗？”

“什么也安抚不了她，除非赶紧把那英国女人撵出门去。当初是谁要让自己孩子学异教徒的学问来着？错处就在她！她原指望用某个女奴拴住贝伊（Bey）的心思，叫他成不了体面的正婚——那女奴还得是她自己的心腹。”

“干脆明说吧，就是我！”古尔贝雅扎打着哈欠笑道。“我就是为着这个打算被弄进这宅子来的。不过我可做不成她的心腹，因为我的主母是穆尔贾娜·哈努姆（Murjânah Khânum），她自会照应我受人善待。倘若那位女教师真对他动了怜悯——我看哪，就跟野蛇肯娶斑鸠一样难得——到那时，我家夫人就得给我安置一门正经亲事了。我可不愿像一颗没人尝过的果子那样干瘪掉。”她又打了个哈欠。“就没人肯进屋去替我捎个信儿来吗？”

===Original===
Up leapt a little Galla girl, a child as yet unveiled, all eyes and teeth with glee in the adventure.

“I go, O lady! I am not afraid. I will even enter the selamlik. I will find out everything.”

“Be very careful, O Fatûmah, lest old Fitnah seize thee. She would rip up thy belly and pluck out thy entrails did she catch thee spying!”

The little black girl laughed and made an impudent grimace.

“And then the eunuchs! They will surely beat thee.”

===Chinese===
一个加拉族（Galla）小姑娘一跃而起——还是个尚未蒙面纱的孩子，满眼放光、一口白牙，为这趟冒险乐开了花。

“我去，小姐！我不怕。我连塞拉姆利克（selamlik，男宾会客区）都敢闯。我什么都打听得出。”

“千万小心，法图麦（Fatûmah），别让老菲特娜撞上你。她要是逮住你窥探，会剖开你的肚皮、掏出你的肠子！”

小黑丫头嘻嘻一笑，做了个放肆的鬼脸。

“还有那些阉人呢！他们准会揍你。”

===Original===
“By Allah, they must catch me first. Sawwâb adores me, and the others are too slow.”

“Good. Run, ere curiosity consume me!”

The little negress shot off like an arrow. Down dark, malodorous stairs, through empty corridors, she glanced, incarnate mischief. In a pleasure court of the harem, where orange trees in tubs grew round a pool, she stopped to listen for the voice of Fitnah. It came from an apartment on her right. Straight forward, where she wished to go, the coast seemed clear. Springing on tiptoe, she plucked a spray of blossom from the nearest tree; then ran on down a passage through the ornate screen, the boundary of the women’s quarters, where a eunuch tried in fun to stop her; and in sight of a great hall where men were lounging, knocked at a door.

The word had scarce been given ere she glided in and held out the sprig of orange-blossom to the English governess, with every muscle of her body fawning, smiling. Without a look, she read the stranger’s face, perceived she had been crying lately but now looked exultant, observed the order of the room, the foreign furniture; and then, before the Englishwoman could find words to thank her for the pretty offering, kissed a white hand which proved as hot as fire, and darted out as noiselessly as she had entered.

===Chinese===
“以安拉起誓，他们得先逮住我才成。萨瓦布（Sawwâb）疼我疼得不得了，旁的又都腿脚慢。”

“好。快跑吧，趁好奇心还没把我吞噬！”

小黑丫头像离弦的箭一般蹿了出去。黑黢黢、气味难闻的楼梯，空无一人的走廊，她一路飞掠而过，活脱脱是淘气的化身。在后宫一座游乐庭院里——桶栽的橙树围着一方水池——她停下来听菲特娜的动静。声音从右手一间屋子里传出。正前方她要去的地方，一路似乎清净无人。她踮起脚尖一纵，从最近的一棵树上掐下一小枝花；随后顺着一条过道继续飞跑，穿过那道镂花隔屏——那是女眷居处的边界——屏边一个阉人闹着玩儿想拦她；最后，在一座男人们闲坐的大厅望得见的地方，她敲了敲一扇门。

话音未落，她已滑进屋去，把那一小枝橙花递向英国女教师，浑身上下每一块肌肉都堆着谄媚的笑意。她瞧也不用细瞧，便把这位陌生女子的面容读了个透：看出她新近哭过，此刻却神采飞扬；看清了屋里的格局和那些洋式家具；随后，不等那位英国女人想出话来为这份可人的礼物道谢，她吻了一下那只白皙的手——竟烫得如同火炭——便像进来时一样悄无声息地蹿了出去。

===Original===
As she was flitting back across the garden-court, she heard a male voice cry:

“Be silent, woman; or, by the Prophet, I shall have to beat thee!”

Crouching behind a tub, she listened eagerly. But though a wrangle was in progress not far off between the Pasha and his wife, the lady Fitnah, she could glean no more than the main tenor of it from the voices, of which the man’s was irritated and the woman’s mad.

At last the Pasha shouted:

===Chinese===
她正掠回那花园庭院，忽听得一个男人的声音喝道：

“住口，女人；再敢出声，以先知起誓，我就不得不打你了！”

她蹲在一只桶后，竖起耳朵偷听。帕夏和他的妻子菲特娜夫人正在不远处吵得难解难分，可是凭声音——男的声音烦躁，女的声音狂乱——她只听得个大意。

终于，帕夏吼道：

===Original===
“It is finished. No word more. I go straight to the Consul. Appeal to the Qadi, I beseech thee; of thy kindness, do so! He will tell thee, just as I do, that thou art demented.”

Another minute and he crossed the court, wearing his best tarbush and his official garb of black frock-coat and narrow trousers﻿—a thing unheard of at that early hour.

Having seen him pass to the selamlik, Fatûmah ran like lightning through the dim old house, till, breathless, she emerged in dazzling sunlight and flopped down on the roof again beside the others.

===Chinese===
“就这么定了。不许再多说一句。我这就直接去见领事。你要不服，尽管去求卡迪（Qadi，伊斯兰教法官）评理；行个好，去吧！他会跟我一样告诉你：你疯了。”

不到一分钟，他已穿过庭院：头戴最好的塔布什帽（tarbush），身穿黑礼服大衣配窄裤的官式装束——这么早便如此穿戴，实属闻所未闻。

望见他往塞拉姆利克去了，法图麦便如闪电一般穿过昏暗的老宅，直跑到气喘吁吁，才一头扎进耀眼的阳光里，瘫坐在屋顶上同伴们的身边。

===Original===
“Well, what news?” they clamoured.

“Great news!” Fatûmah panted. “Only listen! The English governess is going to marry Yûsuf Bey, and she has islamed!”

“Praise to Allah!” cried the others in amazement. “A Frankish woman convert! A great miracle!”

“The Pasha goes this minute to the English Consul, to confer with him and make arrangements for the ceremony.”

===Chinese===
“怎么样，有什么消息？”她们齐声追问。

“天大的消息！”法图麦喘着气说。“你们只管听！那位英国女教师要嫁给优素福·贝伊了，而且她已经入了伊斯兰！”

“感赞安拉！”其余的人惊呼起来。“一个法兰克女人皈依了！天大的奇迹！”

“帕夏这就去英国领事那儿，同他商议，安排婚礼事宜。”

===Original===
“Allahu akbar! Is it possible? But what says Fitnah?”

“What can she say, the poor one? The command is on her.”

“But, for the love of Allah, say, how didst thou learn all this?”

===Chinese===
“安拉至大！这可能吗？那菲特娜怎么说？”

“可怜人，她还能说什么？命令压在她头上呢。”

“可是，看在安拉的分上，说说看，这一切你是怎么打听来的？”

===Original===
Fatûmah shut her lips tight, looking preternaturally cunning.

“Ha, ha!” was all she answered.

“Her tale is nonsense! She is making game of us,” exclaimed Gulbeyzah, breaking out in laughter. “She was not gone five minutes, that is known. Thou shalt be paid full measure, little poison-flower! Confess now that thy story is all lies!”

The marvel was that every word proved true.

===Chinese===
法图麦把双唇抿得紧紧的，一副狡黠得出了奇的神情。

“哈哈！”她只答了这么一声。

“她这是胡说！她拿咱们寻开心呢，”古尔贝雅扎放声笑道。“谁不知道，她去了还不到五分钟。小毒花，回头有你的好果子吃！快招了吧，你那故事全是瞎编的！”

妙就妙在，事后证明这话字字属实。

## II / 二

===Original===
Muhammad Pasha Sâlih was intensely worried. As he drove toward the English Consul’s office, he let deep furrows ravage his benignant brow, and combed his long grey beard with nervous fingers. The ever-shifting crowds, the eager faces, the laden camels rolling on like ships upon the sea of heads; the water-sellers clinking their brass cups, the cries of salesmen and the floating odours﻿—all the pageant of the streets and all their rumour, which filled the sunlight and seemed one with it, went by unnoticed.

In youth he had been wedded to a noble Turkish lady, the sweetest and most gentle of companions. Never an angry word had passed between them. But, alas! when all her children died soon after birth, Murjânah Khânum had grown melancholy and retired from life. She still dwelt in his house, was still the nominal head of his harem; but for more than twenty years she had been dead to pleasure. At first he had amused himself with pretty slaves, being reluctant to infringe her dignity of only wife. Then, at her instance, for she feared debauch for him, he had espoused the daughter of a wealthy native, whom the caprice of a former ruler had exalted. The marriage, besides raising his importance, had brought him four male children. Yet at this moment, with the curses of the termagant still ringing in his ears, he almost wished he had let well alone and kept to concubines.

Allah knew that Yûsuf’s malady was not uncommon at his age; the cure self-evident. The governess was not a heathen. She was of those who have received the Scriptures, therefore marriageable. Moreover, being, as he shrewdly guessed, of no consideration in her native land, she might be tempted by a life of wealth and ease. To save his son from death, he had besought the Englishwoman, imagining that her consent would fill the house with joy-cries. Yet when the cause was won, the only possible objection cancelled by the girl’s unlooked-for turn to El Islam, behold! the lady Fitnah’s grief was changed to fury. The wrangle with her had perturbed him at a moment when he stood in need of all his wits to brave the Consul. Well, Allah saw what trials he endured!

===Chinese===
穆罕默德·帕夏·萨利赫心焦如焚。乘车前往英国领事馆的路上，他任凭深深的皱纹在那慈祥的额头上纵横肆虐，手指紧张地捋着长长的灰白胡须。川流变幻的人群，热切的面孔，满载的骆驼像一艘艘船行驶在人头的海洋上；卖水人敲得铜杯叮当乱响，还有小贩的叫卖声与四处浮动的气味——街头这满目盛景与满耳喧声充满了阳光，仿佛与阳光融为一体——这一切都从他身旁过去了，他竟视而不见。

年轻时他娶过一位高贵的土耳其贵妇，那是天下最甜柔、最温存的伴侣。夫妻之间从未说过一句气话。可是，唉！她的孩子们个个出生后不久便夭折了，穆尔贾娜·哈努姆从此郁郁寡欢，避世不出。她仍住在他宅中，名义上仍是他后宫之主；可是二十多年来，她对一切欢愉早已心如死灰。起初他只拿俊美的女奴消遣，不愿有损她独妻的尊严。后来，因她怕他纵欲堕落，出自她的主张，他娶了一位本地富户的女儿——那户人家是托从前一位统治者的随心所欲才显贵起来的。这桩婚姻除抬高了他的地位，还给他添了四个儿子。可此时此刻，那悍妇的咒骂声还在他耳边回响，他几乎懊悔当初多此一举，倒不如安于旧局，只用姬妾作伴。

安拉知道，优素福这点病在他这个年纪并不少见；疗法也是不言自明的。那位女教师并非异教徒。她属于领受过天启经典的人，因此可以明媒正娶。再说，他精明地料到，她在本国无足轻重，富贵安逸的日子未必打动不了她。为了救儿子免于一死，他恳求过那英国女人，还想着她一旦应允，满宅便会响起欢呼声。然而事已办成，姑娘出人意料地皈依了伊斯兰，唯一说得出口的异议也随之勾销，瞧吧！菲特娜夫人的悲戚竟化作了狂怒。偏偏在他需要拿出全部心机去应付领事的当口，同她的那场口角搅得他心绪不宁。唉，安拉看得见他所受的煎熬！

===Original===
The carriage drew up in a quiet alley, before a gateway ornamented with a coloured picture of lions great and small in funny attitudes. Two Cawasses in silver-braided jackets with long dangling sleeves rose from stools beside the threshold and saluted. Muhammad Pasha passed between them, crossing a courtyard to a second door, wide open like the first. There, in a whitewashed room, two Copts sat at a table, cutting pens. They both sprang up at recognition of the visitor and strove to kiss his hands, which he prevented by patting each upon the shoulder kindly.

“Is the Consul busy, O my children?” he inquired. “I have an errand of importance. Please inform him.”

“Upon my head. I go at once, by Allah!”

One of the Copts leapt to an inner door and knocked thereon. Enjoined to enter, he opened it just far enough for the introduction of his body, and slipped in. Anon returning in the same respectful manner, he beckoned to the Pasha. Then he flung the door wide open, and stood aside, with eyes downcast and hands demurely folded.

Muhammad Pasha entered with a beating heart. His mission was of essence delicate, and he was anxious to avoid all odour of offence towards a foreign representative possessing influence. Having touched hands with the Consul and exchanged greetings, he sat down on the extreme edge of a chair, and toying with his amber rosary, thus broached his business:﻿—

===Chinese===
马车在一条僻静的小巷里停住，停在一家大门前；门上装点着一幅彩色画，画着大大小小、姿态逗趣的狮子。两个卡瓦斯（Cawasses，领事馆武装侍卫）从门槛旁的凳子上站起身来行礼，他们穿着银绦镶边、长袖垂坠的上衣。穆罕默德·帕夏从他们中间走过，穿过一方庭院，来到第二道门前——那门同第一道一样大敞着。在里头一间粉刷得雪白的屋子里，两个科普特人（Copts，埃及基督徒）正坐在桌旁削笔。两人认出来客，双双一跃而起，抢着要吻他的手，他和气地在各人肩上拍了拍，拦住了。

“领事忙吗，我的孩子们？”他问道。“我有要事相商。请替我通报一声。”

“甘愿效劳。以安拉起誓，我这就去！”

一个科普特人跳到里间的门前，叩了叩门。得了让进的吩咐，他把门开到刚够侧身而入的一条缝，溜了进去。不多时，他又以同样恭谨的模样退了出来，向帕夏招手示意。随后他把门豁然推开，垂下眼睛，规矩地拢着双手，侍立一旁。

穆罕默德·帕夏揣着一颗怦怦直跳的心走了进去。他此行万分微妙，一心要避免沾上丝毫开罪这位有权有势的外国代表的嫌疑。他与领事握过手、寒暄已毕，便在椅子的最边沿上坐下，一面把玩着琥珀念珠，如此这般道明了来意：

===Original===
“Monsieur le Consul,”﻿—the conversation was in French of the Byzantine school﻿—“you remember the young lady whom you were good enough to recommend as an instructress for my children. Can you inform me of her origin, her previous history?”

“Excellency, I only know what she herself confided: that she was educated at a religious institution for poor children of good family. She has no relatives. She came here to be governess in an English house which, by the father’s sudden death, was brought to poverty two weeks before she came. She found herself here without a situation and with little money; and as she was well recommended and impressed me as respectable, I thought of you, remembering that you desired an English governess. I trust that you are satisfied of her efficiency?”

“Altogether. She has been a month now in our house, and almost is become like one of us. She is so charming. It is there, the trouble. She is ravishing. Monsieur le Consul,”﻿—here the Pasha changed his tone for that of one who bares his heart, discarding courtesies﻿—“I am very gravely troubled. The anxiety I suffer cuts digestion and gives me frightful belly-pains. My son adores this demoiselle, and she adores him. The affair deprives me of all taste for food. You see my sufferings!”

“Continue, Excellency!” said the Consul grimly. He got up from his chair and paced the room. The Pasha kept the corner of an eye upon him, as he proceeded:

===Chinese===
“领事先生，”——这场谈话用的是一种拜占庭学派的法语——“您可记得您好意荐来教导我孩子们的那位年轻小姐。您能告知她的出身和先前的经历吗？”

“阁下，我只知道她亲口吐露的那些：她在一所专为出身良善而家境贫寒的孩子办的教会学校里受过教育。她没有亲属。她来此地是要到一户英国人家做家庭教师，不想就在她动身前两周，那家的父亲猝然去世，家道败落了。她到了此地，没了差事，钱也所剩无几；她既有得力的荐书，又给我留下端庄的印象，我便想起了您，因为我记得您正想聘一位英国家庭女教师。她的才干想必已令您满意了吧？”

“满意极了。她来敝宅已有一个月，几乎变得同我们自己人一样了。她是那样可爱。麻烦恰恰出在这里。她美得摄人心魄。领事先生，”说到此处，帕夏换了一副腔调，像一个敞开胸臆的人那样抛开了客套，“我心里的烦恼非同小可。这份焦灼败了我的消化，害我肚子疼得吓人。我的儿子爱慕这位小姐，她也爱慕他。这件事弄得我食不甘味。您瞧瞧我受的这份罪！”

“往下说吧，阁下！”领事冷冷地说道。他从椅子里站起，在屋里踱来踱去。帕夏接着往下讲，眼角却一直留神着他：

===Original===
“What can I do? The demoiselle has been secluded from my household, as I promised you. But youth leaps boundaries; love can speak through walls. My son has seen her in the passages﻿—their eyes have met﻿—What know I? Youth is fatal.”

Here the Pasha wiped his eyes.

“Monsieur le Consul, when I heard of this two days ago, I put my son in prison; I went myself and reasoned with the demoiselle. I have reasoned with them both, entreated, threatened; but without result. I fear my son will die if he may not espouse her. The demoiselle implores me not to cast her forth. She says﻿—it is so touching!﻿—that we are her only friends, that she never met with kindness till she came to us.”

“Beg her to come this afternoon and see me,” pronounced the Englishman, whose face had darkened by perceptible gradations as he listened.

“That is precisely what I come to ask: that you will scold her. God knows how the responsibility has weighed upon me. She is not the match I should myself have chosen for my son; but still I should be glad of the alliance, because of the esteem I have for all the English. I stand impartial in the case and greatly worried.”

===Chinese===
“我能怎样呢？正如我向您担保的，那位小姐在我家中一向与人隔绝。可是青春越得过界限；爱情穿得过墙壁。我儿子在过道里遇见过她——他们的目光相遇过——天晓得！青春是致命的。”

说到这里，帕夏抹了抹眼睛。

“领事先生，两天前我一听说这事，就把我儿子关了禁闭；我又亲自去同那位小姐讲道理。我同他们两个都讲过道理，恳求过，恫吓过；然而毫无结果。我怕我儿子若娶不成她，会性命难保。那位小姐则哀求我别把她撵出门去。她说——这话多么动人！——我们是她仅有的朋友，她来我们家之前，从来不曾遇到过善意。”

“请她今天下午来见我。”那英国人发话了。听着这一番话时，他的脸色已一分一分地沉了下来，看得分明。

“这正是我来求您的事：求您训她一顿。真主知道，这副担子把我压得有多苦。她本不是我自己愿给儿子挑的配偶；可我依然乐意结这门亲，因为我敬重全体英国人。在这件事上我立场不偏不倚，只是忧烦得厉害。”

===Original===
“Thank you, Excellency. Send her to me this afternoon. Is there anything else?”

The Pasha had already risen to depart.

“One thing.” He dropped his voice to a stage whisper. “In the frenzy of her love she asks to be of our religion. She has made an oath of her conversion before witnesses. (The Consul swore.) But have no care. We will forget it, if”﻿—the Pasha laid great stress on the condition, and for once looked boldly in the other’s eyes﻿—“if, after consultation with you, she should wish to recant.”

“But you say that there are witnesses to her conversion,” cried the Frank, with bitterness. “I fail to see how it can be forgotten. There would be a riot.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢您，阁下。今天下午打发她来见我。还有别的事吗？”

帕夏早已起身待辞了。

“还有一件。”他把声音压成了舞台上那种耳语。“她在爱情的癫狂之中，要求归入我们的宗教。她已当着证人立下皈依的誓言。（领事咒骂了一声。）不过不必挂心。我们可以忘掉它，只要”——帕夏在这个条件上重重加了口气，而且破天荒头一遭直视着对方的眼睛——“只要与您商量之后，她愿意收回前誓。”

“可你说过，她的皈依是有证人的。”那法兰克人辛酸地嚷道。“我看不出这事怎么能忘掉。那会闹出一场乱子。”

===Original===
“The witnesses are of my house,” rejoined the Pasha suavely. “My command is guarantee of their discretion.”

“Send her to me!” The final words were uttered from tight lips beneath a formidable frown, as the Consul flung the door wide open for the Turk’s departure.

“*Sont-ils fanatiques, ces brutes-là? Peuh!*” respired the Pasha, shaking the dust from off his boots as he regained his carriage. “The girl will have a cruel hour, poor floweret! That dog would like to kill her. But, God be praised, the law of El Islam is still sufficient to protect a convert in a Muslim land!”

His thoughts of the lone foreign girl were full of kindness. She was his daughter. He would care for her true happiness. And then the thought of Fitnah’s rage, recurring, caused him to frown, and swear, and gnaw his underlip.

===Chinese===
“证人都是我宅里的人。”帕夏温文尔雅地答道。“我的一道命令，便是他们守口如瓶的保证。”

“让她来见我！”最后这几句话，是从紧抿的双唇间、在一副骇人的怒眉底下迸出来的；领事说罢便把门大大敞开，送这位土耳其人出去。

“*Sont-ils fanatiques, ces brutes-là? Peuh!*”（这些畜生，真是狂热！呸！）帕夏回到马车前，一面掸去靴上的尘土，一面吁出一口长气。“那姑娘要挨一段狠毒的时光了，可怜的小花！那条狗恨不得要她的命。不过，赞美真主，伊斯兰教法在穆斯林的国土上，仍足以保护一个皈依者！”

他想着那孤身的异国姑娘，满心慈爱。她就是他的女儿。他要看顾她真正的幸福。可随后，菲特娜的暴怒再度浮上心头，惹得他皱起眉头、咒骂出声，直咬自己的下嘴唇。

## III / 三

===Original===

Immediately on his return to his own house, Muhammad Pasha sent a eunuch to announce his coming to the lady Fitnah. He found her lying on a couch in her stateroom. Two slaves, who had been busy fanning her, retired before him. Seeing she lay still with eyes closed as if quite exhausted, he drew near and whispered:

“Now, inshallah, O beloved, thou wilt hear my reasons.”

She opened great brown eyes, bloodshot with wrath, and glared at him a moment.

“Well, what news?” she asked, with studied coldness.

The Pasha then embarked upon his story; but, at mention of the Consul, she sprang up with rage renewed, expectorating:

===Chinese===

穆罕默德·帕夏一回到自己宅中，便打发一名阉人去向菲特娜夫人通报他要来了。他来到她的上房，见她正躺在一张卧榻上。两个原先忙着替她打扇的女奴，在他进来时退了下去。见她阖着双眼、一动不动地躺着，仿佛已精疲力竭，他凑近前去，低声道：

“好了，若安拉意欲（Inshallah），心爱的，你就听我说说其中的缘由吧。”

她睁开一双棕色的大眼睛，眼里布满怒火熬红的血丝，瞪了他片刻。

“嗯，有什么消息？”她问道，故作冷淡。

帕夏于是开始讲述事情原委；可刚一提到领事，她便怒火复燃，霍然跳起，啐骂道：

===Original===

“Curse thy father! ‘She will see the Consul,’ sayest thou? The Consul! May the Consul and his whole race rot with agony! It is simply to evade a duty which is thine and thine alone. Eject her from the house at once, thou paltry coward! She will kill our son. I know thy guile, by Allah! Thou wilt say, ‘The Consul orders her to marry Yûsuf. We must obey the Consul,’﻿—O salvation!﻿—when all the while thyself art father of the mischief. Oh, let her not come here, or, by my fruitfulness! these hands shall cling to her and not leave hold till they have made her so that no man could desire her.”

Expostulation proving vain, her lord retired in great annoyance. He had to fear a scandal in his house, an inquisition by the Consul, ignominy, if Yûsuf’s mother came in contact with the English lady.

In this dilemma, as in every other which concerned the household, he went for counsel to his only love and first of wives. He sent a herald of his coming to Murjânah Khânum, and after a decent interval repaired to her apartments. She received him in a large room, with no other solid furniture than a low desk on which a manuscript of the Koran lay open; but exquisitely clean and sweet, a contrast to those quarters of the house where Fitnah reigned. The windows were constructed of the finest latticework, which made the light within seem rare and delicate. Murjânah, old but stately, fondled her lord’s hand.

===Chinese===

“咒你老子！‘她要去见领事’，你是这么说的？领事！愿领事和他全族老小都在苦痛里烂掉！这不过是推卸一桩本该你一人独担的本分。马上把她撵出宅去，你这卑劣的懦夫！她会害死我们儿子的。你那点诡计我清楚得很，以安拉起誓！你还会说：‘领事命她嫁给优素福，我们只能服从领事’——哦，苍天救命！——可这祸根从头到尾都是你自己种下的。哼，别让她到这儿来；不然，凭我生养儿女之功起誓！我这双手定要死死揪住她，不把她糟蹋得再没有一个男人看得上她，决不松开。”

劝解既然徒劳，她的夫君只得满怀懊恼地退了出来。他不能不怕：优素福的母亲一旦同那位英国女士打了照面，宅中便会闹出丑闻，招来领事的究问，落得一身羞辱。

处此两难之中，正如以往一切关涉家宅的困境一样，他去向自己唯一的心上人、元配夫人讨主意。他先遣人向穆尔贾娜·哈努姆通报驾到，隔了一段合乎体统的工夫，才移步前往她的居所。她在一间宽敞的屋子里接待了他，屋里除了一张矮几，别无其他大件家具；矮几上摊开着一册《古兰经》手抄本。屋子却洁净得精致，甜香满室，与菲特娜当家作主的那几处房舍恰成对照。窗子是用最精细的镂花格窗做成，使屋内的光线显得幽微而柔美。穆尔贾娜人已年老，气度依然庄严，一面轻轻抚弄着她夫君的手。

===Original===

“Thy face is careworn,” she exclaimed, perusing it. “Inshallah, all the news is good.”

“Inshallah,” he replied mechanically. “But Allah knows that I am greatly troubled. I know not what to do.” And he proceeded to describe the madness of the lady Fitnah. At the tale’s conclusion, a light laugh surprised him.

“Thou askest what to do,” exclaimed Murjânah, “when there is danger that a foolish woman, mad with jealousy, may harm a guest of ours! Hear the word of Allah: ‘When ye have cause to fear their disobedience, ye shall reprimand them, ye shall banish them to beds apart, and ye shall beat them.’ Is not that plain? Beat her! It is thy sacred duty. No, no, she will not cry against thee to the Qadi. She will hide her fault. All women look to men for government, and if it is withheld, have cause of grief. Trust me, beloved, there is no good woman who would not rather suffer stripes occasionally than grow for lack of them into a shrieking harridan. Fitnah Khânum is my durrah, and I love her truly, as the mother of our darling children, and for many virtues. Still I say to thee on this occasion: beat her soundly. Bestow on her a perfect beating, O my soul!”

===Chinese===

“你一脸忧色。”她端详着他的脸，说道。“若安拉意欲，一切消息都好。”

“若安拉意欲。”他机械地应道。“可是安拉知道，我烦恼得很。我不知道该怎么办才好。”接着他便讲起菲特娜夫人的疯癫。故事讲完，一声轻笑却令他愕然。

“你问该怎么办，”穆尔贾娜嚷道，“可眼下分明有这般危险：一个嫉妒得发狂的蠢女人，竟要加害咱们的一位客人！听听安拉的话：‘当你们担心她们违逆的时候，应当训诫她们，应当把她们赶到分睡的床上去，应当责打她们。’这还不明白吗？打她！这是你神圣的本分。不，不，她不会到卡迪（Qadi，伊斯兰教法官）那里哭告你。她会把自己的过错遮掩起来。天下女人都指望男人来管教；这管教若被克扣了，她们便有理由悲苦。相信我，心爱的，天下的好女人，宁可偶尔吃几鞭子，也不愿因缺了鞭子，长成一个尖声嚎叫的泼妇。菲特娜·哈努姆是我的杜拉（durrah，贵妇尊称），我真心疼她，念她是咱们心肝宝贝的母亲，也念她许多美德。虽这样说，这一回我还是要对你说：狠狠地打她。赏她一顿十全十美的痛打，我的亲人！”

===Original===

The Pasha kissed his old wife’s hand submissively, and went forth from her presence with a face of awe. The high proceeding needed courage, for a man so kindly. He went to the small chamber where the eunuchs sat when not on duty, and called, “Sawwâb! Meymûn! Bring me a big kurbash. Attend me, both of you!”

The silent, swift obedience of those servants showed the impression made by his unusual sternness. Their help was necessary that the scene to come might wear the aspect of an execution, not a struggle.

Whip in hand, Muhammad Pasha crossed a courtyard and entered a small room remote from others.

===Chinese===

帕夏恭顺地吻了吻老妻的手，面带敬畏之色，从她面前退了出去。这等隆重的程式，对一个如此仁慈的人来说，是颇需要些勇气的。他走进阉人们不当值时闲坐的那间小屋，喊道：“萨瓦布！梅伊蒙！给我拿一条大库尔巴什（kurbash，皮鞭）来。你们两个，都随我来伺候！”

两个奴仆无声而利落的服从，足见他这反常的严厉给人留下的震慑。必须由他们从旁帮衬，接下来那一场才能有个行刑的模样，而不至于沦为一场扭打。

穆罕默德·帕夏手执皮鞭，穿过一方庭院，走进一间远离其他屋舍的僻静小屋。

===Original===

“Bring Fitnah Khânum hither secretly!” he told the eunuchs.

Sawwâb, the fat, was seized with trembling; while Meymûn, a tall, gaunt creature, gave a deathlike grin. They sped, however. Three minutes had not passed before the lady Fitnah, deftly bound and gagged, was borne into the lonely chamber and the door was shut.

Half an hour later, Muhammad Pasha Sâlih sat conversing with the English lady, preparing her intelligence to meet the Consul’s arguments, which he forewarned her would be all misstatements born of blind fanaticism. When married to Yûsuf, he assured her, and himself believed it, she would hardly know the difference from an English home.

===Chinese===

“把菲特娜·哈努姆悄悄带到这儿来！”他吩咐阉人们。

胖萨瓦布浑身打起颤来；瘦长枯干的梅伊蒙则咧出一副死人般的笑。不过两人还是拔腿飞奔而去。不到三分钟，菲特娜夫人已被麻利地捆住手脚、堵上嘴，抬进了那间僻静的小屋，门也随之关上。

半小时之后，穆罕默德·帕夏·萨利赫已坐下来同那位英国女士交谈，调教她如何应对领事的种种说辞——他预先告诫她，那些话全是盲目狂热中生出的不实之词。他向她担保，一旦嫁了优素福，她几乎觉察不出与一个英国家庭有什么两样——这话他自己也信。

## IV / 四

===Original===

The English girl, meanwhile, experienced a passionate elation, like new life. The Pasha’s exhortations were not needed. Rebellion, which had always lurked beneath her trained subservience, now clothed her in its flames and made her terrible for anyone who dared assail her newfound pride.

What had she to regret? From childhood she had been repressed, humiliated, and ordered to be thankful for bare daily bread. In Christian families her lot had been unenviable. Here, in this Muslim household, she was somebody. The month spent here had been the happiest in her life. But, bred up to regard employers as a race apart﻿—impressed, moreover, by the grandeur of the house and by the rank of Pasha﻿—she had never dreamt of being thought an equal by her entertainers. When Yûsuf Bey, whom she had noticed for his beauty, assailed her in the hall, she had imagined his intentions far from honourable, judging from past experience in English houses. She had fled to her own rooms, ashamed and angry, while the image of his face alight with passion remained to trouble her against her will. When the Pasha came and begged her in most flattering terms to condescend to marry his unworthy son, she nearly swooned. All her resistance sprang from incredulity. When once convinced that the demand was earnest, she gave way with grateful tears. Then her resolve became a living faith. It was to break the bondage of the past completely, to cast in her lot forever with these friends who wanted her.

They were wealthy, of exalted rank, and yet they wanted her. They thought her lovely, who had always been esteemed entirely plain, with her squat figure, apple cheeks, and sandy hair. The sleekest youth in all the world desired her. It was so marvellous that she was forced to rub her eyes and fix their gaze on homely objects to dispel the sense of some enchantment. The difference of religion gave her no concern; indeed, the change was welcome, she had been so cramped by English pietism. In this mood, she was fire against the Consul. A world of happiness was opened suddenly, and there were those who would debar her from it Woe betide them!

===Chinese===

与此同时，那位英国姑娘心间涌起一股狂热的欢欣，恍如新生。帕夏的那些劝勉本属多余。反叛的火种素来潜藏在她被调教出来的恭顺底下，此刻化作烈焰将她周身裹住，叫任何胆敢冒犯她新生骄傲的人，都不得不忌惮三分。

她有什么可懊悔的？从小到大，她受尽压制与羞辱，还得奉命为一日粗茶淡饭感恩戴德。在一户又一户基督教徒的家里，她的命运从未令人羡慕。而在这里，在这户穆斯林人家，她总算是个有分量的人了。在此间度过的这一个月，是她平生最快活的日子。只是她自幼被调教得把东家看作另一种族类，又慑于这座宅邸的豪奢气派与帕夏的显贵身份，做梦也不曾想到主人家会拿她当平等人看待。优素福·贝伊的俊美她早已看在眼里；那天他在过道里向她扑来，她凭着一向在英国人家里的阅历，只当他的用意绝谈不上体面。她又羞又恼，逃回自己房里，可他那涨满激情的面容却萦绕不去，违拗着她的心志，搅得她不得安宁。待到帕夏亲来，用最动听的言辞恳求她屈尊下嫁他那个不肖之子，她险些晕了过去。她先前的种种推拒，全出自不敢相信。一旦确知这桩婚事出自真心，她便含着感激的泪水应允了。从此她的决心长成了活的信仰：要彻底斩断往日的枷锁，永远把自己的命运同这些需要她的朋友们拴在一起。

他们有钱有势、门第贵显，却偏偏想要她。她生得矮墩墩的身量、苹果似的圆腮、沙色的头发，一向被人视作再平常不过；在他们眼里，她却是可爱的。普天之下最俊美的少年恋慕着她。这事奇得叫她直想揉揉眼睛，定睛盯住寻常物件瞧上一瞧，好驱散那中了魔一般的错觉。宗教上的歧异她全不挂心；说实在的，改教倒正中下怀——英国的拘谨虔诚早把她拘束得透不过气了。怀着这般心绪，她对领事便是一团怒火。一个幸福的世界蓦然敞开了大门，偏有人想把她拦在门外——那就叫他们遭殃去吧！

===Original===

The Pasha himself escorted her to where a harem carriage waited. Sawwâb the eunuch held the door for her.

“The carriage will be there to bring you back,” the Pasha told her. “I have ordered the servants by no means to return without you, upon pain of death.”

The implied suspicion that she might be kidnapped made her laugh.

“Remember, my son’s life is in your hands﻿—such pretty hands! His earthly happiness is trusted to this carriage, all too vile to hold so sweet a burden. Day and night he dreams of nothing but your charms. If your mind changes he will surely die.”

She laughed and kissed her fingers to the dear old man, as she stepped up into the carriage. The eunuch slammed the door, which was close-shuttered, leaving her in perfumed shade. A burning blush suffused her as she thought of Yûsuf﻿—his strained, eager face, his yearning lips, beheld that once to haunt her consciousness, a naked shape of love. But pride was uppermost in all her thoughts just then﻿—pride in the comfortable carriage, the attentive servants﻿—pride in her newfound value, in her newfound relatives, and in the daring resolution she had made to break with England. The foreign clamour of the streets, the curious, heady odours, flattered her with a sense of strange adventure.

===Chinese===

帕夏亲自送她到一辆深闺马车候着的地方，阉人萨瓦布替她扶着车门。

“马车会留在那儿接你回来，”帕夏告诉她，“我已吩咐仆人们，接不回你决不许空车而归，违者以死罪论。”

这话里暗含着怕她被人劫走的意思，倒把她逗笑了。

“记住，我儿的性命就攥在你的手里——多么好看的一双手啊！他今生的幸福，便托付给这辆马车了，只嫌它委实粗陋，配不上这般甜蜜的贵重担子。他日思夜想，梦里也无一处不是你的姿容。你若变了心，他必死无疑。”

她笑着向那位可敬的老人飞了个吻，抬脚登上了马车。阉人砰地关上车门——车窗板封得严严实实，把她留在一片香馥馥的浓荫里。想到优素福，一阵滚烫的红晕涌上她的脸——他那紧张急切的脸庞、那渴慕的嘴唇，只消照面一次，便化作一个赤裸的爱之形影，纠缠在她的意识里挥之不去。可是那一刻，压倒一切的念头还是骄傲——为这辆舒适的马车、这些殷勤侍候的仆从而骄傲；为自己新得的身价、新得的亲眷而骄傲；也为自己那同英国一刀两断的大胆决断而骄傲。街市上异国的喧嚣、古怪而熏人欲醉的气息，都谄媚地助长着她奇遇临头的心境。

===Original===

Radiant, she alighted at the gate which bore the royal arms of England, near which an open carriage also waited, and passed into the Consul’s office. She expected sternness, but the Consul smiled agreeably, and after shaking hands with her, took up his hat.

“I have been thinking,” he observed, “that all I have to say could be much better said by someone else﻿—a woman. I should be hampered by embarrassment.” He smiled. “So, if you don’t mind, I have sent a note to Mrs. Cameron, asking leave to bring you out to tea with her this afternoon. I have a carriage at the door.”

“I also have a carriage,” she replied, with a light laugh, as they went out together. She could not but admire his strategy, for Mrs. Cameron, the leader of the English colony, was a gentlewoman of the straightest Christian outlook, the last person whom a renegade would care to face. She had, moreover, been all kindness to the stranded girl, hospitably entertaining her until she found a situation. Since going to Muhammad Pasha’s house the governess had spent a Sunday with her, and heard warnings. To brave her now would be an ordeal, but no matter. The destined bride of Yûsuf scorned all fear.

===Chinese===

她容光焕发地在一扇大门前下了车——门上悬着英国王室的纹章，近旁还停着一辆敞篷马车——随后径直走进领事的办公处。她原以为要挨一顿疾言厉色，谁知领事却和颜悦色地微笑着，同她握过手，便拿起了帽子。

“我一直在想，”他说，“我要说的话，若换个人来说会妥帖得多——换个女人。由我来讲，难免因难为情而放不开手脚。”他笑了笑。“所以，倘若你不介意，我已递了一张便条给卡梅伦夫人（Mrs. Cameron），请求今天下午带你到她家去喝茶。我门口备有马车。”

“我也有马车呢。”她轻声笑着答道，两人一同走了出去。她不能不佩服他的战略：卡梅伦夫人是英国侨民的领袖，一位基督教观念最为方正的淑女，叛教的人最不愿当面领教的，正是这一位。况且，夫人待这个落难的姑娘一向仁厚，曾殷勤地款待她，直到她寻着一份差事。自从进了穆罕默德·帕夏的宅邸，女教师曾去她家消磨过一个礼拜天，也听过了她的一番告诫。如今要去顶撞她，免不了一场磨难，但那也无妨。优素福命定的新娘，藐视一切恐惧。

===Original===

Out at the gate the Consul eyed her carriage with intense disfavour, especially Sawwâb the eunuch, who stood ready at the door.

“You will kindly come in mine,” he said peremptorily.

“Then you will kindly tell the Pasha’s man to follow,” she replied, with eyes that twinkled laughter at his show of temper.

He shouted to the Pasha’s coachman, and got in beside her. For a while they drove in silence, the Consul stealing glances at her face from time to time. She knew that he was struck by the new charm of her. His manner had a dash of gallantry which was amusing.

“I hate to see you in that carriage, with those servants,” he exclaimed at length impulsively. “You must forgive me. I have lived here years, and know the country.”

Again she laughed and her eyes quizzed him. The thought that she knew more than he did, possibly, was made conviction by his next remark:

===Chinese===

走到大门外，领事老大不痛快地打量着她的马车，尤其是侍立车门旁的阉人萨瓦布。

“劳驾，请上我的车。”他不容分说道。

“那就劳驾您吩咐帕夏的人跟在后头。”她答道，眼里闪着打趣的笑光，笑他这副使性子的模样。

他朝帕夏的车夫喊了几句，便在她身旁坐进车去。有一程两人默默而行，领事不时偷觑她的脸。她知道，他已被她新添的容光打动了。他举止间带着一分献殷勤的潇洒，怪有趣的。

“我不忍看你坐在那样一辆马车里，用着那些仆人。”他终于按捺不住，脱口说道。“你得多多包涵。我在这儿住了好些年，懂得这个国家。”

她又笑了，一双眼睛揶揄着他。她心想，自己兴许比他知道得还多——他下面这句话，更叫她确信不疑：

===Original===

“Please realize that you are absolutely free. Whatever may have happened﻿—I mean whatever influences have been brought to bear﻿—those people cannot hurt you now, or even reach you.” This man who knew the country suspected the good Pasha of iniquity, and looked upon his palace as a den of vice. She said:

“There has been nothing of the kind. I have never been so kindly treated or so happy.”

He hemmed and hawed, remarking:

“Well, remember what I say. And don’t forget that, as a British subject here, you have great privileges which, whatever happens, you will be unwise to forfeit. I hope you will confide in Mrs. Cameron. There is no one in this world more kind and trustworthy.”

She answered, “Thanks!” and turned from him to contemplate the passing scene. Their carriage flew along a sandy lane between walled gardens of the suburbs, with here and there a mansion closely shuttered towards the street. The road was covered with the long procession of the fellahin returning outward to their villages﻿—men straddled over donkeys between empty paniers; women stalked erect and queenlike in their graceful drapery; here and there a camel sauntered, led by some bare-legged boy﻿—the whole, obscured by clouds of dust, illumined warmly by the rays of the declining sun, or steeped in the deep shadow of mud walls. Foot-farers, forced aside to let the carriage pass, stared at its inmates with contemptuous eyes. The garb of Europe was a blot upon the peaceful scene. Her heart went out to all those people, plodding, contented, in the sunlit dust. Henceforth she would be nothing strange to them, she swore it.

“Here we are!” The Consul’s voice disturbed her reverie. He shouted to the driver and the carriage stopped. The harem carriage drew up close behind it. A door in a high wall was opened by a smiling negro. A minute later she was in a cool verandah, looking on a well-kept garden, outside a very English drawing-room.

===Chinese===

“请你明白，你如今是完全自由的。不论发生过什么——我是说，不论人家使过什么手段——那些人如今伤不着你，连碰也碰不着你。”这位自以为深知此邦的先生，疑心好心的帕夏居心叵测，把他的宅邸看成了罪恶的渊薮。她说：

“从来没有那样的事。我从来没有受过这么好的款待，也从来没有这么快活过。”

他嗯嗯啊啊地支吾了一阵，说道：

“呃，记住我的话。还有，别忘了，你在此地身为英国臣民，享有种种特权；无论出了什么事，轻易弃之不顾是不明智的。我希望你把心事信托给卡梅伦夫人。这世上再没有比她更仁厚、更可靠的人了。”

她道了声“谢谢”，便转过脸去，观赏沿途景色。马车沿着一条沙土路飞驰，路两旁是郊外带围墙的园子，间或有一座宅邸，临街的窗板关得严严实实。路上挤满了长长的队伍，是回各自村庄去的费拉农人（fellahin，埃及农民）——男人们跨在驴背上，两边驮着空筐；女人们裹着飘逸的衣裳，昂首阔步，气度宛若女王；间或有一头骆驼信步而行，由哪个光腿的孩子牵着——这一切隐没在飞扬的尘雾里，或被西斜的太阳照得暖融融的，或浸在土墙深深的阴影中。步行的人们被逼到路边给马车让道，用轻蔑的眼光瞪着车里的人。欧洲装束成了这太平景致上的一块污渍。她的一颗心飞向了所有这些人——在阳光照耀的尘埃里跋涉而自得其乐的人们。从今往后，她对他们再不是什么怪物了——她立誓如此。

“到了！”领事的声音惊醒了她的遐想。他朝车夫喊了一声，马车停了下来。那辆深闺马车紧跟在后头也停住了。一扇高墙上的门开了，开门的是个满面笑容的黑人。一分钟后，她已站在一条阴凉的游廊上，望着廊外一座经管得井井有条的花园；游廊里面，便是一间十足英国派头的客厅。

===Original===

It was a house where all was tidy and precise, a hostile element to one in love with the untrimmed profusion of the Pasha’s palace. She hated it as servants hate a nagging mistress.

“Now, having brought you two together, I shall leave you,” said the Consul pleasantly. “This young lady, Mrs. Cameron, has gone and got herself into a precious fix. Confess her thoroughly, and then we’ll find some way to get her out of it.”

“But I have no desire to get out of it,” cried the girl, exasperated. “The fix, as you are pleased to call it, is my greatest happiness.”

But the Consul was already gone, delighted as it seemed to wash his hands of her. She found herself alone with Mrs. Cameron.

“We’ll have some tea at once, and you must see the children,” was that lady’s first remark, so different from the attack anticipated that the guest, all nerved for battle, felt defrauded. Though ready to resist with fury, she lacked the energy required to open fight. Tea came, and with it the three tow-haired children, whose presence made all talk impossible. The girl sat moody, in abeyance, replying briefly to remarks addressed to her. The garden perfumes became stronger as the sun sank. They, or some kindred but more subtle influence, obscured her brain with fumes in which her purpose loomed unreal and enormous. The homely scene appealed to her against her will. Almost she had the sense of hands held out to her, while Mrs. Cameron was talking nonsense with the children. This playing on her nerves seemed a mean stratagem. Hot anger grew beneath her careless shell.

At length the youngsters were dismissed. The girl then braced herself to meet the blow. Again she felt a keen pang of deception when her hostess said:

===Chinese===

这是一座处处整洁、事事精审的宅子，对于一个爱上了帕夏宅邸那种不加修剪的繁盛的人，它是格格不入的敌对之物。她恨这宅子，就像仆人们恨一位唠叨挑剔的主母。

“好了，我把你们两位引到一处，就此告退。”领事愉快地说。“这位年轻小姐，卡梅伦夫人，自个儿招来了一身天大的麻烦。你好好听她招供个彻底，然后咱们再想法子帮她脱身。”

“可我压根儿不想脱身，”姑娘气恼地嚷道，“您所谓的麻烦，正是我天大的幸福。”

可领事早已走了，仿佛乐得甩手脱了干系。她发觉自己单独同卡梅伦夫人待在一处。

“咱们马上就喝茶，你还得见见孩子们。”这是夫人的第一句话，与预想中的发难如此不同，以致这位绷足了劲准备开战的客人，倒觉得像是受了骗。她虽准备拼命抵抗，却缺少主动开战所需的气力。茶端上来了，随茶而来的还有三个亚麻色头发的孩子，有他们在跟前，什么正话也没法谈。姑娘闷闷地坐着，一副神不守舍的模样，旁人同她说话，她也只三言两语地应付。太阳沉下去，园子里的香气越来越浓。那香气，或是某种同源而更为微妙的影响，化作熏熏雾气罩住了她的脑子，她的决心在其中显得虚浮而庞大。这家常的情景违拗着她的本意打动了她。当卡梅伦夫人同孩子们说着傻话的时候，她几乎觉出有一双双手正向她伸来。这种撩拨她心神的手段，显得卑劣而狡诈。她漠然的外壳底下，怒火渐渐炽了起来。

孩子们终于被打发出去了。姑娘便打起精神，准备迎接那记打击。女主人一开口，她又感到一阵遭了愚弄似的锐痛：

===Original===

“I am going to ask you a great favour. Stay the night with me! My husband is away at Alexandria. I am really lonely.”

“Thank you very much, but it is really quite impossible,”﻿—there was poison in the honey of this sweet reply﻿—“I have a carriage waiting.”

“We can send it with a message.”

“No, really, thank you! I have stayed too long already.” She suddenly bethought her of the master move, and rose determined.

“No, sit down, my dear!” cried Mrs. Cameron. “I have to talk to you. And though I would rather have had the night in which to think things over, I must, since you force me to it, speak quite simply now. I say: Don’t do it, child! Don’t take the step the Consul tells me that you contemplate! He thought that you had been seduced by unfair practices; but that, I see from your behaviour, is not so. It is just the charm of novelty, the spirit of adventure﻿—is it not?﻿—with just, perhaps, a little mischief prompting, a little grudge against the dull life you have led. My love, you must not be allowed to do it﻿—you, an Englishwoman! It degrades us all. I have lived out here for years, and I assure you that, if a daughter of mine declared her will to marry one of them, sooner than it should happen I would kill her with my own hands. A girl!﻿—It is unheard of! With their view of women!”

===Chinese===

“我想请你帮我一个大忙。今晚留下来陪我过夜吧！我丈夫到亚历山大港去了。我真是一个人冷清。”

“多谢您了，可那实在是万万不能的。”——这句甜答话的蜜里掺着毒——“我的马车还候着呢。”

“我们可以打发它先回去，捎个信儿就是。”

“不，真的，谢谢您！我已经待得太久了。”她蓦地想起一步先手好棋，便断然站起身来。

“别走，坐下，亲爱的！”卡梅伦夫人嚷道。“我有话要同你谈。我本想留你过夜，好从容细想；可是你既然逼得我不留余地，如今只好直截了当地说了。我说：别这么做，孩子！领事告诉我你打算走的那一步，你千万别走！他原以为你是被不正当的手段诱骗的；可我从你的神气看来，并非如此。这不过是新奇的魅力、冒险的心性——是不是？——兴许还掺着一点淘气的怂恿，一点对你过惯了的那份沉闷生活的小小怨怼。我的宝贝，决不能容你这么做——你，一个英国女人！这是叫我们大家蒙羞。我在这里侨居多年，我可以向你担保，倘使我自己的女儿扬言要嫁给他们那族人，与其容这种事发生，我宁愿亲手杀了她。一个姑娘家！——真是闻所未闻！何况他们是那样看待女人的！”

===Original===

“It is plain you know nothing about them,” sneered the other; “at any rate, about the class of people I have mixed with. They have the greatest reverence for women. You suppose, because we veil﻿—”

“We!” interjected Mrs. Cameron.

“Yes, we; for I am one of those whom you so grossly slander.” A drum of battle beat at either temple of the girl thus brought to bay. Her brain reeled with indignation, and her voice grew husky. “I say, you think because we veil that we are quite degraded, the same as we do when we see your faces bare. The difference is one of custom only. Underneath our veils, in our own houses, we are just as happy and as free as you are.﻿   … It is too droll! You fancy that Muhammadan women have their lives made miserable? Why, I have never known such happy women. From my rooms, I hear them laughing, playing, singing all day long.”

“Poor things! They know no other life. You do, and would be miserable in the same conditions. Have you ever thought of what polygamy involves﻿—for women, anyhow?”

“It seems to me extremely sensible and kind to women. It takes into consideration facts which we slur over, cruelly. It gives to every girl a chance of motherhood.”

===Chinese===

“很显然，您对他们一无所知，”对方讥诮道；“至少，对我如今朝夕相处的那一等人家，您一无所知。他们对女人怀着最深的敬意。您以为，就因为我们戴面纱——”

“还‘我们’！”卡梅伦夫人插嘴道。

“是的，‘我们’；因为我正是被您如此恶意诽谤的那些人中的一个。”姑娘被逼到这般绝地，两侧太阳穴里咚咚擂起了战鼓。她愤得头昏目眩，嗓音也嘶哑了。“我是说，您以为我们戴面纱，便是十足的下贱了，正如我们看见你们露着面孔时，心里也那样想你们一样。这不过是风俗的差异罢了。在我们面纱底下，在我们自己的宅院里，我们同你们一样快活、一样自由。……这真是滑稽透了！你们以为穆斯林妇女的日子过得很惨？嘿，我从没见过比她们更快活的女人。从我自己的房里，我整天听得见她们笑呀、闹呀、唱呀的。”

“可怜的东西！她们不知道还有别的活法。你知道，同样的境况底下，你会苦得受不了。你可曾想过一夫多妻意味着什么——反正，对女人意味着什么？”

“依我看，这制度极其通情达理，对女人极其仁慈。它正视了那些被我们残忍地遮掩过去的事实。它让每一个姑娘都有做母亲的机会。”

===Original===

“My dear!” exclaimed the mentor, greatly shocked.

“I don’t care what you think. It is quite true.”

“You are young and inexperienced. We who live in the country hear of things of which you cannot possibly know anything﻿—things that I wish most heartily that you may never know. That is why I beg of you earnestly to change your mind.”

“Nothing will make me do that.”

“Then you are honestly in love, and we will say no more on that point.” The forbearance was so unexpected that the governess was startled and stared at Mrs. Cameron with unbelieving eyes. The elder lady showed such trembling earnestness that she grieved for the necessity to shock and wound her. “There remains another question, on an altogether higher plane﻿—I mean the question of religion.” Mrs. Cameron’s voice turned awestruck. “The Consul tells me﻿—but oh, no! It is too fearful!”

===Chinese===

“哎呀，亲爱的！”这位负有开导之责的夫人大为震惊，喊道。

“您怎么想我不管。这完全是实情。”

“你年纪轻，阅历浅。我们这些住在此邦的人，听说过种种你根本无从知晓的事——那些事，我打心眼里盼着你永远别知晓。所以我恳求你，回心转意吧。”

“什么都休想叫我改主意。”

“那么你是真心爱上了他，这一点我们便不再多说。”这般隐忍大出意料，女教师不由一怔，用难以置信的眼光望着卡梅伦夫人。老夫人那副颤声的恳切，又叫她为不得不惊扰她、刺伤她而暗自哀痛。“还剩下一个问题，层次要高得多——我指的是宗教问题。”卡梅伦夫人的声音变得敬畏起来。“领事告诉我——哦，不！这太可怕了！”

===Original===

“I don’t see why!” returned the other doggedly. “They worship God as we do, and they count Christ as a prophet. They are no more fearful than the Unitarians in England. And I am sure they think much more about religion in their daily lives than people do at home.”

“They deny the essence of Christianity﻿—the Redemption. How can you turn your back upon that marvel of Divine Love? Their ideals are all much lower, more material.﻿   … My dear, I see that you have come here primed with specious arguments, and I regret that I am not clever enough to make you see their falseness. I wish I had the tongues of all the angels at this moment grieving over you, to show you how terrific is the gulf you view so lightly.”

The girl laughed nervously. “I don’t suppose the angels bother much. You talk as if God only cared for Christians. I’m sure He thinks the Muslims just as valuable. If you are so much better, why don’t you mix with them and try to do them good?”

===Chinese===

“我不明白有什么可怕的！”对方倔强地答道。“他们敬拜真主，同我们一样；他们把基督尊为先知。他们并不比英国的一位论派（Unitarians）信徒更可怕。而且我敢说，他们平日里对宗教的用心，远胜过英国国内的人们。”

“他们否定了基督教的精髓——救赎。那神圣之爱的奇迹，你怎么能背过身去？他们的理想全都低下得多、物质得多。……亲爱的，我看你是满肚子装好了似是而非的论调来的，只恨我自己不够聪明，不能叫你看穿它们的虚妄。但愿我此刻能有众天使哀伤垂怜的口舌，好叫你看见，你等闲视之的那道鸿沟有何等可怖。”

姑娘不安地笑了。“我看天使们未必肯多操心。您说起话来，倒好像真主只眷顾基督徒似的。我敢说，他看穆斯林也一样宝贝。你们既然这样高明，为什么不与他们往来，试着为他们做些好事呢？”

===Original===

“Some of us are doing so.”

“In such a way!﻿   …”

“We are not discussing missionary methods, dear. Your case is the only one before us.”

“Well, you say that missionaries mean to do good in their way; but it never seems to strike you that I may hope to do a little good in mine!”

===Chinese===

“我们中间就有人正在这么做。”

“用那样一种方式！……”

“亲爱的，我们不是在讨论传教的门道。摆在我们面前的，只有你这一桩事。”

“哼，您说传教士想用他们的方式行善；可您怎么就想不到，我也指望用我自己的方式行一点善呢！”

===Original===

Her tone grew plaintive; the long contest wearied her. The bloom of shadow on the garden, underneath the rose of sunset, the voices of the evening made her wistful; while the sadness which attends all partings clutched her heart. The whine of doing good had slipped from her at unawares﻿—an echo from her former life of hired hypocrisy. It had been the natural tone of conversation with a lady of the class “employer.”

“That rings untrue. You’re simply talking for effect!” cried Mrs. Cameron, indignant. “It is unkind when I am speaking from my heart of hearts.﻿   … Now, only one word more. If you ever loved anyone﻿—father, or mother, or friend﻿—at home in England, think of that person and just ask yourself what he or she would think of your denying Christ. The act is so uncalled-for that it seems like wanton wickedness. You can marry your Muhammadan without renouncing Christianity, and by so doing you would have more honour in your husband’s eyes. You could retain your status as a British subject, which means something here; and if you really have a purpose to do good among those people, you would be in a better position to do so than by sinking to their level.”

“I won’t hear a word more! Oh, you are brutal!” The girl started up with hands and teeth clenched, past endurance. “Oh, you are brutal to bully me like this! I tell you once for all, I love those people, whom you and all your kind hate and tell lies about. No one was ever really nice to me before. They are a million times better than any Christians I have ever known. I tell you I belong to them, and not to you! I mean to have the same religion as my husband, and if he goes to Hell, well, I’ll go too! Do you understand?” Her words now came in gusts, for she was sobbing heavily. “You’ll never see me any more, of course, for I’m a wicked Muslim and you’re so fanatical! I don’t care; I can do without you. I have truer friends, who really like me and don’t only patronize. Oh, how can you make me cry like this, when I was so﻿—so happy!”

To her surprise, she found herself in her tormentor’s arms.

===Chinese===

她的语气渐渐带了哀恳；这场持久的较量把她磨疲了。园中暮色的青翳，衬着落日的玫瑰红，黄昏里的种种声息，都惹得她怅惘；而一切离别所伴生的哀愁，又攥紧了她的心。那套“行善”的哀调不知不觉又从她口中滑了出来——那是她先前受雇装假的生涯留下的一声回响。从前同“东家”阶级的太太们说话，这本是最自然的腔调。

“这话听着虚假。你不过是故意说漂亮话罢了！”卡梅伦夫人愤然嚷道。“我从心底深处同你说话，你这样待我，未免刻薄。……好了，只再说一句。倘若你在英国的家里曾经爱过什么人——父亲、母亲，或是朋友——就想想那个人，只消问问你自己：你否认基督，他或者她会作何感想。这举动全无必要，简直近乎无端的作恶。你尽可以嫁给你的穆斯林，而不必放弃基督教；这样做，你在丈夫眼里反而更有光彩。你还可以保住英国臣民的身份，这在此地是有些分量的；倘若你当真存心在那些人中间行善，守住这个身份，也远比降格屈就他们的水准更能有所作为。”

“我一个字也不要再听了！哦，您真狠心！”姑娘霍地站起，双手紧攥，咬牙切齿，忍无可忍。“哦，您这样欺压我，太狠心了！我郑重告诉您，我爱那些人——您和您那一类人都恨他们、编排他们的谎话。从前从来没有人真正对我好过。他们比我认识过的任何基督徒都好上一百万倍。我告诉您，我属于他们，不属于你们！我就是要同我丈夫信一样的宗教；他若是下地狱，好啊，我也跟着下！您明白吗？”她的话一阵一阵地涌出来，因为她已泣不成声。“您自然再也见不着我了，因为我是个邪恶的穆斯林，而您是这样狂热！我不在乎；没有你们我也过得下去。我有更真的朋友，他们真心喜欢我，不是光拿恩主架子打发我。哦，您怎么把我弄成这样哭，我本来是那么——那么快活的！”

出乎意料，她发觉自己已被那位折磨她的人搂在了怀里。

===Original===

“You wrong me, dear. I’m not fanatical, nor yet so narrow-minded as you think. Now, will you promise that, whatever happens, you will look upon me as a friend and come to see me sometimes? I have said all I can to dissuade you, because I fear you may repent of your decision when too late. My hope is, now and always, that you may be happy. You’ll promise, won’t you, still to make a friend of me?”

The girl nodded, sobbing, speechless with emotion.

“Well, then, God bless you, dear, among the Muslims, and may you always bear the standard of true Christian womanhood!”

With that two-edged blessing in her ears, the renegade, a bowed and shrinking figure, traversed the garden in the blue of twilight. She felt guilty and unnerved, irresolute, until she saw the Pasha’s carriage waiting in the lane, when pride returned. The tears yet wet upon her cheeks, she stood erect and sniffed the evening air. There was still much traffic on the sandy road, running between dark garden-walls to where, beside a little dome, a single palm-plume stood up black against the sky. The dust kicked up by donkeys’ hoofs, by people’s footsteps, rose greenish like wood-smoke. Some wayfarers already carried lighted lanterns which made coloured circles in the gloaming like the peacock’s eyes. A life of passionate adventure lay before her, most curious and rich and warm with human failings, much better worth than that which she had left behind.

Sawwâb the eunuch held the carriage door for her, and murmured “Praise to Allah!” as he shut her in. She saw him merely as a well-trained servant, having as yet no inkling of his grim significance.

===Chinese===

“你冤枉我了，亲爱的。我并不狂热，也不像你想的那样心胸狭窄。如今，你肯不肯答应我：无论将来如何，都把我当朋友看待，偶尔来看看我？我能说的劝阻话都说了，因为怕你这决定到头来悔之晚矣。我的希望——如今如此，永远如此——是你能得到幸福。你肯答应的，是不是？往后还肯拿我当朋友？”

姑娘点着头，抽噎着，激动得说不出话来。

“那么，愿真主赐福于你，亲爱的，在穆斯林们中间；愿你永远高举真正基督教女性风范的旗帜！”

耳边带着那句双刃的祝福，那叛教的人弓着背、缩着肩，穿过暮色青蓝的园子。她只觉愧疚、气馁、心神不定，直到望见帕夏的马车候在小巷里，骄傲才又回到她身上。泪水还湿在腮边，她昂然站直，吸了吸晚间的空气。沙土路上行人车辆依然不少，路在黑魆魆的园墙之间蜿蜒而去，尽头处一座小圆顶旁边，一茎棕榈羽叶黑黢黢地竖在天幕前。驴蹄和人脚踏起的尘土，青幽幽地升腾，像燃木的烟。有些行路人已经提点了灯笼，在暝色里晃出一圈圈彩晕，宛如孔雀羽上的眼斑。一派热烈奇诡的冒险生涯铺展在她前面，离奇、丰厚而温暖，充盈着人性的弱点，远比她抛在身后的那种日子更值得一活。

阉人萨瓦布替她扶着车门，把她关进车里时，低声念了一句“赞美安拉！”她只把他看作一个训练有素的仆人，对他那阴冷的意味，还毫无察觉。

## IX / 九

===Original===

The Englishwoman had surrendered to the importunities of all the household, and submitted to be dressed entirely as an Eastern bride. Her feet and hands had been well dyed with henna overnight; her hair was intricately plaited, smeared with ointment smelling strong of ambergris and sprinkled with gold dust until it made a close and shining covering; her lips and cheeks were painted, and her eyes enlarged with kohl. Then came the putting on of splendid clothes amid a din of chatter, above which strains of music could be heard, wafted by gusts from the selamlik, where festivity had reigned for two days past. A jewelled crown completing her apparel, she was led with joy-cries to the great reception-room, and there enthroned upon the dais. The room was fairly full of visitors already, and every minute there were fresh arrivals.
Early that morning, Gulbeyzah had shown Barakah her future lodging﻿—five rooms within the women’s portion of the house, but self-contained, and with a private door to the selamlik. She had beheld a salon hung with mirrors, full of gilded chairs and tables; and then the nuptial chamber, the bed with silken bedclothes, much too good to use, beneath a canopy of cloth-of-gold embroidered. Four monstrous candles placed around the bed looked ceremonial, and the perfume of rare flowers reminded her of English death-rooms.

===Chinese===

英国女人终究拗不过合宅上下的纠缠，只得顺从，任人把她从头到脚装扮成一位东方新娘。她的手脚连夜用指甲花染料好好染过；头发编成繁复的辫子，抹上气味浓烈的龙涎香膏，又撒上金粉，直到盘成一顶紧贴发亮的冠盖；嘴唇和脸颊涂了脂粉，眼睛用眼墨描得大了。随后便在一片嘈嘈切切中穿上华服，喧声之上还阵阵飘来乐声，是被风一阵阵从塞拉姆利克送来的——那边已经连日欢宴了两天。待一顶镶珠嵌宝的冠冕为她的盛装收了尾，她便在欢呼声中被领进大客厅，安置在高台上端坐。厅里已经坐满了宾客，而且每一分钟都有新客到来。
那天清早，古尔贝雅扎已领巴拉卡看过她日后的居所——在宅子女眷区里的五间房，却自成一体，另有一扇私门直通塞拉姆利克。她看了一间四壁挂镜的客厅，满摆着镀金的椅子和桌子；随后是洞房——床上铺着丝绸衾褥，好得叫人不忍使用，头顶罩着绣花的金锦华盖。床的四周立着四支粗大的蜡烛，一派仪典的气象，稀有花卉的芬芳却使她想起英国人家停灵的房间。

===Original===

The vision of that room oppressed her now as she sat idle, feeling like a wooden image, and met the criticizing stare of strangers who perfunctorily blessed her. At first Gulbeyzah stayed with her and played interpreter. Murjânah Khânum came and kissed her, praying: “May the crown upon thy brow inure thee to the burden of responsibility, may the rich robes and the throne foreshadow honour for thee; may the ordeal of long stillness teach thee patience and long-suffering with dignity. May all our blessings and our prayers today secure thee fruitfulness, and mayst thou live to see thy children’s children flourish round thee. Our Lord preserve thee ever in His grace. Amîn.”
Apart from this soft murmur of the Turkish lady, she discerned no hint of a religious feeling with regard to marriage. After an hour Gulbeyzah mingled with the throng of visitors, and Barakah was left alone to face the curiosity, the unknown talk about her. Every one of all those women used strong scent, and the smoke of divers kinds of incense dimmed the air. The bride herself was saturated with perfumery; which, however, could not drown the odour of her own new garments. This grew sickening. Her brain swam. She was stuck there like a painted doll to be appraised, inspected.

===Chinese===

此刻她枯坐着，那房间的景象沉沉压在心头，只觉自己像一尊木偶，迎着陌生女客们品评的目光，听她们敷衍地念一句吉利话。起初古尔贝雅扎还陪着她充当通译。穆尔贾娜·哈努姆走来吻了吻她，祷告道：“愿你额上的冠冕使你习惯于担子的分量，愿锦衣与宝座为你预示尊荣；愿长久端坐的磨难教你有耐心、教你能带着尊严忍受。愿我们今日一切的祝福与祷告保你多生多养，愿你活着见到孙儿孙女在你身旁繁盛兴旺。愿我主永远以他的恩典护佑你。阿敏（Amîn）。”
除了这位土耳其夫人轻柔的祝祷，她察觉不出这桩婚事里还存有半点宗教的意味。一个钟头后，古尔贝雅扎便汇入宾客的人潮，巴拉卡被独自撇下，独自面对众人的好奇，面对那些她听不懂的、关于她自己的谈论。那些女人个个用着浓烈的香脂，种种熏香的烟气把空气熏得迷蒙。新娘子自己也叫香脂香粉浸了个透；然而这仍旧盖不住她一身新衣的气味。那气味愈来愈叫人发呕。她脑子里发晕。她被钉在那里，像一尊上了彩的偶人，任人估价、任人验看。

===Original===

Anon the crowd was drawn away from her. She sat unnoticed. A group of female musicians had arrived, with them a well-known singer. There ensued a frightful caterwauling, as it seemed to Barakah, but the rest were charmed, to judge from their enraptured “Ah’s!” and ravished gestures.
Then a brown girl, clad diaphanously, writhed a dance of lewd suggestion, ogling the bride the while maliciously. Her performance was applauded even by Murjânah Khânum. Gulbeyzah flew up to the bride and whispered: “We are in great luck! Tâhir, the greatest singer in the world, has been performing for the bridegroom’s friends in the selamlik. He is coming here to sing to us, behind that screen. Look! Those are his children.” A small boy and girl had stolen shyly in, and were made much of, being passed from hand to hand. Gulbeyzah ran off to convey the news to other rooms.

===Chinese===

不多时，人群便从她身边引开了。她坐在那里，无人理会。一队女乐师到了，随行的还有一位有名的歌者。接着便响起一片可怕的嚎唱——在巴拉卡听来是如此；可瞧众人那心醉神迷的“啊——”和销魂荡魄的手势，分明是个个听入了迷。
随后一个棕色肌肤的姑娘，一身薄纱半透，扭摆出一场旖旎撩人的舞蹈，一面不怀好意地朝新娘卖弄眼波。她的表演博得满堂喝彩，连穆尔贾娜·哈努姆也鼓了掌。古尔贝雅扎飞跑到新娘跟前，悄声说：“咱们交了大运！塔希尔（Tâhir）——全世界最伟大的歌者——方才在塞拉姆利克为新郎的朋友们献唱来着。他就要到我们这儿来，隔着那道屏风唱给我们听。瞧！那是他的孩子。”一个小男孩和一个小女孩已经怯生生地溜了进来，众人你传我递地好生疼爱他们。古尔贝雅扎又跑开了，往别的屋子报信去了。

===Original===

Another minute and dead silence fell. All watched the screen. Up leapt an eerie note, sustained till it became a terror to the ear, when all at once it broke into a shower of trills like impish laughter. This was repeated thrice, and then the singer struck a solemn and majestic measure﻿—a religious strain, which his strange voice embroidered with all human passions in their natural tones. Barakah forgot her weariness. This singing was like nothing she had ever heard. It seemed to dignify all life with a tremendous meaning. All unawares she joined the gusty sigh which swept the whole assembly when the last note died. There followed a quick panting melody of lover’s sighs, more like a bird’s song than the effort of a human voice; then came a wail of more than human anguish, and then the singing ceased quite unexpectedly. There was a storm of moans and prayers for more, but Tâhir, the great singer, had already gone.
Barakah became once more aware of stiffness, headache, and a burning mouth. She called to Hamdi, Yûsuf’s little brother, one of her former pupils, to bring water to her. He ran off at once, but brought, instead of water, cloying sherbet which increased her thirst. Her eyelids were so stiffened they would hardly close; her eyeballs ached; the stiffness of the paint upon her cheeks became an iron mask. She felt pilloried, derided, miserably alone, when lo! a small soft hand touched hers confidingly. It was the singer’s little daughter, who, grown tired of sweets and petting, had come to the one lonely person in the room, the quiet place. She looked up in the face of Barakah and smiled. Her brother, a still smaller child, had followed her. They both sat down without the slightest ceremony, and with their heads against her knees, their hands in hers, fell fast asleep. This little group, when it was noticed, caused much laughter and a shout: “Mabrûkah!” (lucky). The bride, a statue of endurance, paid no heed.

===Chinese===

再过一分钟，全场死一般的寂静落了下来。众人都望着那道屏风。一个诡异的音符腾地跃起，绵绵不绝，直到变成耳朵的恐怖，忽然又迸散作一阵颤音的急雨，好似小鬼的嬉笑。这样反复了三遍，歌者才转入庄严恢宏的节拍——一支宗教的曲调，他那奇异的嗓音在上面绣出人间一切激情的本色。巴拉卡忘了自己的疲惫。这样的歌唱她从未听过。它仿佛以一种巨大的意义为整个人生加冕。末一个音消歇时，一声浩叹席卷全场，她不知不觉也随着叹出了口。随后是一支急促喘息的旋律，尽是爱人的叹息，与其说是人声的功夫，不如说更像鸟儿的歌啭；接着来了一声哀号，痛楚得超出凡人；然后歌声戛然而止，全出意料。满堂顿时一片呻吟，纷纷恳求再唱，可是那位伟大的歌者塔希尔，早已去了。
巴拉卡重新觉察到周身僵直、头痛、口里如烧。她唤哈姆迪（Hamdi）——优素福的小弟弟、她从前的学生之一——去给她端水来。他立刻跑去了，可端来的不是水，而是一杯甜得发腻的果子露（sherbet），越发添了她的渴。她的眼皮僵得几乎合不拢；眼球发痛；两颊上脂粉的干硬成了一副铁面具。她自觉像上了枷示众，受人哄笑，凄凄惶惶孤身一人——不想忽地有一只软软的小手信任地碰了碰她的手。原来是那歌者的小女儿，糖果吃腻了、疼爱受够了，自己寻到屋里唯一冷清的人、那冷清的角落来了。她仰起脸望着巴拉卡，笑了。她弟弟，一个更小的孩子，也跟了来。两个小家伙毫不拘礼地坐下，头靠着她的膝，手放进她的手里，很快睡熟了。这一小簇人儿让人瞧见时，引起哄堂大笑，有人喊了一声：“穆巴鲁卡！”（Mabrûkah，有福的）。新娘呢，一尊忍耐的雕像，全不理会。

===Original===

At last a great noise came from the selamlik. A eunuch rushed to say that the procession of the bridegroom to the mosque had just returned. At once, a heavy veil, precluding sight, was flung on Barakah. The bride’s train formed. With tapers and with garlands, amid joy-cries, she was led to her own gilded salon, and there left alone. In the same instant, so it seemed to her, the bridegroom came. Her veil was lifted. She felt like to die. She dared not raise her eyes for fear of weeping. The ritual words she had been schooled to say escaped her memory. But, as luck befell, they were unneeded.
“*Grand Dieu!*” cried Yûsuf Bey. “The fools﻿—the miscreants have made you look like one of them. Your face﻿—your hair! *Ah, mon amour! Ma colombe!*”
She was obliged to laugh, and the nice-looking, eager youth laughed with her. Fatigue and headache fell off from her like a garment.

===Chinese===

终于，塞拉姆利克那边起了一阵大喧哗。一名阉人奔进来禀报：新郎往清真寺去的仪仗刚刚回来了。立刻，一幅厚重的面纱抛上巴拉卡的头上，遮得密不透光。新娘的送亲队列排成了。人们擎着小蜡烛、捧着花环，在欢呼声中把她领进她自己那间金碧辉煌的客厅，随后将她独自留在那里。在她感觉里几乎是同一瞬间，新郎来了。她的面纱揭起了。她只觉得自己要死过去了。她不敢抬眼，只怕泪水夺眶而出。人家教熟了她的那几句仪文，此刻全从记忆里逃了个干净。也是机缘凑巧，那些话竟用不着。
“*Grand Dieu!*（我的上帝！）”优素福·贝伊叫道。“这些傻瓜——这帮恶棍把您弄得跟她们一个模样了。您的脸——您的头发！*Ah, mon amour! Ma colombe!*（啊，我的爱人！我的小鸽子！）”
她不由得笑了，那俊俏热切的年轻人也跟着她笑。疲惫和头痛像一件衣裳似的从她身上脱落了。

===Original===

On the next afternoon, when Barakah, at peace with all the world, was sitting in her gilded parlour, on the cushioned windowsill, peeping through the lattice at red masts and flags, the decorations for her wedding not yet taken down, it happened that she called for water. That cry resounded through the whole haremlik in the hours of heat, and slaves with pitchers waited always ready to obey it. The girl who answered brought a vase of amber fluid, which she proclaimed the most delicious sherbet known to woman. The lady Fitnah had herself prepared it for the bride’s delight. Barakah took one sip, disliked the taste, and, only waiting for politeness till the maid had gone, poured out the rest upon a plant of jasmine in a flowerpot which stood upon a shelf within the lattice. A little later she was very sick, and went and lay down on her bed. She was feeling better when her husband was announced.
“Yûsuf!” she cried, as he came in, “it is so curious. Madame your mother sent me up some special sherbet. I tasted it, and found it disagreeable, so I emptied all the rest upon the plant there. Then I felt so ill﻿—”
She got no further. Yûsuf, following the direction of her gesture, had fixed his eyes upon the flowerpot. They were riveted. The plant was dead, a shrivelled, blackened object. With one despairing cry he clutched his forehead and rushed headlong from the room.

===Chinese===

次日下午，巴拉卡与全世界都相安无事，坐在她那间金碧辉煌的客厅里铺了垫子的窗台上，从格棂里张望红色的桅杆和旗帜——她婚礼的装点还没有撤下。这时她恰巧唤人要水。在暑热的时辰，这一声呼唤会响彻整个哈雷姆利克，捧着水壶的女奴们总候在一旁，随时听命。应声而来的姑娘端来一壶琥珀色的饮料，说这是女人家所能尝到的最可口的果子露，是菲特娜夫人亲手为新娘调制的。巴拉卡抿了一口，嫌那味道不对，只碍着礼数等那女仆退下了，才把剩下的尽数倒在窗棂内搁板上一盆茉莉花上。过了一小会儿，她大吐起来，便走过去躺到床上。仆人通报丈夫到时，她正觉得好些了。
“优素福！”他进来时她喊道，“真是奇怪。您母亲大人打发人给我送上一种特制的果子露。我尝了尝，觉得味道不好，就把剩下的全倒在那一盆花上了。后来我就难受得——”
她的话没能说完。优素福顺着她手势的方向，双眼已经落在那盆花上。目光钉在那里，再也拔不动。那株花已经死了，成了一件枯萎发黑的东西。他一声绝望的喊叫，双手抱住前额，夺门狂奔而去。

## V / 五

===Original===

Muhammad Pasha Sâlih went again to see the Consul, this time upon receiving a peremptory summons. He came away with smart sensations of indignity, the unbeliever having warned him to take care of his behaviour to the English governess. The marriage contract, he was told, must be in order, and every detail of her treatment strictly honourable. These admonitions thrown as to a dog, to him, the known embodiment of goodness, made him cry. When he got home it was to find a note from the Grand Qadi, requesting him to call at once upon that dignitary, who besought him, for the honour of the Faith, to be precise in all his dealings with the English convert. And when, that afternoon, he waited in his duty on the lord of Egypt, that prince demanded tidings of the Englishwoman and, jesting, told him to be sure and use her kindly.

“She must be a rare pearl,” the sovereign chuckled. “The English Representative is maddened by her loss. By God and His Apostle, I have half a mind to snatch her from thee.”

===Chinese===

穆罕默德·帕夏·萨利赫又去见了领事，这一回是接到一纸不容分说的传召。他出来时，周身刺痛，尽是受辱之感：那异教徒竟警告他，要他留神自己对那位英国家庭女教师的举止。人家告诉他，婚约必须办得规规矩矩，待她的一切细节都必须严格合乎体面。这等训诫，像丢给一条狗似的丢给他——丢给他这个有口皆碑的善人化身——直叫他哭了出来。到家时，又见着大卡迪送来的一张条子，请他即刻趋访那位贵人；贵人恳求他，为信仰的荣耀起见，同那位皈依伊斯兰的英国女子的一切往来，务求一丝不苟。当天下午，他循例进谒埃及之主，那位君主又探问英国女人的消息，还打趣似的嘱咐他，务要好好待她。

“她准是颗罕见的明珠，”君主呵呵笑道，“英国代表为失掉她都快发狂了。凭真主和他的使者起誓，我简直有一半心思，要把她从你手里抢过来。”

===Original===

For one whose house had always been a guarded sanctuary, who never made nor brooked the least allusion to his women, such language from licentious lips, in hearing of the throng of courtiers, was sheer ignominy. He cursed the parents and religion of the English Consul, the cause of this indecent noising of a private matter. The dog appeared to fancy that he had to do with fellahin or small officials; for he had spoken of the facility of divorce and the danger of the Englishwoman being cast adrift. Among the vulgar there were men who changed wives constantly, even persecuting her they had till she herself besought the Qadi for release, thus forfeiting the dowry which was justly hers. Such men might be, who thought it clever to defraud poor maidens. But that anyone could think that he, Muhammad Pasha, or a child of his, could harbour such iniquity seemed barely credible. The hot tears stung his eyeballs at the thought of it.

“Just Allah!” he exclaimed within himself. “Does he suppose that we have no morality? Would he, whose native customs are as shameless as the ways of beasts, leaving females unprotected and at large, instruct us how to cherish and to guard a woman? He talks as if I were some pimp or ruffian, when I am dealing with the maid as faithfully as if she were my only child!”

In truth, before this trouble with the Consul, at the ceremony of betrothal, when he himself had prompted the bride’s proxy, he had assigned to her a dowry of three thousand pounds﻿—a sum sufficient to make Yûsuf hesitate, however angry, before he gave the order for divorce. He had, moreover, spoken to his son most gravely, pointing out the friendless state of the young woman, and informing him that if he took her it must be for life. Yûsuf had made frantic answer in the way of lovers, comparing his fidelity to stars and blossoms. The Pasha bound him by a solemn oath always to show forbearance to his foreign wife. He then drove him forth to spend the time until the wedding in a cousin’s house; where, as he had heard this afternoon from the said cousin, Yûsuf kept raving of his love﻿—in abstract terms, for decency﻿—till the whole selamlik was infected with the trick of sighing. Nothing could have been more honourable than his conduct. The girl was better off than ever in her life before, and knew it. He swore an oath to let the Consul know it too.

===Chinese===

他的宅邸向来是一座戒备森严的圣所，他自己从不提起、也容不得旁人对他的女眷有半句影射；这等话竟从一张淫邪的嘴里说出，还当着一群朝臣的面——简直是赤裸裸的奇耻大辱。他咒骂那英国领事的爹娘和宗教——这桩私事被闹得如此不成体统，根子全在他们。那条狗俨然自以为对付的是费拉农人或小官吏；因为他竟大谈离婚何等便易，又说什么那英国女人有被弃置无依之虞。市井小人中间固然有那样的男人，换妻子如同换衬衣，甚至百般糟践家里那一个，直到她亲自去哀求卡迪恩准离异，白白断送了理应归于她的嫁资。这种以为坑害贫家姑娘算得聪明的男人，世上或有。可居然有人会想到，他穆罕默德·帕夏、或是他的骨血，竟会包藏这等恶行——简直令人难以置信。一想到这里，滚烫的泪便刺痛了他的眼珠。

“公道的安拉啊！”他在心里呼喊。“他莫非以为我们全无道德？他那本乡的风俗，无耻得同畜生的行径相仿，任女人们抛头露面、无人庇护——倒要他来教导我们怎样疼惜、怎样守护一个女人？他说话的口气，倒像我是个拉皮条的、一个流氓恶棍；其实我待那姑娘忠心耿耿，就当她是我的独生女儿！”

其实，早在这场同领事的纠葛之前，订婚仪式上，他自己从旁给新娘一方的代言者递词指点，已为她定下三千镑的嫁资——这笔数目，足以叫优素福无论怎样动怒，在下达离婚之命以前，也要踌躇一番。他还极其郑重地告诫过儿子，指出这年轻女子举目无亲的处境，并明言：他若娶她，便须终身相守。优素福以恋人们惯有的痴态作了热烈的回答，把自己的忠贞比作星辰和花朵。帕夏又用庄严的誓言把他缚住，要他永远宽宥这位异国妻子。随后便把他打发出去，在婚期之前住到一位堂兄家里；据他当天下午听那位堂兄说，优素福在那里不住口地痴叹情爱——为着体面，只说些不着形迹的空话——闹得整座塞拉姆利克都染上了动辄叹息的把戏。他的行事再体面也没有了。姑娘的境遇比她平生任何时候都好，她自己也心中有数。他发了誓，也要叫领事知道这一点。

===Original===

Accordingly, returning to his house that evening, he craved immediate audience of the sometime governess; and shortly entered her apartments, which, providing simply for an upper servant of his house, he had furnished in the Frankish manner to seem homelike. If he had gone to so much trouble for a stranger’s comfort, was it likely he would prove a niggard towards his dear son’s bride? The pig who thus traduced him must be taught.

The girl was sitting in a chair beside the window, reading an English book. It pleased him to reflect that she was highly educated. In these bad times, when Frankish lore was in demand, her instructions might secure advancement to a man like Yûsuf, who knew French already.

She laid aside her book and rose to meet him with a charming blush. He took her hand and raised it to his lips; then sank down on a chair and clasped his brow.

===Chinese===

于是，那天傍晚回到府中，他便请求立刻见一见那位昔日的女教师；不多时便进了她的居室。那几间屋子，原不过是按府中上等仆人的份例安置的，他却特意照法兰克款式布置起来，好叫它像个家的样子。为一个外人的舒适尚且肯下这许多功夫，难道他还会在爱子新娘的身上做吝啬鬼？那头如此诽谤他的猪，非受一受教不可。

姑娘正坐在窗边一把椅子里，读着一本英文书。想到她受过极好的教育，他心里颇为受用。在这世风日下的年头，法兰克的学问正吃香，有她教导点拨，像优素福这般已经通晓法语的人，兴许能谋得个高升。

她撂下书，带着动人的红晕起身相迎。他握住她的手，举到唇边吻了吻；随即跌坐进一把椅子，双手抱住额头。

===Original===

“Ah, mademoiselle!” he moaned, “I am so troubled. God knows my heart is sad, profoundly wounded. You are kind and generous, and you know our hearts. But those others of your nation.﻿ ﻿… Pouf! How bitter! How fanatical! They treat me and my house as dirt. Here is the case: You honour my poor house; you are alone; you have no parents. I say to myself, ‘She is an orphan; I will be her father.’ I therefore do what parents do according to our customs. I provide the trousseau; I also bargain with the bridegroom’s people to endow you richly.

“Let me explain what that means, since it must be quite unknown to you. With us, divorce is easy; it suffices for the man to say a little formula; but the husband must support the wife for three months afterwards, and he must pay the balance of the dowry stated in the marriage-contract, or, if no portion has been paid beforehand, then the whole of it. That makes him think. And the greater the dowry, the longer will he meditate before divorcing her. Now I, your father, have talked the matter over with myself, the bridegroom’s father, and have obtained for you a dowry of three thousand pounds Egyptian. This sum will be stated in the contract, signed and sealed before the judge, and my son will have to pay it if ever he desires divorce, which God forbid! Your trousseau, with the jewels and the slaves that I am going to give you, the furniture of these rooms and more which I shall buy to supplement it﻿—I wish your house to be the kind you are accustomed to﻿—all this, I say, will be your absolute property, and so stipulated in the contract.”

===Chinese===

“唉，小姐！”他呻吟道，“我心里烦难极了。真主知道，我的心悲伤，受了深重的创伤。您仁慈，您大度，您懂得我们的心。可是贵国其余的那些人。……呸！何等刻毒！何等狂热！他们把我和我一家看得如同尘土。事情是这样：您赏光垂顾我这寒舍；您孤身一人；您父母双无。我对自己说：‘她是个孤儿；我来做她的父亲。’于是我照着我们的风俗，行父母所行之事。我置办嫁妆；我还同新郎那边的人讲定，给您置一份丰厚的嫁资。”

“让我解释一下这是什么意思，因为您必定全然不知。在我们这里，离婚是容易的；男人只消说一句小小的套语便够了；但此后三个月，丈夫必须供养妻子，还必须付清婚约中订明的嫁资余款——倘若事先未曾付过分毫，便须全数付清。这就叫他不能不思量。而嫁资越重，他在离婚之前思量的工夫便越长。如今我，您的父亲，已经同我自己——也就是新郎的父亲——把这事商量停当，为您争得三千埃及镑的嫁资。这笔数目将载入婚约，当着法官签字用印；我儿子日后但凡想离婚，便须如数照付——真主不容！您的嫁妆，连同我将要赠您的珠宝与奴婢、这几间屋子里的家具，以及我还要添置补充的——我盼着您的住处是您住惯的那一种——这一切，我说，都将是您绝对的私产，并在婚约中如此订明。”

===Original===

The girl had seized his hand. She pressed it to her lips and sighed:

“How good you are!”

His own emotion was no less than hers. The humiliations of that day had taxed his fortitude, and the sense of his integrity beneath aspersion was like a bubbling fount of tears in outer darkness. The warm touch of her gratitude unmanned him quite. He sobbed aloud:

===Chinese===

姑娘早已一把抓住他的手。她把它按在自己唇上，叹息道：

“您多好啊！”

他自己的激动也不亚于她。这一天的桩桩羞辱耗损了他的坚忍；清白之身横遭诽谤，那滋味便像沉沉黑暗中一泓汩汩上涌的泪泉。她感激的暖意一贴上来，更叫他全然把持不住。他失声呜咽起来：

===Original===

“Ah, mademoiselle! God knows that I have done my best! Yet here is the Consul threatening me, and moving all the Government to watch me closely; as if I had entrapped you for some evil purpose!﻿—as if I were the worst of criminals, intent to harm you!﻿ ﻿… I cannot vindicate myself. It would be too degrading. And if he thinks me such a first-class canaille he would not believe me. Therefore I come to beg you, mademoiselle, yourself to deign to write a little word to this good monsieur, assuring him that we are not the monsters he supposes.”

The girl’s face flamed. “I write at once!” she said, and rose to do so.

===Chinese===

“唉，小姐！真主知道，我已经尽了全力！可是那领事威胁我，还煽动整个政府紧紧地盯住我；仿佛我设局诱骗您，是图什么邪恶的目的！——仿佛我是顶顶恶劣的罪犯，存心加害于您！……我没法为自己辩白。那未免太失身份。况且，他既把我看作头一等的无赖（canaille），便也不会相信我。因此我来求您，小姐，求您亲自屈尊给那位好先生写几句短简，向他保证：我们并不是他所设想的那种怪物。”

姑娘的脸腾地烧红了。“我马上就写！”她说着便起身要去动笔。

===Original===

But the Pasha cried: “One moment, mademoiselle!” He wiped his eyes and struggled to recover firmness. “Do not suppose that I complain! Even if the happiness of my dear son were not concerned, I would suffer more than this﻿—much more﻿—abominations!﻿—to serve so beautiful and good a lady. I fear my words have saddened you. Oh, God forbid! Never, I pray you, think of it again, your letter written. You must be all happy. Tomorrow you must go among our ladies. You will find there mothers, sisters, longing to embrace you. They will help you choose the stuffs for your trousseau. They speak Arabic, of which you know few words as yet, or Turkish, which is quite unknown to you. But my widowed sister speaks a little French, and Murjânah Khânum owns a young Circassian who can talk it fluently. She is a present from relations in Constantinople who have bred her from a child in every elegance. At the time of the great war with Russia, French was much the mode; even girls learnt it, and this maid of whom I speak, Gulbeyzah, talks it well. She shall be attached to you as interpreter. The wedding, if it please you, can take place next week. We will have it in the mode of Europe﻿—nothing barbarous!”

===Chinese===

可是帕夏喊道：“且慢，小姐！”他拭了拭眼睛，挣扎着定住神。“您切莫以为我在诉苦！即便不算我爱子的幸福，为侍奉这样一位又美丽又善良的夫人，比这更重的我也受得——重得多——便是种种奇耻大辱，我也受得！我怕我的话叫您伤心了。哦，真主不容！信写完之后，求您千万别再想它。您必须满心快活。明天您得到我们的女眷中间去。您会在那里见到许多母亲、姊妹，都巴望着拥抱您。她们会帮您挑选嫁妆的衣料。她们说阿拉伯话——您如今只懂得几句——或者土耳其话，那是您全然不懂的。不过我那位守寡的姊妹会说一点法语，穆尔贾娜·哈努姆手下有个年轻的切尔克斯女子，法语说得流利。她是君士坦丁堡的亲戚们送的一份礼，人家自她幼年便把她调教得事事风雅。当年与俄国大战的时候，法语大大时兴，连姑娘们也学；我说的这个丫头古尔贝雅扎，就说得一口好法语。叫她拨给您做通译。婚礼，倘若您中意，下星期便可举行。咱们按欧洲的款式操办——绝不带半点野蛮气！”

===Original===

“I love your customs!” she replied. “Let it be just as if I were a native bride.”

“No, no,” remarked the Pasha, with a chuckle. “There are many usual ceremonies here in Egypt which are condemned by our religion, strictly speaking. These we shall exclude, preserving only one or two which may amuse you. My son also will modify his life to suit your foreign standards; it is only just; although the life of our own ladies is by no means terrible, as you will find. Tomorrow you shall spend in the haremlik. You will find there many friends. All, all will love you and make glad your heart. And now, with your permission, mademoiselle, I shall retire. Forget not that small letter to the Consul.”

===Chinese===

“我喜欢你们的风俗！”她答道。“就全当我是个本地新娘那样办吧。”

“不，不，”帕夏呵呵一笑，说道。“埃及此地有许多惯常的礼数，严格说来是咱们的宗教所不容的。这些咱们一概免去，只留下一两项，或许能博您一乐。我儿子也会把生活改一改，来就您外国的标准；这是应当的；虽说咱们自家女眷的日子，您自会看到，绝不是那般可怕。明天您就在哈雷姆利克（haremlik，女眷居处）里过。您会在那里找到许多朋友。大家，大家都会爱您，叫您心里欢喜。现在，请您允准，小姐，我就告退了。别忘了给领事的那封短信。”

===Original===

Muhammad Pasha, coming from that interview, was traversing the hall of the selamlik towards his study, when a sudden clamour at the house-door startled him.

“Curse thy father! Wait, I say! Be still a minute!” cried the doorkeeper; while another voice yelled madly, “I must see the Pasha. Where is he? Let me pass, I say! The need is urgent!”

“Cut short thy life! Wait only! Are these manners? He has entered the harem, I tell thee!”

There followed sounds as of a struggle, and before the Pasha could divine the meaning of the uproar, a youth in poor attire rushed in and fell before him, panting:

===Chinese===

穆罕默德·帕夏从那番晤谈中出来，正穿过塞拉姆利克的大厅往书房去，宅门口忽然爆起一阵喧嚷，吓了他一跳。

“该死的！等着，我说！站住一会儿！”守门人嚷道；另一个声音狂吼：“我非见帕夏不可。他在哪儿？让我过去，我说！事情火烧眉毛！”

“短命鬼！只有等着！这是甚么规矩？他进了后宫，我告诉你！”

接着传来一阵厮打般的声响；帕夏还未及猜透这场喧闹的缘由，一个衣衫寒碜的少年已经冲了进来，扑倒在他面前，喘着气道：

===Original===

“He told me to win to thee, O my lord﻿—to fight my way through armed hosts if necessary, to seek thee even in the secrecy of the harem, saying that the letter which I bear would be my full excuse.”

It was a poor familiar of the palace, named Ghandûr, one who from early childhood had been Yûsuf’s humble shadow, a youth so simply honest in his judgments that to subtler wits they wore the look of imbecility.
