But yesterday he had been here as usual, sitting in the entrance on the watch for Yûsuf. Today he had been absent, but without disloyalty: he had been sitting in the entrance of the house where Yûsuf sojourned temporarily.

===Chinese===

“他叫我务必闯到您跟前来，我的老爷——必要时不惜从千军万马中杀出一条路，哪怕寻到后宫的隐秘深处，也要把您寻着；他说，我身上带的这封信，便是我十足的凭证。”

原来是府里一个贫穷的常客，名叫甘杜尔（Ghandûr），打从孩提时候起，便是优素福身边一条谦卑的影子；这少年看事情实心眼儿到了家，落在心窍活络的人眼里，简直带着几分痴傻。昨天他还照旧在这里，坐在门口守候优素福。今天他没来，却绝非起了二心：他一直在优素福暂时寄住的那户人家门口守着。

===Original===

“He bade me run, and Allah witness I have done his bidding. I am thy slave, give pardon, O my lord the Pasha!”

“Salvation be upon thee, O Ghandûr. What letter, now, is this of which thou speakest? Give!”

Reassured by the kind tone, Ghandûr arose, and smiling with a flash of perfect teeth, produced a letter from his bosom, touched his forehead with it, then reverently laid it in the Pasha’s outstretched hand. It ran:

> “My garden of delight is in thy custody. The palpitations of my heart inform me danger shadows it. Alas! the grievous power of jealousy, which can make of a gazelle a tigress, and turn a mother’s love into a sword. This is the third time I have written to thee, yet no answer. Say that thou hast taken measures to preserve my lovely blossom from envious trampling and from poisoned water.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“他叫我一路飞跑，安拉作证，我照办了。我是您的奴仆，求您开恩，帕夏老爷！”

“愿你平安，甘杜尔。你说的这是封什么信？拿来！”

听得这和善的口气，甘杜尔放了心，站起身来，一口整齐的皓齿一闪，从怀里掏出一封信，先用信碰了碰额头，然后毕恭毕敬地放进帕夏伸出的手掌。信上写道：

> “我那座欢愉的花园，如今托在你的手中。我心的悸动告诉我，危险正笼罩着它。唉！嫉妒的恶力何其酷烈——它能把一头羚羊变成母虎，把母亲的爱化作一柄剑。这是我第三次写信给你，却杳无回音。告诉我，你已采取措施，保住我那可爱的花朵，莫叫嫉妒的践踏和下了毒的水毁了它。……”

===Original===

The Pasha crumpled up the letter and stood wrapped in thought. Coming so close upon his promise to the English girl that all the women in the house would love and cherish her, the warning had a flavour of fatality. He recalled the lady Fitnah’s frowardness. She had been punished. Who could say that she had changed her mind? And, with the Consul’s evil eye upon the house, the shame of any outbreak would be doubled.

“Run to my son!” he told Ghandûr. “Assure him that a guard is kept, none safer, under Allah. Bid his soul have rest.”

Having watched the youth depart, he called the eunuchs and ordered them to guard the English lady as their life. Then he proceeded to the kitchens and there gave command that every dish and drink prepared for the table of the governess should come first to him that he might taste and judge its quality. And he took good care to let the women know of this precaution.

===Chinese===

帕夏把信揉作一团，伫立着出了神。他刚向那英国姑娘许下诺言，说府中所有女眷都会爱她惜她；这封警信来得如此紧随其后，字里行间透着一股在劫难逃的气味。他想起菲特娜夫人的桀骜不驯。她是受过惩戒的。可谁敢说她已经回心转意？何况领事的毒眼正盯着这座宅子，一旦闹出乱子，耻辱便要加倍。

“跑去找我儿子！”他吩咐甘杜尔。“向他担保，防护布置得严密无比，托安拉的庇佑，再没有更稳妥的了。叫他把心放下。”

目送那少年去了，他唤来阉人，吩咐他们像护卫自家性命一般护卫那位英国女士。随后他亲到厨房，传下命令：为女教师席上预备的每一道菜肴、每一盏饮品，都必须先送到他跟前，由他亲口尝过、评断好坏。他还特意留心，把这道防备让女眷们都知道。

## VI / 六

===Original===

The women’s quarters were a rambling place, with three small courtyards all on different levels, tunnels, staircases inside and out, and passages which ran in all directions. Besides the ladies Fitnah and Murjânah and their households, a widowed sister of the Pasha, and a former slave who had enjoyed his favour, kept separate state, with children and attendants. Freed slaves and poor relations, recognized go-betweens and sycophants came in and out, and slept there when they chose﻿—a privilege extending to their offspring. Old women with a secret, knowing look edged through the corridors; untidy children sprawled upon the stairs; outside the door of each of the great ladies stood rows of coloured slippers, signifying humble callers. The place seemed always populous and full of noise. In a sense, good order reigned there; but it was the order of a township rather than a private residence, including all degrees of cleanliness, of wealth and squalor. The corps of eunuchs, ten in all, were the police.

This little world of women had its liberties. From the third hour of the day until the sunset call to prayer, the lord of the harem was absent. If he happened to return, it was his duty to announce the fact beforehand, allowing time for visitors to veil and slip away. The inmates had their private interests, their games and jokes. The clash of tambourines, the quick soft beat of darabukkas made a pulse of glee. They all seemed happy and in love with life, although they hardly ever saw the sun or breathed free air; for when they drove abroad it was in shuttered carriages; and the family mausoleum, where they went for picnics, was a second palace with its own haremlik.

But what surprised the Englishwoman more than anything was the charm of majesty﻿—the exquisite prestige﻿—which certain of these Eastern women radiated; making her feel small. They called her “Barakah”; it was her name thenceforward, and meant a Godsend, so the courtly Pasha told her. That name increased her awkwardness at first, sounding sarcastic from the lips of queenly women.

On the morning after she had written her indignant letter to the Consul, she was awakened by soft singing. A beautiful and stately girl sat by her bed, who, seeing her at last awake, sprang up and kissed her. Murjânah Khânum, claiming Yûsuf’s bride as her own guest until the wedding, had sent her slave Gulbeyzah to attend her to the bath, attire her in a robe of honour (which was shown), and then escort her to Murjânah Khânum’s rooms, where Barakah was asked to breakfast and to spend the day. It was useless to resist. Gulbeyzah knew her duties, and performed them scrupulously. By the time they left the bathhouse, Barakah arrayed in gorgeous silk, her fingers hennaed and her eyes enlarged with kohl, they were laughing friends.

===Chinese===

女眷的居处是一片曲折蔓延的所在，有三座小庭院，高低错落各不相同，有地道，有屋里屋外的楼梯，还有四通八达的走廊。除了菲特娜与穆尔贾娜两位夫人和她们各自的家眷，帕夏的一位守寡姊妹和一个曾蒙他恩幸的前女奴也各自别居一院，带着孩子和仆从。获释的奴隶和穷亲戚、公认靠得住的媒婆与帮闲食客进进出出，高兴了便留宿在那里——这份恩典还泽及他们的儿女。带着讳莫如深、万事通晓神气的老妇人贴着走廊侧身而过；衣冠不整的孩子在楼梯上四仰八叉；每位贵妇的门外都摆着一排彩色拖鞋，昭示着谦卑的来客。这地方似乎总是人烟稠密、喧声不断。某种意义上，那里治理得井井有条；但那是一座市镇而非私人宅邸的秩序，洁净与腌臜、富足与穷陋，种种等第一应俱全。阉人这一班人马，共计十名，便是此地的警察。

这女人的小天地自有它的自由自在。从白昼的第三个钟点直到日落宣礼的呼喊，内宅之主一概不在。倘若他碰巧要回来，按规矩他有义务事先传话知会，好让客人们有时间蒙上面纱悄然溜走。宅中女眷各有个人的营生，各自的游戏和玩笑。铃鼓的铿锵、达布卡鼓（darabukka）急促而轻软的敲击，汇成一脉欢悦的搏动。她们个个显得快活，热爱生活，尽管她们几乎见不着太阳，也呼吸不到自由的空气；因为出门兜风，坐的是遮得严严实实的马车；她们去野餐的家族陵墓，也是第二座宫殿，有它自家的哈雷姆利克。

而最叫这英国女人惊奇的，莫过于某些东方女子周身焕发出的威仪的魅力——那种精微入妙的尊贵气派——直叫她自惭形秽。她们唤她“巴拉卡（Barakah）”；从此这便是她的名字，意思是“天赐之福”，那位殷勤有礼的帕夏如此告诉她。起初，这名字反而添了她的局促：从仪态如后的贵妇口中说出，听来颇有讥诮之意。

她给领事写完那封愤懑之信的次日早晨，是被一阵轻柔的歌声唤醒的。一个美丽端庄的姑娘坐在床边，见她终于醒了，便一跃而起亲吻她。穆尔贾娜·哈努姆认定，优素福的新娘在婚礼之前算她自己门下的客人，便打发她的奴隶古尔贝雅扎来伺候她上浴场，替她穿上一袭荣典锦袍（那袍子还展示给她看了），然后引她到穆尔贾娜·哈努姆的房里，请巴拉卡在那里用早餐、消磨一整天。抗拒也是枉然。古尔贝雅扎深知自己的职分，且办得一丝不苟。等到两人离开浴场——巴拉卡一身华艳的绸衣，十指染了指甲花红，眼睛让眼墨（kohl）描得大了——她们已成了笑闹着的好友。

===Original===

Murjânah Khânum took the Englishwoman in her arms and kissed her; then sitting down beside her, subjected her to a prolonged inspection, none the less embarrassing for being tender.

“Mashallah!” she exclaimed, and added some soft words in Turkish, looking to Gulbeyzah, who translated:

“Madame says you are more beautiful than she was told. Your beauty is more excellent than the rose. Your eyes remind her of the Bosphorus. You make her think of her own country. The desire which you inspire is like homesickness.”

Barakah could only blush and hang her head﻿—a posture which drew down fresh compliments upon her modesty.

Slaves brought in trays of fruit and set them down, retiring silently. Then an old negress came in with a brazier and made coffee, with which was served a kind of fritter smeared with honey. Then a young girl appeared with ewer and basin and fine towels, going first to Barakah, who rinsed her hands. Murjânah and Gulbeyzah, she saw afterwards, used soap and washed their teeth as well﻿—a cause of spluttering.

===Chinese===

穆尔贾娜·哈努姆把那英国女人搂进怀里亲吻了；随后在她身边坐下，对她施行了一番长久的端详，目光虽温柔，却照样叫人窘迫。

“马沙安拉（Mashallah，真主所愿）！”她赞叹一声，又用土耳其语添了几句软语，眼睛望着古尔贝雅扎，由她通译：

“夫人说，您比人家告诉她的标致得多。您的美胜过玫瑰。您的眼睛叫她想起博斯普鲁斯海峡。您叫她惦念起自己的故国。您惹起的渴慕，就像乡愁一般。”

巴拉卡只得红着脸垂下头去——这份娴静的姿态，又为她引来一番新的赞美。

奴隶们捧进来一盘盘果子，放下便悄然退去。随后一个上了年纪的黑人老妇端着火盆进来煮咖啡，随咖啡一同奉上的，是一种涂满蜂蜜的炸果子。接着一个小姑娘捧着水壶、水盆和精细的毛巾出现了，先到巴拉卡面前，她涮了涮手。后来她看见，穆尔贾娜和古尔贝雅扎还使了肥皂，连牙也洗了——免不了咕噜噜一阵漱溅。

===Original===

Murjânah Khânum rolled a cigarette. She lounged at ease with eyes intent on Barakah, and while she smoked, gave vent to her reflections, which Gulbeyzah rendered into French as best she could.

“It is a great distress to me not to be able to convey my loving thoughts directly to the mind of one so near. Ask the dear one if she speaks Romaic, or a little Persian. No? A pity! She is learning Arabic? Inshallah, she will soon acquire that tongue and Turkish too.﻿ ﻿…

“I fear she must feel strange and lonely in a life so different: I wish I could expound its beauty to her. Ask her whether she has read the tragedies of Sophocles, an ancient Greek. No? That surprises. I had thought them known among the Franks. Say, I have read them in the Turkish version and admired them greatly.﻿ ﻿… At least, she knows that, in old times, before the prophets, there were priestesses who guarded mysteries of the false gods?﻿ ﻿… Well, we secluded women of the East are the guardians of the mysteries of God Most High﻿—the verities of life and death, of birth and growth and of decay﻿—of all those things which come directly from the hand of God. These are the sense of life; though much obscured by all the surface agitation which disturbs the life of men. We, in our calm retirement, always view them﻿ ﻿…

“And then, when one regards the strife of tribes, the tumults and rebellion in this world, is it not well that womanhood should be kept sacred and aloof, respected in the strife of Muslims﻿—the ark which bears the future of the Faith?﻿ ﻿… Then, even as it is, much crime is caused by love and jealousy. What would it be if women went unveiled? I say not, in her land where men’s blood may be more equable; but here.﻿ ﻿… Just Allah! Youth would be a curse. If marriageable girls were barefaced, what could preserve them from atrocious accidents? We guard their youth and train them to be lovers, child-bearers; we send forth healthy boys to serve the Faith.﻿ ﻿…

“Tell her that I myself, by Allah’s visitation, have lost all my children; yet, thanks to El Islam, I am not desolate. I have her Yûsuf and a score of others for delight.”

===Chinese===

穆尔贾娜·哈努姆卷了一支烟。她慵懒闲适地半倚着，眼睛专注地盯着巴拉卡，一面吸烟，一面抒发她的所思所想，由古尔贝雅扎勉力译成法语。

“不能把我的爱心直接传达给一位如此亲近的人，于我是莫大的苦楚。问问这位亲爱的，她说不说罗迈语（Romaic，近代希腊语），或是懂一点波斯话。不？可惜！她在学阿拉伯话？若安拉意欲，她不久便能把那话学会，连土耳其话一并也会。……

“我怕她在这般迥异的生活里必定觉得陌生、孤单：但愿我能向她阐说这生活的美。问问她可读过索福克勒斯——一个古希腊人——的悲剧。没有？这倒叫我诧异。我原以为法兰克人中间无人不晓呢。告诉她，我读过它们的土耳其文译本，倾慕之至。……至少，她总该知道，古时候，在众先知之前，有过守护伪神奥秘的女祭司吧？……那么，我们这些深居简出的东方女子，便是至高真主奥秘的守护者——生死之真谛，生发、成长与衰朽之真谛——凡直接自真主手中降临的事物，皆由我们守护。这些才是生活的真义；尽管它们大半被搅扰男人生活的种种表面喧嚣所遮蔽。而我们，在宁静的深闺中，永远观照着它们。……

“再者，试观这世上的部族纷争、喧嚣与悖逆，让女性保持圣洁与超然、在穆斯林的争战中受人敬重——作为承载信仰未来的方舟——岂非好事？……话虽如此，眼下的许多罪案已因爱与嫉妒而生。倘若女人们除去面纱，又当如何？我不是说她那国土——那里的男人血性或许多些平和；我说的是这里。……公道的安拉！青春会成一场灾祸。待嫁的姑娘们若都光着一张脸，还有什么能保她们不出可怕的意外？我们守护她们的青春，把她们调教成爱侣、生育者；我们送出康健的男孩去为信仰效力。……

“告诉她，我本人因安拉的降罚，失掉了全部的孩子；然而，托伊斯兰的福，我并不孤寂。我有她那个优素福，还有二十来个孩子承欢取乐。”

===Original===

Hearing these words translated by Gulbeyzah, Barakah felt abashed to insignificance. The habit of confronting the brute facts of life, which Europeans cover over, clothed this old woman in a tragic grandeur which was almost terrifying. She was relieved when other ladies came and talk grew shallow. Silks and fine linen fabrics were spread out before her. Hearing that she was required to choose among them for her trousseau, she implored Gulbeyzah with despairing gestures to say that she resigned selection to the ladies. The answer caused relief. The ladies set to work methodically, feeling, stroking, comparing the materials in the best light, discoursing all the while like happy birds. Fitnah Khânum was less forward than the others in politeness, and kept her face averted from the gaze of Barakah. She took her leave before the service of the midday meal.

The Pasha’s widowed sister begged of Barakah to spend the following day with her in her apartments. Murjânah was approached and gave consent.

“I can give you dinner on a proper table with chairs and knives and forks,” the widow said in broken French.

Murjânah Khânum’s tables were brass trays on little stands, and everybody ate with fingers from the dish.

===Chinese===

听了古尔贝雅扎译出的这些话，巴拉卡羞惭得自觉渺小。直面人生粗粝事实的习惯——欧洲人总把它们掩埋起来——给这老妇人披上了一层近乎骇人的悲剧性庄严。待到别的女眷来到、话题转浅，她才松了口气。绸缎和细麻织物在她面前一一铺陈开来。听说要她从中挑选自己嫁妆的料子，她绝望地打着手势，恳求古尔贝雅扎代为传话：挑选之事，她全权奉托各位夫人。这个回答令人如释重负。夫人们有条不紊地干起活来，摸一摸、捻一捻，就着最好的光线比对料子，一路叽叽喳喳，像一群快活的鸟儿。菲特娜·哈努姆不及旁人热络客气，始终侧着脸避开巴拉卡的目光。午饭端上来之前，她便告辞了。

帕夏那位守寡的姊妹恳请巴拉卡次日到她房中消磨一天。先禀过穆尔贾娜，得了允准。

“我能请您吃一顿正经饭：有桌子，有椅子和刀叉，”寡妇用蹩脚的法语说。

穆尔贾娜·哈努姆的桌子，是小支架上搁着的黄铜托盘，人人都伸出手指从盘里取食。

===Original===

The day with Leylah Khânum was less serious. The widow’s talk was all of love and lovers. A perfect host of go-betweens was kept employed to find her a fresh husband; but, though ageing fast, she was fastidious and asked perfection.

“God grant she may not die a widow,” sighed Gulbeyzah, who explained the case to Barakah.

Leylah Khânum was much exercised to know whether Barakah had had much love-experience in England. Hearing “No,” she raised her hands in marvel. One so beautiful! The mistress of so much charm! And unveiled among men! She asked the reason.

“I was poor,” said Barakah.

At that there was loud outcry; Leylah Khânum and Gulbeyzah called on God for pity.

“But you are beautiful! Men pay for beauty, need no bribe with it. And you mean to say they would have let you die a virgin﻿—with that loveliness? O Lord of Heaven! What a wicked waste!”

===Chinese===

在莱拉·哈努姆（Leylah Khânum）那里度过的一天便没那么严肃了。寡妇的谈吐不离爱情和恋人。一大帮媒婆受着雇用，为她张罗一位新丈夫；可她虽说老得飞快，人却挑剔得很，非要十全十美不可。

“真主保佑，可别叫她守着寡死去，”古尔贝雅扎叹道，是她把这情形讲给巴拉卡听的。

莱拉·哈努姆一心想知道，巴拉卡在英国可有过多少恋爱的历练。听得一声“没有”，她惊异地举起双手。这样标致的人儿！坐拥这般妩媚的一位！还在男人堆里抛头露面！她问缘故。

“我穷，”巴拉卡说。

这话激起一片喧嚷；莱拉·哈努姆和古尔贝雅扎连声呼唤真主怜悯。

“可你生得美呀！男人肯为美色掏钱，有了美貌，旁的贿赂一概用不着。你的意思是说，他们竟肯让你守着这般姿色，到死还是处子之身？哦，上天之主！何等罪过的糟蹋！”

===Original===

Their dread of dying in virginity appealed to Barakah as something comical when she remembered the ideals preached in Christendom.

Leylah Khânum told her stories of true love, all far from proper judged by English taste; and shocked her by the cool assertion that poison was a woman’s natural weapon. In the afternoon they were invited to Murjânah Khânum’s rooms, where the business of the trousseau still proceeded. It went on for days. Each morning when she woke, the bride-elect found some fresh present from the Pasha in her room, which Gulbeyzah made her carry forth and show to everyone. The whole haremlik frolicked round her in excitement.

Gulbeyzah’s status in the household puzzled her. The Circassian seemed the equal of the ladies, yet was called a slave.

===Chinese===

她们这种惟恐以处子之身死去的恐惧，在巴拉卡听来颇有滑稽之处——她想起了基督世界所宣扬的那些理想。

莱拉·哈努姆讲了许多真爱的故事给她听，拿英国的口味衡量，个个都有伤风化；她又不动声色地断言毒药是女人的天然武器，叫她骇然。下午她们被邀到穆尔贾娜·哈努姆房里，置办嫁妆的事务仍在进行。这买卖一连进行了好些天。每天早晨醒来，待嫁的新娘总能在房里发现帕夏送来的新鲜礼物，古尔贝雅扎非要她捧出去给众人一一过目。整个哈雷姆利克围着她兴奋地嬉闹。

古尔贝雅扎在宅中的身份叫她纳闷。这切尔克斯女子看上去与夫人们平起平坐，却被唤作奴隶。

===Original===

She said to her one day:

“You are as white as I am. How can you bear to be a slave like Wardah or Fatûmah?”

“Not like Wardah or Fatûmah, if you please!” was the superb rejoinder. “They or their fathers were captured in a warlike raid and made to islam, I, God be praised, was born in the Faith. Look!” she cried, and with a splendid gesture bared her bosom. “This is the paste of which they make sultanas. My parents sold me﻿—they were poor﻿—that I might come to honour, as others of the family have done before me.”

“But what chance have you here? Do you expect to captivate the Pasha?”

“God forbid! I never even see him. Here I serve the sweetest of all ladies, who will one day find me a rich husband. It is a famed harem, and my lady is renowned for goodness and refinement. The greatest in the land would not disdain a fair Circassian girl of her instructing.”

===Chinese===

一天，她对她说：

“你跟我一样白。你怎么受得了当奴隶，像瓦尔达（Wardah）或法图麦那样？”

“承情别提，可别把我同瓦尔达或法图麦相提并论！”这就是那句神气十足的回答。“她们或是她们的父辈是在一场掳掠战事中被俘，被迫皈依了伊斯兰；我呢，赞美真主，生来就在信仰之中。瞧！”她喊道，豪迈地一挥手，袒开了胸襟。“这就是造苏丹娜的坯子！我爹娘把我卖了——他们穷——好叫我来日享得尊荣，我们家的女孩先前也有走这条路的。”

“可你在这里有什么前程？你指望迷住帕夏么？”

“真主不容！我连他的面都见不着。我在这里侍奉天下最甜美的夫人，她日后会给我寻一门富贵的亲事。这是有名的宅门，我的主母以贤德风雅著称。全国顶顶体面的人家，也不会嫌弃一位由她调教出来的标致切尔克斯姑娘。”

===Original===

“But do you never miss your freedom? You can form no projects, being, it seems, entirely in the hands of others. Surely your thoughts are not so ruly? You must sometimes dream?”

Gulbeyzah fixed her great eyes on the questioner as though debating whether she were to be trusted. Then, with a smile, she grasped her hand and whispered, “Come!”

She led the English girl across the court where grew the orange trees, down a foul-smelling passage towards the kitchens, and up a flight of stairs into a corridor which served the chambers of the humblest servants. In its wall was a recess with a small window neither barred nor latticed. Here Gulbeyzah stopped.

The reason why that window had been left uncaged was plain, since it looked out upon blind walls and distant housetops. But one small angle of a terraced roof appeared within clear seeing range, and on that angle sat a man. When Gulbeyzah leaned her elbows on the windowsill, he sprang to his feet and made despairing gestures. She watched his antics for a moment, then drew in her head.

“It is a secret, mind!” she cautioned Barakah. “I spent an afternoon here once, when I was sulky, and he was walking on that roof by chance. Ever since then I see him every day. He always sits there. I sign to him to climb up, but I know he cannot.” She laughed scornfully. “I make romances in my mind about him. It is evident he dies of love. He has grown thinner.”

===Chinese===

“可你从不想念自由么？你似乎全然握在旁人手里，什么打算也做不成。你的心思断不会这般听话吧？你总有时候要做做梦吧？”

古尔贝雅扎把她那双大眼睛盯住发问的人，仿佛在掂量她可不可信托。随后她嫣然一笑，一把攥住她的手，悄声道：“来！”

她领着这英国姑娘穿过长着橙树的庭院，顺着一条气味难闻的过道往厨房去，再登上一段楼梯，进了一条走廊，通往最低微仆役们的居室。墙上有个壁龛，开着一扇小窗，既没装铁栅，也没有窗棂花格。古尔贝雅扎在这儿停住了脚。

那窗子为何不设栅笼，道理一望即知：窗外只见无窗的死墙和远处的屋顶。唯有一方平台屋顶的小小屋角，落在看得真切的范围内，那屋角上正坐着一个人。古尔贝雅扎把胳膊肘往窗台上一支，那人便一跃而起，做出种种绝望的手势。她瞧了片刻他那副把戏，随即缩回了头。

“这是秘密，记着！”她提醒巴拉卡。“有一回我使性子，在这儿消磨了一个下午，他恰巧在那屋顶上走动。打那以后，我天天见到他。他总坐在那儿。我打手势叫他爬上来，可我知道他爬不上来。”她讥诮地笑了。“我在心里拿他编恋爱的故事。明摆着他害相思害得要死。人都瘦了。”

===Original===

“How cruel! How can you torment him so?”

“He is a man, you understand. One does not feel compassion as one would for girls. Perhaps if he could climb up here I should reward him, but, thanks to God, he cannot, poor young man!”

“But are you not ashamed to think such thoughts﻿—you, the pupil of Murjânah Khânum? So immoral!”

“It is my fancy, there! Morality is not our business. We are strictly guarded. One gets a conscience﻿—what you call a soul﻿—when one has children. How droll you are! You talk just like a man. God knows I love you, and should like to be your durrah.” (The word means colleague in the married state.)

Gulbeyzah flung her arms round Barakah. A sound of footsteps in the passage made them turn and peep.

“It is a eunuch!” the Circassian whispered. “He has been there all the time. He attends you like your shadow, have you noticed? How sweet to be so precious; and so respected, for he keeps his distance!”

===Chinese===

“多狠心！你怎么能这样折磨他？”

“他是个男人，你懂的。人对男人起不了对姑娘们那样的怜惜心。倘若他真能爬到这儿来，我说不定会犒赏他；可是，感谢真主，他爬不上来，可怜的年轻人！”

“可想这样的心思，你不害臊么——你，穆尔贾娜·哈努姆调教出来的人？太不道德了！”

“那不过是我的闲情罢了！道德不干我们的事。咱们被看守得严严实实。人要等有了孩子，才长出良心来——就是你们叫作灵魂的东西。你多可笑呀！你说话活脱是个男人。真主知道我爱你，真愿做你的杜拉（durrah）。”（这个词的意思是婚姻里的同侪。）

古尔贝雅扎张开双臂搂住巴拉卡。过道里一阵脚步声，叫她们回头张望。

“是个阉人！”切尔克斯姑娘悄声道。“他一直在那儿。他像影子一样跟着你，你留意到了么？这般金贵多叫人心甜；又这般受敬重——因为他还懂得保持距离！”

===Original===

Barakah preferred these confidences with Gulbeyzah to the endless fuss and noise about the trousseau. The hive was in commotion over the approaching marriage; angry, Gulbeyzah told her, with the Pasha for his wish to shear the festival of ancient ceremonies regarded as the woman’s right. When approached upon this subject in a crowded conclave, she said that she was anxious to conform to all their customs﻿—an answer which was hailed with cries of triumph.

Mrs. Cameron appeared one afternoon, the Consul’s envoy, to ascertain that all was well with the perverted girl. She was shown to the stateroom, and there regaled with tea in glasses and sweet biscuits, in what was thought to be the English manner. The ladies pestered her with eager questions, persisting, despite frank denials, in regarding her as a near and dear relation of the bride. She glanced reproachfully at Barakah from time to time. “You’re quite at home with them, I see,” she said at parting. “It sounds unkind, but I must say I wish you weren’t. It is a fall for any woman bred as you were. How can you put that kohl round your eyes?﻿ ﻿… Goodbye, my dear, and don’t forget our compact.”

The visit leaving an unpleasant, sad impression, Barakah withdrew to her own room, alleging headache. She was lying on her bed with eyes half closed, endeavouring to lay the ghost of former days, when someone entered without knocking, shut the door with care, and crept towards her. It was a strange old woman. She sidled up with much grimacing; whispered “Yûsuf,” laid her shrivelled cheek upon her hand; “Yûsuf,” again, and smacked her lips delectably; “Yûsuf Bey, thy bridegroom,” and made the motion of embracing with ecstatic grins.

===Chinese===

比起围着嫁妆没完没了的忙乱喧腾，巴拉卡更爱同古尔贝雅扎说这些体己话。这蜂房般的宅院因迫近的婚事一片骚动；古尔贝雅扎告诉她，众女眷正为帕夏恼火，因为他想把庆典里自古相传、被视作女眷权利的老礼数一概裁去。在一回济济一堂的聚议上，众人拿这个话头来探她的口风，她说自己巴不得遵从她们的一切风俗——这个回答顿时赢得一片得胜的欢呼。

一天下午，卡梅伦夫人露面了，是领事的使者，来察看那误入歧途的姑娘是否一切安好。众人把她让进正厅，用玻璃杯盛着的茶和甜饼干款待她——那被认为是英国做派。夫人们拿热切的问题纠缠她，尽管她再三坦率否认，众人仍旧一口咬定她是新娘的近亲密眷。她不时责备地瞥巴拉卡一眼。“我看你跟她们处得挺自在，”临别时她说。“这话听着不客气，可我必须说，我但愿你不是这样。对任何一个像你这样教养出来的女人，这都是堕落。你怎么能把那种眼墨涂在眼圈上？……再会，亲爱的，别忘了咱们的约定。”

这一趟来访留下不快而伤感的印象，巴拉卡推说头疼，退回了自己房里。她半阖着眼躺在床上，正竭力想安顿旧日的幽灵，忽有人不叩门便走了进来，小心地带上门，蹑手蹑脚朝她挨近。是个面生的老妇人。她挤眉弄眼地侧身挨过来；悄声道“优素福”，把干瘪的脸颊贴在她手上；又一声“优素福”，咂着嘴，津津有味；再唤“优素福·贝伊，你的新郎”，随即喜不自胜地咧开嘴，做出拥抱的姿势。

===Original===

Barakah grew interested. She longed to see the man she was to marry and, fresh from Mrs. Cameron’s reproach, was feeling reckless. She tried to question the old woman, but without result. The crone kept nodding, “Yûsuf Bey” and “Come.” She had brought with her a habbarah and mouth-veil, which Barakah put on by her direction. Then they stole forth, the temptress in high glee.

But they had not made ten steps in the hall before two eunuchs pounced on them and stared into their eyes. One beat the hag, whose screams were frightful. The other, smiling, dragged back Barakah, pushed her inside her room and locked the door.

The meaning of the whole adventure remained dark to her. Gulbeyzah, when informed of it, declared that the old woman could not have been employed by Yûsuf, who was much too honourable and obedient to his father to indulge in such low games. She ascribed the incident to machinations of the lady Fitnah, beheld a plot to lure the English girl to some lone place, there to be ravished if not slain. Barakah laughed at such wild fancies. That Yûsuf’s mother did not like her much was plain to see; she had doubtless cherished other projects for her firstborn; but to impute the thought of crime to her was too absurd.

“I bring good news,” Gulbeyzah said to change the subject. “The Pasha has granted us the visit to the bath with you. He has engaged the best musicians and some famous dancers, and all the maidens of good houses are to be invited Oh, what joy!”

===Chinese===

巴拉卡来了兴致。她渴望见一见将要嫁的那个男人，何况刚挨过卡梅伦夫人一顿责备，正自无所忌惮。她试着盘问那老妇人，却毫无结果。老妪只顾点头：“优素福·贝伊”，“来”。她随身带来一件哈巴拉（habbarah，罩袍）和一方口纱，巴拉卡依她的指点穿戴起来。随后两人偷偷溜了出去，那引诱人的老太婆乐得心花怒放。

可两人在大厅里还没走上十步，两个阉人便扑将上来，直盯住她们的眼睛。一个动手揍那老婆子，她的尖叫凄厉骇人。另一个笑吟吟地把巴拉卡拉回来，推进她房里，锁上了门。

这场历险究竟是什么意思，她始终参不透。古尔贝雅扎听了始末，断言那老妇人决计不会是优素福雇来的——他太体面、太孝顺父亲，断不会沉溺于这等下作的把戏。她把这事归因于菲特娜夫人的阴谋诡计，看出这是一条毒计：要把这英国姑娘诱到什么冷僻去处，纵不死也要遭玷污。巴拉卡只笑这般异想天开。优素福的母亲不太喜欢她，这是明摆着的；她多半曾为长子另有过盘算；可把犯罪的念头栽到她头上，就未免太荒唐了。

“我带来了好消息，”古尔贝雅扎换了个话头说。“帕夏恩准了我们同你一起赴浴场的那一遭。他请了最好的乐师和几位有名的舞娘，还要把好人家的姑娘们统统请来。哦，多叫人欢喜！”

## VII / 七

===Original===

The party at the bath with all its ritual was one of the ordeals which Muhammad Pasha had wished to spare the English girl. As a man he hated all the pranks that women play alone, and deemed them of necessity immodest. But the feeling roused in the harem was too intense for him; and as Barakah, he was told, herself desired the entertainment, he could adduce no cogent reason for refusal. The place in the haremlik being ill adapted to a large assembly, he hired the finest of the public baths for the occasion. The dependants of the household clamouring for a procession through the streets, he gave them one, putting in place of Barakah a humbler bride whose nuptials would be celebrated at his cost.

About the first hour after noon, the bride of Yûsuf left the house, sped by the ululations of the whole harem. In a carriage with the Pasha’s nieces and Gulbeyzah, she was driven through the streets to the Hammam. There, at the entrance, stood two eunuchs, and in the antechamber many women-servants of the Pasha’s house. The ladies on arrival were conducted to a second anteroom and there divested of all clothing. Each put on a pair of clogs and had her hair tied up in an embroidered kerchief. While they were disrobing, other veiled ones entered who laughed heartily at Barakah’s confusion. The procession of the humbler bride had arrived some minutes since, they were informed.

The elder of the Pasha’s nieces and Gulbeyzah took each a hand of Barakah and led her on from room to room, pausing in each to get accustomed to the growing warmth. Suddenly they came upon a noisy crowd. Two shiny negresses sprang forth, and, singling out the bride, lifted her up and bore her to a corner of the hall, beneath a tap. They flung her on her back. Seeing a razor flash, she uttered shriek on shriek the while they fell to rubbing, making her joints crack, kneading her very bones with their hard fingers. With eyes half blind with soapsuds, she beheld a wreath of naked figures moving round her in a kind of dance. The wall and vaulted ceiling of the building sweated. The windows were high up and gave no light; there entered not a whiff of outer air. A pulse beat at her temples. She felt suffocated.

===Chinese===

携着全套礼数的浴场之会，原是穆罕默德·帕夏有心替这英国姑娘免去的一场磨难。身为男人，他厌恶女人们独处时玩的一切嬉闹，认定它们势必有失体统。无奈内宅里激起的情绪太过炽烈，他拗不过；又听说巴拉卡本人也盼着这场欢会，他实在提不出一条站得住的理由来回绝。哈雷姆利克里的场地不宜容纳盛大的聚会，他便为这个日子租下了公共浴场里最华贵的一家。宅中的仆众喧嚷着要列队穿街过巷地游行一遭，他也依了，只是让一位出身寒微的新娘顶替巴拉卡的位置，那新人的婚事将由他出资操办。

午后头一个钟点，优素福的新娘离了宅门，满内宅的颤声欢呼为她送行。她同帕夏的几位侄女和古尔贝雅扎坐在一辆马车上，一路穿过街道，被送到了哈曼浴场（Hammam）。那里，入口处站着两名阉人，前厅里聚着帕夏宅中的许多女仆。女眷们一到便被引进第二间前厅，在那里脱尽了衣裳。每人登上一双木屐，头发用绣花帕子包起。她们正脱衣时，又进来几个蒙着面纱的，见了巴拉卡的窘态便放声大笑。她们听说，那位寒微新娘的游行车马，几分钟前已经到了。

帕夏侄女中年长的那位和古尔贝雅扎各牵起巴拉卡的一只手，领着她一间屋一间屋地往里走，每间都略停一停，好让身子渐渐习惯那愈来愈盛的暖热。冷不防，她们撞见了一片喧闹的人丛。两名浑身油亮的黑人女子蹿上前来，一眼挑中新娘，把她举起来，抬到大厅一角的水龙头底下。她们把她仰面朝天地一放。她眼见剃刀一闪，尖叫接着尖叫；她们却已动手搓洗起来，搓得她关节嘎嘎作响，用那硬邦邦的手指揉捏她的骨头。她两眼叫肥皂沫糊得半瞎，只见一圈赤裸的身影绕着她回旋，恍如一场舞蹈。屋子的墙壁和拱顶都渗着汗珠。窗子开得老高，透不进光；外面的空气一丝也进不来。太阳穴上脉搏突突直跳。她觉得透不过气来。

===Original===

At last the women stopped their rubbing, and by playful slaps informed her that her turn was ended. Like a sheep from the shearing she rose up, staggering, intent to flee. But she was caught again and made to sit down while her hair was plaited. Then someone﻿—it was Gulbeyzah﻿—grasped her hand and led her to the other end of the great hall, where were two tanks of water gently steaming. The hall presented a strange spectacle, for it was full of naked figures, ebon and mouse-brown, amber and snow-white. Singers, all naked, sat beside one wall, and hummed and droned and shrilled distractingly.

At a call, “The bride!” the whole crowd rushed on Barakah with ululations. Her shame became acute, an agony. Gulbeyzah led her up to one of the tanks. Someone behind administered a push, and she fell in; when someone else sprawled in upon the top of her. Her head was under water for some seconds. Spluttering, indignant, her throat choked with sobs, she found herself among a group of laughing girls, all colours, who were ducking one another as they splashed about. Gulbeyzah cried, “The butterflies! Look! Look!” and pointed to the smooth stone marge, where all the ripples in the light of smoky cressets were reflected like a thousand fluttering moths. The stir subsiding when all stopped to look, the moths united into one great butterfly, dimly perceived, whose wings beat faint and fainter as the water stilled.

“She has eaten them all! Behold, how fat she is!” cried out Gulbeyzah. “I believe she is just going to have some others. Look!” She plunged, and made fresh ripples. Laughter hailed this sally. A brown girl, lissom as a snake, sprang hard on the facetious one and promptly ducked her.

===Chinese===

到末了，女人们住了搓揉，用戏耍的拍打告诉她：她的这一轮完事了。她像剪过毛的羊一般站起来，摇摇晃晃，一心只想逃走。可她转眼又让人捉住，按着坐下，由人替她编起辫子。随后有人——正是古尔贝雅扎——攥住她的手，把她领到大厅的另一头，那里有两口微微腾着热气的水池。大厅呈现出一幅奇景：满眼赤裸的身影，乌黑的、鼠褐的、琥珀的、雪白的。歌女们也个个赤裸，靠着一面墙坐着，又哼又嗡又尖啸，闹得人心神缭乱。

只听一声高呼“新娘来了！”，整群人便颤声欢呼着朝巴拉卡涌来。她的羞惭陡然尖锐起来，成了苦刑。古尔贝雅扎把她领到其中一口水池跟前。背后有人施了一推，她便栽了进去；随即又有人扑通一声趴到她身上。她的头有好几秒没在水里。她噗噗地呛着水，怒气冲冲，喉咙里哽着呜咽，却发现自己已置身一群笑闹的姑娘中间，肤色形形色色，正互相把对方按进水里，溅着水花嬉闹。古尔贝雅扎喊道：“蝴蝶！瞧！瞧！”一面指着平滑的石沿——灯炬冒着烟，光晕里，一圈圈涟漪映在石上，宛如千百只振翅的飞蛾。待众人停闹观望、波动平息，那些飞蛾便合而为一，成了一只影影绰绰的巨蝶，随着水面归于沉静，它的翅膀扇动得愈来愈微弱。

“她把蝴蝶全吞下肚了！瞧瞧，她多肥呀！”古尔贝雅扎喊道。“我看她马上又要吞几只了。瞧！”她纵身一扎，搅起新的涟漪。这句俏皮话博得一片笑声。一个棕色的姑娘，柔韧得像条蛇，猛地扑到这位说笑的人身上，当场把她按进了水里。

===Original===

Angry, humiliated, feeling lost eternally, Barakah scrambled out to face a row of grinning, dancing hags. They and the shameless girls, the fiendish music, the sweating walls, the fumes of incense hiding the high roof, combined to make her fancy she was underneath the earth assisting at an orgy of malignant jinn.

Someone smote her from behind. She turned round angrily. A fair-haired girl was running. She ran after her. Another struck her lightly as she ran. She turned again. A third sprang on her, pinioned both her arms and kissed her on the mouth, amid applause. Then first she realized that it was all a game; the girls were friendly. In the magnitude of her relief, her shyness vanished. She soon led the romp. It was one long dancing game of follow-my-leader, varied with moods of hide-and-seek and leapfrog. All the while the singers kept up their wild din, the hired dancers never ceased their weird contortions.

Afterwards, when they were all rubbed down and clothed again, there was a feast of most delicious dainties in the anterooms, and Barakah was introduced to her late playfellows, transformed as if by magic to polite young ladies. Every one of them, she found, had brought a present for her. She chattered merrily in French, and ate and drank with appetite unknown before. Driving home in the carriage with three delicately perfumed maidens, whose soft hands caressed her, she experienced a blissful languor, like thanksgiving.

===Chinese===

巴拉卡又恼又辱，只觉万劫不复，手脚并用地爬出水来，迎面却是一排咧着嘴、扭摆着跳舞的丑老婆子。她们，连同那些不知羞耻的姑娘、邪魔般的音乐、淌汗的墙壁、遮住高穹的袅袅香烟，合起来叫她恍惚觉得自己置身地底，正在一场恶灵（jinn）的纵乐狂欢之中。

有人从背后打了她一下。她怒冲冲地回过身。一个金发姑娘正在跑开。她拔腿去追。跑动间另一个又轻轻拍了她一下。她再回身。第三个扑上来，拢住她的双臂，当着喝彩的众人亲了亲她的嘴。直到这时她才醒悟，这一切全是游戏；姑娘们都怀着好意。那如释重负之感如此强烈，她的羞怯顿时烟消云散。不多时，她已领头嬉闹起来。整个是一场长长的“跟着领头人”的舞蹈游戏，间或穿插着捉迷藏和跳山羊的花样。歌女们自始至终喧闹不停，雇来的舞娘那怪异的扭摆也一刻不歇。

随后，待众人都擦拭停当、重新穿好衣裳，前厅里便摆开一席极其精美的甜点盛宴，有人把巴拉卡引见给方才的玩伴——她们仿佛被魔法点化，个个成了彬彬有礼的年轻淑女。她发现，她们人人都给她备下了一件礼物。她用法语叽叽喳喳谈得快活，吃喝的胃口是平生未有的好。归程的马车上，与她同坐的是三位熏着淡淡香气的少女，她们的软手不住爱抚她，她体味到一种幸福酥软的慵懒，恍如发自心底的感恩。

## VIII / 八

===Original===

Meanwhile the anguish of the lady Fitnah had become unbearable. The beating she had received, which kept her silent, was only part of the injustice which prevailed against her. She alone, she had assurance, was vouchsafed clear vision of the horror of this marriage; all the rest were drugged and blinded by the creature’s spells. She had heard of Frankish women, who were barren, holding men entranced for life, thus ending families; and had no doubt at all but this was one of them. A woman of volcanic passions, always righteous, for her to look on evil was to seek to slay it.

She said, “The fiend will suck my Yûsuf’s life out and then vanish.”

Her group of flatterers replied:

“Alas, yes! She will suck him as one sucks an orange, and go her way refreshed,” giving the sad mother a distracting picture of her firstborn as an empty orange-skin flung in the gutter among other refuse.

She cried, “By Allah! she shall die!”

The sycophants replied, “Yes, by thy blessed womb, she shall﻿—an awful death,” and began to meditate the form that death should take.

===Chinese===

与此同时，菲特娜夫人的悲苦已到了难以忍受的地步。她挨的那顿鞭打虽然教她缄了口，却不过是压到她头上的一切不公中的一桩。她深信，唯独她一人蒙赐慧眼，识得这桩婚事何其可怖；其余众人尽教那妖物的咒术灌了迷魂汤、蒙瞎了眼。她曾听说有些法兰克女人，生不出儿女，却能把男人迷住一世，就此教家族绝嗣；她毫不怀疑，这一个正是那路人。她是个火山般性情的妇人，又永远自居正义——在她，看见邪恶，便等于要去诛灭邪恶。

她说：“那妖妇要吸干我优素福的性命，然后遁形而去。”

她那一伙谄媚之徒答道：

“唉，正是！她会像吮橙子那样把他吮干，再神清气爽地扬长而去，”——这话给那伤心的母亲送上一幅揪心的图景：她的头生儿子成了一张掏空的橙子皮，给扔在街沟里，混在别的秽物中间。

她喊道：“凭安拉起誓！她非死不可！”

帮闲们应道：“是，凭你那蒙福的胎宫，她必死——死得惨不堪言，”说罢便开始琢磨，这死该是个什么死法。

===Original===

“But she has islamed,” one objected.

“Who knows if she has really islamed?” was the answer. “Our lord the Pasha is bewitched. He has forgone in her case every ordeal that might test her faith. It is ascertained that she is barren and will drink the bridegroom’s life. Woe! Woe! The end of a most noble race!”

Inspired by hatred of iniquity, fanned and encouraged by her little court, the anguished mother had made sure arrangements for the English girl’s dishonour, thinking no crime to vilify so bad a thing. The scheme, alas! had been frustrated by the eunuchs; whose vigilance redoubled the poor lady’s grief. What dreadful magic must reside in that foul creature to make the Pasha guard her like a pearl? to make poor Yûsuf cling to her and shun his mother? Her cronies recommended her to summon negresses, of those who have familiar intercourse with demons, and hold the mystic ceremony called a zâr﻿—the latest novelty. But Fitnah Khânum feared the Pasha, who denounced such consolations as against religion. She was in despair. The hours flew by towards the wedding; and she, perceiving all its horror, had no power to stop it.

On the very morning of the day appointed for the final ceremonies, she received two visitors, not in her own room, but in a dirty closet used for rubbish. The first to enter was the same old woman who had lured Barakah from her chamber with the name of Yûsuf. The second, throwing off the veil, revealed a goatish face with pointed ears beneath a foul white skullcap. It was Abu Sumûm, the most renowned of sorcerers.

===Chinese===

“可她已经皈依伊斯兰了，”有一人提出异议。

“谁知道她是不是真皈依了伊斯兰？”众人这般回答。“我们的老爷帕夏着了魔。凡是可考验她信仰的试炼，他一概替她免了。已经查明她不能生育，还要吸干新郎的性命。哀哉！哀哉！一个何等高贵的门第，就此断绝！”

这位愁肠百结的母亲，怀着对奸邪的憎恨，又被她那小小的宫廷煽风撺掇，已然布置停当，要叫那英国姑娘身败名裂——她认定，糟践这般歹毒之物算不得罪孽。可惜那计谋啊！竟教阉人们搅坏了；他们的严防，更使这可怜夫人的悲苦倍增。那腌臜妖物身上得藏着多么骇人的法术，才能教帕夏把她当珍珠一般守护？才能教可怜的优素福黏着她还避着亲娘？她的老姐妹们劝她唤几个黑人女子来——专挑那类与魔鬼过从甚密的——行一场称作扎尔（zâr）的神秘仪式，那是眼下方兴的新鲜玩意儿。可菲特娜·哈努姆惧怕帕夏，帕夏公然斥责这类慰藉有违教门。她陷入了绝望。时辰朝着婚期飞逝而去；她呢，眼看那万般恐怖，却无力阻止。

就在择定举行最后仪典的那天清早，她会见了两位来客——不在自己房里，而在一间堆放破烂的肮脏小屋里。先进来的，正是先前拿优素福之名把巴拉卡诱出闺房的那个老妪。第二个一揭下面纱，露出一张山羊似的脸，尖耳朵支棱在一顶腌臜的白色便帽底下。来者正是阿布·苏穆姆（Abu Sumûm），术士中最负盛名的一位。

===Original===

He spread out his hands and chanted:

> “In the name of Allah, Er Rahmân, Er Rahîm,
 

 Who taught the words of might to Suleymân el Hakîm,
 

 And gave the seal of power into his hand,
 

 Lo, here I stand,
 

 Abu Sumûm, your humble servant to command!
 

 Would you love-potions, I can give you those
 

 Will bring the loved one to your feet though walls oppose
 

 And all the doors be guarded by his foes.
 

 Or have you enemies, but name their names
 

 And I will torture them with hellish flames.
 

 Wouldst thou their death? I’ll write a potent spell
 

 Upon an ass’s thighbone, hide it well
 

 Beneath the threshold where they dwell.
 

 Wouldst thou their madness? I will tie their mind
 

 To some low creature of a restless kind,
 

 A bird or fish, that when it moves they rage,
 

 And when it rests their fury they assuage;
 

 And none shall know the secret saving I,
 

 So that for lack of remedy they die.
 

 Abu Sumûm the wily one I am,
 

 State but your need of me and so﻿—Salâm!”

===Chinese===

他摊开双手，朗声念诵起来：

> “奉安拉之名，奉拉赫曼、拉希姆之名，
 

 他曾将威力真言授予智者苏莱曼，
 

 又把权柄之印交进他的手掌，
 

 看哪，我站定在此方，
 

 阿布·苏穆姆，您卑微的仆人听候差遣！
 

 您若要媚药，我这里自有调制，
 

 管教心上人破墙来投您脚下，
 

 哪怕重重门户都由他仇家把守。
 

 您若是有仇敌，只消报出姓名，
 

 我便用地狱烈焰将他们煎熬。
 

 要他们死？我自会书一道猛咒，
 

 写在驴子的腿骨上，藏得严实，
 

 埋进他们居所的门槛之下。
 

 要他们疯？我便把他们的心神
 

 缚上一头低贱躁动的小活物，
 

 或鸟或鱼：它一动，他们便狂怒，
 

 它一歇，他们的暴怒才得平伏；
 

 这秘密除了我无人知晓，
 

 教他们求救无门，唯有殒命。
 

 我就是那诡诈多智的阿布·苏穆姆，
 

 道一声您的差遣，就此——萨拉姆（Salâm）！”

===Original===

Having concluded this doggerel, setting forth his stock-in-trade, the wizard stood with arms crossed, grinning widely.

“I have an enemy,” faltered the lady, “and she is dreadful, being a jinniyeh, and no child of Adam.”

“Think not to instruct me,” said the warlock. “Nothing uncanny comes to Masr, but my hosts of servants who are in the air inform me instantly. Ah, if it is the Englishwoman thou opposest, have a care, for she is full of art, having attained the secret of invisibility, of self-protection, and also of transforming people into dogs. Now, what, I ask, dost thou require of me exactly﻿—a potion that shall make her love thee, or her madness, or a wasting illness?”

“Nothing, nothing, save her instant death,” sobbed Fitnah﻿—“the wedding is today﻿—and then take all my wealth.”

===Chinese===

这段把自家看家行货一一道来的打油诗念罢，术士便抱着双臂站在那里，咧开大嘴笑。

“我有个仇家，”夫人支支吾吾地说，“她可怖至极，是个女灵（jinniyeh），并非亚当的儿女。”

“别想教我本事，”术士说。“但凡有邪祟来到埃及（Masr），都瞒不过我——我那些布满空中的大队仆从，立刻便会来报信。啊，你若要对付的正是那个英国女人，那可得留神，她一身的法术，已参透隐身之秘、护身之秘，还兼一门把人变作狗的秘法。如今我问你，你到底要我做什么——是要一味叫她爱你的药，还是要她发疯，还是要一场耗干元气的痼疾？”

“什么都不要，什么都不要，只要她立刻就死，”菲特娜呜咽道——“婚礼就在今天——事成之后，我的家财尽数归你。”

===Original===

“By thy leave, lady,” cried the wizard, much offended, “I am not him thou seekest! Send for an assassin! My business is with art and not brutality. Find out some chopper-up of wood: I am a carver!”

“But I know of no assassin! How can we women find and bring one hither? O Abu Sumûm, be generous, for Allah’s love!”

“Hear the excellent lady, the very mother of kindness! Hear her, O Abu Sumûm! Behold her sufferings! Grant her petition, for the love of Allah, and our Lord reward thee!” pleaded the old woman who had brought him in.

“I know not. It is not my line of business. And yet, I bethink me, there is art in it,” muttered the sorcerer, relenting visibly﻿—“much art, for she is the most skilful witch on earth; and no one else in Masr, under Allah, could hope to overcome her﻿—Ha! What is this?” He raised his hand to his right ear, and stood intently listening, as if to something just above him in the air. “I thank thee, O Tarshûshak!﻿—What is this?” He turned to Fitnah with a mien of righteous anger. “My servant tells me she has islamed. Is that true? If so, why not inform me at the first? My time is wasted. If she has islamed it is a crime most heinous to assail her. May Allah﻿—”

===Chinese===

“夫人，恕不奉陪，”术士大为冒犯地叫道，“我不是你要寻的人！打发人去请刺客！我经营的是法术，不是凶蛮。去找个劈柴的粗汉：我是雕琢的匠人！”

“可我认不得什么刺客！我们妇道人家，往哪儿去寻、又怎么带得他来？阿布·苏穆姆啊，发发善心，看在安拉爱的份上！”

“听听这位好夫人，简直是仁慈的化身！听听她吧，阿布·苏穆姆！瞧瞧她受的煎熬！看在安拉爱的份上应允她的恳求吧，愿我主报答你！”领他进来的那个老妪恳求道。

“难说。这不归我这一行管。不过，待我想想，这里头倒有艺术，”术士咕哝道，明显地软了下来——“大有艺术，因为她是天下最巧的女巫；在安拉之下，全埃及没有第二个人敢指望胜她——哈！这是什么？”他抬手凑向右耳，凝神伫立着倾听，仿佛在听就在他头顶上方的空中有什么动静。“谢谢你，塔尔舒沙克（Tarshûshak）！——这是什么？”他转过身来对着菲特娜，一脸义愤填膺的神色。“我的仆从告诉我，她已经皈依伊斯兰了。是真的吗？果真如此，为何不一开始就告诉我？我的时辰白白糟蹋了。她若已皈依伊斯兰，谋害她便是滔天大罪。愿安拉——”

===Original===

“Mercy! O my uncle, mercy!” Both the women flung themselves upon the wizard, stopping his mouth and dragging down his arm upraised to curse them. “Wait but a moment! Only listen! They say that she has islamed, being all bewitched. She has not gone through all the ceremonies. She refuses, and our lord the Pasha, by her spells, supports her. Whether or no, she weds today my firstborn son, and she is barren and will keep him from all other women. Thou shalt have much wealth.”

Again the sorcerer went through the process of relenting visibly. “Allah knows,” he groaned, “it is a cruel task you set me. It will take three days and nights of fasting and seclusion spent in ceaseless study, to overcome her servants who are in the air. Not until they are vanquished can I mix the potion, for they would neutralize my spells and make it harmless.”

“But the wedding is today!” wailed Fitnah, out of patience.

“What matter, since her bale is of the lingering sort, and not swift-slaying. Hear what I tell thee! If I fight for thee with demons and obtain the potion, use it not till three whole moons have waxed and waned. Watch how thy son looks; notice his behaviour! It may be she has islamed in good faith.”

===Chinese===

“饶了吧！我的大叔哎，饶了吧！”两个女人一齐扑到术士身上，捂住他的嘴，把他扬起来要诅咒的手臂死命拽下。“且等一等！只管听我说！人家说她皈依了伊斯兰，那是全都着了魔。全套仪轨她并没有行完。她不肯，我们的老爷帕夏中了她的符咒，一味护着她。不管怎样，今天她就要嫁我的长子，她不能生育，还要把他从一切女人身边霸占去。你必有享不尽的财富。”

术士又把那明显心软的做派从头演了一遍。“安拉知道，”他呻吟道，“你们派给我的是桩狠毒的差事。得斋戒闭关三天三夜，不眠不休地钻研，才能降服她那些在空中的仆从。打不赢他们，我就配不出药，因为他们会化解我的符咒，叫它失了灵效。”

“可婚礼就在今天啊！”菲特娜哭喊道，再也按捺不住。

“那有什么要紧？她那祸祟原是慢性缠绵的一路，并非立时夺命的快货。听我吩咐！我若为你与群魔交战、取得了药，须等三轮圆月盈而复亏，方可动用。瞧瞧你儿子的气色；留意他的举止！说不定她是诚心皈依了伊斯兰。”

===Original===

“All that thou wishest, only give the potion!”

“After three days thou shalt have it, by the leave of Allah!”

The sorcerer then changed his tone for one of caution, urging, “The reward, O blessed lady! It is worth much money. And it is usual to give something in advance by way of earnest.”

Fitnah untied a bundle which had lain beside her all the while, and thrust it towards him. It contained the best of all her jewels. Poor lady, all her treasures﻿—nay, her life itself﻿—seemed light to give to save her Yûsuf from that thirsty ghoul. The wizard’s small eyes gloated on the heap.

===Chinese===

“你要怎样都依你，只求把药给我！”

“三天之后你便能到手，若安拉意欲！”

随即术士换上一副谨慎口吻，催促道：“还有谢仪呢，蒙福的夫人！这可是值大价钱的。照规矩，也该先付些定钱，以表诚意。”

菲特娜解开一直搁在身边的一个包袱，朝他推了过去。里面是她全部首饰中最贵重的。可怜的夫人，她所有的珍藏——不，连她的性命本身——只要能救优素福脱离那个嗜血的食尸鬼（ghoul），都显得轻如鸿毛。术士的一双小眼睛直勾勾地贪盯着那堆珠光宝气。

===Original===

“Woe on thee, Abu Sumûm!” cried the old woman. “Art thou not ashamed to take more than is just from so benevolent and kind a lady? Thy heart is of stone, not to be moved to bounty by her pious tears.”

“Silence, woman!” With a dignified and bounteous gesture, the sorcerer pushed back the bag of trinkets, selecting for himself a single ring containing stones of value. “Allah witness, that I did but test the generosity of our good mistress. But, being poor and with some dreadful work before me﻿—having, moreover, risked my two old ears in coming hither﻿—I will, with thy permission, O Most Excellent, accept this trifle. That and thy gracious favour be my only payment!”

Uprising, he resumed again the woman’s headdress, and in a woman’s piping voice enjoined, as he prepared to go, “Forget not to delay three months. A day too soon might cause tremendous evil.”

“Three months﻿—I will remember!” answered Fitnah dutifully; adding beneath her breath, “Three days﻿—too long! I think thou hast a mind to fool me, O thou father of three months! Well, bring thy potion. But first we will essay some common poison without ceremony. Alas for Yûsuf did we wait three months!”

She pressed both hands to her left side as if it pained her.

===Chinese===

“你造孽啊，阿布·苏穆姆！”老妪嚷道。“从这样仁厚和善的夫人手里，拿取超出分内的钱财，你不害臊么？你的心是石头做的，她那样虔诚的眼泪，竟感发不了你半点慷慨。”

“住口，婆娘！”术士做出一个雍容大度的手势，把那一袋首饰推了回去，单给自己挑了一枚镶着宝石的戒指。“安拉作证，我方才不过是试试咱们好主母的雅量。只是，我人穷，前头又有可怕的营生——何况来这一趟，还拿我这两只老耳朵当了赌注——既蒙俯允，至尊的夫人，我便收下这点薄礼。这个，连同您的恩眷，便是我仅有的酬报！”

他站起身来，重新裹上女人的头巾，一面整装欲行，一面拿女人家尖细的嗓音叮嘱道：“别忘了要拖延三个月。早一天动手，都可能招来泼天大祸。”

“三个月——我记下了！”菲特娜恭顺地应道；随即又压着嗓子自言自语：“三天——都嫌太长！我看你是存心耍我，你这‘三个月’老爹！也罢，把你的药带来。可我们先要不拘什么礼数，试试寻常的毒药。真要等满三个月，优素福可就完了！”

她双手按住左肋，仿佛那里作痛。

## X / 十

===Original===

“O wretched day! O death of honour! O calamity! Didst thou not swear to guard my love from danger, O my father? Yet death has reached her﻿—poison! This house is now gehennum. Woe to all of us! O Allah, ease the sorrow of my heart! O Lord, behold me rent in twain﻿—My wife! My mother!”

Yûsuf had burst into the room of the selamlik where his father was transacting business with the steward of his property. Regardless of the stranger’s presence, he gave way to grief and rage, falling prostrate on the pavement, tearing at it with his hands, and biting at it with his teeth convulsively. The steward, a person of discretion, rose at once and asked permission to retire. The Pasha nodded, and, when he was gone, bent over his demented child, inquiring of his cause of grief with heart near broken, for he feared the worst had happened. By dint of patience he elicited the simple facts, which, when he knew them, eased his mind so greatly that he smiled and rendered fervent thanks to the Most High. The Englishwoman was not dead; the poisonous attempt had failed; the vision of an angry Consul, void of decency, transgressing with investigations every man’s intrinsic right to sole and secret jurisdiction in his own harem, raising a scandal far more dreadful than the sad event, receded suddenly.

===Chinese===

“悲惨的日子啊！奇耻大辱啊！大祸临头啊！父亲啊，您不是发过誓，要保护我的爱人不受伤害吗？死亡到底还是追上了她——是毒药！这座宅子如今成了火狱。我们大家全完了！安拉啊，宽解我心头的悲苦吧！主啊，看啊，我被撕成了两半——我的妻子！我的母亲！”

优素福一头冲进塞拉姆利克的一间屋子——他父亲正在那里同他的产业管家议事。他不顾外人在场，任凭悲愤倾泻出来，扑倒在地上，双手抠着地面，牙齿痉挛地啃咬着地砖。管家是个知趣的人，立刻起身请求告退。帕夏点了点头，待人出去后，便俯下身去探问他那神志狂乱的孩子，问伤心的缘由，自己的心也几乎碎了——他唯恐最坏的事已经发生。他耐着性子，好不容易才问出几件简单的实情；一经知晓，心中顿时大为宽解，竟自微笑起来，向至高者热诚称谢。英国女人没有死；下毒的图谋落了空；至于那幅景象——一位怒气冲冲、全无体统的领事，以种种查究侵犯每个男人在自己内宅里独享的、不容窥探的裁断之权，掀起一场远比这桩不幸事件更可怕的风波——倏然退去了。

===Original===

“Be not distressed, my son!” he urged benignly. “Praise God, as I do, that the matter is no worse. Think! a mere plant of jasmine dead in place of her thou lovest. The call is for rejoicing, not for grief. Have patience, O my soul! Control thy spirits!”

“Have patience, sayest thou?” sobbed Yûsuf. “My anguish is more terrible than flesh can bear. My mother, she who bore me, whom I love by nature, has turned my enemy, to poison her by whom alone I live. I hate the murderess of my delight, and would destroy her; but lo! she is my mother, and I can but weep. My soul is torn asunder. All the world is blackened. O Allah, take my life! O Lord, protect me!”

Muhammad Pasha was profoundly moved by this lament. He thanked God for vouchsafing him a son who, in the moment of extreme affliction, could still preserve such justice in his sentiments.

===Chinese===

“别难过，我的孩子！”他慈和地劝道。“跟我一道赞美真主吧，事情没有更糟。想想看！死的不过是一株茉莉，替下了你所爱的人。这时该欢庆，不该悲伤。忍一忍，我的灵魂！克制住你的心绪！”

“忍一忍，您说？”优素福哽咽道。“我的痛苦太可怕，血肉之躯担不起啊。我的母亲——生我养我、我天性所爱的人——变成了我的仇敌，要毒死我唯一靠着她才活着的那个人。我恨这戕害我欢乐的凶手，恨不得亲手毁了她；可是看哪！她是我母亲，我只有哭的份儿。我的灵魂被撕成了两半。整个世界都黑了。安拉啊，收去我的性命吧！主啊，护住我吧！”

穆罕默德·帕夏被这番哭诉深深打动。他感谢真主赐给他这样一个儿子：在极度的苦难关头，情感之中仍能保持这般公正。

===Original===

“Take comfort, O my son! Be thankful that no harm has happened,” he insisted tenderly.

But Yûsuf would not be consoled. The soothing tone enraged him, seeming to make a trifle of his agony. He leapt upon his feet and cried:

“No harm! O Allah! Is it nought then, what I tell thee? Then thou hast no love for me. Thou art my father; thou didst promise to preserve her from my mother’s malice. Thou seest my despair, and yet thou smilest. O Allah, kill me now, for I am orphaned cruelly. Both my parents hate me, and deride my sufferings. I go to my mother Murjânah, who is kind and gracious. She will weep with me.”

===Chinese===

“宽心吧，我的孩子！该感恩的是没有伤着人。”他温存地坚持道。

可优素福怎么也劝不住。那抚慰的声气反倒激怒了他，仿佛把他的苦痛当成了小事一桩。他一跃而起，喊道：

“没有伤着人！安拉啊！那么我告诉您的这些都不算什么了？那您就是不爱我了。您是我父亲；您答应过要护着她，不受我母亲的毒害。您眼见我绝望，却还在笑。安拉啊，现在就杀了我吧，我是被人狠心地弃下的孤儿。我的生身父母都恨我，都笑话我的苦难。我要到我母亲穆尔贾娜那儿去，她仁慈和善。她会陪我一起哭。”

===Original===

And before the older man could grasp his purpose, much less intervene, that victim of a duteous heart had fled the room. After a space of thought the Pasha followed to Murjânah Khânum’s quarters, where he found the young man writhing on a bed of cushions, while his second mother wept with him and prayed.

“Listen, O Yûsuf, O my son!” began the father earnestly. “I have been thinking. Thou and thy bride shall have a house apart﻿—”

But at his voice the young man, foaming at the mouth, sprang up from his couch with teeth and hands clenched in a final spasm, and, flinging up his hands, fell back insensible.

===Chinese===

老人还来不及会过意来，更说不上拦阻，这个被一片孝心驱赶着的苦人儿已经逃出了房间。帕夏沉思了片刻，跟到穆尔贾娜·哈努姆的居处，只见那年轻人正在一榻软垫上翻滚扭动，他的另一位母亲陪着落泪，为他祷告。

“听着，优素福，我的孩子！”父亲恳切地开了口。“我一直在想。你和你的新娘应当另有一处宅子——”

可是听到他的声音，那年轻人白沫涌上嘴角，牙关紧咬、双手紧攥，在一阵最后的痉挛里从榻上直跳起来，随即双臂一扬，仰面栽倒，人事不省。

===Original===

“Go, fetch the leech, the fit will pass, inshallah. Be secret, lest tongues wag to our dishonour,” said Murjânah, and the Pasha went at once to the selamlik, returning with a black slave skilled in surgery. Yûsuf was bled. While assisting in the operation the Pasha asked Murjânah:

“What punishment is meet for her we wot of?”

“Forgiveness, for the love of Allah!” was the answer. “Upbraid her on religious grounds and then forgive her. We know her generous, impulsive nature. Thy sudden kindness will affect her more than blows. Poor soul, she must have suffered very deeply. My slaves inform me that she saw this Englishwoman as a kind of ghoul. Tomorrow, with her nature, she may wish to hug her. Remove the young folks for the present.”

===Chinese===

“去，请医师来，这阵发作会过去的，若安拉意欲。千万保密，免得有人嚼舌，坏了咱们的名声。”穆尔贾娜说。帕夏立刻到塞拉姆利克去了一趟，领回来一个精通外科的黑奴。优素福被放了血。帕夏从旁帮着施行手术，一面问穆尔贾娜：

“咱们心里知道的那个她，该受怎样的惩处？”

“饶恕她吧，看在安拉的情分上！”回答是这么一句。“拿教义的大道理责备她一顿，然后饶恕她。她天性豪爽、性子冲动，咱们是知道的。您这突如其来的宽厚，会比棍棒更打动她。可怜的人，她心里必定受了大苦。我的女奴们告诉我，她把这位英国女人看成了一种食尸鬼。到了明天，以她的性子，说不定倒想搂她亲她呢。眼下先把小两口挪开些时日吧。”

===Original===

“I had thought of that,” rejoined the Pasha. “By Allah, they shall have the garden-house towards Rôdah. Tomorrow I will have the place prepared for them.”

When Yûsuf Bey came back to life he wept anew, but weakly, helplessly. In that condition he was carried to his own apartments by the surgeon, with the Pasha’s help, Murjânah going on before to warn the bride.

This sad procession happened to encounter a slave of Leylah Khânum’s who, hearing Yûsuf’s groans, ran off with screams and told her mistress he was dead. At once the whole harem was filled with wailing. Fitnah Khânum, thunderstruck by the appalling news, defiled her face with dirt and tore her raiment. She rushed shrieking to the bridal chamber, as did every woman and child who by relationship could claim the right to enter. She knelt before the bride, who stood apart, bewildered, and besought her:

===Chinese===

“我已想到这一层了。”帕夏应道。“凭安拉起誓，就把朝罗达（Rôdah）那边去的园宅给他们住。明天我就叫人把那地方给他们收拾出来。”

优素福·贝伊回过神来以后又哭了一场，不过哭得虚弱无力、身不由己。他就这么一副模样，由那外科黑奴在帕夏的帮助下抬回自己的住处去，穆尔贾娜走在头里，先去知会新娘。

这支伤心的队伍偏偏迎面撞上莱拉·哈努姆的一个女奴；她听见优素福的呻吟，一路尖叫着跑回去禀报主母，说他死了。顷刻之间，整个哈雷姆利克充满了号哭。菲特娜·哈努姆被这骇人的消息惊得魂飞天外，抓起泥土污了脸面，撕破了衣裳。她一路尖号着冲向新房，凡沾点亲、按理够格进门的妇人小孩也都跟着涌去。她跪在呆立一旁、满心惶惑的新娘面前，哀告道：

===Original===

“Remove the spell, restore him, for the love of Allah. I sinned. I here confess it. Thou art much too strong for me. Thou, by thy magic, hast turned round the sword to pierce my bosom. I was impatient, I am justly punished. The wisest of mankind advised me I should wait three months. Thou seest how I love thee, how I kneel to thee and kiss thy feet. Accept my life’s devotion: only save him!”

Without seeking for an answer to her prayer, she rose distractedly and went and flung herself upon the bed where Yûsuf lay. He moaned:

“My mother! Oh, alas, thy bitterness! How couldst thou seek to rob me of delight? Behold me dead! Now art thou satisfied? O Lord have mercy on me! O Calamity!”

===Chinese===

“解了这咒吧，让他活转来，看在安拉的情分上。我造了孽，我在这里认了。你远非我能敌。你的妖法把剑调转了头，刺穿了我自己的胸膛。是我性急，我受罚不冤。世上最有智慧的那位劝过我，说该等满三个月。你看我多爱你：我跪在你面前，亲你的脚。收下我毕生的忠心：只求你救活他！”

她不求她答复这番祈告，失魂落魄地站起身，走过去一头扑在优素福躺着的床上。他呻吟道：

“我的母亲！唉，唉，你这份狠心！你怎么竟想夺去我的欢乐？看我死过去了！这下你可称心了？主啊，怜悯我吧！大祸啊！”

===Original===

Blubbering loudly, she implored forgiveness. Soon his arms went round her; they lay, hugging one another, sobbing, cooing, while the spectators wept aloud in tender sympathy. The Pasha’s face was hidden in his pocket-handkerchief. Murjânah Khânum murmured prayers beneath her breath.

“O my despair! my wickedness!” the mother shrieked.

“My grief, my desolation; now my joy!” sobbed Yûsuf.

“O Lord, relieve me, for my heart is bursting,” moaned the Pasha.

===Chinese===

她大声嚎哭着恳求饶恕。不多时，他的手臂已经揽住了她；两人躺着，相互搂抱，又抽噎、又低唤，旁观的人个个动了柔情，放声陪哭。帕夏把脸埋进了手绢。穆尔贾娜·哈努姆低声默诵着祷词。

“啊，我的绝望！我的罪孽！”母亲尖声喊道。

“我的悲伤，我的凄凉；如今我的欢喜！”优素福抽泣道。

“主啊，宽解我吧，我的心要裂开了。”帕夏呻吟道。

===Original===

“Oh, what do I behold. How rapture pains me!” came from bystanders. All, in the selfish orgy of emotion, forgot the terrified and wondering bride, who, understanding not a word of what was said, surveyed a riddle. She asked the Pasha what the matter was. He answered with a hiccup of emotion:

“It is nothing, mademoiselle. It will soon pass. Have no fear!” which only added to her stupefaction.

She had seen such exhibitions in ill-governed nurseries, but never among grownup folks before. To account for all the outcry she imagined some tremendous tragedy, and waited anxiously to learn its nature.

===Chinese===

“啊呀，我这是看见了什么。狂喜竟这样折磨人！”这是旁边看的人发出的。众人全都沉醉在这场自私的、恣情纵泪的大宣泄里，把那吓坏了的、满腹疑惑的新娘忘了个干净；她们说的话她一个字也不懂，眼前只见一个解不开的谜。她问帕夏到底出了什么事。他带着激动得打嗝的声音答道：

“没什么，小姐。很快就会过去的。别害怕！”这话只叫她愈发如坠五里雾中。

这样的场面，她在管教无方的育婴房里见识过，在成人堆里却是头一遭。为了给这一场大呼小叫找个缘故，她想象出了一场天大的惨祸，提心吊胆地等着探明底细。

===Original===

It was close on midnight ere the chamber emptied and, left alone with Yûsuf, she could put her question. Then he told her the whole story with frequent interjection of “Oh, how I suffered!” She learnt that she had narrowly escaped a cruel death. But how her danger bore upon the scenes she had just witnessed, or in what manner they were meant to reassure her, she could not divine. Yûsuf himself bestowed no thought on her predicament, immersed in contemplation of his own emotions. Feeling alone and outcast, she wept a little ere she went to sleep.

In the morning Yûsuf had recovered his accustomed spirits. When she alluded with a shudder to his mother’s wickedness, he bade her have no fear; all that was past. From that day forth his mother would be sure to cherish her. Her mind derived no comfort from that light assurance; it remained perturbed until the Pasha came with tidings of a new arrangement he had made for her and Yûsuf to sojourn in a pleasure-house of his among the suburbs.

===Chinese===

将近午夜，新房里的人才散尽，剩她独自对着优素福，才好把疑问问出口。于是他把事情从头到尾讲给她听，中间不住插进一句“唉，我受了多少苦啊！”她这才知道自己险些惨遭横死。可是她的险境同她方才眼见的那些场面有何相干，那些场面又怎么算得安慰她，她怎么也参不透。优素福自己则全不想想她的处境，一味沉溺在自己的情感里。她只觉孤单、只觉被人抛闪，入睡前掉了几点眼泪。

早晨，优素福已恢复了惯常的兴头。她打着寒噤提起他母亲的歹毒时，他叫她别怕；那一切都过去了。从今往后，他母亲必定疼她。这句轻飘飘的宽慰没能宽她的心；她心里始终惴惴不安，直到帕夏前来报信，说他已替她和优素福作了新的安排，叫两人到他在城郊的一座游乐别业里去住些日子。

## XI / 十一

===Original===

The pleasure-house was a two-storeyed building, much dilapidated, having been unoccupied by the proprietor for many years. The garden, originally made for pleasure by the Pasha’s father, had since been used exclusively for growing vegetables. It was now like several fields with palm trees set at intervals, the whole surrounded by a high mud wall. The Pasha in one day had had the rooms cleaned out, the snakes extracted from their walls by a professional charmer; the next he sent down servants with the furniture, and the same evening Barakah arrived.

The house resembled a gigantic lantern in the blue of night with light exuding from its many lattices. Descending from the harem carriage which had brought her, together with two women and the girl Fatûmah, her own slaves, she was met by Yûsuf, whom she had not seen all day. He introduced to her two men﻿—a new experience, which seemed an earnest of less strict seclusion. One, who bore a torch, bowed low with eyes downcast. He was the gardener. The other﻿—a most honest-looking youth﻿—gazed awestruck at her shrouded form, his large brown eyes dilated to the very utmost, while a vast ecstatic smile bared all his teeth﻿—a smile which told of infinite fidelity.

===Chinese===

这座游乐别业是一栋两层的楼房，久已失修——房主人多年不曾居住了。园子当初是帕夏的父亲开辟的行乐之所，后来一直专用来种菜。如今这园子就像几片田地，棕榈树疏疏落落地立在其间，四周围着一道高高的土墙。帕夏一天之内就叫人把各间屋子打扫了出来，又请了职业的弄蛇人把墙壁里的蛇捉净；第二天他打发仆人把家具送去；当天傍晚，巴拉卡就到了。

夜色湛蓝，宅子像一盏巨大的灯笼，灯光从许多格窗里渗溢出来。她带着两个妇人、小姑娘法图麦——都是她自己的奴仆——下了接她来的女眷马车，迎面遇上了优素福，她已一整天没见着他了。他引她见了两个男人——这在她是桩新鲜事，似乎预示着幽禁将不再那么森严。一个手擎火把，垂着眼深深行礼，他是园丁。另一个——模样极憨厚的少年——敬畏地凝望着她裹在罩袍里的身影，棕色的大眼睛睁到了极处，咧开满口牙齿，笑得如痴如醉——那笑容透着无限的忠心。

===Original===

“His name,” said Yûsuf, “is Ghandûr﻿—my faithful friend. He is your water-carrier, and will be always within call in case you have some errand out of doors.”

Yûsuf then walked apart with the two men, while Sawwâb, the eunuch, showed the lady her apartments. Sawwâb had come as escort to the carriage and returned with it as soon as he had seen her settled comfortably. A leering crone was left to guard propriety, a task which she performed extremely ill on that first evening; for instead of checking the high spirits of the slave-girls, who romped for joy at their release from stricter discipline, she smiled upon their antics, and herself performed a most improper dance before the bride.

For several days Yûsuf remained contented in the house and garden; while Barakah, half-dazed but happy too, beheld him as incarnate passion, not as man. She was the first to tire of loves and doves, and try to talk of something sensible. Yûsuf appeared to think the speech of every day a waste of time between them.

===Chinese===

“他名叫甘杜尔，”优素福说，“是我忠实的朋友。他当你的挑水夫，永远随叫随到，你有什么出门要办的差事，只管找他。”

随后优素福同那两个男人走开去说话，阉人萨瓦布领着女主人去看她的住处。萨瓦布是随车护送来的，见她安顿得舒舒服服，便乘车回去了。留下一个斜眼谄笑的老婆子看守体统——头一个晚上，这差事她当得糟糕透顶：几个女奴刚从更严的管束下放出来，乐得撒欢胡闹，她非但不加管束，反而笑看她们的把戏，自己还在新娘面前跳了一支极不体面的舞。

一连几天，优素福守着宅子和园子，心满意足；巴拉卡也半醉半醒地快活着，在她眼里，他是情爱的化身，而非一个凡人。头一个腻烦了情话呢喃的倒是她，转而想谈些有见识的正经话。优素福却似乎觉得，两人之间说些日常言语，纯属虚掷光阴。

===Original===

Then came the period of tiffs, the fretful wakening. Yûsuf began to deal in sentiment about his mother, proclaiming it a hardship that his wife should still distrust her.

“She is kind and tender﻿—O, how dear to me! Go to her, Barakah! Kneel at her feet, embrace her hands, and she will surely pardon.”

“Pardon? What, pray?” exclaimed the bride indignantly. “It is for her to ask pardon of me whom, kindly recollect, she tried to poison.”

“She is older than you; she is my mother. It behoves you to be modest and submissive towards her. I have forgiven all, and so should you. She is my mother.”

It was a relief one morning when the Pasha came and bore the young man off, declaring jokingly that he would die of too much sweet if he remained immured there longer. Of Barakah he said the same, informing her that Leylah Khânum and Gulbeyzah would call that afternoon to take her out upon a round of visits.

===Chinese===

接着便来了龃龉的时期，一场烦躁的醒悟。优素福开始拿他母亲做起感情的文章，声称妻子至今仍不信任母亲，实在是一桩苦事。

“她仁慈，她温柔——啊，她于我多亲！到她那儿去，巴拉卡！跪在她脚下，捧住她的双手，她一定会饶恕的。”

“饶恕？请问，饶恕什么？”新娘愤愤地叫道。“该求饶恕的是她，不是我。劳您的驾回想一下吧，下过毒想害的可是我。”

“她比你年长；她是我母亲。你对她理当谦恭顺从。我已经饶恕了一切，你也该这样。她是我母亲。”

一天早晨帕夏来了，把年轻人接了走，还打趣说：再幽禁在那里，他怕要甜得腻死。对巴拉卡他也说了同样的话，并告诉她，当天下午莱拉·哈努姆和古尔贝雅扎要来，带她出去作一轮拜会。

===Original===

Then Yûsuf took to being absent all day long, but came home gladly in the evenings, full of love. He volunteered no tidings of his day’s amusements, and when she questioned him about them seemed to think it odd.

“All that is not your business,” he informed her kindly.

She hinted at the pleasures of companionship, the bond of common interests. He laughed, inquiring:

“Are we not companions? Have we not interests in common? You teach me English, and I teach you Arabic; we compare the customs of the races. And we love! Are not these interests much greater than to hear what Fulân said to Zeyd, what Zeyd replied, and whether Hâfiz or Mahmûd obtained the Government appointment? That is the life of men, a passing of the hours till night, when they return to the beloved. If anything of weight befell I should inform you. What pleasure could it give to you to hear repeated the gabble of a lot of people you will never know?”

===Chinese===

打那以后，优素福惯于整日在外，晚上却欢欢喜喜地回家来，满怀爱意。他从不主动讲他白天寻了什么乐子；她问起来，他倒觉得奇怪。

“那些统统不是您的事，”他和气地知会她。

她含蓄地提到伴侣之乐，共同志趣的纽带。他笑了，问道：

“我们难道不是伴侣？难道没有共同的志趣？你教我英语，我教你阿拉伯语；我们比较各族的风俗。还有我们的相爱！这些难道不比听富兰（Fulân）对宰德（Zeyd）说了什么、宰德又答了什么、政府的差事是哈菲兹（Hâfiz）还是马哈茂德（Mahmûd）得了，要大得多吗？男人过的就是那样的日子，把时辰挨到天黑，好回到爱人身边。真有什么要紧事，我自会告诉你。把一堆你永远无缘结识的人的闲话，翻来覆去学给你听，能给你什么乐趣呢？”

===Original===

Perceiving much in Yûsuf’s tastes and conversation which pious English people would have thought ungodly, she gasped a little on discovering he was religious. Attracted by a faith which showed some tolerance of human failings, she was studying the rudiments of El Islam by Yûsuf’s guidance; acquiring prayers and all the rules for saying them, including washings and the proper time and place. Nothing seemed left to the believer’s judgment, it was all laid down. When, at a lesson in prostration, she was moved to laughter, he became quite terrible, and warned her threateningly that in this country any man or woman was likely to be torn in pieces for a hint of blasphemy. The awe she felt was oddly mixed with fascination.

There were details she would not have chosen in her cloistered life, but on the whole it was the happiest that she had ever known. She was waited on hand and foot who had known drudgery; her husband used her as a reigning beauty who, but a few weeks since, had been esteemed uninteresting. Then there were pleasures of society. The Pasha’s carriage often came, with one or other of the ladies and Gulbeyzah, to take her round to call on grand harems. She was received with favour by great ladies. One, a princess, by name Amînah Khânum, insisted on her spending a whole day alone with her.

This dame, though elderly, still dressed to charm. Her rooms were full of European furniture, but she herself sat always on a sofa, smoking a long, old-fashioned pipe with coral mouthpiece.

===Chinese===

她在优素福的趣味和言谈里察觉出许多东西，虔诚的英国人见了必认为渎神；因此发现他竟虔信教门，她不由得小小倒抽了一口气。这门信仰对人的种种弱点颇见宽容，把她吸引了；她正由着优素福引领，研习伊斯兰教的入门功课：学各种祷文和诵祷的全部规程，包括洗浴净礼，以及恰当的时辰与地点。信士的一己判断似乎全无用武之地，一切都规定得死死的。一次学叩拜的功课，她被逗得笑出声来，他登时变得可怕极了，声色俱厉地警告她：在这个国度，无论男女，但凡露出一丝亵渎的口风，都可能被人撕成碎片。她心怀的敬畏之中，奇异地掺着一种心醉神往。

这种深闺生活里，有些细节若由她挑，她是不会要的，但总的说来，这是她生平所知最幸福的日子。她这个尝够了苦役的人，如今事事有人伺候；几个星期之前还被认为乏味无趣的女人，丈夫却把她当作冠绝一时的美人来宠爱。还有社交的乐趣。帕夏的马车时常到来，载着这一位或那一位夫人，加上古尔贝雅扎，接她到各处豪门内宅拜客。贵妇们对她皆另眼相待。有一位公主，名叫阿米娜·哈努姆（Amînah Khânum），执意要她单独陪自己消磨一整天。

这位夫人虽已上了年纪，衣着仍存心讨人欢喜。她的房间里满是欧洲家具，她本人却总坐在一张沙发上，抽着一支旧式的长烟管，烟嘴是珊瑚的。

===Original===

“You are not of the first rank in your own country,” she told Barakah to start with, bluntly; “or you would not be where you are. You do not know the people I have met in France and England, so don’t pretend you do. I value frankness.” It seemed she knew the English pretty thoroughly.

She spoke good French and talked of Western Europe with intelligence, seeming in general to approve its customs. One little speech of hers amazed the visitor, intruding as it did abruptly upon lighter talk:

“The Europeans have degraded love and made life banal. They spread life’s agitation over a vast surface and account it progress; we value depth and stillness. Enlarging each life’s pool, they make it shallow. A woman’s life is of the feelings which are dulled, not quickened, by extensive interests. Their men too suffer, growing superficial, flippant, without depth of character.”

When Barakah retailed this saying to Gulbeyzah, the Circassian sighed: “She knows!” and told a curious story.

It was that years ago a European officer in the Egyptian service had wooed Amînah Khânum secretly; and she had been entirely captivated by his charms. But endeavouring to sound his character, she found him shallow. She made him islam, but his carelessness informed her that conversion meant no more for him than access to her. In the same way she perceived that what he felt for her was nothing more profound than the desire to add a Muslim lady to his list of conquests. The blow was dire, for she was then extremely lovely, and a great examiner of men, having divorced or killed ten husbands. She would not have him tell a tale among his kind, yet could not conquer her intense desire of him. What could she do? She satisfied her heart, and the next morning gave him death in easy form, being well versed in poisons.

===Chinese===

“你在你们本国算不得头等人物，”她劈头对巴拉卡直言道，“不然你也不会落到今天这地步。我在法国和英国结识过什么人，你并不认识，所以别装作认识。我看重坦率。”看来她把英国人摸得透透的。

她法语说得好，谈起西欧来见识分明，总体上似乎也赞成那边的风俗。她有一句短短的议论，突兀地闯进轻快的闲谈里来，叫来客大为惊异：

“欧洲人贬低了爱情，把生活弄得平淡无奇。他们把生命的躁动摊到一片广大的面上，便称之为进步；我们看重的是深度与宁静。他们把每个人的生命之池越拓越宽，也就把它越搅越浅。女人的生命系于情感，而广泛的兴味只会把情感磨钝，不会叫它敏锐。他们的男人也吃苦，变得浅薄、轻浮，性格没了深度。”

巴拉卡把这话转述给古尔贝雅扎，那切尔克斯姑娘叹道：“她是真懂的！”接着讲了一个离奇的故事。

故事说的是多年以前，一个在埃及军中服役的欧洲军官秘密向阿米娜·哈努姆求爱；她也被他的魅力整个儿迷住了。可待她要掂量他的品性时，却发觉此人浅薄。她使他皈依了伊斯兰，然而他那份漫不经心让她明白：改宗于他，不过是亲近她的一道门径。同样她也看出，他对她的情意，深不过想在猎艳的名单上再添一位穆斯林贵妇。这一击非同小可——她那时艳丽绝伦，又是相看男人的大家，先后休掉或杀掉的丈夫已有十位。她不愿让他在同类中间拿她当谈资，却又降伏不了对他的炽烈欲望。她能怎么办呢？她遂了自己的心，第二天早晨，便以一种轻巧的手段赐了他一死——她对诸般毒药无不精通。

===Original===

Barakah cried out in horror; but Gulbeyzah shrugged.

“What else could woman, not a harlot, do? He was an infidel, and would have bragged of her. Ever since then Amînah Khânum has a kindness for the Franks, though she deplores their levity.”

“And would you do the same?”

“One cannot tell beforehand. I am not a princess. Either that or kill myself. May God preserve us from unsanctioned love of all kinds!”

Barakah felt overwhelmed by the intenseness, the tragic vigour of these women, who seemed mild and playful.

===Chinese===

巴拉卡惊骇地叫出声来；古尔贝雅扎只耸了耸肩。

“一个不是娼妇的女人，还能怎么样呢？他是个异教徒，往后必定会拿她去夸口。打那以后，阿米娜·哈努姆对法兰克人一直存着几分好感，虽然她叹惋他们的轻浮。”

“换了你，也会这么做么？”

“不到事头上说不好。我又不是公主。要么那么办，要么自杀。愿真主保佑我们，远离一切不合礼法的情爱！”

巴拉卡只觉被压倒了：这些女人看似温婉戏谑，内里却有这般浓烈、这般惨烈的气性。

===Original===

Mrs. Cameron called at the garden-house one afternoon, and Barakah was proud to give her a real English tea. Except for the costume, which was much richer, and an added glow of happiness, the visitor, she felt convinced, could not detect the slightest change in her. One thing at least was certain, she had not deteriorated, as Mrs. Cameron before the marriage had foretold she would. The visitor was amiable, and made no allusion to the past. Before departing she made Barakah an offer of some knitting wool and needles she had just received from England. The wife of Yûsuf Bey accepted gladly, for she began to feel the weight of idle hands.

The wools arriving an hour later, she debated what to make with them; and, being at the time in English mood, decided on a pair of slippers for her husband. But when she told him of her purpose, he frowned wonderingly, and asked:

“Are you a shoemaker?”

Utterly disconcerted by so apt a question, she tried to paint the beauty of the project, but he could not see it.

“If you want slippers, buy them in the market. It is not your trade. When one like you employs the needle, it is not for use. Ask my mother; she will show you the right work to do.”

He had his own ideas. The coloured wools were given to Fatûmah, who made anklets of them, and other personal adornments, which amused her for a week.

===Chinese===

一天下午，卡梅伦夫人到园宅来访，巴拉卡得意地请她喝了一道地道的英国茶。她深信，除了衣饰华贵了许多、又添了一层幸福的光彩，这位客人绝察觉不出她有半点变化。至少有一点是确凿的：她并没有变坏——婚前卡梅伦夫人可是预言过她会变坏的。客人和蔼可亲，对往事只字不提。临别前，她把刚从英国收到的一些毛线和织针赠给巴拉卡。优素福·贝伊的妻子欣然接受，因为她已开始感到一双闲着的手沉得难受。

一个钟头后毛线送到，她盘算着拿它们织点什么；那时正怀着英国式的心绪，便决意给丈夫织一双拖鞋。可她把打算对他一说，他诧异地皱起眉头，问道：

“你是做鞋匠的么？”

这一句问得再恰当不过，她顿时窘得没了主张，还竭力把这件事的佳妙描画了一番，他却怎么也领略不到。

“你要穿拖鞋，上市场上买就是。那不是你的行当。像你这样的人拿针线，不是图实用的。去问我母亲；她会指点你该做的正经活计。”

他自有他的一套主见。那些彩色毛线都给了法图麦；小姑娘拿它们编了脚镯，又做了些别的贴身饰物，足足玩了一个星期。

===Original===

Deducing from her wish to make him slippers that she found the hours long in his absence, Yûsuf procured her books in French and English. He also brought her a fine musical box, which played dance-music in stentorian tones to the rapture of the slaves, who kept it going all day long. The Pasha came and begged her not to imagine that she was debarred from every pleasure. It would be cruel to confine a damsel of her breeding as strictly as a native of the country. Let her but name her wishes; they should be deferred to. He even threw out hints that she and Yûsuf might possibly see Paris in the coming summer.

Thus exhorted, and encouraged by the sight of women like Amînah Khânum, who seemed to order everyone their way, she forsook the timid attitude which had been hers since marriage, and viewed existence with commanding eyes. The old woman who had been engaged to play propriety, was horrified one day to see her talking barefaced at a window to Ghandûr, the water-carrier. The crone expostulated, coaxed, entreated, and at length, when all proved vain, informed the husband, who, to her utter consternation, laughed.

“Ghandûr?” he cried; “Ghandûr is my right foot,” and immediately applied that member to the beldame’s person.

The old woman did not dare to speak again to Barakah, though the latter plagued her mercilessly, crying “Ghandûr!” here and “Ghandûr!” there, for the treat of seeing her curvet and wring her hands.

===Chinese===

优素福由她想给自己织拖鞋一事推断：他不在家的时辰，她必是嫌长，便给她弄来了法文和英文的书。他还给她带回一只精美的音乐盒，奏起舞曲来声如洪钟，喜得众女奴心花怒放，成天让它响个不停。帕夏也来了，恳请她千万别以为样样欢娱都与她无缘。把一位她这样教养出来的小姐，关禁得同本地闺秀一般严厉，未免残忍。她但有所愿，无不依从。他甚至透出口风：来年夏天，她同优素福说不定能去看看巴黎。

经这样一番勖勉，又眼见阿米娜·哈努姆这类仿佛人人任其调遣的女人，她便抛却了婚后一贯的小心怯懦，用一双发号施令的眼睛看待生活。那雇来撑持体统的老妇，有一天惊骇地撞见她敞着脸面、在窗口同挑水夫甘杜尔说话。老婆子又规劝、又哄诱、又哀求，末了见一概无效，便去禀报丈夫——丈夫竟放声一笑，把她惊得魂飞魄散。

“甘杜尔？”他喊道；“甘杜尔是我的右脚。”说罢即刻把这只脚使到老虔婆身上去。

老妇从此再不敢对巴拉卡进一言，尽管后者毫不留情地作弄她，这里喊一声“甘杜尔！”那里又喊一声“甘杜尔！”，专为看她惊跳绞手的把戏。

===Original===

One morning, after Yûsuf had departed, she grew conscious of a great oppression due to lack of outlet. The feeling had been with her vaguely for some days. Now she knew it for a craving; she must see an English person to revive her fading interest in the strange things around her.

“Ghandûr!” she cried.﻿—He answered “Hâdir!”﻿—“Fetch me a carriage for the fifth hour after noon.”

“Hâdir!” he said again; and from her lattice she saw him speed off on his errand like the wind. There were few carriages for hire in Cairo in those days, and it was necessary to bespeak one early.

“The lady wishes to go out? Shall I accompany her?” cooed the old woman, who was hovering near.

“No. I go alone!”

===Chinese===

一天早晨，优素福出门之后，她觉出一阵莫大的郁压——因为郁积无处宣泄。这感觉已隐隐跟随她好几天了。此刻她认清了，那是一种渴念：她必须见一个英国人，好把她在周遭奇事物上日渐黯淡的兴致重新唤醒。

“甘杜尔！”她喊道。——他应一声“在！”（Hâdir）——“给我雇一辆马车，午后五时用。”

“在！”他又应了一声；她从格窗里望见他一阵风似的飞奔而去。那时候开罗可供雇用的马车还少，得早早预订。

“太太要出门？老身陪您去好么？”老妇柔声咕哝道，她正在近旁打转。

“不。我一个人去！”

===Original===

“I had better accompany the lady.”

“No, I tell thee!”

The lady stamped her foot, when the duenna shuffled off, wagging her head forebodingly and mumbling.

“How absurd!” thought Barakah. “Haven’t Yûsuf and the Pasha told me twenty times that women, in the kind of shroud they make us wear, can go anywhere alone without attracting notice?”

When the carriage came﻿—a hooded one﻿—she sallied forth, correctly veiled, escorted by Ghandûr, who, seeing no one with her, asked leave to mount the box beside the driver. She gave it, feeling sure that the old woman was watching the departure through some upper lattice. Ghandûr sprang up with a delighted grin, quite rigid with the pride of high preferment.

===Chinese===

“老身还是陪着太太的好。”

“不，我告诉你！”

太太跺了跺脚，陪媪这才趿着脚步走开，预兆不祥地摇着头，嘴里念念有词。

“多荒唐！”巴拉卡想。“优素福和帕夏不是跟我说过二十遍了么——女人裹在他们叫我们穿的那种尸衣似的袍子里，独自去哪儿都不惹眼？”

马车来了——一辆带篷的——她按规矩蒙好面纱，出得门来，由甘杜尔护送；他见没有别人随行，便请求准他坐上车夫旁边的驭座。她准了，心里断定那老妇正从某扇高处的格窗里张望这场出发。甘杜尔咧嘴一笑，一跃而上，那份高升的荣耀使他挺得笔直，浑身发僵。

## XII / 十二

===Original===

In the sandy lane outside the Camerons’ garden-gate some carriages already waited; a saddle-horse or two and many donkeys, all in charge of servants, twitched their ears and swished their tails in the deep shadow by the wall. Barakah felt disappointed and annoyed. It seemed that she had lighted on a great reception, when her desire had been a quiet chat with Mrs. Cameron. Prevision of Ghandûr’s amazement if she gave the order to turn back, and the satisfaction which her quick return would give the mother of propriety, made her go on; but she determined to stay only a few minutes and then walk home, the evening being cool, to spend the time. With this in view, upon alighting she gave money to Ghandûr, bidding him dismiss the carriage and himself go home. He made a good deal of remonstrance, but at last submitted, understanding that the people of the house would furnish means of transit. He considered it his place, however, to remain in waiting.

Barakah then went in, much hampered by the stare of squatting servants which seemed to cling like fetters to her ankles. A Berberi butler ushered her into the drawing-room and announced her with the single word:

“Harem.”

===Chinese===

卡梅伦家花园门外那条沙土巷子里，已经停着几辆马车；一两匹鞍马和许多头驴，都由仆人们照看着，在墙根的浓影里扇动耳朵、甩着尾巴。巴拉卡又失望又气恼。看来她是撞上一场盛大的招待会了，可她原想的是同卡梅伦夫人安安静静聊聊天。但一想到倘若下令掉头回去、甘杜尔该会多么错愕，一想到她这样快快返回将给那位看守体统的老妇多大的满足，她便只好往前走；不过她拿定主意只待几分钟，然后就趁着傍晚凉爽步行回家，把这段时光消磨在路上。为着这个盘算，下车时她把钱交给甘杜尔，吩咐他打发马车回去，自己也回家去。他絮絮叨叨劝了许多，末了还是依了，心想这家人家总会替她备好回去的脚力。只是他认定，留下来听候差遣仍是他的本分。

巴拉卡这才走进门去，蹲坐一旁的仆人们盯着她看，那目光像镣铐似的缠在她的脚踝上，叫她举步维艰。一个柏柏里男管家领她进了客厅，只用一个词通报她的到来：

“女眷。”

===Original===

The room was even fuller than she had expected. Her entrance seemed to cause a great sensation. Her heart sank, there was singing in her ears; she encountered all those faces with a sense of drowning. Moving mechanically in a trance of apprehension, it was with surprise a minute later that she found herself ensconced in a deck-chair beside an open window, alive and quite uninjured, though her pulse beat high. She removed her mouth-veil then and looked about her. It seemed to be a gathering of the whole English colony, with the addition of some French and German ladies. The Consul, her aversion, was talking with some other men, who formed a standing group. He took no notice of her, rather pointedly. The women, thirty at the least, kept up a din of chatter.

The hostess came and introduced her to the ladies near her. Though the manner in which this was done was very kind, Barakah felt that Mrs. Cameron disliked her coming. That lady looked upon her as a fallen creature, to be visited and seen occasionally out of charity, no longer to be classed with English women. The prejudice stung Barakah to downright impudence. Abashment left her. She began to chatter and laugh loudly just to let her hostess know that she was somebody. Sipping her tea, she talked of harem life, deriding the false notions which prevailed concerning it. It was perfectly delightful, not a bit what Europeans thought. She proceeded to retail her own experiences. In a trice she gathered half a score of eager listeners.

But is not this or that the case invariably? they inquired. She was able to confute them always, with amusing instances. She sank her voice, the listening heads drew nearer; there were stifled giggles. Certain stories she had picked up from Gulbeyzah were quite killing. She told of the old woman who was set to guard her﻿—“an Oriental Mrs. Grundy,” she assured them﻿—and her horror at her going out alone that afternoon.

===Chinese===

屋里的人比她预想的还要满。她的进门似乎引起了一场大轰动。她的心往下一沉，耳边嗡嗡作响；迎着那么多张面孔，她有一种没顶之感。她在惶惑的恍惚中机械地挪动着，一分钟后竟惊讶地发觉，自己已在一扇敞开的窗边安坐进一张帆布躺椅，人活着，毫发无伤，只是脉搏跳得厉害。她这才摘下口纱，环顾四周。来的像是英国侨民的全员聚会，另外添了几位法国和德国的太太。领事——她一见就厌憎的人——正同另外几个男人说话，那几个人站着围作一小堆。他没有理睬她，分明是有意的。太太们至少有三十位，叽叽喳喳闹成一片。

女主人走过来，把她介绍给近旁的太太们。这事做得虽然十分和气，巴拉卡却觉出卡梅伦夫人并不喜欢她来。那位夫人把她看作堕落的女人，出于慈善还可以偶尔探望探望，却再不能列入英国女人之中了。这偏见刺得巴拉卡索性放肆到底。羞窘离开了她。她高声谈笑起来，偏要让女主人知道她也是个人物。她啜着茶，大谈深闺生活，讥笑外间流行的那一切谬见。那日子快活极了，跟欧洲人想象的毫不相干。她接着把自己亲历的事一件件兜售出去。转眼间她身边就拢起了十来个听得入迷的人。

“可这样的、那样的事，难道不向来就是如此么？”她们问。她总能搬出有趣的事例，把她们一一驳倒。她压低嗓音，听着的人头凑拢过来；一阵阵压住的嗤笑。她从古尔贝雅扎那里听来的几个故事，实在叫人笑破肚皮。她讲起那个派来看守她的老妇人——她向她们担保，那是一位“东方的格兰迪太太”（Mrs. Grundy，英国戏剧里拘守体统、好议人长短的典型人物）——又讲起当天下午她独自出门时，老婆子那份大惊失色。

===Original===

“But my husband doesn’t mind a bit, of course. The dear man lets me do just what I like. It is only middle-class people nowadays who are strict about seclusion.﻿ ﻿… Oh, by the way, do you know Princess Amînah?﻿ ﻿…”

She had never in her life talked so effectively. The stored frivolity of weeks was spent in one short hour; while with the tail of an eye she noted Mrs. Cameron’s disgust at her small social triumph, the shrugs and glances she exchanged with her own kind.

While her success was at its height, she readjusted her white muslin mouth-veil and got up to go.

“Thank you for a most delightful hour,” she gushed at taking leave, receiving in reply a look which plainly said: “You have deteriorated.”

===Chinese===

“不过我丈夫当然一点儿也不在意。那可爱的人儿事事都由着我。如今还在深闺禁锢上顶真的，不过是些中等人家罢了。……哦，对了，你们认得阿米娜公主么？……”

她生平从没把话讲得这样奏效。积攒了好几个星期的轻浮话，在一个短短的钟头里花了个干净；她一边说，一边用眼角留意卡梅伦夫人对她这点小小社交得意的厌恶，留意她同自己的同类交换的耸肩和眼色。

正当她的成功达于顶点，她重新掩好白色细纱的口纱，起身要走。

“多谢您，这真是愉快极了的一个钟头。”临别时她热切地絮叨着，得到的回答却是清清楚楚的一瞥，那一瞥分明在说：“你变坏了。”

===Original===

Going out upon the wave of her excitement, she suddenly remembered that she had dismissed her carriage. It was no matter. The distance to be traversed was no more than half a mile, the road a straight one, shady at that hour. The little walk would serve to cool her wits.

But Ghandûr, who was squatting by the outer door, sprang up at sight of her. He bade her “Wait!” with a profusion of engaging grins and frantic gestures. Taking her assent for granted, when she stopped to argue, he set off down the lane at a great pace, trailing a plume of dust from either heel.

Seeing she still moved on, despite her servant’s warning, the doorkeeper of the house stepped forward and, saluting, begged her to return indoors. When she refused, he shrugged despairingly and with some word which sounded like an oath went back to his own seat. The waiting grooms and donkey-boys called out, and standing together in a little crowd stared after her. She thought them merely rude.

===Chinese===

乘着兴奋的余波走出门来，她才忽然想起，马车已被自己打发走了。这无关紧要。要走的路不过半英里，一条直路，那个时辰里一路有荫凉。这一小段步行正好让她的头脑凉一凉。

可是甘杜尔正蹲在外门口，一见她便跳起身来。他堆起一连串讨好的笑，比画着急慌的手势，叫她“等一等！”她停下脚步正要分辩，他已认定她应允了，迈开大步顺着巷子飞奔而去，两只脚跟后各拖起一缕烟尘。

见她不顾仆人的警告仍然往前走，这家的看门人抢上前来，行了个礼，恳求她回屋里去。她拒绝了，他便绝望地耸耸肩，抛下一句听着像咒骂的话，回自己的座位上去了。那些候着的马夫和赶驴的童子喊叫起来，聚作一小堆，目送她走远。她只当他们无礼罢了。

===Original===

She moved against the stream of country people returning homeward from their business in the city. They stared at her in passing, and occasionally made remarks which sounded friendly. The dust raised by the trail of robes, and by the donkeys’ hoofs, was some annoyance; but the dust itself became a splendour where the sunset caught it; the shadows were deep blue, enhancing colours of the crowd; and the balm of evening was in every breath she drew. To Barakah, who had not walked for months, the very motion was a comfort. She stepped forward briskly, musing on the scene she had just quitted.

What were those women saying of her now? Mrs. Cameron was no doubt declaiming, and they all agreed with her. Every word that she had said was turned against her. On that perception she was filled with shame. The unkindness, the indecency of holding up her husband’s people to provide amusement for a hostile race appeared unthinkable, the basest treachery. A wave of tenderness for Yûsuf, for Ghandûr, the slave-girls, even the old woman﻿—all the home surroundings﻿—overcame her; while her mind abhorred the frigid, callous English, who had lured her on to make a mock of her. Why should she ever see them more? She hated them. Phrases which had passed her lips ten minutes since were now abominable﻿—a source of shame that could not cease, it seemed, but must flow on forever till the end of time. How had she uttered them? It was their fault for scorning her, for placing her on an unnatural footing, making speech a pitfall. The harem was her natural refuge, her true home. She never wished to quit its shade again.

Thus fiercely musing, she pursued the sandy lane until she reached a point where a road branched off from it at right angles.

===Chinese===

她逆着人流走——乡人们到城里办完了事，正各自回家。他们走过时盯着她瞧，偶尔说上几句，听着倒还和善。袍裾扫起的、驴蹄踏起的尘土，有些恼人；可那尘土本身，被落日映照之处，竟成了辉煌；影子是深蓝色的，把人群的诸般颜色衬得愈加鲜明；她吸入的每一口气里，都有傍晚的温香。对好几个月不曾步行的巴拉卡来说，单是走动本身就是一种安慰。她迈着轻快的步子，一面想着她方才离开的那一场合。

那些女人这会儿正在怎么说她呢？卡梅伦夫人无疑正在振振有词，她们个个都附和她。她说过的话，句句都调转头来对着她自己。一想到这里，她便满心羞愧。把丈夫族中的人抖搂出来，给一个敌对的种族取乐，这样的刻薄、这样的不体面，想来简直不可思议，是最下作的背叛。一阵柔情漫过她——为优素福，为甘杜尔，为那些女奴，甚至为那老妇人——为家中的一切；而她的心憎恶那些冷淡、狠心的英国人，是他们引逗着她，拿自己作了笑柄。往后何必再见他们呢？她恨他们。十分钟前才出口的话，此刻已可憎至极——那羞愧仿佛不会止息，似乎定要绵绵不断地流下去，直到时间的尽头。她怎么会说出那些话来？都怪他们轻看她，把她搁在一个不自然的地位上，使言语成了陷阱。深闺内宅才是她天然的庇护所，她真正的家。她再也不想走出它的荫庇了。

她这样激越地想着，顺着沙土巷一路走去，走到一处，一条大路从巷子上成直角岔了出去。

===Original===

Upon the corner stood a whitewashed shrine, pink in the glow of sunset, the crescent flashing on its egg-like dome; beside it a great tree under whose foliage a crowd of men were sitting out on stools, smoking and drinking coffee in the shade. Some of these took notice of her, pointing rudely, attracting the attention of the others and the passersby. Supposing something wrong with her attire, she quickened step. Her road ran through a village. She heard shouts and laughter. A well-dressed man strode past her from behind, and turning searched her eyes.
