Spurred now by fear, she tried to hurry on; but found herself the centre of a crowd, whose members, moving with her, jabbered, pointed, jeered. One tweaked her habbarah; another seized her arm as if to feel the muscle. Her heart beat loud, her throat was choked with sobs repressed by terror.

The mob grew every moment bolder in its menace. A stalwart peasant-woman barred the way before her, grinning﻿—prepared, it seemed, to pluck away her mouth-veil.

Barakah had paused, cowering, not knowing where to turn for succour, when the shout of a familiar voice relieved the strain and let her tears have vent. Ghandûr came on the scene, leading a saddled ass. His explanations soon dispersed the mob. He lifted her upon the donkey; and in a moment, as things happen in a dream, she was at home again, confronting Yûsuf, who approached the gate as they arrived.

He seemed thunderstruck at her appearance. Hearing Ghandûr’s story, he asked God for help, and raised his arm to strike her. She fell fainting at his feet.

===Chinese===

拐角上立着一座粉刷过的圣祠，在落日的红光里泛着粉色，蛋形圆顶上的新月熠熠闪耀；旁边一株大树，树荫下满坐着一群男人，坐在小凳上，乘着凉抽烟喝咖啡。这里面有几个人留意到她，粗鲁地指指点点，引得其余的人和过路人都来看她。她只当自己的衣装出了差错，便加快了脚步。她走的那条路要穿过一座村庄。她听见了喊叫和哄笑。一个衣冠齐整的男人从背后大步赶过她，又回过身来，直探她的眼睛。这时她已被恐惧催逼，只想快些走完；却发现自己成了一群人的中心，那些人随着她移动，叽里咕噜，指指戳戳，一味嘲弄。有人扯了扯她的哈芭拉罩袍（habbarah）；有人抓住她的胳膊，像要掂掂筋肉的分量。她的心跳得响，喉咙里堵着被恐怖强压下去的呜咽。

那群人的威逼一分钟比一分钟放肆。一个健壮的农妇横身拦在她面前，咧嘴狞笑——瞧那样子，就要伸手扯掉她的口纱。

巴拉卡瑟缩着停住了，不知该向哪里求救，这时一声熟悉的喊叫松了这重紧绷，让她的眼泪得了出口。甘杜尔到了，牵着一头备了鞍的驴。他几句解释便把人群驱散。他把她扶上驴背；转眼之间，事情如在梦里一般，她又到了家，迎面正遇着优素福——他们到的时候，他正走向大门来。

他见了她的样子，如遭雷击。听了甘杜尔的讲述，他祈求真主佑助，扬起手臂就要打她。她晕倒在他的脚下。

## XIII / 十三

===Original===

When Barakah came to herself, she was lying in her bedroom, which was dim and seemed unusually lofty, for her bed was on the floor, and a feeble lamp confined in perforated brass, which gave what light there was, stood down beside it. The pattern of the brass-work, much enlarged, was faintly reproduced on wall and ceiling. She was alone, but from a distance sounds of wailing reached her, and she heard her husband cursing the old woman for neglect of duty.

When she recalled her glee at setting forth that afternoon, the course of subsequent events seemed very cruel. After such misfortunes, consolation was her due; instead of which the house was in commotion, Yûsuf mad. Self-pity overwhelmed her. She was all alone among strange savage beings without sympathy; while those who might have understood and shared her feelings were her enemies. She lay with face down on her pillow, weeping silently.

By and by Yûsuf came into the room. She could tell by his hard breathing he was still enraged. Afraid that he was going to beat her, she lay quiet, as though still unconscious; but in a little while a sob betrayed her. Then his wrath descended. French deserting him, he raved at her in Arabic and Turkish; and her inability to catch his meaning made him angrier. She lay in terror, crying bitterly, replying to such questions as she understood, until his fury sank to lamentation and his French returned.

===Chinese===

巴拉卡苏醒过来时，正躺在自己的卧室里。屋里昏暗，显得异乎寻常地高阔，因为她的床铺就设在地上；一盏微弱的灯收在镂空的铜罩里——屋里仅有的光亮便由它而来——立在床畔的地上。铜器上的花纹放大了许多倍，淡淡地投映在墙壁和天花板上。屋里只有她一个人，但远处传来恸哭声，她还听见丈夫正咒骂那老妇玩忽职守。

想起那天下午动身时的欢天喜地，后来的种种际遇便显得格外残忍。遭了这样的祸事，本该轮到她受人宽慰；偏偏宅子里乱作一团，优素福疯了一般。自怜之情将她淹没。她孤身一人，夹在一群冷漠无情的野蛮生人中间；而那些本可以理解她、与她同感的人，却是她的仇敌。她伏在枕上，无声地哭泣。

不多时优素福进了屋。听他那粗重的呼吸，她知道他还在盛怒之中。她怕他要动手打她，便一动不动地躺着，装作仍未苏醒；可没过多一会儿，一声抽噎把她出卖了。于是他的怒火倾泻下来。法语弃他而去，他用阿拉伯语和土耳其语对她咆哮；她听不懂他的意思，这更叫他火上加火。她惊恐地躺着，哭得伤心，听得懂的问题便答上一句，直到他的狂怒沉降为悲叹，法语也回到了他嘴边。

===Original===

“My honour!” was his cry. “You have betrayed my honour in thus going forth alone. The servants of the English house who know you will send a whisper and a laugh through all the markets. And those who saw you walking in the dust!﻿ ﻿… Have you no shame, no delicacy? What will my father say? The news will kill him! You have killed my father!”

“You do not think of me at all,” sobbed Barakah. “Here have I been insulted, scared to death by your vile people, and you scold me! I wish that I had never seen you. I am so unhappy! In England people would be punished for the things you do. Those horrible men and women who attacked me﻿—”

“May Allah burn them, every one!” cried Yûsuf in fierce Arabic. “Gladly would I pluck out all their tongues! They witnessed the dishonour of my name, and will relate it.”

===Chinese===

“我的名誉！”他喊的是这个。“你这样独自出门，把我的名誉给出卖了。认得你的那家英国宅子里的仆人，会把一句耳语、一阵哄笑传遍所有的市场。还有那些瞧见你在尘土里走路的人！……你不知羞耻、不知体面吗？我父亲会怎么说？这消息会要了他的命！你已经杀了我的父亲！”

“你根本就没把我放在心上，”巴拉卡抽泣道。“我在这里受了凌辱，被你那些下作的人吓得要死，你反倒骂我！但愿我从来没见过你。我多不幸啊！在英格兰，你做的这些事是要受惩罚的。那些攻击我的可怕的男人女人——”

“愿安拉烧死他们，一个也不饶！”优素福用凶狠的阿拉伯语喊道。“我恨不得拔光他们所有人的舌头！他们亲眼看见我名声受辱，必定会去传扬。”

===Original===

The wrangle lasted far into the night. At last, however, Yûsuf’s tone relented; they embraced, and he demanded the whole history of her ill-starred visit. But when he heard that men had been in the same room with her, his wrath redoubled. He beat his breast, he gnashed his teeth, he slapped her face, he paced the room denouncing her depravity.

“You are a brute!” she cried hysterically. “What harm if men were present? They did not come near me. I am not like your women﻿—bred up to think of one thing only. Nor are Englishmen like you; they have respect for women. You are mad.”

Yûsuf was really mad, or seemed so, at that moment. He called her evil names in every tongue of which he had a smattering; and then in French, made childish by his rage, accused all Europeans of disgusting conduct.

“You deny it﻿—*hein*? You are a liar, for the fact is known. We are not ignorant; we travel, and we have their books. What say you of their balls, their public dances, where women﻿—nay, young virgins﻿—choose what man they please, deserting husband or fiancé﻿—empty names!﻿—and dance and afterwards retire with him? The fact is known! The race is shameless﻿—may God punish them! It is forbidden for us to cast up former things in marriage; but for the future I command you to forsake their filthiness. Go once again, and we shall know you worthless! Swear to renounce their company, or I will kill you!”

===Chinese===

这场争吵一直闹到深夜。不过到末了，优素福的口气软了下来；两人拥抱在一起，他要她把那趟倒霉出访的始末从头讲来。可一听到曾有男人和她同处一室，他的怒火又翻了一倍。他捶胸，他咬牙，他打了她一个耳光，他在屋里踱来踱去，痛斥她的堕落。

“你是个畜生！”她歇斯底里地喊道。“有男人在场又有什么害处？他们并没有近我的身。我不像你们的女人——从小被调教得心里只想着那一件事。英国男人也不像你；他们尊重女人。你疯了。”

优素福那一刻是真疯了，或者说看着是真疯了。他用自己懂个皮毛的每一种语言咒骂她；随后又用法语——狂怒把他变回了孩子——指控全体欧洲人行为龌龊。

“你不认账——嗯（hein）？你就是个骗子，事实尽人皆知。我们不是无知的人；我们出门游历，也读他们的书。他们的舞会、他们的公共舞会，你怎么说？在那儿，女人们——不，是年轻的闺女——看中哪个男人便挑哪个，抛开丈夫或未婚夫——空名分罢了！——同他跳舞，过后又同他一起退场。事实尽人皆知！这个种族寡廉鲜耻——愿真主惩罚他们！照我们的规矩，结了婚便不许翻旧账；但从今往后，我命令你戒绝他们的污秽。再去一回，便知你一钱不值！发誓同他们断绝往来，不然我就杀了你！”

===Original===

She sat up and confronted him with eyes of fire.

“Oh, brute!” she panted; “monster! rabid dog! I have had enough of you and your behaviour. I shall leave you. Tomorrow I shall go to the Consul and tell him how you struck me!”

“You shall not leave this room. I am your master.”

“Lock the door, block up the window, bind me, guard me, I still will find some way to let the Consul know. You shall be punished﻿—I have sworn it. I have had enough, I say. I shall return to England.”

“Your talk is madness! Have a care! The punishment is death for one renouncing El Islam. Say, is that your meaning? Your own slaves will kill you!”

===Chinese===

她坐起身来，一双喷火的眼睛直逼着他。

“哦，畜生！”她喘着气说；“恶魔！疯狗！你和你这套行径我受够了。我要离开你。明天我就去找领事，告诉他你是怎么打我的！”

“你休想离开这间屋子。我是你的主人。”

“锁上门，堵上窗，把我捆起来，看守着我，我还是能想出法子让领事知道。你会受到惩罚的——我发过誓了。我受够了，我说。我要回英格兰去。”

“你说的是疯话！放明白些！叛离伊斯兰的人，刑罚是死刑。你说，你打的可是这个主意？你自己的奴仆就会杀了你！”

===Original===

He put the question in bloodcurdling tones. But Barakah, dissolved in tears, made no rejoinder. A minute later he was once more at her side, imploring mercy, declaring her his light of life, his pearl of pearls. She still whimpered, “I shall tell the Consul.”

At last she fell into a troubled sleep.

===Chinese===

他用令人毛骨悚然的声调发出这一问。但巴拉卡已化作一团泪水，没有作答。一分钟后他又回到她身旁，求她开恩，称她是他生命之光、他珍珠中的珍珠。她仍旧抽抽噎噎：“我要告诉领事。”

她终于坠入一场躁扰不安的睡眠。

===Original===

When she woke again it was broad daylight; her coffee and a kind of pancake, which composed her breakfast every morning, steamed upon a tray beside her. Yûsuf had left the room. He came back presently, and, kneeling down, implored her to forget his madness. Enjoying her advantage in a listless way, she put on an exaggerated air of feebleness, and moaned:

“You were too cruel. I shall tell the Consul.”

At that he sprang up as a man demented and rushed out. No sooner was he gone than she relapsed to weeping, stricken by the curse of utter helplessness which underlay her pitiful pretence at pride. To have been beaten black and blue by Yûsuf would have been less ignominious than to let the Consul know she was unhappy. She had walked into this guarded life with open eyes, aware of the conditions which must thenceforth fetter her existence, boasting love for them. The least complaint, much more retreat, was thus impossible. Even in the heat of anger she had had no real intention to go back. Yûsuf had enraged her, the mob upon the previous day had frightened her exceedingly; but after all they were her chosen people, though so strange. She could never come to hate them as she did the English.

===Chinese===

她再醒来时已是天光大亮；她的咖啡和一种煎饼——那便是她每天早晨的早饭——在身旁的托盘上冒着热气。优素福已经离开房间。过了一会儿他回来了，跪下来恳求她忘掉他的疯癫。她懒洋洋地享受着自己的优势，装出一副添了几分的虚弱模样，呻吟道：

“你太狠心了。我要告诉领事。”

这话使他像个失心疯似的跳将起来，冲了出去。他前脚刚走，她又哭了起来——她那可怜的骄傲做派底下，原压着彻底无助的诅咒，此刻它击中了她。就是被优素福打得青一块紫一块，也比让领事知道她过得不幸福来得体面。她是睁着眼睛走进这种深闺生活的，明知从此要锁住她一生的种种条规，还夸口说自己爱它们。这样一来，发一句最小的怨言尚且不能，退缩就更不必提了。即便在怒火最盛的时候，她也从未真心想回去。优素福激怒了她，前一天的人群也把她吓得不轻；可他们终究是她自己拣选的人，尽管如此古怪。她永远不可能像恨英国人那样恨他们。

===Original===

She rose at last with mind to go into another room. The door was locked. Upon her trying it a slave-girl shouted:

“It is forbidden to go out. Does my lady require anything that I can bring her?”

Barakah bit her lip and flushed as she turned back. Remembrance of her boasting yesterday before those Englishwomen rose to taunt her.

===Chinese===

末了她起了身，打算到另一间屋去。门锁着。她一推门，一个女奴在外头喊道：

“不许出去。太太有什么要的，小的能给您拿来？”

巴拉卡咬着嘴唇，红着脸转身回去。昨天在那些英国女人面前夸下的海口，那记忆涌上来嘲弄她。

===Original===

A little sunlight entered through the lattice like gold-dust. The gardener was at his work of watering﻿—a lengthy process﻿—assisted by his little son and by Ghandûr, Fatûmah playing round and teasing them. She heard their shouts and the familiar noises marking stages of the work; and by degrees, as she sat idle, listening, a measure of contentment came to her. Her troubles were of her own making; she had tempted Providence by flouting rules she had herself accepted. Henceforth, she vowed, she would be passive, of a boldness purely speculative, like Gulbeyzah.

It was not very long before the room door opened, admitting Yûsuf and his father, both with faces of concern. Saluting, in his courtly way, the Pasha offered an unqualified apology for everything that might displease her in the customs of the country. His son had told him of the trouble which had come between them. It arose from a simple and entirely pardonable misunderstanding, as he hoped at once to demonstrate to her well-known intelligence, if she would pay him the distinguished compliment of attending for a little moment to his explanation.

With that, he crossed one leg beneath him on the sofa, a compromise between the Eastern and the Western attitude, and began:

===Chinese===

一缕阳光透过格窗洒进来，好似金粉。园丁正做着浇水的活计——那是桩费时的工夫——他的小儿子和甘杜尔从旁帮着，法图麦绕来绕去地逗他们。她听见他们的吆喝，还有标志着活计一道道工序的那些熟悉的响动；她闲坐着听着，渐渐地，心里生出几分满足。她的烦恼原是自己招来的；她嘲慢了自己亲口接受的规矩，无异于试探天意。她发誓，从今往后要一味顺受，胆气只揣在心底，如同古尔贝雅扎一般。

没过多久，房门开了，进来的是优素福和他父亲，两人脸上都带着关切。帕夏行了个礼——带着他那一副彬彬有礼的气派——为这个国度的风俗中可能拂逆她意的一切，致了无保留的歉意。他儿子已把两人之间起的这场风波告诉了他。这风波起于一场单纯的、完全可以原谅的误会；只要她肯赏脸，稍稍听一听他的解释，他希望立刻就能向她那众所周知的聪慧证明这一点。

说罢，他在沙发上把一条腿盘起来——那是东方坐姿与西方坐姿之间的一个折衷——开口道：

===Original===

“I told you, if you will have the goodness to remember, when first the question of a marriage with my son arose between us, that we had stricter rules for the protection of our women than prevail in Europe. I also told you that, those rules once honoured, a woman had all freedom and consideration. I did wrong, I now perceive with infinite regret, not to explain to you precisely the reason and the nature of those rules; for, see, entirely owing to that fault of mine, you have transgressed them innocently. I should like, if you permit it, to expound their general tendency and benevolent intention.”

===Chinese===

“您若肯费心回想，当初我们之间初次提起与我儿子结亲一事时，我便说过：我们保护自家女人的规矩，比欧洲通行的严格。我还说过：那些规矩一旦受到敬重，女人便享有一切的自由与体面。如今我怀着无限的悔恨看出，我错在没能向您精确讲明那些规矩的缘由和性质；因为您瞧，全然由于我这一过失，您才在懵懂无知中触犯了它们。您若容许，我愿阐明它们的大旨与仁善的用意。”

===Original===

Barakah was sore abashed. The Pasha’s entrance, the intervention of so dignified a person in a childish quarrel due to her misconduct, overwhelmed her. At this point in his speech she interjected:

“Pardon! I did wrong, I know! But I had no idea﻿ ﻿… I wore the habbarah and mouth-veil. You had told me that a woman dressed like that was safe from insult.”

“I spoke in too general a sense. It is my fault entirely. You sinned through ignorance, and Yûsuf should not have been angry﻿—though, indeed, to our ideas your conduct was abominable.”

“But what wrong did I do, beyond going out without permission? Why did the people on the road beset me? Oh, I am so miserable!”

===Chinese===

巴拉卡窘迫极了。帕夏的驾临，这样一位尊严人物竟来调停一场因她失行而起的孩子气的争吵，叫她招架不住。他的话讲到此处，她插嘴道：

“恕罪！我错了，我知道！可我怎么也没想到……我穿着哈巴拉（habbarah）罩袍，蒙着口纱。您从前告诉过我，那样装束的女人不会受辱。”

“我说得太笼统了。全是我的过失。您是在无知中获的罪，优素福本不该动怒——虽说，的确，按我们的观念，您的行止实在可憎。”

“可除了未经许可出门，我究竟做错了什么？路上的人为什么围攻我？哦，我苦死了！”

===Original===

The Pasha shrugged his shoulders with a smile to Yûsuf, as who should say:

“Observe her innocence!”

“No, no, don’t cry, I beg of you!” he pleaded. “God be praised you have derived no hurt from the adventure. It is entirely owing to respect for you that I and my son are so concerned about it. Beloved daughter, women are for us so sacred﻿—the spirit of the house, the secret fount of life﻿—that we never even speak of them with friends for fear some light word or unseemly thought should go towards them. Nothing must be known of them, no talk made about them, outside the world of women and our own harem.

===Chinese===

帕夏朝优素福耸了耸肩，微微一笑，仿佛在说：

“瞧她这份天真！”

“别哭，别哭，我求您了！”他恳求道。“赞美真主，您在这场历险里毫发无伤。我与犬子对这事如此挂心，全然是出于对您的敬重。爱女啊，女人于我们是何等神圣——是家宅的精魂，是生命隐秘的泉源——我们连对朋友都绝口不谈她们，唯恐只言片语或一丝不当的念头飘向她们。在女人的世界和我们自家内宅之外，关于她们的一切都必须无人知晓，无人议论。

===Original===

“Yesterday, by going out alone in an open carriage, you attracted notice all unconsciously. Your habbarah is of a rich material, your mouth-veil of the kind only worn by ladies of good houses. No such lady would have gone abroad thus unattended. The servants of your English friend would comment on the strange proceeding, and, knowing who you were, think shame of us.

“But that is the least part of what you did. That, by itself, would have been nothing. But you walked. Great God! What made you walk? That is for me inexplicable!”

“I felt the wish to walk. It was a lovely evening.”

===Chinese===

“昨天，您乘一辆敞篷马车独自出门，浑然不觉地招来了注目。您的哈巴拉是华贵的料子，您的口纱是好人家贵妇才用的那一种。这样的贵妇绝不会这样无人随侍地出门。您那位英国朋友的仆人们会对这桩稀奇事议论纷纷，而且一旦知道您是谁，就要觉得我们丢脸。

“可这还只算您所做之事里最轻的一部分。单就这一条，本也算不得什么。可您居然走路。伟大的真主！您为什么要走路？这在我真是无法索解！”

“我想走一走。那晚天色多好啊。”

===Original===

“Great God!” the Pasha gasped, with eyes upturned. “Does anybody walk for pleasure here in Egypt? The natives have a proverb: ‘Better ride on beetles than walk upon rich carpets.’ Well, well, there!” He shrugged as giving up a hopeless puzzle. “You walked. A lady dressed as you were had never been seen walking in this world before. More than that, you did not walk like other people. Ghandûr informs me that the rascals who beset you were all persuaded that you were a man dressed up. You say you walked for pleasure in the dust?﻿—and in a habbarah? Astonishing!

===Chinese===

“伟大的真主！”帕夏双眼上翻，倒抽一口气。“在埃及这儿，有谁会为了消遣而走路？本地人有句谚语：‘宁可骑着甲虫走，也不踩着华贵的地毯步行。’罢了，罢了，不说了！”他耸耸肩，像是对一道无望的难题撒手。“您走路了。您那样装束的一位贵妇，在这个世界还从没有人见过走路的。还不止如此，您走路的样子也与别人不同。甘杜尔告诉我，围攻您的那些无赖个个认定您是个男扮女装的。您说您在尘土里走路是为了取乐？——还穿着哈巴拉？骇人听闻！

===Original===

“So, you see now, Yûsuf was not angry altogether without cause. I trust you will not now esteem it necessary to see the Consul, and produce a scandal which I think would kill me.”

Thoroughly disgusted with her whole behaviour, Barakah began to sob again.

“I never truly meant to go,” she blurted.

“I thank you infinitely,” said the Pasha grandly, again saluting as he rose to go. “You relieve me of a terrible anxiety. Our house has never known the breath of scandal.﻿ ﻿… But pleasure!﻿—you assure me that you walked for pleasure?” he gasped, reverting to the former wonder. “I could understand it in a garden, round and round. But when it is a case of going anywhere﻿—Grand Dieu!”

===Chinese===

“这样您就明白了，优素福动怒也不全然无因。我相信您如今不会觉得还非去见领事不可，闹出一桩丑闻来——我想那会要了我的命。”

巴拉卡对自己这一整副做派厌烦透了，又啜泣起来。

“我从没真心想去，”她脱口道。

“我无限感激您，”帕夏郑重地说，起身告辞时又行了一个礼。“您卸下了我一场可怕的焦虑。我们这个宅门，还从未沾过一丝丑闻的气息。……可是取乐！——您担保，您走路是为了取乐？”他倒抽一口气，又落回先前那桩奇事上。“要是在园子里绕着圈子走，我还能理解。可一旦是要到什么地方去——天哪！”

===Original===

That was a marvel which for weeks convulsed the harem world. The Pasha mentioned it at home. Within an hour the wondrous news was known to every woman. The English bride of Yûsuf Bey Muhammad had walked from such a house to such a crossways, all in thick dust, amid the crowd of wayfarers﻿—for pleasure, so she said! Insanity, a love appointment with an Englishman, a touch of sunstroke, the insensibility to comfort of a woman of coarse origin, were solutions of the riddle freely offered and discussed. But the theory which found most favour for its probability was that the Englishwoman was the sport of some malignant wizard or afrît, who made her walk to show his power upon her.

===Chinese===

这件事成了一桩奇闻，一连几个星期搅动着整个内宅世界。帕夏在家里提起了它。不出一个钟头，这桩奇闻便人人皆知，传到了每一个女人耳朵里。优素福·贝伊·穆罕默德的英国新娘，竟从某宅一路走到某个路口，全程厚尘弥漫，夹在熙来攘往的行人当中——还是为了取乐，她是这么说的！癫狂，同某个英国人的情约，一点中暑，还有出身粗贱的女人对安逸的麻木不仁，这些谜底被人们竞相提出、品评。但因言之成理而最得人心的说法是：这英国女人是被某个歹毒的巫师或阿弗里特妖灵（afrît）捉弄了，那妖物驱使她走路，好显一显他加在她身上的神通。

===Original===

Leylah Khânum and Gulbeyzah were the first to call and question her upon the strange performance. They asked point-blank why she had walked; and when she answered, “Just for exercise,” they eyed her in a way that showed they thought her mad. Then came the throng of mere acquaintances, not less curious, but infinitely too polite to ask a question; who watched for symptoms of derangement through the flow of compliments. The elderly princess, Amînah Khânum, alone showed understanding and some sympathy.

“My dear,” she said, “you’ve set the parrots talking. Do you know that ‘durrah,’ which means fellow-wife, means parrot too? Bear that in mind. Their tongues!﻿—They fail to comprehend. They think you are bewitched or mad. For me, your conduct was entirely natural. But I fancy you will give up walking here in Egypt. Were not your clothes a mass of dust beneath your habbarah? Whenever you are in a difficulty, come to me. I have some jurisdiction, and I wish you happy.”

===Chinese===

莱拉·哈努姆和古尔贝雅扎头一个上门，就这桩怪举盘问她。她们直截了当地问她为什么走路；她答一声“不过为了运动”，两人看她的眼神便透出心里认定她疯了。随后涌来的是一群泛泛之交，好奇不遑多让，却客气得过了头，绝不肯发一问，只在一连串恭维话里窥察她精神失常的症候。唯有上了年纪的公主阿米娜·哈努姆，表现出理解和几分同情。

“亲爱的，”她说，“你把鹦鹉们惹得开了腔。你可知道‘杜拉’（durrah）这个词，既是同夫之妻的意思，也是鹦鹉的意思？把这个记在心上。她们的舌头！——她们无法领会。她们认为你中了邪，或是疯了。照我看，你的行止再自然不过。不过我想，在埃及这儿你要戒掉走路这回事了。你哈巴拉底下的衣裳，还不成了一片尘土？往后你一有难处，只管来找我。我多少有些权柄，我愿你幸福。”

===Original===

Barakah was far from happy in those days. For one thing, she had felt the bars confining her. And then a vision of the English sneering lurked ever in the background of her mind, a fount of gall. With Yûsuf she was once more upon loving terms, and any differences that arose between them came from her ill-temper. She was growing irritable. The food, too highly spiced, did not agree with her; the sanitary arrangements were disgusting; she noticed failings not observed before, particularly in the behaviour of the servants to her.

At first, on coming to that nest of love, released from the restrictions of a great harem, her slave-girls had been lazy, but obsequious. At that time the old woman had commanded them, relieving Barakah, whose little knowledge of the language would have placed her at their mercy. But now the crone had been dismissed; the servants, with respect diminished by the quarrel they had witnessed, were grown insolent and offhand in their service. The child Fatûmah, who had been a pet with Barakah, made rude grimaces and ran off when called.

===Chinese===

那些日子，巴拉卡远谈不上幸福。一来，她实打实地摸到了囚禁她的栅栏。再者，英国人讥诮的面影总潜伏在她心底，如同一眼苦泉。她同优素福重归于好，恩爱如初；两人之间偶有龃龉，都起于她的坏脾气。她变得日渐烦躁。饭菜香料下得太重，吃了不合她的肠胃；如厕的安排令人作呕；她还察觉到从前未曾留意的种种毛病，尤其是仆人们对她的态度。

起初，刚到那座爱巢的时候——从大户内宅的层层约束中解脱出来——她的女奴们虽然懒散，却还殷勤巴结。那时有那老妇管着她们，替巴拉卡省了心；她对当地语言所知寥寥，不然难免任她们摆布。可如今老婆子已被辞退；仆人们目睹过那场争吵，敬畏打了折扣，服侍起来日渐放肆怠慢。小姑娘法图麦原是巴拉卡的宠儿，如今做个鬼脸，一唤就跑。

===Original===

One hot midday, feeling extremely ill, she called for water. There came no answer, though she heard them chattering. She called again and clapped her hands. Still no one came. The cruelty of such neglect incensed her. With fevered strength she rose and went to scold them. She met a slave arriving at her leisure. At the words, “Ready, O my lady!” proffered with an undisguised yawn, she sprang upon the girl and clutched her throat, exclaiming: “Bring water, dost thou hear, O daughter of a dog! Bring water quickly!”

The slave, beholding murder in the lady’s eyes, made haste and ran. Another girl looked in to learn the reason of the noise. Barakah picked up an earthen jar and flung it at her head. The change was magical. In a trice five several vessels full of water were being offered to her by as many servile creatures; while Fatûmah snuggled up to her and kissed her hand, receiving in return a box on the ear, which made her howl the praises of her dear, kind mistress.

===Chinese===

一个炎热的正午，她觉得浑身难受，唤人拿水来。虽然听得见她们在一旁闲磕牙，却没人应声。她又唤了一遍，还拍了拍手。仍旧没有人来。这般怠慢的狠毒激怒了她。她鼓起发烧般的气力，起身要去训斥她们。迎面正碰上一个女奴慢条斯理地走来。一句“来了，太太！”还伴着一个不加掩饰的哈欠，她扑上去扼住那姑娘的喉咙，喊道：“拿水来，你听见没有，狗养的丫头！快拿水来！”

那女奴见太太眼里起了杀机，慌忙撒腿就跑。另一个姑娘探头进来，想知道喧闹的缘故。巴拉卡抄起一只陶罐，朝她头上掷去。变化神奇极了。转眼间，五个奴才各捧一件盛满水的器皿，一齐献到她面前；法图麦偎上来亲她的手，换来的却是一记耳光，打得她尖着嗓子赞美亲爱的好太太。

===Original===

When Barakah returned to her own room she fainted, her borrowed strength departing with her wrath. The servants, in a flutter of solicitude, put her to bed, and sent Ghandûr to fetch the master. She, knowing nothing of the flight of time, heard presently, as in a dream, the Pasha saying:

“Call a European doctor! That dog must know that she has had the best attendance!” and Yûsuf weeping uncontrollably. Then the next minute, as it seemed to her, an English voice above her muttered: “Typhoid! Bound to come, with native food.” That was the last she knew.

===Chinese===

回到自己房里，巴拉卡晕了过去——借来的气力随着怒气一同散了。仆人们手忙脚乱、关怀备至地把她安顿上床，又打发甘杜尔去请主人。她全然不知时辰的流逝，不多时，恍如在梦中，听见帕夏说：

“去请个欧洲医生来！得让那条狗知道，她受到的是最精心的照护！”还有优素福不可遏制的哭泣。随后，在她感觉只是下一分钟，一个英国口音在她上方低声道：“伤寒！吃本地饭食，迟早要得的。”这是她知道的最后一件事。

## XIV / 十四

===Original===

Ghandûr, had borne the summons to the Frankish doctor. Having delivered it, he wandered to the Pasha’s house. A creature witless save for love, existing by it, the kindness shown him by the lady Barakah had raised her to the throne of Yûsuf in his mind. Her freak of walking had imparted to his sentiments that touch of pity for one too innocent to face the world which makes of service an angelic trust. He blamed himself for the adventure. When he heard that she was in disgrace and looking wan, he beat his breast. Now that she was like to die through his demerits, his grief was such as caused him actual pains.

Upon arriving at Muhammad Pasha’s house, before he could divulge his woe, he was informed:

“The lady Fitnah has been asking for thee. Go indoors, and wait while they announce thee!”

===Chinese===

甘杜尔受命传话，去请那位法兰克医生。口信送到之后，他信步走到帕夏的宅邸。这是个除了爱以外一无所知的生灵，全凭爱活命；巴拉卡夫人待他的那点恩慈，已把她在他心里抬上了优素福的宝座。她那场走路的奇想，又给他的情愫添了一缕怜惜，怜惜一个天真得不足以面对人世的人——正是这缕怜惜，把侍奉变成了天使般的信托。他把那场历险归罪于自己。听说她失了宠、形容憔悴，他捶胸痛悔。如今她竟要因他的罪过而丧命，他的悲伤竟化作真真切切的疼痛。

一到穆罕默德·帕夏的宅邸，还没等他吐露悲声，就有人告诉他：

“菲特娜夫人一直在找你。进屋里去，等着人替你通传！”

===Original===

He was standing in the hall, cocooned in sorrow, when a mob of children burst through the mabeyn, as the great screen which bounds the women’s realm is called, and fell upon him.

“Oh, Ghandûr, where hast thou been?”﻿—“I have a new tarbush.”﻿—“The bitch beneath our windows has five puppies﻿—blind, by Allah’s mercy! Come and see!”﻿—“My doll! Like a daughter of Adam﻿—a bride arrayed﻿—a virgin﻿—almost a sin for thee to look on! Come and see!”

Half weeping as he was, Ghandûr responded; and, unaware of his preoccupation, the children led him towards the women’s doorway.

“Go in as far as to the second screen﻿—no farther!” said the eunuch there on guard.

Ghandûr was careful to obey; but his attendant imps, regarding all authority as ground for sport, banded together suddenly and dragged him on. He shook them off and drew back quickly; the eunuch came and scattered them with swishing cane; and then the children, tumbling over one another, began to fight among themselves with fearful insults.

===Chinese===

他正站在厅堂里，裹在一团悲伤之中，忽有一群孩子冲破马贝因（mabeyn）——圈出女眷地界的那道大屏便叫这个名字——一拥扑到他身上。

“哦，甘杜尔，你上哪儿去了？”——“我有了一顶新塔布什帽。”——“我家窗子底下那条母狗下了五只崽——瞎的，托安拉的慈悯！快来看！”——“我的娃娃！像亚当的女儿——装扮齐整的新娘——一个闺女——你看了简直算犯罪！快来看！”

甘杜尔半哭着应付他们；孩子们没察觉他心事重重，领着他往女眷的门户那边去。

“进去走到第二道屏为止——不许再往里！”守在那儿的阉人说。

甘杜尔小心遵命；可他身后那群小鬼把一切权威都当作取乐的由头，忽然串通一气，把他往前拖拽。他甩脱他们，急忙退回；阉人赶过来，抡着手杖飕飕几杖把他们轰散；随后孩子们滚作一团，彼此用骇人的恶言对骂起来。

===Original===

“By my maidenhood, I swear to kill thee and devour thy liver!” screamed out a girl of eight to a small boy who pushed against her.

“I will ravish thee, abandoned one, and then eject thee on a dunghill!”

The lady Fitnah from behind the screen cried out for order, naming Hamdi, her own son, as probably the cause of tumult. The eunuch fell upon that wayward, dreamy adolescent, whom Ghandûr did his utmost to protect, for he was Yûsuf’s brother; while Fitnah Khânum asked what sin she had committed to be punished with a boy so lazy and so mischievous. She cared for Hamdi, but without indulgence. Her love was made a whiplash for his good. At last came silence, and Ghandûr poured forth his grief.

===Chinese===

“凭我的贞操起誓，我要杀了你，吃你的肝！”一个八岁的女孩冲着挤她的小男孩尖声喊。

“我要奸污了你，你这贱货，然后把你扔到粪堆上去！”

屏后的菲特娜夫人喝令安静，点名哈姆迪——她自己的儿子——说这场喧闹多半由他而起。阉人扑向那个任性出神、半大不小的少年；甘杜尔竭力护着他，因为他是优素福的弟弟。菲特娜·哈努姆则自问造了什么孽，要罚她得一个这样懒、这样淘气的儿子。她疼哈姆迪，却毫不纵容。她的爱化作一条鞭子，专为他的好处。末了终于静了下来，甘杜尔这才倾吐他的悲伤。

===Original===

“O Lord, have mercy! Woe upon us all! O most gracious lady, rare pearl of beauty and refinement, companion of my dearest lord and brother! Behold the glory of our house is in the dust.”

“By Allah, in the dust! Thou sayest truly!” scoffed the lady Fitnah. “It is of that very business that I wish to speak with thee. What is the truth about her walking in the dust, thou who wast with her? Is it true that she had been alone with Frankish men? Was no man following﻿—didst thou look well?﻿—when she walked off alone, rejecting thee? Was not her chin upon her shoulder, and her gaze behind her, ogling? Did I not well to rail against that marriage? Now it is clearly proven that she has no modesty.”

“O my despair! O evil day! The fault is mine!” cried out Ghandûr, beside himself. “Blame not her Grace; she is the noblest lady﻿—as innocent as is a babe; she thinks no evil. O bitter grief! O Allah! O calamity!”

“Now Allah heal thee! It is plain she has bewitched thee too. She is for all men, like the rest of her foul race﻿—for strangers, servants, donkey-drivers, even scavengers! Pray, pray to God till I bestow on thee a charm of power!”

===Chinese===

“主啊，开恩！我们都遭殃了！啊，最仁慈的夫人，美丽与教养的稀世珍珠，我最亲爱的老爷兼兄弟的伴侣！看哪，我们家的荣光落在尘土里了。”

“凭安拉起誓，落在尘土里！你说得一点不差！”菲特娜夫人讥诮道。“我正要同你说这件事。她在尘土里走路，真相到底如何——你不是跟着她的吗？她真的同法兰克男人单独待过？她甩开你独自走掉的时候，当真没有男人跟在后头——你仔细看了吗？她的下巴是不是搭在肩上，眼波朝后抛，一路卖俏？我当初反对那桩亲事，难道反错了？如今清清楚楚证明，她全无廉耻。”

“啊，我的绝望！啊，可咒的日子！过错在我！”甘杜尔忘乎所以地喊道。“您别怪夫人；她是最尊贵的太太——像婴孩一样清白；她心里不存半点恶念。啊，锥心的悲痛！啊，安拉！啊，灾殃！”

“愿安拉治好你！明摆着她把你也迷住了。她跟谁都行，同她那下贱种类的其余人一个样——生人也罢，仆人也罢，赶驴的也罢，连清粪的都行！快祷告，向真主祷告，祷告到我赐你一道有神通的符咒为止！”

===Original===

“Hush! Let him speak! Let Ghandûr tell his story!” cried a second voice. Ghandûr became aware of other ladies pressing to the screen. He lifted up his voice and wept.

“O lady, speak no bitterness against her. She lies this moment at the point of death. Our house is as a tomb, a haunt of ominous owls. My lord the Pasha frowns and looks distressful; my lord Yûsuf weeps as if his heart would break. I myself have been to call a Frankish doctor, who, on reading my lord’s message, rode off like the wind. Allah knows the dear one may be dead this minute!”

He buried his face in his hands, while a hubbub of concern arose behind the screen.

===Chinese===

“嘘！让他说！让甘杜尔讲他的事！”另一个声音喊道。甘杜尔这才察觉还有别的女眷挤在屏前。他放声哭了出来。

“啊，夫人，请别说她的坏话。她这会儿正躺在生死一线。我们宅子像一座坟，是恶兆猫头鹰出没的地方。帕夏老爷愁眉紧锁，满面忧苦；优素福老爷哭得心都要碎了。我亲自去请了一位法兰克医生，他看了老爷的字条，打马如飞地去了。安拉知道，那位心爱的这会儿兴许已经死了！”

他把脸埋进双手，屏后随即响起一片关切的喧哗。

===Original===

“O poor darling floweret! O despair!” wailed Yûsuf’s mother, all her feelings turned right round. “What is her illness? Quick, describe! May Allah heal her!”

“Fever﻿—the worst sort!”

“I go at once to her.”

A sick-nurse of experience in charms and nostrums, the lady Fitnah always quickened to the scent of illness and adored the sufferer. From a creature hardly to be named by modest lips, the wife of Yûsuf was become the apple of her eye Having sent an order for the carriage, she went through her store of medicines, discoursing wisely to the other ladies; while Ghandûr, retiring, heard from the attendant eunuch:

===Chinese===

“哦，可怜的宝贝花儿！哦，绝望啊！”优素福的母亲哀声哭喊，一副心肠整个翻了过来。“她得的是什么病？快，讲来！愿安拉治愈她！”

“发热——最凶的那种！”

“我这就去她那儿。”

菲特娜夫人惯用符咒秘方看护病人，是个中老手，一嗅到疾病的气味便精神抖擞，把害病的人奉若心肝。优素福的妻子，先前在她嘴里还是个体面嘴唇几乎说不出口的东西，如今成了她的眼珠子。她吩咐备车，把自己的药库检视了一遍，同其余女眷娓娓讲论药理；甘杜尔退出去时，听见值守的阉人说：

===Original===

“Thou hast done it! We had word of this; Sawwâb was summoned. But the command was, not to tell the ladies.”

He could only shrug.

Illness, like death and birth, was woman’s great occasion, when, guarding the traditions, she stood forth as priestess. The whole harem was in a flutter of excitement.

===Chinese===

“都是你干的好事！我们得了信儿；萨瓦布也被唤去问过了。只是有令在先，不许告诉女眷们。”

他只能耸耸肩。

疾病，同死亡和生育一样，是女人的大典；在这大典上，她守护传统，俨然以女祭司的身份挺身而出。整个内宅沸腾在一片兴奋之中。

===Original===

“Gulbeyzah must come with us,” pronounced Fitnah Khânum, “because our poor sick darling always loved her.”

The ladies Fitnah and Murjânah, the Pasha’s widowed sister and two nieces, goodly persons, together with the well-grown, plump Gulbeyzah, and a bundle of medicaments, including a whole plant of garlic and a donkey’s thighbone, were all packed somehow into one close carriage. The sun was setting when they reached the pleasure-house. The eunuch went to herald their arrival; and all the ladies, nothing doubting of their glad reception, freed themselves from the crushed mass they formed together. They were shaking out and smoothing crumpled raiment when the messenger returned to say they were refused admittance by the doctor’s orders. The ladies stood stone-still and looked at one another. Fitnah Khânum broke the silence.

“This is our son’s house! May Allah slay the doctor! Come, my sisters!”

Just then Sawwâb, chief eunuch of the guard, appeared, and barred the entrance with the word “Forbidden!”

===Chinese===

“古尔贝雅扎必须随我们同去，”菲特娜·哈努姆宣示道，“因为我们那位可怜的病中宝贝一向喜欢她。”

菲特娜与穆尔贾娜两位夫人，帕夏那位守寡的姊妹和两个侄女——都是些体面人物——连同出落得丰盈饱满的古尔贝雅扎，再加一包袱药物——里头有整棵的大蒜，还有一根驴的腿骨——不知怎么统统塞进了一辆密闭的马车。抵达那座爱巢时，日头正落。阉人进去通报她们驾到；众女眷深信必受热诚迎接，从挤作一团的窘态里各自解脱出来。她们正抖开衣衫、抚平褶皱，去通报的人回话说，奉医生之命，不得入内。女眷们石头般呆立，面面相觑。菲特娜·哈努姆打破了沉默。

“这是我们儿子的宅子！愿安拉杀了那医生！来，我的姐妹们！”

正在这时，护卫的首席阉人萨瓦布现身，一声“禁止入内”，拦住了入口。

===Original===

“Whose order, say?”

“The order of our lord.”

“Praise to Allah! That is better than the doctor. To hear is to obey, though Allah knows that the command is wicked and against religion. Tell thy master we shall lay a case before the Qadi.”

With this menace, which afforded her some satisfaction, Fitnah Khânum turned back towards the carriage; and the work of packing those redundant bodies was performed anew.

“Heard one ever the like? To hide our dearest from us at the point of death! To keep a mother from a daughter’s sickbed﻿—a woman from a woman! O Protector!”

===Chinese===

“谁的命令，说？”

“老爷的命令。”

“赞美安拉！那比医生的命令强。听命即顺从，虽说安拉知道，这道命令悖谬违教。告诉你家主人，我们要到卡迪那里告上一状。”

菲特娜·哈努姆撂下这句威胁——它多少给了她几分快意——转身朝马车走去；把那几具冗赘的躯体重新塞回车里，这桩工程又从头干了一遍。

“谁听说过这等事？至亲的人咽气在即，却把我们瞒住！拦着母亲，不让她上女儿的病榻——拦着女人，不见女人！啊，护佑者啊！”

===Original===

The incident, when known, incensed the harem world. The sickroom had been woman’s temple from of old. To be forbidden access to the bedside of a near relation appeared an outrage, even to the calm Murjânah. The indignation of the slaves was riotous. The injured ladies received many visits of condolence, when Fitnah Khânum’s lamentations were applauded as the voice of right.

“O cruelty,” she sobbed. “To keep us from our darling, when she has most need of us! The Frankish doctors are all monsters, hearts of stone. It is known that they snatch dying people from their friends, to practise on them, omitting even to return the bodies afterwards. They may have skill, but many things they know not, being infidels. The pain I suffer when I think of that sweet girl﻿—the very liver of my darling Yûsuf﻿—lying senseless, an empty house for any demon to inhabit, and not a charm put up for her protection, is excruciating!”

It is characteristic of the harem life that, though the ladies were thus irritated, near rebellion, no clear word of their grievance reached the Pasha’s ear. There is a wall between the women and the man more real than the mabeyn screen which man erected. The women raise it to secure their privileges; the man, if he perceives it, cannot throw it down. His anger meets with a subservience which foils its aim as surely as loose sheets will stop a bullet. Even Murjânah, who adored the Pasha, kept the harem secret.

Fitnah Khânum had foretold that Barakah would die, thanks to the ministrations of the Frankish doctor. When she heard that she was fast recovering, she gave praise to Allah, who had saved her life in spite of them. From wishing well to the sick woman, she had grown to love her with all the strength of her impulsive, loyal nature.

===Chinese===

这件事一经传开，激怒了整个内宅世界。病房自古以来就是女人的神庙。近亲的病榻竟不许靠近，这在对谁看来都是一桩横暴，连沉静的穆尔贾娜也不例外。奴仆们的愤慨闹得沸反盈天。受辱的女眷们收到一次又一次慰问的造访，菲特娜·哈努姆的哀号被众人喝彩，奉为公道之声。

“啊，狠心啊，”她抽泣道。“在我们宝贝最需要我们的时候，把我们从她身边拦开！法兰克医生全是怪物，石头心肠。谁都知道，他们把垂死的人从亲友手里抢走，拿他们练手，事后连尸首都不归还。他们或许有本事，可有许多事他们不懂，因为他们是异教徒。我一想到那可爱的姑娘——我宝贝优素福的心肝——不省人事地躺着，成了一座任什么恶魔都能住进的空宅，身边连一道护身的符都没挂，我心里的痛楚便如刀绞！”

内宅生活自有这样的特性：女人们纵然这般激愤、几乎到了作乱的地步，她们委屈的明白话却一句也传不到帕夏耳中。女人与男人之间隔着一堵墙，比男人立起来的马贝因屏更加真实。女人们筑起它，以保障自家的特权；男人纵然察觉，也无法把它推倒。他的怒火迎上一片唯唯诺诺，正如松散的布片必能挡住子弹，那怒火注定落空。连敬慕帕夏的穆尔贾娜，也守着内宅的秘密。

菲特娜·哈努姆早已断言，巴拉卡经法兰克医生一治，必死无疑。待听说她康复神速，她便赞美安拉，说是安拉不顾那些人的手，救了她的命。她由盼着病人好转，渐渐变为全心全意地爱她，把那冲动而忠厚的天性的全部力量都用了出来。

===Original===

The love she bore to Yûsuf was eclipsed. His neglect of her for weeks was scarcely noticed. When at last he did appear, haggard but joyful, her “Praise to Allah” was upon his wife’s account. She made him tell her every detail of the doctor’s treatment, and vowed it was a miracle the girl survived it. From him she learnt the reason of the Pasha’s deference to every edict of that ignoramus. The English Consul had his eye upon the house, watching to note that all was done correctly.

“Consume the Consul!” she exclaimed peremptorily.

“Our Lord consume him utterly!” said Yûsuf. “Yet for one boon I have to thank him. My father, to propitiate him, gives command that I shall visit Paris in the summer with my bride.”

“Allah forbid!” his mother screamed in horror. “Our pearl of pearls to be exposed to vulgar handling, to be cast back into the mire from which she was with pains extracted! Thou wilt not suffer her to go unveiled? For shame, O Yûsuf! To let foul infidels survey thy secret joy.”

===Chinese===

她对优素福的爱黯然失色了。他几个星期疏于问安，她也几乎不曾留意。待他终于露面——形容枯槁，喜气盈盈——她那一声“赞美安拉”，是为他的妻子而发。她逼他讲出医生诊治的每一个细节，并断言这姑娘竟能挺过来，实乃奇迹。从他嘴里，她得知了帕夏何以对那个无知妄人的每一道谕旨都恭敬奉行。英国领事正盯着这座宅子，要看是否一切都办得合乎规矩。

“灭了那领事！”她斩钉截铁地喊道。

“愿我们的主把他灭个干净！”优素福说。“不过有一桩恩情，我还得谢他。我父亲为讨好他，下令今年夏天让我带着新娘去一趟巴黎。”

“安拉不许！”他母亲惊恐地尖叫。“我们珍珠中的珍珠，竟要任俗手摆弄，被扔回那费了千辛万苦才捞出来的泥淖里！你竟肯让她揭了面纱见人？可耻啊，哦，优素福！让污秽的异教徒参观你的秘藏之欢。”

===Original===

“Nay, she will veil her face as the Frenchwomen use.”

“Those veils are nothing, for the mouth is visible.”

“Our ladies wear them in that country to avoid publicity. Be reassured, my mother; we shall guard the decencies. My father grumbles greatly at the cost, but vows that he will show the Consul we are not fanatical. We go to see the dog tomorrow, to tell him all that we have done for her.”

===Chinese===

“不会的，她会像法国女人那样蒙住脸。”

“那种面纱形同虚设，嘴巴还露在外面呢。”

“我们的女眷在那个国度也戴它，为的是免惹注目。放心吧，我的母亲；我们会守全体统。我父亲为花销叫苦连天，却发誓要让领事看看，我们并非狂热之徒。我们明天就去见那条狗，把我们为她做的一切都告诉他。”

===Original===

But on the morrow Yûsuf and his father met with cruel disconcertion. The Consul welcomed them and listened to their story with politeness, but at its end he murmured blandly:

“I altogether fail to see how this concerns me, though highly honoured by your visit and your confidence. The lady is, no doubt, extremely fortunate.”

Muhammad Pasha, flushing hotly, licked his lips as might a panther, and glanced sidelong at his son. He offered a profusion of excuses as he rose to go. The Consul answered, “Always charmed!” and smiled them out.

“May the All-Powerful corrupt his bones and blind him! May the All-Merciful frustrate his heart’s desire!” exclaimed the Pasha as the two regained their carriage. “It seems he has deceived us, has renounced all claim. Here have I spent more than I can afford﻿—coined money, hard to come by﻿—what with her establishment, this doctor and the nursing, and that trip to Paris, which cannot now be dropped, for I have boasted of it; and lo! the dog cares nothing for my trouble. May his limbs rot off!”

“May Allah cut his life!” said Yûsuf savagely.

The women never heard that tale of shame.

===Chinese===

谁知次日，优素福和他父亲遭到残忍的难堪。领事客客气气地迎纳他们，恭而有礼地听他们讲完来意，末了却温吞吞地低声道：

“我全然看不出这事与我何干，尽管二位的造访与见信令我深感荣幸。这位夫人，毋庸置疑，是极其幸运的。”

穆罕默德·帕夏腾地涨红了脸，像豹子似的舔了舔嘴唇，斜眼瞥了瞥儿子。他起身告辞，连声不迭地致歉。领事答一句“随时乐意！”，笑吟吟把他们送了出去。

“愿全能者烂他的骨头、瞎他的眼！愿至慈者叫他心愿落空！”两人回到马车上，帕夏喊道。“看来他骗了我们，弃绝了一切权利。我在这里花的钱超出了我的财力——白花花的银钱，来之不易——什么她那一应宅邸排场啦，这位医生和这趟看护啦，还有那趟巴黎之行——如今也打不得退堂鼓，因为我已经夸下了口；可你瞧！那条狗半点不体恤我的辛苦。愿他的四肢都烂掉！”

“愿安拉断他的寿数！”优素福狠声道。

女人们始终不曾听到那件耻辱的故事。

## XIX / 十九

===Original===

Never in her life had Barakah seen so many strange old women. There were always four or five of them within her chamber, squatting on mats along the wall, conversing in low tones, ready at a breath to rearrange her pillows, or fetch some posset that was ordered for her. They were all of apelike ugliness, and, going barefoot, moved as noiselessly as ghosts.

The Frankish doctor﻿—an Italian﻿—had pronounced her much too frail to nurse her baby﻿—a decision which excited such dependants of the house as were eligible for the post of foster-mother. This was a great prize, kinship by milk, among the Muslims, being esteemed as genuine and binding as by blood. The wet-nurse thus became a near relation of the family, and all her race had claims upon its bounty. Barakah felt jealous of the woman who usurped her function, till she heard from Fitnah Khânum that the choice had fallen on the wife of her old friend Ghandûr. The girl, a former slave of the harem, was then presented to her, the baby in her arms; and won her heart by her excessive gratitude. She was touched, too, by the transports of Ghandûr, who sang thanksgiving to her lattice in his simple way. His chant was something in this manner:

===Chinese===

巴拉卡有生以来，从没见过这么多古怪的老太婆。她房里总坐着四五个，蹲在沿墙的席子上低声交谈，一声招呼便随时替她重新摆好枕头，或去端为她配好的热奶饮。她们个个丑得像猿猴，赤着脚，走动起来悄无声息，如同鬼魅。

法兰克医生——一位意大利人——判定她身子太弱，哺育不了自己的婴孩——这一裁断，让宅子里够格竞争乳母之位的仆妇们好一阵激动。这可是份厚赏：在穆斯林中间，乳汁结下的亲缘，被看得同血亲一样真切、一样有约束力。乳母就此成了这家亲近的亲戚，她全族的人都有权沾这家的恩泽。巴拉卡起初还嫉妒那个僭夺了她职分的女人，直到听菲特娜·哈努姆说，人选落在她老朋友甘杜尔的妻子身上。那姑娘原是内宅的一个奴婢，随即被领来见她，怀里抱着婴孩；她那过分的感激赢得了巴拉卡的心。甘杜尔欣喜若狂的样子也叫她感动，他用他那质朴的方式，对着她的窗棂唱起了谢恩歌。他唱的大致是这样：

===Original===

> “The sun is in my eyes! O happy day!
 

 I grope as one half-blind. Behold the bounty of my lord!
 

 I, the poor slave of Allah,
 

 Am now the father of his son Abdallah,
 

 My wife the mother of his son, by leave of Allah,
 

 My little boy the brother of a child most blest, inshallah!
 

 The gracious consort of my lord, istaghfar Allah!
 

 Has granted to our lowliness a share in her good gift from Allah.
 

 May Allah bless my lord and lady in their noble offspring, and preserve his life to be the luminary of our future days.”

===Chinese===

> “太阳晃花了我的眼！哦，快乐的日子！
 

 我像半盲的人一般摸索。看啊，我老爷的恩泽！
 

 我，安拉可怜的奴仆，
 

 如今做了他儿子阿卜杜拉的父亲，
 

 我的妻子做了他儿子的母亲，蒙安拉的允准，
 

 我的小子做了那至福孩子的兄弟，若安拉意欲！
 

 我老爷仁德的夫人，求安拉宽恕（istaghfar Allah）！
 

 竟恩准我们这份卑微，分沾她自安拉领受的厚礼。
 

 愿安拉降福我老爷和夫人，并他们尊贵的子嗣，保他长命，做我们往后岁月的明灯。”

===Original===

She liked to hear him, his voice so near at hand produced a sense of true devotion and security. Missing his chant upon the following day, she inquired what had become of him. She was informed that, consequent upon his wife’s preferment, he had been appointed to a small position in the Government. It made her sad.

Her son was given over to the harem midwives to fulfil no end of ceremonies destined to frustrate the powers of evil. For a week he was not left alone or in the dark a single second. They carried him in a procession through the house, his future kingdom, and as each door was opened, sprinkled salt mixed with the seven seeds to exorcize the jinn who lurked within. Soon after birth his face had been defiled with certain powders, which Barakah could not persuade the women to wash off. It was a necessary precaution, they assured her, against jealous powers of darkness who, if they had an inkling of his beauty, would destroy him. Chief among these was the discarded wife of Adam, alluded to as El Carînah (the companion), the cause of man’s first fall, who hates Eve’s daughters and resents their great fertility. Where a child seems lovely and the mother shows delight in its appearance, she attacks them both; if, on the contrary, she sees it ugly and hears words of disappointment, she lets it live to spite the seed of Adam. For one so powerful, she must be very stupid to be taken in by such pretences, Barakah remarked; but they cried out that such was not the case, but Allah in His mercy had set limits to her sight and hearing. Each day the infant was the central figure in some ancient rite believed essential to its welfare.

===Chinese===

她喜欢听他唱；他的声音就在近旁，令人生出一种真切的虔敬与安稳之感。第二天没听见他的歌声，她便打听他去了哪里。有人告诉她，随着他妻子的高升，他已被安插进政府里的一个小职位。这让她难过。

她的儿子被交给内宅的接生婆们，去履行没完没了的仪式，专为挫败邪魔之力。整整一个星期，没有一秒钟让他独处，也没有一秒让他待在黑暗里。她们抱着他巡行全宅——他未来的王国——每开一扇门，便撒上掺了七种籽实的盐，驱逐潜伏在门内的恶灵。出生后不久，他的脸便被抹上某些粉末，巴拉卡怎么也劝不动那些女人给洗掉。她们向她担保，这是必要的防备，防的是好妒的黑暗之力——它们若嗅出他的俊美，就会毁了他。其中的头一号，便是亚当被休弃的发妻，人称埃尔·卡里娜（El Carînah，“同伴”之意），人类最初堕落的根由；她恨夏娃的女儿们，忌恨她们旺盛的生育。哪里的孩子看着可爱、做母亲的又面露得意，她就对母子俩一齐下手；反之，她若见孩子生得丑、又听得失望的话，便放他活着，好气一气亚当的子孙。巴拉卡说，这样厉害的角色，竟会被这点伎俩瞒过，想必蠢得很；她们却叫嚷起来，说绝非如此，而是安拉大发慈悲，给她的眼和耳设了限度。每一天，这婴孩都是某项古老仪式的中心人物，那些仪式被信为他的福祉所系。

===Original===

As Barakah lay in bed and watched the pattern of the lattice, her whole existence passing like a dream before her, she sought to reconcile her former English with her present Eastern life. Her son was a fine boy, they all assured her. It saddened her that she had no relations of her own to take a pride in him. In this mood she asked Yûsuf to write a little note to Mrs. Cameron entreating her to come one day and see the baby. He did so, and the answer was that she would come with pleasure.

Elated by the prospect of this visit, Barakah wished to have her offspring made presentable; but when she gave command to wash his face and wrap him in nice clothes, the goodies screamed aloud, and fetched the lady Fitnah to remonstrate with her. She gave way, perforce; and Mrs. Cameron beheld the infant at his worst.

===Chinese===

巴拉卡躺在床上，望着窗棂的格影，自己的一生如一场梦从眼前掠过，她想把自己前半生的英国旧我，同眼前的东方生活调和起来。她的儿子是个漂亮男孩——她们全都这样向她担保。难过的是，她自己没有亲族，不能为他骄傲。正是在这种心绪下，她请优素福写了张小便条给卡梅伦夫人，恳求她哪天过来瞧瞧婴儿。他写了，回话说她乐意前来。

一想到这次造访，巴拉卡喜不自胜，想把孩子拾掇得体面些；可她一下令给孩子洗脸、裹上漂亮的衣裳，那班老妈子便高声尖叫起来，搬来菲特娜夫人劝阻她。她只得让步；卡梅伦夫人看到的，便是这婴孩最不堪的模样。

===Original===

The visitor was very kind in her address to Barakah; but, when she held the baby for a minute, looking down at it, the latter, watching keenly, saw upon her face a quiver of extreme disfavour mixed with pity. The whiteness of her hands and face showed the child yellow; Barakah had thought him white as snow till then. A flush of anger and humiliation reached her brain.

“His face is dirty, the poor little one! Our Lord preserve him!” the visitor remarked in Arabic as she returned the baby to his nurse; at which there was an outburst of applause from the onlookers. They called down blessings on the lady’s head, desiring she might have herself a thousand children, not like this one, puny and unpleasant, but most beautiful.

Barakah, consumed with rage, murmured hoarsely in response to Mrs. Cameron’s farewell. The moment she was gone she burst out weeping.

===Chinese===

访客对巴拉卡的言辞极尽亲切；可当她把婴儿抱了片刻、低头端详时，一旁冷眼紧盯的巴拉卡，看见她脸上掠过一丝极度的嫌恶，混着怜悯。她手和脸的白皙，衬得孩子发黄；在那之前，巴拉卡一直当他白得像雪。一股愤怒与屈辱的热血直冲上头。

“他的脸脏兮兮的，可怜的小家伙！愿我们的主保佑他！”访客用阿拉伯语说着，把婴孩交还给他的乳母；这话博得旁观众人一阵喝彩。她们连声为夫人祝祷，愿她自己生上一千个孩子，个个不像这一个——又羸弱又不讨喜——而是漂亮极了。

巴拉卡强压怒火，哑着嗓子含混地应答卡梅伦夫人的道别。她一走，巴拉卡便放声大哭。

===Original===

“She did not like the child! She scorned my son, because he is not altogether white as she is.”

“Thou mistakest, O my dear! Be comforted!” cried Fitnah Khânum, while the other women round her exchanged pitying glances.

“Thou art not yet perfect in our prudent customs; but thy friend, though not a Muslimah, has learnt them, having been much longer in the land. Hast thou forgotten my instructions touching El Carînah? Nor is she alone to be redoubted, since Allah Himself abhors a boastful spirit, and dishonours those who make too much of any creature.﻿ ﻿…”

“O Lord! I know all that!” wailed Barakah. “But she disliked my child, despised him! I﻿—I saw it!”

===Chinese===

“她不喜欢这孩子！她看轻我的儿子，就因为他不像她那样浑身白。”

“你弄错了，哦，亲爱的！想开些！”菲特娜·哈努姆喊道，周围的其余女人交换着怜悯的眼色。

“你还未尽通我们这些谨慎的风俗；可你的朋友虽非穆斯林女子（Muslimah），却也学会了，因为她在这片土地上住得久得多。你可忘了我关于埃尔·卡里娜的那番叮嘱？何况要忌惮的还不止她一个，因为安拉自己也厌恶自夸的心，贬辱那些把任何造物夸得太甚的人。……”

“哦，主啊！这些我都知道！”巴拉卡哭喊道。“可她不喜欢我的孩子，看轻他！我——我亲眼看见的！”

===Original===

Conviction that the portion of the human race from which she sprang beheld her son as little better than a monkey, tortured Barakah. She had looked upon him as a mediator, but now sought revenge. Hot, feverish dreams of hate disturbed her rest; and when a spell of khamsîn weather robbed the world of energy she grew as weak and fretful as her thoughts were wild.

Already Barakah had kept her bed a fortnight longer than any Eastern woman would have dreamt of doing after childbirth. The lady Fitnah, seeing she did not gain strength, believed that some debilitating vile afrît was in her. The Frankish doctor said there was no cause for fear. She called him fool and worse, in her own circle; since by his disregard or ignorance of pious formulas he had left the door ajar for evil spirits. Resolved to stop the mischief, when no man was by she hung a plant of garlic in the room, burnt potent odours till its air grew suffocating, and dosed the patient with a paste compounded of the dust of mummies mixed with human milk. When these means failed to drive away the enemy, she sat down in despair among her cronies, and braced herself to try the last resort of all.

===Chinese===

她由此确信，自己出身的那一族人，把她儿子看得比猴子好不了多少；这念头折磨着巴拉卡。她本把他当作联结两边的中间人，如今却一心复仇。炽热狂乱的仇恨之梦搅扰她的安眠；待到一阵喀姆辛（khamsîn）热风天气夺去全世界的元气，她便愈发虚弱烦躁，恰如她的思绪一般狂乱。

巴拉卡产后卧床，早已比任何东方女人梦中所敢想的多躺了半个月。菲特娜夫人见她不见起色，认定她体内盘着某个蚀人元气的下作阿弗里特妖灵。法兰克医生说没有可惧之处。她在自己的圈子里骂他是蠢货，还有更难听的；因为他无视或不懂敬神的仪轨，等于给邪灵留了半掩的门。她决心堵住这祸患，趁没有男人在旁，在屋里挂起一整棵大蒜，焚烧浓烈的香气，直到满屋空气令人窒息，又给病人灌下一种用木乃伊粉末调着人乳做成的膏糊。这些法子都赶不走那仇敌，她便绝望地在老姐妹们中间坐下，横下心来试那万不得已的最后一招。

===Original===

This was the “zâr”﻿—a very awful ceremony, of which she was exceedingly afraid. Her wish to hold it in the house﻿—risking the Pasha’s favour, and her life through terror﻿—was proof of her devoted love for Barakah. The dear one must be healed at any price.

Accordingly, she summoned negresses of those who hold familiar intercourse with demons, bought a kid and several fowls alive, and made arrangements to secure the sickroom to herself and her confederates for two good hours upon a certain afternoon.

===Chinese===

这就是扎尔——一场可怖至极的仪式，她对它怕得要命。她偏要在这宅子里行它——冒着失掉帕夏恩宠、因恐惧而送命的风险——正证明了她对巴拉卡的挚爱。这位心爱的人儿，无论如何也要治好。

于是她唤来几个黑人女子——正是那类与魔鬼过从甚密的——买了一只小山羊和几只活鸡，又安排妥当，要在某个下午把病房包占下来，归她和她的同伙们足足用上两个钟头。

===Original===

Barakah was roused out of a troubled sleep by women moving out the furniture from both bedchamber and salon, and covering the floor with worn-out linen and the cheapest matting. They went, and she lay wondering, when Fitnah Khânum came and bade her have no fear. The ceremony she was going to witness was a potent medicine, well calculated to restore her health completely. Many servants, female children, and familiars of the household trooped in with noiseless feet and squatted down along the wall. Then came a group of half a dozen negresses, fantastically dressed in rags of finery, with ringing anklets; one of whom embraced a struggling little goat, while the others bore live chickens by the feet. Bold-eyed and with a swaggering gait, they marched up to the bed, and seemed to offer up the fowls and kid to Barakah, who could not understand the words they uttered in a screeching chant. They then danced back to the adjoining room, of which the door stood open. Upon the threshold madness seemed to seize them. They fell upon the kid with cries of glee. The creature, bleating piteously, was flung into an earthen bowl placed there in readiness. Amid mad laughter knives were brandished and brought down, hands helping to extract the creature’s life. The fowls were likewise gashed and torn asunder; the matting round grew foul with steaming entrails. Another minute and the slayers reappeared, their black arms purpled to the elbow, dripping blood, their faces and their lips defiled with it; and then began a devilish dance of self-abandonment, all the more horrible for its approach to beauty. The sleek skin of the dancers caught blue lights; their fixed eyes gleamed enormous, like those painted on the lids of mummies. Barakah believed herself in hell, forever lost; it was as if an iron hand compressed her throat. Her heart beat wildly. One of the women, the most shameless, lurched towards her, stretching out a bloodstained hand. Her heart gave one tremendous beat and then stood still.

===Chinese===

巴拉卡从纷乱的睡梦中惊醒，女人们正把家具从卧室和客厅里搬出去，往地上铺破旧的亚麻布和最便宜的草席。她们去了，她躺着纳闷，菲特娜·哈努姆便走来，嘱咐她别怕。她即将亲眼目睹的这场仪式，是一剂猛药，保管能让她的身子完全康复。宅子里许多仆人、女童和常来常往的熟人蹑着脚鱼贯而入，沿墙蹲坐。随后来了六个黑人女子，穿着绫罗的破片扮出的奇装异服，脚镯叮当作响；其中一个怀里搂着一只挣扎的小山羊，其余的手里倒提着活鸡。她们眼神泼辣，步态张扬，一路走到床前，仿佛是把鸡和羊献上给巴拉卡，口中尖声唱着什么，她一句也听不懂。接着她们舞回隔壁的房间，那边的门敞开着。一上门槛，疯狂似乎攫住了她们。她们欢呼着扑向那小山羊。那生灵哀哀地叫着，被扔进一只预先备好的陶盆。疯狂的笑声中，刀子挥起又落下，一双手也上来帮着结果它的性命。那些鸡同样被剁开、撕碎；四周的草席被冒着热气的肠肚弄得污秽不堪。又过了一分钟，杀生者们重新露面，黑色的手臂直到肘弯都染成了紫红，滴着血，脸上唇上尽是血污；随后开始了一场自弃的魔舞，因其逼近于美而愈发骇人。舞者们光洁的皮肤映出幽蓝的光；她们僵直的眼睛瞪得灼灼极大，就像画在木乃伊眼睑上的那些。巴拉卡只当自己落进了地狱，永世沉沦；仿佛一只铁手扼住了她的喉咙。她的心狂跳。其中一个最不知羞的女人，踉跄着朝她走来，伸出一只血污的手。她的心猛跳了一下，随即停住了。

===Original===

When she recovered consciousness it was to find the lady Fitnah bending over her. The negresses had gone, the room was cleansed, the furniture replaced exactly as before. She might have thought she had been dreaming had not Yûsuf’s mother whispered eagerly:

“Breathe not a word to Yûsuf or our lord the Pasha. Deny by Allah that thou sawest anything. If not, the afrît which we have with pains extracted will return and kill thee.”

In her weak state of mind, oppressed with dreadful and disgusting images, Barakah believed the words and shuddered. She was ill for weeks.

===Chinese===

她恢复知觉时，发现菲特娜夫人正俯身望着她。黑人女子们已经走了，屋子收拾干净了，家具原封不动地放回了原处。若不是优素福的母亲急切地附耳低语，她简直要以为自己做了一场梦：

“对优素福和帕夏老爷一个字也休提。凭安拉起誓，一口咬定你什么也没看见。不然的话，我们费尽力气拔除的那阿弗里特妖灵就会回来，要了你的命。”

巴拉卡心神虚弱，被可怕又可憎的景象压着，信了这话，打了个寒噤。她一病便是好几个星期。

## XL / 四十

===Original===
It was strange how, with her broken spirit, she regained a kindly interest in all around her. She had found the keynote of harem existence﻿—resignation; not merely passive, but exultant as an act of worship. The gross, full-blooded speech, the something cruel in these women, which in the day of sentimental pride had seemed intolerable, was but the natural outcome of relentless vision. In the first fervour of her self-abasement she stood beside the deathbed of Murjânah Khânum, watched her last struggle, and endured the death-room orgies without flinching. Thenceforth she took up the old Muslim standpoint, denouncing all the fallacies of Europe. Having won from Yûsuf the confession that he kept three other women, she had them brought to the old Pasha’s palace, where she lived thenceforward, to rid his dealings of the surreptitiousness which smacks of vice. She received them sometimes in her rooms, and took benignant notice of their children, but remained aloof. They called her “the great lady,” and deferred to her.

When the festivals of visitation of the dead came round, she would withdraw into the tomb for days together, but showed no mournfulness at other seasons. When Englishwomen called on her (as sometimes happened, for Yûsuf held a high position in the Government), she spoke in stilted French, and never hinted that she knew their language, or was other than the thing she seemed﻿—a Turkish lady. She felt assured that, had she carried out her plan and fled to Europe after her son’s death, she would have gone mad in that sentimental atmosphere with all her memories. More than the English, she disliked some French and German ladies who, without renouncing their religion or their nationality, had married Muslims. These, in their visits, showed a curiosity, and used a tone of patronage, which was offensive. Of races less exclusive than the English, they kept their European friends, maintained their liberty. They had no real conception of the harem life.

===Chinese===
说来奇怪，她心已摧折，反倒重新对周遭一切生出一种亲切的兴趣。她找到了内宅生存的要义——顺从；不止是消极的顺从，而是欢欣踊跃、当作一场敬拜来行的顺从。这些女人粗豪血气的言语，她们身上那一点残酷，在她自伤自怜的年月里原是难以容忍的，如今看来，不过是无情洞见的自然结果。在自甘卑下的最初热忱里，她守在穆尔贾娜·哈努姆的临终榻旁，看着她最后的挣扎，眼睛也不眨一下地受住了停灵房里那场放浪的喧闹。从那以后，她站上了旧日穆斯林的立场，痛斥欧洲的一切谬见。她从优素福口中问出了他另养着三个女人的实话，便把她们接进老帕夏的宅邸——她自己此后便住在那里——好除去他行事中那股透着邪僻味的鬼祟气。她有时在自己房里接见她们，对她们的孩子也和颜悦色地垂顾一二，却始终保持着距离。她们称她“大夫人”，对她恭敬从命。

每逢谒墓的节期来临，她便一连数日隐进墓室里去，别的时节却不见半点哀容。有英国女人来拜会她时（这样的事间或有之，因为优素福在政府里身居高位），她便拿一口生硬做作的法语应答，绝不露半点口风，说自己懂得她们的语言，也不叫人疑心自己不是眼下这副身份——一位土耳其贵妇。她深信，倘若当年果真依计而行、在儿子死后逃往欧洲，带着满腹记忆置身那种伤感多情的空气里，她早就疯了。比起英国女人，她更厌恶某些法国和德国的女人——她们既不舍弃自己的宗教，也不舍弃自己的国籍，却嫁了穆斯林。这一类人来拜访时，总带着几分猎奇，端着一副居高临下的腔调，实在叫人反感。她们所属的种族不像英国人那样排外，她们保住了自己的欧洲朋友，维持着自己的自由。她们对内宅生活却全无真切的领会。

===Original===
She was angry with her daughter when the latter told her:

“At marriage I shall make my husband promise to have me alone before I yield to him. It is become the fashion in the noblest houses. Of course, if I should fail to bear a son, I should release him.”

“Endeavour to retain him by thy charms,” the mother scolded. “O foolish one, to make him promise is to make him sin. In following the madness of the Frankish women, thou dost but court deception in the Frankish manner. It all comes of the reading of French stories without knowledge or intelligence.”

It vexed her soul to see young girls forsaking the old stately way to hanker for the trash of Europe, which they misconceived. Afîfah had no notion of that mutual love and comradeship which is the sole excuse for monogamic marriage; she merely thought it fine to be an only wife. When harem ladies talked of feminine emancipation, they understood it to involve licentiousness. Their genius was at once too indolent and too direct ever to harbour European vapours.

===Chinese===
女儿对她说了下面一番话，她不禁动怒：

“成婚的时候，我要我的丈夫许下诺言：在我委身于他之前，答应此生只有我一个。这如今已成了最高门第八家的风尚。当然，倘若我生不出儿子，我便放他自由。”

“要用你的魅力去拴住他，”母亲呵斥道。“哦，傻丫头，叫他许诺，便是叫他犯罪。你追着法兰克女人的疯癫跑，不过是照法兰克的路数自招欺瞒。说到底，都是没见识、没头脑，偏偏又读法国小说读出来的。”

眼见年轻姑娘们抛开旧日端庄的做派，去渴慕欧洲的那些玩意儿——还尽是误解了的玩意儿——她心里便有气。阿菲法（Afîfah）全然不懂那种两情相悦、同心相伴的情谊，而那正是一夫一妻婚姻唯一说得过去的理由；她不过是觉得做唯一的妻子很有体面。内宅的女人们谈论妇女解放，她们所理解的解放，必然连着淫荡。她们的性子又太懒散、又太直截，断然容不下欧洲的那些虚浮气泡。

===Original===
But these vagaries were restricted to a score of wealthy houses, and even there the harem life went on the same. There were the lattices, the veils, the eunuchs, and some few slaves in spite of many edicts; the ladies still had their old interests and rules and customs; the same old women hawked the news and bawdy tales from house to house; and superstition flourished more than ever. Young wives who had been bred up in the Frankish culture, and insisted on the husband talking French in private, consulted witches when the baby ailed, or sent a portion of his clothing to be bloodstained at a zâr.

Afîfah married in due course a high official, and Barakah spent half the year with her. The mother had her little circle of old friends, and many protégés﻿—in particular the house of Ghandûr, whose firstborn, Ali, she regarded always as her son. Her age seemed not unhappy.

===Chinese===
然而这些奇思异想，也不过限于二十来家富贵宅门，便在那些人家，内宅生活也照旧运转。窗棂还是窗棂，面纱还是面纱，阉人照旧当差，纵然法令颁了一道又一道，也还留着少数奴隶；太太们照旧有她们的老兴致、老规矩、老风俗；还是那班老太婆，挨家挨户兜售新闻和荤话；迷信反倒比以往任何时候都昌盛。在法兰克文化里调教长大、非要丈夫私下里讲法语的小媳妇们，孩子一病，照样去问巫婆，或者把孩子的一件衣裳送去，叫它在扎尔仪式上沾上血。

阿菲法到时嫁给了一位高官，巴拉卡一年里便有半年同她一起过。做母亲的有她自己一小圈老友，还有许多受她照拂的人——尤其是甘杜尔一家：他家头生的儿子阿里，她始终看作自己的儿子。她的晚年，看上去并不算不幸。

===Original===
On a summer evening, she was sitting on the roof of the old Pasha’s palace, watching the sunset with Gulbeyzah, Na’imah, and two of Yûsuf’s sisters who had come to visit her. Red light as of a conflagration shone around them. The shadow cast towards them by the parapet was vastly elongated and as black as ink. A tray with fruit and sherbet rested on the ground, and a slave-girl, squatting on her heels before them, awaiting their good pleasure to remove it, followed their conversation with an eager smile.

The English had been five-and-twenty years in Cairo, and mighty changes had distressed the world of men, but the harem seemed changeless in its calm seclusion. Beliefs as old as Egypt lingered there, and new things introduced were made to serve old customs. Yet the ladies had been sighing at the growth of innovations.

“Dost thou remember, O my sweet one, the little window in the servants’ passage where I used to sit and dream as a young maiden?” sighed Gulbeyzah. “Is it still there? I must go down and see it! And the little lover on the roof who waved his arms so wildly? I wonder did he die of me, the poor young man! Thou didst blame me for that small amusement; but, by Allah, girls in these days are less innocent. My granddaughters read French books till their brains are addled. They had better sit alone and dream as I did.”

“The best of life is thinking with hands idle,” answered Na’imah. “All women do it, and so form their minds. But the girls today have no resources. They despise embroidery. They needs must be amused by some strange sight, excited by unhealthy reading, or they die of ennui.”

===Chinese===
一个夏日的傍晚，她坐在老帕夏宅邸的屋顶上，同古尔贝雅扎、纳伊玛（Na’imah），还有来看她的优素福的两个妹妹，一起望着日落。大火也似的红光罩着她们。女儿墙朝她们投来的影子拉得极长，黑得像墨。地上搁着一只托盘，盛着水果和果子露；一个小女奴蹲踞在她们跟前，只等她们兴尽了便好撤下，脸上带着热切的笑，听着她们谈话。

英国人到开罗已经二十五年，翻天覆地的巨变搅扰了男人的世界，内宅却在它静穆的幽居里纹丝未改。与埃及一样古老的信仰在那里流连不去，新引进的东西，也都被拿来供旧风俗驱使。然而太太们一直在为种种新潮的滋长而叹息。

“你还记得吗，我的心肝，仆人过道里那扇小窗？我做小姑娘的时候，常坐在那里做梦。”古尔贝雅扎叹道。“它还在吗？我一定得下去瞧瞧！还有屋顶上那个小情人，两条胳膊挥得那么疯？我真疑心他是为我死的，那可怜的年轻人！你那时还为那桩小小的消遣怪过我；可是凭安拉起誓，如今的女孩子们可不如我那时清白。我的孙女们读法国书，把脑子都读糊涂了。她们还不如像我当年那样，独自坐着做梦呢。”

“人生至乐，就是闲着双手出神。”纳伊玛答道。“女人人人都这么着，心思也就这么养成。可如今的姑娘们全无寄托。她们瞧不起刺绣。她们非要有什么稀奇景儿逗趣，靠不健康的读物来刺激，不然就要闷死了。”

===Original===
“Look, look!” exclaimed Gulbeyzah suddenly.

They all stared in the direction which her finger pointed.

The slave, who had been waiting their good pleasure to remove the tray, had started up and stood against the parapet, looking out towards the sunset, with her back towards them. Both her arms were raised as for an incantation. The rosy light enveloped her as with a halo. Her shadow, grown enormous, covered half the roof.

“I seek refuge in Allah. Is she worshipping the sun?” gasped Na’imah. “She should be punished for such gross impiety.”

“She is going to give that crow his salutation,” said Gulbeyzah.

The bird had wheeled away, but now drew near again.

===Chinese===
“看哪，看哪！”古尔贝雅扎忽然喊道。

众人都朝她手指的方向望去。

那个一直候着撤托盘的小女奴，这时已一跃而起，背朝她们立在女儿墙边，望着落日的方向。她双臂高举，仿佛在念诵咒文。玫瑰色的光把她整个人裹住，像一圈光轮。她那涨得巨大的影子，罩住了半个屋顶。

“我求安拉庇佑。她莫不是在拜太阳？”纳伊玛倒吸一口气。“这样恶俗的不敬，该受责罚。”

“她这是要向那只乌鸦行礼呢。”古尔贝雅扎说。

那鸟儿本已盘旋远去，这时又飞近了。

===Original===
> “If good the news, O bird, alight and welcome;
 

 If bad, draw up thy claws and hie away!”

===Chinese===
> “鸟儿啊，若是佳音，请落下来，受我欢迎；
 

 若是凶讯，就收起利爪，快快飞去！”

===Original===
The slave-girl having chanted the time-honoured formula, turned to resume her attitude of patient waiting. She grinned to find herself the object of all eyes.

“I shamed him,” she remarked, with a wide flash of teeth, as she sat down once more.

“Thou knowest the history﻿—not so, O my flower?” said Na’imah.

“Umm ed-Dahak has related it a thousand times﻿—the Lord have mercy on her!” Barakah made answer in a tone of fond remembrance.

===Chinese===
小女奴唱罢这句世代相传的老话，便转过身去，重新摆出耐心等候的姿态。发觉自己成了众目所瞩，她咧嘴一笑。

“我臊了他一臊。”她说着，齿光一闪，重新蹲坐下去。

“这个典故你是知道的——对不对，我的花儿？”纳伊玛说。

“乌姆·埃德-达哈克（Umm ed-Dahak）讲过它一千遍了——愿主怜悯她！”巴拉卡用一腔温存的怀念答道。

===Original===
“O light of my eyes, surely every woman here in Egypt knows it!” giggled Yûsuf’s youngest sister. “They say it has been handed down among us from the days of our lord Noah, when we sent up the petition.”

“That every girl might be allowed four husbands?” asked Gulbeyzah.

“More! more!﻿—or so old Umm ed-Dahak used to tell me﻿—as many as she could endure, my sweet!” laughed Barakah.

“May Allah destroy the house of that most wicked crow, who has kept us waiting all these thousands of long years till now!” groaned Na’imah.

===Chinese===
“哦，我眼里的光，埃及这里的女人哪个不知道它！”优素福最小的妹妹咯咯笑道。“据说打从我们的主努哈的时代、我们递上那道请愿书的时候起，它就在我们中间一代代传了下来。”

“就是求让每个姑娘准许配四个丈夫的那道？”古尔贝雅扎问。

“不止！不止！——老乌姆·埃德-达哈克当年就是这样告诉我——她受得住多少，便有多少，我的心肝！”巴拉卡笑道。

“愿安拉毁了那只最歹毒的乌鸦的家，害我们干等了这几千年漫长的岁月，一直等到今天！”纳伊玛呻吟道。

===Original===
“How long! How long, O Lord!” sighed out Gulbeyzah in a comic ecstasy.

“Never in my time, that is certain, under Allah,” answered Barakah. “But perhaps you young ones.﻿ ﻿…”

“Young ones! O Allah, listen to her! Ah, alas for us!” cried Yûsuf’s youngest sister, with a sobbing laugh. Whereat the ladies looked into each other’s faces, illumined by the greenish light which follows sunset. A silence and a shudder fell upon them.

“Allah have mercy on us!” Gulbeyzah broke the stillness with a shrug. “Behold us finished for the joys of this low world.”

“The praise to Allah!” answered Na’imah devoutly.

They were all old withered bodies, for the grave.

===Chinese===
“还要多久！还要多久，哦，主啊！”古尔贝雅扎长叹一声，做出如痴如狂的可笑劲儿。

“在我这辈子是等不上啦，这是千真万确的，凭安拉说。”巴拉卡答道。“不过你们这些年轻的，或许……”

“年轻的！哦，安拉啊，听听她说的！唉，我们好苦哇！”优素福最小的妹妹喊道，笑声里带着呜咽。众夫人闻言面面相觑，落日之后那泛青的绿光照亮了她们的脸。一阵沉默、一阵战栗，落到她们身上。

“安拉怜悯我们吧！”古尔贝雅扎耸了耸肩，打破了寂静。“瞧我们这份儿，尘世的乐子算是享到头了。”

“赞美安拉！”纳伊玛虔诚地应道。

她们个个都是衰老干瘪的身躯，只等着进坟墓了。

## XV / 十五

===Original===

With the return of reason a new spirit came to Barakah. At the moment of her seizure she had been exasperated with her Eastern life. She awoke to rapture in it, to impatience of the European nurse and doctor. The smell of them, as they leaned over her, was an offence; their voices jarred so that often she would hide her head beneath the bedclothes to shut out the sound.
On the other hand, she listened eagerly to noises out of doors﻿—the creak of the shadûf which tipped up water on the garden, the camel-bells, the chant of passing funerals; she watched the sunlight stud with gems the inky lacework of her lattice, and eagerly inhaled the breeze which entered; and Yûsuf’s daily visits were her joy. In the forest of distorted memories through which her soul had wandered friendless like a ragged child, the Europeans she encountered had reviled her; the love of Yûsuf and his people had been all her hope.
In the hunger which distressed her convalescence, the growing disaffection for a diet all of milk, her fancy pictured feasts of Eastern dishes, English cookery appearing loathsome in the memory. Strangest of all, she could now think in Arabic, of which, before her illness, she had scarce a sentence.

===Chinese===

理智一经回归，一种新的精神便来到巴拉卡身上。发病的那一刻，她还在对东方生活满怀愠怒；一觉醒来，她却对它欢欣沉醉，对欧洲的护士和医生再难容忍。他们俯身探看时身上那股气味，本身就是一种冒犯；他们的嗓音聒耳刺心，她常常把头埋进被褥底下，好隔绝那声音。

另一方面，她热切地聆听户外的声响——沙杜夫（shadûf，桔槔）往园中倒水的吱呀声、驼铃、送葬队伍行过时的诵唱；她看阳光在窗棂墨色的花格上缀满宝石，急切地吸进拂面的微风；而优素福每日的探望，便是她的欢喜。在记忆扭曲成的森林里，她的灵魂曾像个衣衫褴褛的孩子，无亲无友地游荡；途中遇上的欧洲人尽对她辱骂；优素福和他家人的爱，便是她全部的指望。

康复期里，饥饿煎熬着她，她对清一色牛奶的饮食日益生厌，幻想中摆开一席席东方盛馔，英国烹调在记忆里显得可憎。最稀奇的是，她如今竟能用阿拉伯语思考了——生病之前，她连一句囫囵话都难得。

===Original===

As soon as she had licence to see visitors, the Pasha’s harem came in force to greet her. The lady Fitnah fell upon her in a transport of affection, and she responded with entire abandonment, thankful to have at last the love of Yûsuf’s mother. The elderly princess, Amînah Khânum, and other ladies of importance, paid her visits and, as her health improved, carried her off to their own houses﻿—not for an hour, but for whole days together. There, in the perfumed shade, she was enthroned with cushions, fanned and sprinkled, nourished delicately, and sung to sleep when she showed signs of weariness. The sense of frailty and of worth was exquisite. She was content to be the guarded pet, and let them plan; regarding them as beings of a higher race, with whom it would be vanity to try to cope. Their freedom from the sentimental mists of Europe helped this feeling, and so did their bold vision of existence, blinking nothing. The potential cruelty which lurked beneath their gentleness subdued her; the way they talked of death habitually made her feel a timid child.
Thus, with the body pampered and the mind enslaved, she studied and observed their life, completely fascinated. The world of women was, she found, a great republic, with liberties extending to the meanest slave, and something of the strength which comes of solidarity. Unless in jealous fury, no woman would inform against another, bond or free; nor fail to help her in the hour of need. They had their shibboleths, their customs, rites, and ceremonies, even their courts of justice, independent of the world of men. Each lady owning slaves controlled them absolutely. Her husband never saw their faces, hardly knew them. The law against his making love among them, except by her command, was very drastic. The child of such a union would have been her slave. If he required a concubine, he had to buy, not steal one. So sacred with the Muslims was the married woman’s right to property﻿—a right which was not recognized at all in England Occasionally Barakah heard talk of cruelties which chilled her blood; but her friends excused them on the ground of anger, which was for them a visitation from on high. The very victims, they assured her, never felt as she did.

===Chinese===

她一获准见客，帕夏的内宅女眷便倾巢前来问安。菲特娜夫人满怀狂喜扑到她身上，她也全然忘我地回应，庆幸终于得到了优素福母亲的爱。年长的公主阿米娜·哈努姆（Amînah Khânum），连同另几位有身份的女眷，都来探望她；待她身子渐好，索性把她接到自家宅中去——不是待一个钟头，而是一连整日。在那香气氤氲的荫凉里，她被安置在软垫围就的宝座上，有人打扇，有人洒水，有人奉上精美的滋养，她一露倦意，便有人唱着歌儿哄她入睡。娇弱之感与贵重之感，微妙已极。她甘愿做一只受看护的宠儿，任凭她们筹谋，把她们看作更高一等的族类，想同她们较量不过是徒劳。她们身上没有欧洲那层感伤的迷雾，助长了这份心境；她们直面人生、无所避讳的大胆眼光，也一并助长。温存底下潜伏的残忍把她镇住了；她们惯于谈论死亡的口吻，更使她自觉是个怯生生的孩子。

就这样，身子受着娇养，心神受着收服，她研究着、观察着她们的生活，心醉神迷。她发现，女人的世界是一个庞大的共和国，自由一直延伸到最卑微的奴隶身上，还带着团结所生的几分力量。除非妒火狂怒，没有女人会告发另一个女人，不论对方为奴为自由身；也没有女人会在她急难之时坐视不管。她们有自己的一套口令暗语，自己的风俗、仪礼和庆典，甚至有自己的公堂，独立于男人的世界之外。凡蓄有奴隶的女眷，对奴隶握有绝对之权。她的丈夫从不见那些人的面，几乎不认得她们。除奉她之命，严禁他在她们中间沾惹情爱，这道法令极是严酷。这般结合生下的孩子，便归她为奴。他若要一名姬妾，须得花钱去买，不许巧取。在穆斯林这里，已婚妇人掌管财产的权利神圣不可侵犯——这权利在英国根本无人认账。巴拉卡偶尔也听得几桩令她血冷的残酷事；但她的朋友们把那些开脱为暴怒，而在她们眼里，暴怒乃是上天的降罚。她们还一口担保：受害的本人，断不会有她这样的感受。

===Original===

One feature of the harem life which shocked her was the equalling of black with white. The Muslim faith disowning all race prejudice, a strain of negro blood appeared in the best families; and any negro having fortune was esteemed as marriageable as the fairest Turk. Then the black slaves, though less regarded because they cost less than the white Circassians, possessed great influence, particularly in the article of superstition, which they quite controlled. Weeds from the heathen Sudan, brought to Cairo in the convoy of the slave-dealer, luxuriated in that tank of guarded ignorance; and many an enlightened Muslim would have died of horror had he known the works of darkness countenanced by his harem﻿—the sacrifices to malignant beings; the veneration paid to hoary negresses for demoniacal possession; the use to which the name of God was sometimes put. To Barakah, however, in those early days, such fancies﻿—what she heard of them﻿—seemed merely comic. She ranked them with the women’s playfulness, their funny stories. She was enamoured of their life as she conceived it, enslaved and thrilled by its unblushing candour. This was the season of her real conversion, which reached its climax on a certain morning, when she was carried in a guarded litter to the citadel to witness the departure of the yearly pilgrimage. From a place reserved for ladies on the ramparts she beheld the troops, the guilds of dervishes, defile before the Khedive’s tent, and then the great procession wind away. Fanfares sounded, cannons roared, and from the multitude which hid the square and covered every roof and balcony in sight, beading with heads the very summits of the citadel, a sigh went up.

===Chinese===

内宅生活里令她震惊的一桩，是黑与白的平起平坐。穆斯林信仰摒弃一切种族成见，最上等的世家也掺着一缕黑人血统；而任何有点家财的黑人，都被看作同最白皙的土耳其人一样堪配嫁娶。再说黑奴，虽说因价钱比白皮肤的切尔克斯人便宜而不甚受看重，势力却极大，尤其在迷信这一门上，全由他们把持。从异教的苏丹挟来的莠草，随着奴隶贩子的队伙到了开罗，便在那座闭锁无知的池沼里恣意疯长；多少开明的穆斯林，倘若知道自家内宅纵容着那些黑暗的勾当——向恶灵的献祭；对白发苍苍的黑人老妪奉若神明，只因她身附邪魔；真主之名有时竟被派上的用场——只怕要吓死过去。然而对那时的巴拉卡来说，这类玄虚——她所听来的那些——只显得滑稽。她把它们同女人们的嬉闹、她们的趣闻笑话归作一类。她倾心于自己心目中的那种生活，被它那份不害臊的坦白迷住、慑住。这正是她真正皈依的时节，并在某个上午达到了顶点：那天，她被人用一顶有护卫的轿舆抬上城堡，去观瞻一年一度的朝觐启程。从城墙上专为女眷留出的位置，她望见军队、望见德尔维希（dervish，苦行僧）的各路行会，一队队从赫迪夫（Khedive）的帐前鱼贯而过，随后那浩大的行列蜿蜒远去。号角齐鸣，炮声隆隆；广场被人海掩没，目力所及的屋顶与阳台无不挤满，连城堡的顶上都缀满了攒动的人头——人海之中，升起一声浩叹。

===Original===

Barakah was in an ecstasy. When her eyes wearied of the flash and movement, she surveyed the vast blue sky, the coloured, sunlit walls, the minarets where doves were circling. She pictured the long journey of the pilgrims, on the shining sea, across the burning sands, to the eternal sanctuary. What scene in Christian Europe could be matched with this? Religion, but a mummy there, here lived and moved.
Returning home, she felt a craving to unbosom, and bethought her of a girl in England, once her friend. She called for ink and pens, and wrote forthwith to Julia Long, recounting her changed fortunes, and extolling Egypt. She described the scene she had that morning witnessed, and concluded:

===Chinese===

巴拉卡如痴如醉。看倦了那流光攒动，她便放眼环顾辽阔的蓝天、阳光下色彩斑斓的墙垣、鸽子盘旋其上的宣礼塔。她想象朝觐者的漫长旅程：渡过波光潋滟的大海，穿过灼人的沙漠，直抵那永恒的圣所。在信基督教的欧洲，有什么场面能与此相提并论？在那边，宗教不过是一具木乃伊；在这里，它却活着、动着。

回家之后，她心痒难搔，只想一吐为快，便想起英国的一位姑娘，她旧日的友人。她唤人取来墨水和笔，当即写信给茱莉亚·朗（Julia Long），细说自己际遇的变迁，把埃及夸上了天。她描绘了那天上午亲眼所见的场面，末了写道：

===Original===

> “Julia darling, you will think it strange, but I am sure that this religion is the true one. Here every woman has a chance to marry, and the accidents of wealth and birth are not the barriers they are at home. Polygamy is not at all what people think. The Muslims are as strict as Puritans about morality; and the women here are happier than those at home. Europe has gone all wrong, and so has Christianity. Here we believe in Jesus just as you do: we know that His religion is the true one; but St. Paul and others after him corrupted it. Do think of this, and learn about Muhammadanism. I would give anything that you might find the happiness that I have found. My husband will be taking me to Paris at the end of June. Do try and join us there. We will pay all expenses.
> “With true affection from your old friend 

 **Mary** 

 Madame Yûsuf Bey Mohamed, 

 c/o Mohamed Pasha Sâlih, 

 Cairo, Egypt.”

===Chinese===

> “茱莉亚亲爱的，你会觉得奇怪，可我确信这个宗教才是真正的宗教。在这里，每个女人都有出嫁的机会，财富与出身那些运气，并不像在家乡那样筑成高墙。一夫多妻全不是人们想的那样。穆斯林在道德上严谨得同清教徒一般；这里的女人也比家乡的女人幸福。欧洲全走岔了路，基督教也一样。在这里，我们同你们一样信奉耶稣：我们知道他的宗教才是真的；只是圣保罗和他之后的那些人把它败坏了。务请想想这些，去了解一番穆罕默德教。只要你也能寻到我所寻到的幸福，要我舍弃什么都行。我丈夫六月底要带我去巴黎。务请设法到那里与我们相会。一切费用全由我们承担。
> “怀着真挚的情爱，你的老朋友

 **玛丽**

 优素福·贝伊·穆罕默德夫人，

 转穆罕默德·帕夏·萨利赫收，

 埃及，开罗。”

===Original===

This letter was read out to Yûsuf in the evening. He applauded it, and vowed she had a natural gift of eloquence. He asked for a minute description of her friend, seeming much pleased to think that they would meet in Paris; and when Barakah had satisfied him to the best of her remembrance, chuckled:
“And you love her? Then you would not object to have her for your durrah!”
She warned him archly that she could be jealous.

===Chinese===

当晚，这封信念给优素福听了。他击节称赏，一口咬定她天生一副辩才。他要她把那位朋友细细描摹一番，想到两人将在巴黎相见，显得大为欢喜；等巴拉卡凭记忆把他想问的都交代尽了，他嘿嘿一笑：

“你爱她？那你总不会反对让她做你的杜拉吧！”

她狡黠地警告他，自己可是会嫉妒的。

===Original===

Barakah called often on the lady Fitnah, who just now was in high feather, having been commissioned by Murjânah Khânum to find out a husband for the latter’s slave, Gulbeyzah. At once she sent out go-betweens in all directions, threads of a gigantic web, in which she sat and waited. Flies soon came﻿—ladies with eligible sons or husbands needing matrimony﻿—whose claims the shrewd Egyptian sifted, smelling out the slightest fraud. Barakah was interested in these doings, naturally, seeing they concerned the welfare of her closest friend.
Murjânah Khânum wished to emancipate a charming slave and place her in a good position, at the same time seeking some remuneration for her previous outlay. She appointed Fitnah Khânum her intendant. Those were the naked facts. But the word “price” was never mentioned in discussion of the subject; it was always “dowry,” of which a third part would be paid, of course, to the bride’s people. Gulbeyzah was referred to as a cherished daughter of the house; her wishes were consulted with regard to each proposal; and no one was annoyed when she seemed hard to please.

===Chinese===

巴拉卡时常去拜访菲特娜夫人。夫人这些日子正得意风光，受了穆尔贾娜·哈努姆的委托，要替后者的女奴古尔贝雅扎觅一位丈夫。她当即遣出媒人，四路出动，像一张巨网张出的丝线，自己端坐网心静候。苍蝇不久便来——都是些女眷，有的替条件相宜的儿子物色，有的替有待续娶的男人张罗——这位精明的埃及女人细细筛检她们的来历，一丝诈伪的气味也瞒不过她的鼻子。巴拉卡关切这些动静原是自然，因为这关系到她最亲密的朋友的幸福。

穆尔贾娜·哈努姆的心愿，是放免一个可人的女奴，替她安顿一个好去处，同时也为先前的破费讨回几分报偿。她委任菲特娜·哈努姆做总管。赤裸裸的事实不过如此。但议论起这件事，“价钱”二字从不出口；说来说去总是“嫁妆”，而嫁妆的三分之一，自然要付给新娘的娘家人。古尔贝雅扎被口口声声称作宅里受宠的女儿；每有一桩提亲，都要征询她本人的意思；她显得百般挑剔，也没人见怪。

===Original===

“Thou art like Leylah Khânum,” whispered Barakah. “Thou wilt choose and choose away till none are left.”
“By no means,” was the laughing answer. “I am a young maid. Moreover, it is not the man I stickle for; it is society.”

===Chinese===

“你活脱像莱拉·哈努姆，”巴拉卡悄声道。“你要挑呀挑呀，一直挑到挑光了才算完。”

“才不呢，”回答的人笑着。“我是个年轻的闺女。再说，我讲究的并不是男人，我要的是个往来的圈子。”

===Original===

Whenever ladies whom she did not choose came to inspect her, Gulbeyzah donned a rustic air and talked to shock them. Barakah had no idea of what she meant when saying she required society, until one day she told her:
“Praise to Allah! Only think, beloved! Three Circassians, young like me, from the same district! Their lord﻿—a Pasha of the richest﻿—wants another like them. They are gratified. I have been recommended. They come today for my inspection. Thou shalt see them presently, as also a Gulbeyzah no one ever saw before. O day of milk! O wave-crest of all days!”

===Chinese===

每逢不相中的女眷来相看，古尔贝雅扎便装出一副乡气，说话专拣骇人的说。她说她要个“圈子”，巴拉卡一直不明白是什么意思，直到有一天她告诉她：

“赞美安拉！你想吧，心爱的！三个切尔克斯姑娘，同我一样年轻，还同是一个地方的人！她们的老爷——一位顶有钱的帕夏——还想再添一个同她们一样的。她们都欢喜。我给举荐上了。她们今天就来相看我。你一会儿就能见到她们，还能见到一个谁也从没见过的古尔贝雅扎。啊，乳白的日子！啊，一切日子的浪尖！”

===Original===

Barakah had been summoned by the ladies and a carriage sent for her. Gulbeyzah had waylaid her on the way to the reception-room.
“But what of the man﻿—the husband?” she inquired.
“Splendid! Rich and generous; impartial as the prophet in division of his favours. If God wills, I shall bear him children. What more could girl require? Think﻿—four of us, like sisters! Four pearls strung together, and inseparable! Thou wilt visit us, and we shall all four love thee dearly. O joy! Now go! I will rejoin thee presently.”

===Chinese===

女眷们召唤了巴拉卡，还派了马车去接她。古尔贝雅扎在去客厅的半路截住了她。

“可那个男人呢——那位丈夫？”她问道。

“好极了！有钱又大方；分起恩宠来，像先知一样不偏不倚。若真主愿意，我还会替他生儿育女。姑娘家还能图个什么？你想啊——我们四个，像亲姐妹一样！四颗珍珠串成一串，再也拆不散！你会来看我们的，我们四个都要真心疼你。啊，快活！去吧！我随后就来。”

===Original===

The clack of tongues was heard from the reception-room. Before the door stood rows of coloured slippers. All the dependants of the household, all the go-betweens, had rallied to support the ladies on a great occasion. Hardly had Barakah concluded greetings ere the three Circassians were announced. They were all charming, and all bore, she fancied, some resemblance to Gulbeyzah in their childlike faces and huge eyes. They had pretty, deferential manners, seeming to speak by prearrangement and to think in concert, obedient to some rule which bound them, just like nuns. They were still amid the storm of formal compliments when Gulbeyzah entered clad in soft apparel, and paused as if in awe at finding strangers. Then, blushingly, she went and kissed their hands, going on to kiss the hands of all the ladies present. In so doing she gave Barakah a little bite, and when her tour was ended sank down humbly at her feet.

===Chinese===

客厅里已是一片叽叽喳喳。门前排着一列列彩色的拖鞋。宅里所有的仆从、所有的媒人，都已聚拢来给女眷们助阵——今天是桩大日子。巴拉卡刚见罢礼，三个切尔克斯姑娘便通报进来。她们个个动人，而且在她看来，那稚气的脸庞、大大的眼睛，都同古尔贝雅扎有几分相像。她们仪态姣好而恭顺，说话像预先串通好的，想事像商量定了的，仿佛同受着某条规矩的约束，同修女一般无二。她们还在那阵客套话的旋风里打转，古尔贝雅扎已一身轻软衣裳走了进来，见了生人，仿佛敬畏似地顿住脚。随即她红着脸走上前去，吻她们的手，又接着把在座各位女眷的手一一吻遍。吻到巴拉卡时，她轻轻咬了她一小口；一轮行完，便谦卑地伏倒在她脚边。

===Original===

“They will unmask thee. Thou canst never keep this up for life,” the Englishwoman whispered.
“By Allah! only look!” was the reply. “They too are acting. See now, the plump one: there is inward mirth.”

===Chinese===

“她们会揭穿你的。这一套你一辈子也装不下去，”英国女人悄声说。

“凭安拉！你只管瞧！”对方答道。“她们也在做戏呢。喏，瞧那个胖乎乎的：心里正乐着呢。”

===Original===

The visitors, impressed by her demeanour, put certain questions, which she answered to the point. It appeared that she could dance and sing; spoke Turkish, Arabic, and some Armenian. At mention of French also, they raised hands and eyes, declaring her a perfect prodigy. They then addressed her in their native dialect, when sudden smiles broke up their shy decorum. Turning to the hostesses, they asked forgiveness for employing private speech. They had but asked the dear one of her native village, and smiled to hear that it adjoined their own. They begged for leave to call again, which meant the bargain was acceptable; and then withdrew with every blessing on the house.

===Chinese===

来客们叹服于她的仪态，提了几个问题，她句句答在点上。原来她会跳舞，会唱歌；说土耳其语、阿拉伯语，还懂些亚美尼亚语。再听说她还会法语，她们举手抬眼，齐称她是个十足的奇才。随后她们用家乡土话同她交谈，腼腆的矜持顿时绽作满面笑容。她们转向主人家告罪，说不该当众讲私房话。她们不过问了几句亲爱的姑娘家乡的村子，听说同她们自己的村子紧挨着，都笑了。她们告请改日再来拜访——这意味着这桩买卖谈妥了；随后把满口的祝福留给这户人家，告退而去。

===Original===

No sooner had they vanished than Gulbeyzah threw off her demureness and performed with energy a naughty dance which terminated in a sudden swoop to clasp Murjânah Khânum’s feet. Her mistress bent and kissed her forehead tenderly; the lady Fitnah was convulsed with glee; the humbler women gave forth wedding-cries. And the cause of all this joy, the object of that motherly consideration, was a slave! In Europe, people thought of slaves as miserable. Here was a story to be told to Julia Long.
“O disappointment! Thou wilt be in Paris! Thou wilt miss my wedding!” cried Gulbeyzah suddenly. “Yûsuf Bey should take some low girl with him since he needs must go. It is sinful to expose thy worth to risks of travel.”
“Have I not told him?” cried the lady Fitnah. “The world will be quite black when she is gone. A girl for whom his father paid three thousand pounds. It is absurd to fling her into boats and filthy trains.”
Barakah smiled at their desire to keep her, thinking with rapture of the coming talks with Julia. She had not then had Julia’s answer to her letter. It arrived within a fortnight of the time of starting.

===Chinese===

她们前脚刚没影，古尔贝雅扎便甩脱了那副端凝，起劲地跳了一支顽皮的舞，末了猛一个俯冲，抱住穆尔贾娜·哈努姆的双脚。她的主母俯下身，温柔地吻她的额头；菲特娜夫人乐得浑身乱颤；身份低微些的女人们放出了婚礼的欢呼。而这一切欢腾的因由、那份慈母般体恤的对象，竟是个奴隶！在欧洲，人们一提起奴隶，总以为是苦命的。这里头有个故事，正好讲给茱莉亚·朗听。

“啊，扫兴！你要上巴黎去了！你要错过我的婚礼了！”古尔贝雅扎忽然喊道。“优素福·贝伊既然非去不可，就该带个低贱的丫头同去。让你这样的身价去冒旅途的风险，简直是罪过。”

“这话我没同他说过吗？”菲特娜夫人喊道。“她一走，世界就要黑成一片。他父亲为她花了三千镑的姑娘啊。把她扔上船、塞进污糟糟的火车，简直是胡闹。”

巴拉卡对她们挽留的心意报以微笑，满心陶然地想着即将与茱莉亚的重聚长谈。那时她还没收到茱莉亚的回信。信在离动身不到半个月的当口到了。

===Original===
