> “… How can you write such wickedness?﻿ ﻿… I heard that you had married a Turk, but thought of course he was converted.﻿ ﻿… I do not envy you your riches nor your rank at such a price!﻿ ﻿… No, I will not join you in Paris, and abet you in your infamy. I banish your most impious suggestions from my thoughts forever.﻿ ﻿… I am poor and shall remain so; but I have incalculable treasure.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

> “………听说你嫁了个土耳其人，我原想着他自然早已皈依基督。……这样的代价换来的富贵与身份，我一概不羡！……不，我不去巴黎与你会合，不去助你成那丑行。我要把你那些大不敬的念头，永远逐出我的心。……我贫穷，也将一直贫穷下去；可我怀有无从计量的珍宝。…………”

===Original===

She crumpled up the closely written sheets, then flung them on the ground and stamped upon them. Yûsuf found her weeping uncontrollably, and asked the cause.
“Then their women are fanatical like ours!” he sighed when told. “Take heart, O fountain of my life! By Allah, such a friend is not worth weeping. We will none the less enjoy ourselves in Paris.”
“I have no wish to go at all,” sobbed Barakah.

===Chinese===

她把那几页写满密字的信纸揉成一团，摔在地上，还踩了几脚。优素福见她哭得不能自已，问她缘故。

“这么说，他们那边的女人也同我们的一样狂热！”他听罢叹道。“打起精神来，啊，我生命的泉源！凭安拉起誓，这样的朋友不值得哭。我们在巴黎照样尽兴。”

“我压根儿就不想去，”巴拉卡抽泣道。

## XVI / 十六

===Original===

Ghandûr attended Yûsuf in the train to Alexandria, and accompanied the pair on board the steamer. Kissing hands at parting he wept uncontrollably, and in that condition was propelled by sailors to the boat awaiting him. Barakah would have liked to stand and watch the harbour, which offered charming pictures in the evening glow; but Yûsuf drew her down into a stuffy cabin, where he left her, bidding her secure the door against intrusion. He told her she must take her meals down there, since there was no separate dining-room assigned to women. Directly afterwards his voice resounded in the corridor, with others talking Arabic, by which she knew that he had friends on board.

A stewardess knocked at her door, bringing her supper, which consisted of a single dish of meat and vegetables. By then the pulse of engines could be felt; there was a noise of running overhead, shouts, and the clank of chains; the ship was moving. Having made an end of eating, she retired to bed and, being tired, went to sleep immediately. The slamming of the door by Yûsuf partly roused her. She could hear him swearing, asking Allah to be put on shore, and knew that he was seasick; but it seemed no matter. Next morning, as the sea was rather rough, she kept her bunk until eleven o’clock, when she got up and put on English clothes she had brought with her. Yûsuf, more dead than living, asked what for.

===Chinese===

甘杜尔一路陪优素福坐火车到亚历山大，又随他们夫妇一同登上轮船。临别吻手时，他哭得不能自已，就这样一把眼泪一把鼻涕地被水手们搀上了等他回岸的小艇。巴拉卡本想站着看一看港口——暮色霞光里，那边是一幅幅迷人的图画；可优素福把她拉进一间闷热的舱房，撂下她走了，嘱咐她锁好门，谨防有人闯入。他告诉她，一日三餐也得就在这舱里用，因为船上没有另拨给女人的餐室。紧接着，走廊里便响起了他的嗓音，还夹着旁人说阿拉伯话的声音，她由此知道，船上有他的朋友。

一个女侍敲门，给她端来晚饭——不过一盘荤素混作的菜。那时机器的搏动已经感觉得到了；头顶上是奔跑的脚步声、呼喝声、铁链的哗啦声；船开动了。她吃罢便上了床，身子乏透，即刻睡去。优素福摔门的声音把她惊醒了一半。她听得见他赌咒发誓，求安拉让他上岸，知道他是晕船了；不过这似乎无关紧要。第二天早晨，海上风浪不小，她一直赖在铺上到十一点钟才起身，换上自己带来的英国衣裳。优素福半死不活地问这是干什么。

===Original===

“I go to smell the air.”

He sobbed: “With face exposed! Behold me dead, while dogs defile my grave.”

Supposing his mind wandered﻿—for she wore the English veil which he himself had said would be sufficient after leaving Egypt﻿—she found her way on to the deck and spent an hour there, pacing up and down, enjoying the strong wind. When she returned to Yûsuf he was inarticulate. She stayed with him until the evening, when she went on deck again for a few minutes before turning in. It was five days before the gale abated.

===Chinese===

“我去透透气。”

他哭腔哭调地说：“把脸露在外头！我算是死了，一群狗正糟践着我的坟墓。”

她只当他是烧糊涂了说胡话——她蒙的正是英国面纱，出了埃及这就尽够了，本是他自己说过的——便一路摸上甲板，在那里待了一个钟头，来回踱步，享受着劲风。回到优素福身边时，他已语不成声。她陪他挨到傍晚，临睡前又上甲板待了几分钟。大风足足刮了五天才平息下来。

===Original===

At length one morning they awoke to ease of movement, and Yûsuf rose. His smile was tentative at first, but soon grew confident. “I could not tell thee for my sickness,” he informed her, “but there are common people of our faith on board. I would not have their talk asperse my wife. It mattered less while I myself made no appearance. No doubt they took thee for some Frankish woman. But now keep close in here. Wait till we get to Fransa.”

===Chinese===

终于有一天早晨，船行变得安稳，他们醒来，优素福起了身。他的笑容起初还怯生生的，很快便笃定了。“先前晕船晕得厉害，没能告诉你，”他对她说，“船上有咱们教里的平民。我不能让他们的闲话玷污我的妻子。先前我自己不露面，还无伤大雅。他们无疑把你当成个法兰克女人了。可如今你要紧守在这屋里，等到了法兰萨（Fransa，法国）再说。”

===Original===

Without waiting for her answer, he went out. But in a minute he was back again, exclaiming:

“The wife of Hâfiz Bey, my friend, lies near to death! Come thou and see what can be done for her, and God reward thee! Put on thy habbarah. My friend will guide thee.”

===Chinese===

不等她回答，他便出去了。可不到一分钟又回来了，喊道：

“我朋友哈菲兹·贝伊（Hâfiz Bey）的妻子眼看要死了！你来吧，看看有什么法子可想，真主会报答你！把你的哈巴拉穿上。我的朋友会给你引路。”

===Original===

It was the first time he had spoken of his friends to her. She followed him and was presented to a fat, good-tempered-looking youth, exceeding swarthy, clad in a European suit too tight for him, who apologized in baby French for thus “deranging” her. He opened the door of an adjacent cabin, bowed her in, and then retreated arm in arm with Yûsuf.

It was a two-berth cabin. In the lower bunk a buxom girl of eighteen years or less﻿—a perfect blonde﻿—lay with her eyes closed, making moan with every breath. The childish face was flushed, discoloured round the eyes with weeping; the hands clenched. Whatever her complaint, it was not seasickness.

===Chinese===

这是他头一回在她跟前提起自己的朋友。她跟着他走过去，被引见给一个胖乎乎、模样和善的年轻人，肤色黑得不能再黑，一身欧洲西服绷在身上小了一号，用娃娃腔的法语为这般“打扰”了她连声告罪。他推开近旁一间舱房的门，躬身请她入内，随后便同优素福手挽手退了出去。

那是一间两张铺位的舱房。下铺躺着一个丰腴的姑娘，年纪十八岁或者还不到——十足的金发美人——闭着眼睛，每喘一口气便呻吟一声。那张稚气的脸烧得通红，哭得眼圈发乌；双手攥得紧紧的。不论她害的是什么症候，总不是晕船。

===Original===

“How is thy health?” the visitor asked softly.

“O Lord! I die! I perish! O fresh air! O sun!” gasped out the sufferer. “O Allah! Was I born a fish to be thus thrown upon the sea﻿—a snake, to be imprisoned in this box?”

“Be brave! The voyage is now almost ended. In two days or three, at most, we are released. Tell me thy pains! What ails thee?”

The prostrate beauty opened great blue eyes of injured innocence and asked: “Who art thou?”

“I am the wife of Yûsuf Bey, thy husband’s friend.”

“The Englishwoman!” She sat up and clung to Barakah. “How canst thou bear it, thou, an honoured wife! Will not thy parents take account for the indignity? Oh, end my life, I pray thee; it is unendurable!”

===Chinese===

“你身子可好？”来客轻声问。

“主啊！我要死了！我不活啦！啊，新鲜空气！啊，太阳！”受苦的人喘着气喊。“安拉啊！我生来是条鱼么，竟这样被扔在海上——是条蛇么，竟这样被关在这匣子里？”

“勇敢些！航程眼看就要到头了。顶多再过两三天，我们就解脱了。把你的苦处说给我听！你到底哪儿难受？”

那躺倒的美人睁开一双湛蓝的大眼睛，满眼无辜的委屈，问道：“你是谁？”

“我是优素福·贝伊的妻子，你丈夫的朋友。”

“英国女人！”她坐起身，一把搂住巴拉卡。“你，一位尊贵的太太，怎么受得了这个！你的爹娘难道不为这份羞辱讨个说法？啊，求你了结了我的性命吧；这实在熬不下去了！”

===Original===

Slowly, by force of patience, Barakah elicited that the girl, by name Bedr-ul-Budûr, a pet slave of the mother of young Hâfiz Bey, had been presented to him for his comfort on this journey, since his bride, of high ideas, refused to travel. She had been a little frightened in the train, a new experience, but much elated till she came on board this ship and felt the sea. Then she realized that she had been beguiled, defrauded, enticed to an undignified and hideous death. Hiccuping sobs broke in upon her narrative, which ended in a storm of tears.

===Chinese===

巴拉卡耐着性子，一点一点问出了底细：这姑娘名叫白德尔-布杜尔（Bedr-ul-Budûr），是年轻的哈菲兹·贝伊母亲的宠奴，因为这趟行程，被赏给贝伊作伴解闷——他的新娘心气高，不肯出门旅行。坐火车时她有点害怕——那是破天荒的新鲜事——可一路上兴高采烈，直到上了这条船、尝到海上的滋味才算完了。这时她才明白，自己是被哄骗了、被坑害了，是被诱来受一场不体面又难看的死罪。打嗝似的呜咽时时打断她的叙说，末了化作一场泪水的风暴。

===Original===

Barakah tried to soothe her mind with cheerful talk, depicting all the charms of life in Paris.

“Thy voice is sweetness!” she entreated. “Stay with me! Turn out my consort: let him house with thine. What does one want with men when one is dying?”

===Chinese===

巴拉卡用好话宽解她，把巴黎生活的种种美妙描画给她听。

“你的声音多么甜！”她恳求道。“陪着我吧！把我那口子撵出去，叫他同你那口子住去。人都要死了，还要男人做什么？”

===Original===

Going out on that injunction, Barakah found Hâfiz and her husband waiting close at hand. The former, greatly scared by his companion’s illness, was prepared for any sacrifice to save her life; and Yûsuf raising no objection, Barakah’s effects were moved into the other cabin, while Hâfiz took his baggage to the “house of Yûsuf,” as he called it, jesting.

Bedr-ul-Budûr gave praise to Allah. The presence of a lady of acknowledged standing relieved her of the sense of singular and base ill-treatment, which was all her illness.

At length the ship stood still and filled with voices. It was night. The men called from the corridor to warn them that the landing would take place at the third hour next morning. Thus bidden, they took out their Frankish garments and compared them.

Barakah’s were old, of sober hue. Bedr-ul-Budûr’s brand-new and something garish. They slept but little, talking through the night.

===Chinese===

领了这道懿旨出来，巴拉卡见哈菲兹和自己丈夫正守候在近旁。前者叫同伴的病吓得不轻，为救她性命，什么牺牲都肯应承；优素福既不反对，巴拉卡的箱笼行李便搬进了那间舱房，哈菲兹则拎着自己的行李，搬进了“优素福公馆”——他是这么打趣叫的。

白德尔-布杜尔赞美安拉。有了一位众所公认有身份的贵妇在场，她那种独独自己受了下作作践的感觉便消散了——而那原是她全部的病。

船终于停住不动，四下里响起了人声。正是夜里。男人们隔着走廊喊话，通知他们明晨三点钟上岸。得了这话，他们取出各自的法兰克衣裳，彼此比较。

巴拉卡的是旧的，颜色素净；白德尔-布杜尔的却是崭新，还有几分扎眼。他们睡得很少，谈了一整夜。

===Original===

When Barakah had finished dressing in the early morning, her companion, waking, screamed with horror at the English veil.

“Merciful Allah! It is dreadful. It hides nothing. It is what the wantons wear. Wait but a minute! I have more than one. I will provide thee. My kind princess advised me what was right to wear.”

Tumbling out of her berth, Bedr-ul-Budûr found in her box a fold of thick white gauze, which she proceeded to throw round the face of Barakah, attaching it to the bonnet with two little brooches.

“By Allah, that is better!” she remarked, and then gave all her mind to her own dressing.

===Chinese===

清晨巴拉卡穿戴停当，她的同伴一觉醒来，见了那英国面纱，吓得尖叫起来。

“慈悲的安拉！可怕死了。它什么都遮不住。那是浪荡女人才戴的东西。等一等！我不止有一幅。我来给你配备。我好心的公主娘娘指点过我，什么才是正经该穿戴的。”

白德尔-布杜尔翻身滚出铺位，从箱子里找出一幅厚厚的白纱，便往巴拉卡的脸上围裹，用两枚小小的胸针别在软帽上。

“凭安拉，这样才好！”她说，随即把全副心思都放到自己的梳妆上。

===Original===

When this was finished, her appearance smote the eye. Her bonnet was sky-blue, the thick white veil depending from it like a curtain, her dress a lively pink, her stockings white, her boots and gloves bright yellow, shining in their newness; she had a pale blue parasol adorned with frills of lace.

“The Franks wear many colours,” she remarked to Barakah, adding with childish wonder, “Why are yours so dull?﻿ ﻿… By Allah, I feel naked in the middle.”

So did Barakah. To one accustomed to go shrouded, a dress which emphasized the hips and bust seemed vile at first.

Yûsuf and Hâfiz fetched them up on deck, where they found two more ladies garishly arrayed, and two more men in French-made suits and fezes.

===Chinese===

梳妆完毕，她这副模样直刺人眼：软帽是天蓝的，厚厚的白面纱从帽上垂下来，像一道帷幕；裙子是鲜嫩的粉色，长袜雪白，靴子和手套是明晃晃的黄色，崭新发亮；还有一柄浅蓝的阳伞，缀着花边的褶饰。

“法兰克人穿的颜色真多，”她对巴拉卡说，又带着孩子气的惊奇补上一句，“你的怎么这样素？……凭安拉，我觉得身子中间像是光着的。”

巴拉卡也有同感。对一个出门惯于全身裹罩的人，一件把胯部和胸脯都勾勒出来的衣裳，起初看着简直不堪入目。

优素福和哈菲兹把她们接上甲板，只见那里已有另外两位衣着艳丽的女眷，另外两位穿法国式西服、戴塔布什帽的男人。

===Original===

After the introduction all stood awkwardly, gaping like children who have lost remembrance of their part. Barakah, to ease the strain, remarked to Hâfiz Bey upon the beauty of the morning, the bustle of the harbour of Marseilles; but his response was marred by evident embarrassment; his eyes kept veering round to look at Yûsuf, whom he soon rejoined. The ladies formed a group apart, in titters at each other’s odd appearance. Presently a man, clad as a Frank, approached with Arab greetings. He kissed the hand of Hâfiz Bey, who welcomed him. It seemed he had been warned by letter to prepare the way for them.

“All is ready, lords of bounty!” he exclaimed. “Deign but to follow me, the ladies with you.”

===Chinese===

见礼过后，众人都僵在那里，张着嘴，像一群忘了词的戏班孩子。巴拉卡为缓和这股僵劲儿，向哈菲兹·贝伊称道早晨的美景、马赛港的繁忙；可他的应答叫明显的窘迫搅得七零八落；他的一双眼睛直往优素福那边溜，不多时便归队去了。女眷们自成一群，瞅着彼此的古怪装束吃吃地笑。不大会儿，一个法兰克人打扮的汉子走上前来，道了阿拉伯式的问安。他吻了哈菲兹·贝伊的手，贝伊向他表示欢迎。看来早有信来知会过，教他为众人预先开道。

“一切都齐备了，恩主老爷们！”他喊道。“只请屈尊随我来，夫人们一并。”

===Original===

The drive along the quays through noisy streets to the hotel, the breakfast which their guide assured them had been cooked and chosen in accordance with religious law, were trammelled by constraint, and went off sadly. Only in the train, where they were separated, each sex enjoying a reserved compartment, did conversation flow. Among the women it was soon uproarious. They talked and laughed half through the night, appealing constantly to Barakah, a European born, for information. The appearance of the men at every station, to ascertain that they were well, produced a hush; but no sooner were the despots gone again than the mad talk and laughter raged anew.

At length they tired and tried to rest. They cursed the narrowness of the divans, the work of devils. When morning came, Bedr-ul-Budûr was at the point of death once more, asking her Maker what she had done to earn such disrespectful treatment; while Barakah, beside the window, looking out at Christian villages, was haunted by remembrance and grew sad.

===Chinese===

沿着码头、穿过喧闹街市驶往旅馆的一路，还有那顿向导拍胸脯担保从烹到选都合乎教法的早餐，全叫拘谨绊住了手脚，草草收场，悻悻无欢。只有在火车上，男女分开、各据一节包厢，话头才畅通起来。女眷这边不多时便闹翻了天。她们连说带笑闹了半个通宵，不时向巴拉卡讨教——她是生来的欧洲人，无所不晓。每到一站，男人们露面查问她们安好，车厢里便顿时鸦雀无声；可这班主子前脚一走，疯话和笑声便又卷土重来。

末了她们乏了，想要歇息。她们咒骂那窄得要命的软榻，骂它是恶魔的造物。到了早晨，白德尔-布杜尔又一度奄奄一息，问她的造物主，她造了什么孽，要受这般不知轻重的作践；巴拉卡则倚在窗边，望着窗外一座座基督教的村庄，被旧忆纠缠，心里生出悲凉。

===Original===

The sun had long been up when they reached Paris. Yûsuf and Hâfiz, Bedr-ul-Budûr and Barakah, packed in one cab, were driven with a rattle through tumultuous streets to the hotel where rooms had been engaged for them. The hostess, a stout woman elegantly dressed in black, and the entire staff stood out to welcome them. The woman bowed incessantly, addressing Yûsuf and his friend as “Monseigneur.” Finding that Barakah knew French she drew to her and poured a smooth flow of amenities into her ear.

“Madame has only to command﻿—all that she desires. The nobility of the Orient are our most valued clients. Should madame require conversation, I am always at her service. The princes come to Paris for diversion, that is understood. Young men so rich! They must amuse themselves! But then their ladies must not find the life too sad.”

===Chinese===

到巴黎时，太阳早已升起多时。优素福和哈菲兹、白德尔-布杜尔和巴拉卡，塞在一辆马车里，一路哗啦啦驶过喧嚣的街市，来到替他们订好房间的旅馆。老板娘是位黑绸衣裳、装束入时的胖妇人，率着全班人马站在门前迎接。那妇人鞠躬不迭，一口一个“Monseigneur”（大人）地称呼优素福和他的朋友。见巴拉卡懂法语，她凑到她跟前，把一串溜滑的殷勤话灌进她耳朵。

“夫人只管吩咐——想要什么有什么。东方的贵族是我们最尊贵的主顾。夫人若要人陪谈，我随时听候差遣。王爷公子们到巴黎来散心，这是不言而喻的。多么有钱的少年郎！他们总要寻欢作乐！可他们家里的夫人，也不能把日子过得太凄凉哇。”

===Original===

Thus prattling, she conducted them upstairs and flung open a door, exclaiming: “Voilà!” Crossing the landing to another door, she flung that open also. “Voilà!” she cried again. Bedr-ul-Budûr, so tired that she could hardly drag her feet up, chose the left-hand room, which happened to be nearest. Yûsuf and Barakah proceeded to the other. Both parties ordered coffee and some light refreshment, and after breakfasting went straight to bed. They rested until evening, when the men went out to find their friends, whose lodging was close by. They returned with sundry purchases, hats, gloves, and scarves, which they declared they needed for complete disguise.

On the next morning the whole party, in two carriages, went out to smell the air and view the city. It was a cloudless day and the streets sparkled, the trees along the boulevards were like fat green posies. They were feeling happy when, in an important thoroughfare, they discovered people pointing at them, drivers shouting. Yûsuf and his seat companion Hâfiz grew uncomfortable. Cries of amazement reached them from the other carriage. Their cabman turned round with a grin and told them:

“ *‘Place aux dames,’ messieurs!*﻿—That is what they cry. These ladies are not slaves with us, *que diable!*”

===Chinese===

她就这样絮絮叨叨，领他们上了楼，推开一扇房门，喊一声：“Voilà（请看）！”又穿过楼梯平台，推开另一扇门，再喊一声：“Voilà！”白德尔-布杜尔累得两条腿几乎拖不动，挑了左手那间——恰是最近的一间。优素福和巴拉卡进了另一间。两拨人都要了咖啡和些许点心，吃罢便径直上床。他们一直歇到傍晚，男人们才出门寻访他们的朋友——朋友的落脚处就在近旁。他们带着零七八碎的采买回来了：帽子、手套、围巾，声称要把行头装扮周全，这些东西缺不得。

第二天早晨，全队人马分乘两辆马车，出去透透气，看看市容。那天万里无云，街道熠熠生辉，林荫道两旁的树木像一束束肥硕的绿色捧花。他们正自欢喜，忽然在一条通衢大道上，发觉路人对他们指指点点，赶车的连声呼喝。优素福和同座的哈菲兹渐渐坐不安稳。另一辆马车上也传来了阵阵惊呼。他们的车夫咧着嘴回过头来，告诉他们：

“*‘Place aux dames’（请给女士们让路），先生们！*——他们嚷的就是这个。我们这儿的太太们可不是奴隶，*que diable*（见鬼）！”

===Original===

The two men had been lounging in the roomy seat which faced the horse. They at once resigned it, addressing bows and smiles of deference to the angry multitude; and called out to their friends to do the like. But the incident destroyed their pleasure in the drive; nor were the ladies happy in the seat of honour, a gazing-stock for infidels who might possess the evil eye.

“Saw one ever such fanaticism?” groaned Yûsuf. “And they call this country free﻿—a place where everyone does what he likes!”

===Chinese===

两位先生方才正瘫坐在面朝马儿的那张宽敞座席上。他们当即让了出来，朝着恼怒的人群连连鞠躬赔笑；还大声招呼他们的朋友照样办理。但这场风波败尽了兜风的兴致；女眷们坐在尊座上也不自在——成了异教徒围观的活靶，而异教徒里保不齐有长着邪眼的。

“谁见过这样的狂热迷信？”优素福呻吟道。“他们还管这个国家叫自由——一个人人随心所欲的地方！”

===Original===

That afternoon was spent in the hotel in a strange manner; Barakah, at the demand of Yûsuf, instructing the four men in foreign customs. They posed and pirouetted in her salon, rehearsing bows, the flourish of a hat, the proper compliments; while the three girls looked on with saucer eyes. After dinner they again appeared before her, this time without their fezes, wearing hats which gave to them a very villainous and sleek appearance. Required to criticize their dress and bearing from a Frankish standpoint, she suggested some improvements which were hailed with gratitude. Yûsuf returned home after midnight, tired but garrulous. It seemed that they had lighted on a charming Frenchman, who undertook to show them all the sights. Next day the men rose late and then went out together, leaving the women to their own devices; returned to dinner, then went off again, remaining out this time till nearly morning.

===Chinese===

那天下午是在旅馆里以古怪的方式打发的：应优素福的要求，巴拉卡给四位先生传授外邦的规矩。他们在她的客厅里摆姿势、转圈儿，操练鞠躬、挥帽、合乎体统的客套话；三位姑娘则瞪着茶碟般的大眼睛在旁观看。晚饭后他们又到她面前亮相，这一回摘了塔布什帽，改戴礼帽，看上去一个个油头粉面、透着几分匪气。她受命站在法兰克人的立场上评点他们的衣着举止，提了几条改进意见，众人感激不尽。优素福后半夜才回旅馆，乏透了，话匣子却关不上。原来他们碰上了一位讨人喜欢的法国人，自告奋勇领他们游遍各处名胜。第二天，先生们起得晚，随后一齐出门，把女人们撂下任其自便；晚饭时回来，吃罢又走了，这一趟直玩到将近天亮。

===Original===

The programme did not vary on succeeding days. The girls, deserted, clung to Barakah. They wailed and prayed to God, and dreamed of Cairo. At length one of them﻿—it was Bedr-ul-Budûr﻿—took courage to reproach her lord; when all four men were stricken with amazement. They had thought the ladies would be gay indoors without them, as they were at home. To cheer them up, a trip to Versailles was arranged. It passed off gaily, with less shyness than usually appeared when they all mixed together. As they strolled about the park, a youth named Izz-ud-dîn made up to Barakah, and with the greatest diffidence implored her to confide to him the secret how to win the love of Frankish ladies. When she smilingly assured him there was none, he cried:

===Chinese===

往后几天，日程一成不变。姑娘们被撇得无依，都黏着巴拉卡。她们哭哭啼啼，向真主祷告，梦里全是开罗。末了她们中的一个——正是白德尔-布杜尔——鼓起勇气，埋怨起自己的老爷；四位先生顿时惊得呆若木鸡。他们原以为，没有他们在跟前，太太们在屋里照样欢欢喜喜，就像在开罗家中那样。为哄她们开心，安排了一趟凡尔赛之游。这一趟玩得畅快，众人混作一处时，也比往常少了几分羞怯。大家在园中漫步时，一个名叫伊兹丁（Izz-ud-dîn）的青年蹭到巴拉卡身边，万般腼腆地恳求她吐露一个秘诀：如何赢得法兰克女人的爱情。她微笑着担保并无此秘诀，他却喊道：

===Original===

“O Lord of Heaven! Then thou wilt not tell it. They are so easy to their own men, as we know from books; to us so difficult. It cannot be fanaticism, since we seem as Franks.”

“But what need hast thou of women, with a pearl of beauty here beside thee?” questioned Barakah.

“One who has beheld thy loveliness must evermore desire the like of it! Oh, that thou hadst a sister for me!” he made answer glibly.

===Chinese===

“上天的主啊！那便是你不肯说了。她们对自己国家的男人多么容易，书里都是这么写的；对我们却这样难。总不会是宗教狂热，我们瞧着明明同法兰克人一样。”

“可你身边明明有一位美的珍珠，还要女人做什么？”巴拉卡问道。

“见识过你这般美貌的人，从此必定时时刻刻想着同样的美人！唉，你若有个妹妹给我就好了！”他伶伶俐俐地接口答道。

===Original===

He moved away, but presently another came and made the same preposterous request, retreating with the same forced compliment; and on the journey home, when Yûsuf closed his eyes and seemed to sleep, Hâfiz Bey, whom she had thought more sensible, approached her in his turn. When she denied all knowledge of the matter he answered in low tones:

“There is a secret, that is known, by Allah. Thou hast it, and hast given hints to Yûsuf; else why should he be more successful than the rest of us?”

“Because he is better looking,” it was on the tip of her tongue to say, as she surveyed the fat, good-tempered face of Hâfiz with its Chinese eyes. It was all that she could do to keep from screams of laughter.

“It is my dream,” he whispered. “By Allah it disturbs my nights with cruel pain﻿—to take a lady just like thee in all respects﻿—a Frank and noble, of extreme refinement﻿—back with me to Masr.”

She derided him. He still continued pleading, supporting his petition with the grossest flattery, till they reached home, when Yûsuf suddenly sprang up and glowered at Hâfiz. He had been feigning sleep. It was a thunderbolt. Bedr-ul-Budûr screamed warning to her lord, who gave but a single look and fled indoors, the jealous one pursuing like a madman. In the hall the harmless youth was overtaken and turned round to plead. With a howl of “Dog!” Yûsuf sprang at his throat and bore him to the ground. Like dogs in very truth they fought till parted by the hotel servants with the help of broomsticks; while Barakah strove in vain to make her explanation heard; Bedr-ul-Budûr appealed to Allah and the prophet; and the landlady from the third step of the stairs, with hands and eyes thrown up, exclaimed repeatedly: “*O ciel! C’est monseigneur!*”

===Chinese===

他走开了，可不多时又来一个，提出同样荒唐的请求，又拿同样勉强的恭维话垫着退了下去；归途上，优素福阖眼似睡之际，她原以为更明白事理的哈菲兹·贝伊也凑了过来。她说自己对此一无所知，他低声答道：

“有个秘诀，这是明摆着的，凭安拉。你有，还点拨过优素福；不然他凭什么比我们谁都顺遂？”

“因为他生得俊，”这话已涌到她舌尖，一面端详着哈菲兹那张胖乎乎、和和气气的脸，还有那双中国式的细眯眼。她拼命忍着，才没有放声大笑出来。

“这是我的梦，”他悄声道。“凭安拉，它搅得我夜夜痛楚难当——娶一位处处同你一样的贵妇——法兰克人，出身高贵，极有教养——带回埃及（Masr）去。”

她把他挖苦了一顿。他还只管央告，拿最露骨的谀词给他的请求作保，一直闹到抵家，优素福猛然跳将起来，恶狠狠瞪住哈菲兹。他方才一直在装睡。这真是晴天霹雳。白德尔-布杜尔尖叫着向她的老爷报警，老爷只望了一眼便逃进屋去，那妒火中烧的人疯子一般追赶。门厅里，这无害的青年被追上，回过身来还想求告。优素福怒吼一声“狗东西！”，扑上去扼住他的咽喉，把他掀翻在地。两人当真像狗一样厮打，直到旅馆仆役抡着扫帚把他们拆开；巴拉卡竭力想把事情解释清楚，却是徒劳；白德尔-布杜尔向安拉和先知呼救；老板娘站在楼梯第三级上，双手一扬、两眼上翻，连声喊道：“*O ciel! C'est monseigneur!*（天哪！是大人！）。”

## XVII / 十七

===Original===

Half an hour later Yûsuf and Hâfiz were in each other’s arms, sighing gustily and rocking to and fro in the ecstasy of reconciliation. Barakah had explained things to her husband in the interim, taking him to task severely for his savage conduct. To be thought uncivilized had always been his dread, and just then, with red eyes and all dishevelled, a-quiver from the fray, he stood convicted. With repentant tears he ran to ask forgiveness of his late antagonist.

It was decided that they twain, with their respective consorts, should spend the evening quietly in Yûsuf’s room; in pursuance of which resolution they had supped together, and Bedr-ul-Budûr, who owned a lute, was going to sing, when a card was brought to Hâfiz by the chambermaid. He frowned and clenched his teeth as he examined it.

“It is the Prince, my uncle!” he exclaimed. “He has been told our whereabouts; it must be by my father, since we have been careful not to call on any of the Turks in Paris. O Calamity! My uncle is correct and cold, a madman who condemns all pleasure.”

With haste he sent his concubine into her own apartment, while Yûsuf hustled Barakah into the dressing-room and locked the door. No would-be Franks received the exiled Prince, but a pair of ceremonious Orientals, with fezes carried at the most respectful angle, who strove with one another to be first to kiss his hand.

===Chinese===

半个钟头之后，优素福和哈菲兹已经搂作一团，长吁短叹，前俯后仰地摇晃着，沉浸在言归于好的狂喜里。这中间巴拉卡已向丈夫把事情讲明，狠狠数落了他那番野蛮行径。被人当作未开化的蛮子，从来是他最怕的事，而方才他两眼通红、衣冠不整，一场恶斗之后兀自浑身发颤，正是罪证昭然。他流着悔恨的眼泪，跑向先前的对头讨饶去了。

两个男人议定，当夜各携女眷，在优素福房里安安静静消磨一晚；遵着这个决议，他们已共进晚餐，白德尔-布杜尔——她有一把琵琶——正要开口唱，旅馆女侍给哈菲兹送进来一张名片。他端详着名片，眉头拧紧，牙关咬得死死的。

“是亲王，我的叔父！”他失声道。“有人把我们的落脚处告诉他了；准是我父亲说的，因为我们小心着，巴黎的土耳其人一家也没拜会过。啊，大祸临头！我这位叔父一丝不苟、冷若冰霜，是个把一切享乐都判了罪的疯子。”

他急忙把姬妾打发回她自己的房间，优素福则把巴拉卡推进梳妆室，锁上了门。前来接待这位流亡亲王的，不是两个冒牌法兰克人，而是一对礼数周到的东方人，塔布什帽端到最恭敬的角度，两人争先恐后，抢着第一个吻他的手。

===Original===

The Prince was a tremendous talker. A scion of the ruling house of Egypt, enduring banishment for his political opinions, he began upon the state of that unhappy country for which he saw no hope save in a European form of government. He wished the young men to attend the meetings of his club, “the Friends of Progress,” at a café on the Boulevard des Italiens; and the young men swore to do so on the first evening they could spare from the study of French thought and institutions which at present took up every minute of their time.

From national affairs the Prince passed on to household matters, advocating education for all women and promotion to an equal rank with men. At this his nephew cried:

“We think as you do, having each a lady whom we treat precisely in the Frankish manner. Yûsuf here present has espoused a noble Englishwoman, who instructs us. Introduce her, Yûsuf, since my uncle shares our views.”

Barakah expected her release, which she had long desired, for the Prince’s voice was wonderfully sweet and winning, and she burned with curiosity to see his face. But the talk sheered off from her. The Prince, resenting the intrusion of a concrete instance on ideas, rebuked the young men sternly, causing both to cringe.

===Chinese===

这位亲王是个了不得的话痨。他是埃及统治王室的后裔，因政见而遭流放，一开口便谈那个不幸国家的时局——在他看来，除非实行欧式的政体，别无指望。他希望两个年轻人去参加他那个俱乐部的聚会——“进步之友”，设在意大利人大道上的一家咖啡馆里；两个年轻人便赌咒发誓，说等眼下这阵子腾出空来，头一个晚上就去——研习法国的思想与制度，如今占去了他们每一分钟的光阴。

从国家大事，亲王又转到家务上来，主张让一切女子受教育，并升到与男人平等的地位。听到这里，他的侄儿嚷道：

“我们正是这么想的：我们各有一位女眷，待她的做派地地道道是法兰克式的。眼前这位优素福就娶了一位高贵的英国女人，由她来指点我们。把她请出来吧，优素福，既然叔父赞成我们的见识。”

巴拉卡巴望着获释——这是她盼了许久的——因为亲王的嗓音出奇地甜润动人，她好奇得心焦，想看看他的长相。可话头偏偏从她身上擦了过去。亲王恼恨一个活生生的实例闯进他的理论，把两个年轻人严词训斥了一顿，训得两人瑟瑟缩缩。

===Original===

“You mistake my meaning,” he informed them. “God forbid that I should wish our ladies to resemble closely those of Europe. If you desire that, you are very foolish. The harem life, or something like it, is the best for women. It only needs reform and elevation. It is a system founded on the laws of God expressed in nature, whereas the European way of treating women has no sanction. The latter seems entirely meretricious when one sees how ladies here make sport of marriage and shun motherhood﻿—how children flout and override their parents. If the understanding of our women were improved, their status raised, I think our way would be acknowledged better by impartial judges. No, all that I would borrow from the Franks would be a weapon. They excel us in mechanical contrivances, in practical education, and in method. These gifts I covet, for with equal weapons we should be their masters; our Faith exceeding any motive power which they possess.”

===Chinese===

“你们误会了我的意思，”他告诉他们。“真主不容，我岂会盼着咱们的女眷处处学欧洲女人的样。你们若存这份想头，就蠢得很了。深闺内宅的生活，或者与此相仿的生活，才是对女人最好的。它需要的无非是改良与提升。这套制度，奠基在显于自然之中的真主之法上；欧洲人对待女人的方式，却没有任何根据。看看此地的太太们怎样拿婚姻作耍、怎样躲避生养——看看儿女们怎样顶撞、踩踏父母——便知道后者全然是伤风败俗。倘若咱们女人的见识得到开启、地位得到抬升，我想公正的评判者自会承认，咱们这套更好。不，我肯向法兰克人借的只有一样：武器。他们在机械机巧上、在实用的教育上、在方法上胜过我们。这些长处我眼馋，因为武器既然相当，我们就该做他们的主人；而咱们的信仰，胜过他们拥有的任何原动力。”

===Original===

He went on talking in this strain till nearly midnight, when he left abruptly. Barakah was then let out of her dark prison. Alone with Yûsuf, she inquired his real opinion of the Prince’s views, which seemed to her inspiring.

“Like pitch! Like dung!” he answered in the vulgar speech of Egypt. “The production of ideas is an amusing pastime. It is strange, the things a man can think of if he applies his mind to it. And when a Prince is speaking one admires, of course; though this one is a madman who has lost a fine position and will lose his life merely for love of argument. What we are and do belongs to Allah. No thinking or wild talk affects it, praise to Him!”

He seemed glad to change the subject. Putting his arm round Barakah, he begged her in seductive tones to confide to him the secret about Frankish women.

===Chinese===

他照着这个调门一直谈到将近午夜，随即扬长而去。巴拉卡这才从那间黑黢黢的牢房里放出来。单独同优素福在一起时，她问他，对亲王的见解有何真切的看法——那些见解在她听来颇能鼓舞人心。

“沥青一般！粪土一般！”他用埃及的俚俗土话答道。“炮制思想是桩好玩的消遣。也真怪，人只要肯用心思，什么念头想不出来。亲王讲话的时候，人当然要表示钦佩；只是这一位是个疯子，好端端的地位已经丢了，往后还要单为爱抬杠把性命也丢了。咱们是什么人、干什么事，都归于安拉。任凭什么思想、什么疯话都撼动不了它，感赞真主！”

他像是巴不得撇开这个话头。他伸臂搂住巴拉卡，语调缠绵，央告她把法兰克女人的那个秘密吐露给他。

===Original===

“It is not for myself I ask,” he whispered fondly; “but Hâfiz, Izz-ud-dîn, and Saïd die to know. Where are these balls at which distinguished women fling aside all shame? We have been to dances, but the women there are base and ribald, showing none of that refinement in depravity which charms the mind in writings of this country.”

In vain did she assure him that good Frankish women were every whit as moral as good Orientals.

“We have their books for testimony,” was his answer. And again he told her: “It is for my friends I plead. I myself, as is well known, desire thee only.”

===Chinese===

“不是为我自己求你，”他柔情蜜意地悄声说；“是哈菲兹、伊兹丁和赛义德（Saïd），他们想晓得想得要死。那些体面女人把廉耻抛到九霄云外的舞会，到底在哪儿？舞我们倒是跳过，可那儿的女人粗鄙淫猥，这个国家的书里写的那种堕落之中的雅致——教人读了心荡神驰的那种——她们身上一丝也没有。”

她向他担保，规矩的法兰克女人同规矩的东方女人一般贞静，却是白费唇舌。

“她们的书就是凭证。”他答道。接着又告诉她：“我是替朋友们求告。至于我自己，人所共知，心里只有你一个。”

===Original===

The women were left more and more alone, the private explorations of their lords bereaving them by day as well as night. Barakah did her best to entertain them. Together with the landlady she planned excursions, and took them to the ateliers where modes are created. But the sense of desolation dogged them everywhere. Scenes which they might have viewed with pleasure had their lords been faithful, encounters which might then have given them a thrill of mischief, appeared heartrending in their luckless state. The very gaiety of the Parisian streets seemed gruesome. If a man in passing touched them they were seized with trembling, and once or twice came very near to fainting from pure shame; and their terror was intense at passing unknown doorways, though the landlady assured them there was not the slightest danger.

Their haunting fear was lest male unbelievers should abduct them; still more, perhaps, lest they should come to wish for such a fate﻿—the most appalling that could be imagined for a Muslim woman. Bedr-ul-Budûr declared she knew a girl who, married to an infidel, brought forth black beetles﻿—“not one, but thousands! millions!”﻿—she related graphically﻿—which at length devoured her. Such stories were received with acclamation, as justifying the extreme abhorrence which they felt for Frenchmen. And Barakah, though she tried to reason with them, shared their feelings in some measure, dismayed by the vulgarity of Western life. When, added to all this, it rained for five days in succession, her friends resigned their cause to God and ceased to worry, while she herself grew thoroughly despondent.

The girls shrugged shoulders at the sinful folly of their owners, now too far gone in dissipation to endure reproaches.

===Chinese===

女人们越来越被撂下独守，老爷们私自出去猎奇探胜，白天黑夜都不见人影。巴拉卡想尽法子给她们解闷。她同老板娘一道筹划出游，带她们去看创制时新款式的工作室。可那份凄凉之感如影随形，到哪儿都甩不脱。倘若老爷们忠心，那些景致原可看得满心欢喜，那些邂逅原可带着淘气的刺激，可在她们这般背运的境地里，只显得撕心裂肺。连巴黎街头的花天锦地，看着都森然可怕。有男人走过时蹭着了她们，她们便浑身战栗，有一两回险些纯粹出于羞耻晕死过去；打那些认不得的门户前经过，她们的惊恐更是逼人，尽管老板娘一力担保，说绝无半点危险。

她们心里盘桓不去的恐惧，是怕异教徒的男人把她们掳走；或许更怕的是，怕自己到头来竟盼着这样的下场——对一个穆斯林女人来说，那是想得到的顶顶骇人的命运了。白德尔-布杜尔声称，她认得一个姑娘，嫁了个异教徒，生下满窝的黑甲虫——“不是一只，是成千上万！数不清的百万！”——她讲得绘声绘色——那些甲虫到末了把她啃吃干净。这样的故事人人拍案叫好，正好坐实了她们对法国人切齿的憎恶。巴拉卡虽然还想同她们讲讲道理，心里却也几分赞同——西方生活的粗鄙庸俗叫她丧气。再加上一连落了五天雨，朋友们索性把一切交托真主，不再操心，她自己则彻底地心灰意懒了。

姑娘们对自家主人的荒唐罪过唯有耸肩——他们如今荒淫得太深，受不得半句数落了。

===Original===

“It is a malady, a madness,” said Bedr-ul-Budûr, with resignation. “It is the air of infidelity in this accursed city. We did wrong to travel unprovided with the antidote, which must be known to sages and obtainable. It is bad enough for us, but what of Barakah﻿—a chief wife, a great lady? How can she endure it?”

Barakah did at last think fit to make a protest. One night and early morning she sat up for Yûsuf, and her reproaches met with a success which startled her. He wept aloud and flung himself upon the floor. His face was ghastly. When questioned, he confessed that he had sinned most foully, having that night consumed so much abomination that on his way home he had been struck down by God with awful sickness and had nearly died. He swore that none but devils lived in Paris, and implored her to transport him back to Egypt.

===Chinese===

“这是一种病，一种疯病，”白德尔-布杜尔听天由命地说。“是这个可诅咒的城市里异教的空气作祟。我们错在出门时没备下解药——那解药想必是有的，只是须问圣贤才知道、才寻得着。我们受着也就罢了，可巴拉卡呢——她是一位正妻，一位大夫人啊！她怎么受得了？”

巴拉卡终于觉得该抗争一下了。一夜直坐到清晨，她守着等优素福回来，这番数落的收效之大，把她自己都吓了一跳。他放声大哭，扑倒在地上。他的脸白得吓人。追问之下，他招认自己造了大孽，那晚吞下的污秽东西太多，回家路上被真主一击，害了场可怕的重病，险些死了。他赌咒说巴黎城里住的全是魔鬼，哀求她把他带回埃及去。

===Original===

A picture seen the previous morning in a shop upon the boulevard had roused in Barakah the wish to visit Switzerland. She longed to walk by forest streams, beneath great mountains, in solitude, with keen, cool breezes to restore her spirits.

“Paris is not the whole of Europe,” she informed him gently. “There are scenes of famous beauty which we ought to visit. Take me to Switzerland!”

“At once!” he cried. “This very day now dawning! By Allah, I would go to Gebel Qaf with thee alone to get away from Paris.”

She bade him tell his friends to treat their women better, which he swore to do; and directly after breakfast took him out, while his resolve was eager, to obtain money from the bank where he had credit, and buy tickets to Geneva, the first name occurring to her. She was glad that she had taken this precaution when, later in the day, she saw his purpose weaken. The tickets actually bought alone sustained it, for he had the Oriental’s shrewd regard for money’s worth. That night they spent in the train, both cherishing sensations of deliverance, though those of Barakah were chequered by the vision of three weeping girls, who at the moment of departure had embraced her knees and tried to hold her.

===Chinese===

前一天早晨，巴拉卡在林荫大道上一家铺子里看见一幅画，起了去瑞士的念头。她渴望在森林的溪水边、在大山的脚下独自走走，让凛冽清凉的风吹一吹，把精神提起来。

“巴黎不代表整个欧洲，”她婉婉地告诉他。“还有些以美景闻名的地方，咱们该去看看。带我去瑞士吧！”

“马上就去！”他喊道。“就在今天破晓的这一天！凭安拉，只要能逃出巴黎，就是只带你一个去卡夫山（Gebel Qaf）我也情愿。”

她嘱咐他知会朋友们，叫他们待自家的女人好些，他赌咒照办；早饭一过，趁他这股决心还热着，她便拉他出门，到那家有他信用往来的银行取了钱，买了去日内瓦的车票——日内瓦是她脱口想出的头一个地名。当天晚些时候，她见他这股决心有了松动的意思，庆幸自己防了这一手。车票当真买下了，单是这一桩就把他的决心撑住了，因为东方人对钱财的价值，自有一份精明的计较。当夜他们宿在火车上，两人都怀着得救的欢畅，只是巴拉卡的欢畅里夹着杂音：眼前总晃着三个哭哭啼啼的姑娘，临别那一刻抱住她的膝盖，死活想留住她。

===Original===

Their Alpine tour, however, was of short duration. Yûsuf was contented in Geneva, giving praise to Allah for the vast supply of drinking water. But when, at her suggestion, they moved on to Chamounix, his feeling changed. His face went green as on that night in Paris. His nostrils and his eyes distended to their utmost, reminding the observer of a frightened horse. The sight of the great mountains closing in and hanging over him oppressed his soul with terror which was not diminished by the occurrence in the hour of their arrival of a dreadful thunderstorm. When he saw the numbers of the visitors he gasped and questioned: “Come these here for pleasure? Is it possible? A place so frightful, so appalling, like Gehennum! If one came with a large company, with music and loud songs that never ceased, and kept his eyes shut all the time, it might be bearable; supposing one were forced to do it, for some crime﻿ ﻿… For pleasure, sayest thou? What pleasure can they find?”

“They walk and climb the mountains. They love Nature. And the air is excellent.”

“By Allah, wild beasts! Human beings are more sensitive. How can they love Nature who approve her in most horrid mood? It is evident that God Most High designed such scenes for a warning and a menace, to be shunned. Yet these applaud. They are utterly devoid of feeling. May Our Lord destroy them!”

A prey to panic, he no longer heard her arguments. His one desire was to rejoin his friends as soon as might be, to see once more the visage of a true believer; and two days later they were back in Paris.

===Chinese===

然而他们的阿尔卑斯之游为期甚短。在日内瓦，优素福心满意足，感赞安拉赐下这么浩大的饮用水源。可等他们依着巴拉卡的主意续程去夏蒙尼，他的心情就变了。他的脸泛出绿色，同巴黎那一夜一般。鼻孔撑到最大，眼睛瞪到极限，教看的人想起一匹受惊的马。大山四面围拢、当头悬压的景象，把恐怖压上他的心头；他们到达的那个钟头里又偏偏来了一场骇人的雷雨，恐怖只增不减。看见游客的人数，他倒抽一口冷气，问道：“这些人到这儿来是为了取乐？这可能吗？这样可怕、这样骇人的地方，活像火狱！若是带着一大伙人，带着音乐、带着吵个不停的响亮歌声，再把眼睛一路闭紧了，兴许还受得住——还得是犯了什么罪、硬被逼着来的，才说得通……你说，为了取乐？他们能寻着什么乐子？”

“他们走路，爬山。他们爱大自然。再说，这儿的空气好得很。”

“凭安拉，一群野兽！人可比这娇嫩。大自然摆出这副最狰狞的面目，他们反倒认可叫好，这等人怎么会爱它？明摆着，至高的真主造出这般景象，是拿来警世、拿来威吓，叫人躲开的。这些人却鼓掌喝彩。他们全无心肝。愿我们的主毁灭他们！”

他成了惊弓之鸟，再也听不进她的道理。他一心只想尽快回到朋友们中间，再见一见真正信士的面孔；两天以后，他们已经回到巴黎。

===Original===

Barakah’s return was hailed with rapture by the hapless girls, who had not ventured out of doors during her absence. Things, they declared, were even worse than ere she left, their men more shameless. Yûsuf had sworn beforehand to discountenance nocturnal outings, and for the first two days he kept his word; though Hâfiz and the others begged her to release him from it, protesting that their occupations were most innocent. Indeed, their childlike zest in evildoing so resembled innocence that she felt cruel when refusing, as if denying babies some small pleasure. But on the third day Yûsuf came to her, with worried frown, and said:

“Hâfiz and the rest, I fear, are going much too far. I feel responsible for them, since we are all one party. They do not tell me all their pranks. I have been thinking. It is my duty to be with them and restrain their conduct.”

“Do what thou judgest right and God preserve thee!” answered Barakah, with a point of irony which he did not perceive.

“My conscience is relieved,” he cried. “I thank thee. God knows how it has troubled me since our return.”

===Chinese===

巴拉卡归来，苦命的姑娘们欢声雷动——她不在的这些天，她们连大门都没敢出。据她们说，事情比她走前更糟了，她们的男人越发无耻。优素福事先立誓不再纵容夜间出游，头两天也果然守约；虽说哈菲兹等人一再求她开恩，放他免掉这个誓，赌咒说他们的营生再清白无辜不过。说真的，他们作起恶来那股孩子气的兴头，同天真无邪实在像得很，她回绝时竟觉得狠心，仿佛不许娃娃们讨一口甜头。可到第三天，优素福愁眉不展地来见她，说：

“我怕，哈菲兹他们闹得太过火了。咱们是一伙的，我觉得对他们有责任。他们的胡闹，并不桩桩都告诉我。我一直在想。我的本分是同他们在一起，管束他们的行止。”

“你只管照自己认为对的去做，愿真主保佑你！”巴拉卡答道，话里带着一点讥诮，他却没有察觉。

“我这颗良心放下了，”他喊道。“谢谢你。你知道，打从咱们回来，它一直折磨着我。”

===Original===

That evening he departed with his friends, leaving Barakah to hear the lamentations of the girls.

“They are all bewitched,” cried Bedr. “Hâfiz is by nature pious. Even now he names the Name of Allah when he opens any door and curses the religion of the infidels when passing by their idols in the streets and squares. Our Lord preserve his life! Each night I see him dead in some disgraceful haunt, his house dishonoured. Oh that I knew a good magician, a true believer, in this land of mangy dogs!”

Their fears, against her will, infected Barakah; during the long night-watches they became a sickness, and when day broke again they seemed confirmed. Yûsuf had not returned. She went to Bedr-ul-Budûr and found her in the same anxiety. They sat together, wondering what to do. Grey light at the window, raindrops coursing down the panes, made anguish visible.

===Chinese===

当晚他便随朋友们去了，撇下巴拉卡去听姑娘们的哀哭。

“他们全叫妖法迷住了，”白德尔喊道。“哈菲兹本性是虔诚的。就是如今，他开哪扇门都要念安拉的尊名，在街上广场上经过异教徒的偶像跟前，还要咒骂异教徒的宗教。愿我们的主保他性命！每天夜里我都看见他死在哪处不光彩的窝子里，家门蒙羞。唉，在这个遍地癞皮狗的地方，我要是认得一个真正信士的好法师就好了！”

她们的恐惧不由分说，也感染了巴拉卡；漫长的守夜里，这恐惧熬成了心病，待到天再亮，仿佛句句应了验。优素福没有回来。她到白德尔-布杜尔房里，见她也是一般焦灼。两人对坐着，不知如何是好。窗前灰白的天光，雨珠顺着玻璃一道道往下淌，把愁苦画在了眼前。

===Original===

At length, when eight o’clock had struck, there came a note for Barakah. It was in French, and from the exiled Prince, the revolutionary. It bade her have no fear; her husband would be with her in an hour, when the writer hoped, with her permission, to present his compliments in person and explain the case. The other girls had come by that time from their lodgings to get strength from Barakah. Conjecture ravened round the simple statement in the letter. At ten o’clock the Prince sent up his card; the three girls fled across the passage just in time to avoid encountering the visitor, who led into the room the errant youths. The Prince, a lean, ascetic-looking man, with boyish eyes, bowed low to Barakah.

===Chinese===

好不容易挨到八点钟敲过，巴拉卡接到一封短信。信是法文的，出自那位流亡的革命亲王之手。信上嘱咐她不必害怕；她丈夫一个钟头内即可回到她身边，届时写信人希望——若蒙她允许——当面致意，并把事情原委说明。那时另外两位姑娘也已从住处过来，想从巴拉卡身上讨些气力。众人的猜疑围着信上这简单的一句，饿鹰般扑啄不休。十点钟，亲王递上名片；三位姑娘仓皇穿过走廊，恰恰避开来客，来客把那班迷途少年领进屋来。亲王是个清瘦的、苦行者模样的人，一双少年人的眼睛，向巴拉卡深深一躬。

===Original===

“Madame,” he opened, with a flourish of his hand towards the group of reprobates, “I ask you to remember of your husband, and also beg you to remind the fair companions of my nephew and these other gentlemen, that they are young, these boys, and therefore capable of progress. It is a proof that they possess some germ of sense, which later may develop into mind, that, being terrified at last, they sent to me. I found them in a most equivocal position﻿—in fact, dear madame, at the Conciergerie. Thanks to my relations with important people in this city, I had no difficulty in procuring their release, since they were not precisely guilty, only imbecile. I am glad to have been able to assist them, for the love I bear their parents and our common Faith. But they will allow me to remark that vicious boys should travel only with a tutor, who should have a whip. It disgusts me even to conceive that any man could be so foolish as to quit the side of one so lovely and so virtuous as you, madame, to follow beastliness. Dear madame, your servant!”

===Chinese===

“夫人，”他朝那伙败家子把手一挥，开口道，“我请您体谅您的丈夫，也请您转告我侄儿和这几位先生的美丽女伴：他们都还年轻，这些孩子，因此还长进得。他们终于吓怕了，晓得打发人来找我，这就证明他们心里还有几分见识的胚芽，日后或能发育成头脑。我寻着他们的时候，他们的处境再暧昧不过了——说实在的，亲爱的夫人，在巴黎古监狱（Conciergerie）里。多亏我与此地的要人有交往，把他们保释出来毫无难处，因为他们算不得真正的罪人，只是蠢材罢了。能帮上这个忙我很高兴，这是看在我对他们父母、对我们共同信仰的情分上。不过，请恕我进一言：品行恶劣的孩子出门旅行，只该带着一位家庭教师，教师手里还该有一条鞭子。一想到世上竟有男人蠢到这般田地，撇下您这样美丽贞洁的夫人，去追逐禽兽般的行乐，我便觉得恶心，夫人。亲爱的夫人，您忠实的仆人！”

===Original===

He retired; when Yûsuf and the others pressed round Barakah, a group of penitent and frightened children. Hâfiz, the fat, knelt down before her, tears coursing down his cheeks; Saïd kissed her raiment; Yûsuf pleaded in her ear. They had done wrong, they owned, though nothing very dreadful. Some elegant ladies had admitted them to their society; they were sitting in a café communing in all refinement, when horrible low men arrived and claimed those ladies. One threw a glass at Saïd and cut his face﻿—the wound was shown﻿—on which there was a scuffle; gendarmes came and, siding with their coreligionists, conveyed the righteous Muslims straight to prison.

“Where we should have stayed forever, had not Hâfiz thought of calling in his uncle,” blubbered Izz-ud-dîn; “simply for being Muslims, they are so fanatical.”

All four were bent upon return to Egypt, since Paris had become a place of terror. The rapture of the girls was indescribable. They danced and clapped their hands, embraced each other, laughed, cried, and gave way to all kinds of folly. Bedr-ul-Budûr made vows to divers saints, and held delighted conversations with her mother long since dead.

===Chinese===

他退了出去；优素福等人立刻围拢到巴拉卡身边，一群又悔又怕的孩子。胖胖的哈菲兹跪倒在她跟前，泪珠顺着脸颊滚滚而下；赛义德吻她的衣裳；优素福在她耳边百般央告。他们承认自己做了错事，虽说倒也没什么了不得的祸端。几位风雅的太太请他们入了圈子；他们正坐在一家咖啡馆里文文雅雅地清谈，忽然来了几个下流透顶的男人，声言那些太太是他们的相好。有一个朝赛义德摔了一只杯子，划破了他的脸——伤口当场验看——于是一场混战；宪兵来到，偏袒他们的教友，把这几个正直的穆斯林径直押进了监狱。

“要不是哈菲兹想起搬他叔父来，我们就得一辈子蹲在那儿了，”伊兹丁一把鼻涕一把眼泪地说；“不过是因为我们是穆斯林罢了，他们的宗教狂热就是这样。”

四个人都铁了心要回埃及，巴黎已成恐怖之地。姑娘们的欢腾难以言表。她们手舞足蹈，拍掌，互相搂抱，又笑又哭，种种痴态都使了出来。白德尔-布杜尔向好几位圣徒一一许了愿，还同她死去多年的母亲快快活活地絮叨了半晌。

===Original===

Four days later they were all on board a steamer, quitting France. The sea was smooth; the ladies stayed on deck. There was no longer any question of confining them in stuffy cabins; experience of Frankish manners had done that much good.

Yûsuf turned round from cursing the fair country they were leaving, to look ahead across the vast expanse of sparkling sea.

“O land of Egypt! Blessed one!” he sighed. “Most beautiful of all that see the sun! In thee are no hideous and shocking mountains, no cataracts, no chasms, no ferocious beasts or savage people such as appal the traveller in other lands. All is flat and smooth and debonair in thee; and if thou housest infidels they dare not bite. Thy Nile is smooth and good to drink, not putrid and forever kicking like this sea. May Allah bring us to thy shores in safety and never let us leave them any more, but live in honour, eating, drinking, fasting in due season, praising God, doing good deeds, and getting many children!”

At this conclusion there was laughter and applause.

“Amîn!” cried Hâfiz. “By Allah, it is true. The air of lands of infidelity breeds madness. Hail, O Egypt!”

===Chinese===

四天以后，一行人已登上离开法兰西的轮船。海面平静，女眷们都待在甲板上。再没有人提把她们关进闷舱的事；见识了法兰克的规矩，总算见了这一点成效。

优素福咒罢他们离去的那个美丽国度，回过身来，眺望前方波光万顷的茫茫大海。

“埃及的土地！蒙福的土地啊！”他叹道。“一切得见太阳的地方，数你最美丽！你这里没有骇人的怪山，没有飞瀑，没有深渊，没有猛兽，也没有恶人——别处教旅人心惊胆战的，你一样也没有。你这里处处平坦、和婉、安详；纵然你窝藏着异教徒，他们也咬不了人。你的尼罗河平稳甘甜，可供饮用，不像这片海，腐臭冲鼻，永不安分地踢腾。愿安拉平平安安把我们送到你的岸边，从此再不教我们离开，就在那里体体面面地活着：按时吃喝，按时斋戒，赞颂真主，多行善事，多生儿女！”

这一篇颂词收梢，满场笑声掌声。

“阿敏！”哈菲兹喊道。“凭安拉，这话不假。异教之地的空气滋养疯狂。万岁，埃及！”

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===

“A rare place, by Allah!﻿—full to the brim of education and refinement. It is there that one acquires the latest mode and learns to view all creatures with fastidious eyes. In Paris people would be angered at the ignorance which prevails even among our greatest learned men. Thou too shouldst go to Paris, O my dear!”

Thus Hâfiz Bey at Alexandria, to a relative who came on board to welcome him. Barakah was much amused to overhear him, as also Yûsuf vaunting Paris to Ghandûr; who, weeping all the time and sighing “Praise to Allah!” heard not a word of what his lord was pleased to say. Great was the joy of seeing Egypt once again. Even for the girls, it wiped out all unpleasantness, making a plaintive tone impossible.

Shrouded once more in habbarah and face-veil, they stood and watched the crowd of buildings faint with sunshine, seeming diaphanous between the sapphire sky and a blue sea that looked opaque as lapis lazuli. A gaily coloured people thronged the quays and crossed the harbour in innumerable little boats. A din as rousing as a clarion call, composed of many simple noises, filled the sunlight. The girls, exhilarated, danced on tiptoe as they waited for the word to go ashore. They chattered like small birds, inconsequently, and every minute interjected “Praise to Allah!” Barakah inclined to silence, though she shared their rapture.

===Chinese===

“好地方，凭安拉！——满得往外溢的教养与风雅。最新式的时派是在那儿学的，用挑剔的眼光打量众生，也是在那儿学会的。在巴黎，人们会为那种连咱们最伟大的学者也沾身不去的无知动怒。你也该去巴黎看看，啊，我亲爱的！”

这就是哈菲兹·贝伊在亚历山大港，对登船迎接他的一位亲戚所说的话。巴拉卡无意间听见，暗暗好笑；同样，优素福正对着甘杜尔夸耀巴黎，那位甘杜尔却自始至终淌着眼泪，一声连一声地叹“感赞安拉！”，他家老爷高兴说些什么，他一个字也没有听进去。重见埃及，欢喜无量。就连对姑娘们来说，这欢喜也把一切不快冲刷干净，哀音愁语再不可能了。

她们重新裹上哈巴拉罩袍、蒙上面纱，站在那里望着阳光下影影绰绰的一大片房屋——在蓝宝石色的天宇与一片蓝得厚实如青金石的大海之间，那片屋宇仿佛是半透明的。衣色斑斓的人群挤满码头，乘着数不清的小船往来渡越港湾。一片喧嚣，振奋如同号角，由种种简单的声响汇成，充满了阳光。姑娘们心花怒放，踮着脚尖雀跃，只等一声吩咐便好登岸。她们像小鸟般叽叽喳喳，语无伦次，每隔一分钟便要插上一句“感赞安拉！”巴拉卡虽然同她们一样欢腾，却宁可沉默。

===Original===

The face-veil, which she had not worn for many weeks, seemed strange at first. It gave the sense of prying and slight mischief one has in peeping over a forbidden wall. Her eyes above it seemed more penetrating. She turned them from the crowd on shore to follow Yûsuf’s movements. He was now himself again, correct and dignified, commanding as of right, entirely rehabilitated in her good opinion. It seemed to her that the contempt she had so lately felt for him was undeserved. Sinking in a strange element, he had lost his head and for a moment clung to her. The case had been her own at first in Egypt. A minute previous she had said goodbye to Hâfiz, Izz-ud-dîn, and Saïd. It was curious to know that though they would be dwelling near her in the city, meeting Yûsuf daily, she would very likely never see them in this world again. But the prospect did not sadden her at all. Shade and seclusion seemed just then the highest good.

Having spoken their polite farewells, Yûsuf and his companions took no further notice of the group of veiled ones. Ghandûr had been deputed to look after them. He ushered them on shore, and sat beside the driver of the carriage which conveyed them to the railway station, praising Allah all the while and weeping tears of joy. In Barakah’s absence, he declared repeatedly, there had been no breeze in Egypt nor any spot of shade for man’s repose. He found them their reserved compartment in the train, and supplied their many needs, procuring sweets, chickpeas, pistachio nuts, and hard-boiled eggs from venders on the platform, as well as two large porous jars of drinking-water. The girls asked Allah to take note how good he was, and called him brother.

The dazzle and intoxication of great light remained with them even when the door was shut and they were in warm shade. The sunlight here was not like that of Paris, a thing to stare at, but a blinding glory. It danced in flakes of all the colours of the rainbow, making the buildings and the people pale and ghostlike. The very heat which soon reigned in their moving box, the very dust which drifted through its shutters, were welcome, being heat and dust of Egypt; and at the stations, when familiar cries were heard, the speech of true believers built upon the name of Allah, the girls could not contain their sentiments, but bounced upon the seats and shrieked for joy.

===Chinese===

那方好几个星期不曾蒙过的面纱，起初戴上去觉得陌生。它给人一种感觉，像是从禁墙后探头上偷看，带着几分窥探、几分淘气。面纱上头她那双眼睛，仿佛更加锐利。她把眼光从岸上的人群收回来，去追随优素福的动静。他又恢复本来面目了：端正、庄重，发号施令如同行使天赋的权利，在她心里的评价全然翻了身。她觉得，自己新近对他的那番轻蔑，原是委屈了他。他是沉沦在一个陌生的环境里，昏了头，一时把她抓得死紧。她自己当初初到埃及，也是这般光景。就在片刻之前，她刚同哈菲兹、伊兹丁和赛义德道了别。想想也怪：他们明明要住在这座城里，离她不远，天天会同优素福见面，而她今生今世多半再见不着他们了。可这前景一点也不叫她伤感。绿荫与深藏，此刻在她看来便是至高的福分。

客气的道别说过之后，优素福和他的同伴们便不再理会这一群蒙面的女眷。照应她们的事，委派给了甘杜尔。他领她们登岸，坐在送她们去火车站的那辆马车的车夫身旁，一路赞美安拉，淌着欢喜的眼泪。他再三念叨：巴拉卡不在的日子里，埃及没有一丝风，也没有一片供人歇凉的荫凉地。他在火车上替她们找好预留的包厢，又张罗她们种种需求，从月台上的小贩那里买来甜食、鹰嘴豆、开心果仁、白煮蛋，还有两只多孔的大陶水罐，盛满了饮用水。姑娘们求安拉明鉴他这人何等好法，都唤他做兄长。

即使车门关上、她们已坐在暖融融的荫凉里，那强光的炫目与醺然仍缠着她们。此地的阳光不像巴黎的阳光——那不过是一件可以瞪着看的东西——这里是叫人睁不开眼的荣光。它化作虹霓百色的光斑飞舞，把房屋和人都照得苍白如幽灵。不多时便主宰了她们这只流动小匣子的暑热，从窗板缝隙里飘进来的尘土，也都受欢迎，因为那是埃及的热、埃及的尘；到了车站，听得那些熟悉的呼喊——真正信士的话语，句句筑在安拉的尊名上——姑娘们再也按捺不住，在座位上直蹦，欢喜得尖声叫喊。

===Original===

“Hear what I am going to do, by Allah’s leave,” cried Bedr. “Immediately on my arrival at the palace, before seeing anyone, I shall go to the hammam and make our old bellânah scrub and knead me till every vestige of the dust of Paris is abolished. Then she shall dye my hands and feet with henna and shall kohl my eyes and eyebrows﻿—if we had not been forbidden to take kohl to Paris, our men would not have left us as they did﻿—and then I shall stretch myself like a sleek cat and looking at my pretty hennaed toes, shall say, ‘I seek refuge in Allah from the abomination of the infidels.’ That done, and being dressed in my most splendid robes, I shall present myself before my ladies, and shall lie to them; declaring that I was most happy there in Paris, that Hâfiz Bey refused to leave me for a single instant. The ladies will not doubt me, seeing my great beauty, and Hâfiz Bey, you may be sure, will not deny my story. Thus shall I gain more favour in his eyes, and make his wife﻿—the proud one!﻿—wish that she had gone instead of me. What say you?”

“By Allah, we will do the same in all respects!” her companions cried delightedly. “But what of Barakah? Promise, O Barakah, to hide the truth from the harem!”

Barakah promised; when they made her swear to love them always, though they were but slave-girls and she a dignified and noble lady, for the sake of the misfortunes they had borne together. They all clung round her when the train reached Cairo. The door of their compartment was flung open by Sawwâb in person, grinning welcome, with other eunuchs close behind him on the platform.

===Chinese===

“听听我打算怎么办，若蒙安拉允许，”白德尔嚷道。“一到宅邸，什么人也不先见，头一件事就进浴场，叫咱们那位老贝拉娜（bellânah，浴场里伺候的搓澡妇）把我又搓又揉，把巴黎的尘土一丝痕迹都除尽。然后叫她用指甲花染我的手脚，用眼墨描我的眼睛眉毛——当初若不是不许我们把眼墨带去巴黎，咱们的男人也不会那样撇下我们——然后我就舒展开四肢，像一只毛光水滑的猫，望着自己染得漂漂亮亮的脚趾，说道：‘我求安拉庇佑，免遭异教徒的污秽。’这样办完，穿起我最华丽的衣裳，我就到我家夫人们跟前去，对她们撒谎；一口咬定我在那边快活得很，哈菲兹·贝伊一刻也不肯离我左右。夫人们见我这绝色的容貌，必不疑心；哈菲兹·贝伊呢，你们只管放心，也不会拆穿我的话。这样我在他眼里更添恩宠，还要叫他那位夫人——骄傲的那一位！——懊悔没替我去。你们说好不好？”

“凭安拉，我们样样都要照这么办！”同伴们欢欢喜喜地喊道。“可巴拉卡呢？答应吧，巴拉卡，别把真情透露给内宅！”

巴拉卡答应了；她们还要她起誓永远爱她们——她们不过是女奴，她却是端严高贵的一位夫人——看在一同受过那些患难的份上。火车抵达开罗时，她们全都偎依着她不肯放。包厢的门由萨瓦布亲手拉开，咧着嘴笑迎，月台上紧随他身后还立着几个阉人。

===Original===

Sawwâb conducted Barakah with honour to the harem carriage, entering which she was hugged breathless by the lady Fitnah, while Leylah Khânum and her daughters started chattering, telling her all the news at once and in a single breath.

Gulbeyzah had been married a whole month. She was absent in the country with her lord’s whole house, but would return, it was expected, in a week or two. Had Barakah heard in Europe﻿—no doubt she had﻿—that the Sea Canal was to be opened in the coming year, with great festivities?﻿—the King and Queen of France were coming, it was rumoured. Murjânah Khânum had been far from well. That was why she had not come to welcome Barakah, to whom she sent her warmest salutations. Barakah was not going to the garden-house this time, but to the Pasha’s palace, to remain with them, the praise to Allah! Fitnah herself had seen the rooms cleaned out and perfumed. One of the blacks, Zamurrudah, was dead, the Lord have mercy on her! The old striped cat had kittens, lucky one! The Pasha’s nieces were quite positive about the fact, though no one had been able to find out their hiding-place.

As Barakah, caressed by all of them, received this outpour, her feeling of homecoming was complete. And when she came to her own gilded salon﻿—the same where she had sipped the poison which seemed now a dream﻿—there was a slave-girl of Murjânah Khânum’s waiting to conduct her to the bath, with a present of rare flowers and fruit, and a robe of honour which she was to don, when she had rested, for supper in Murjânah Khânum’s rooms, where all the ladies were invited to meet her.

===Chinese===

萨瓦布把巴拉卡体体面面送到深闺马车上；她一进车厢，就被菲特娜夫人搂得气都透不过来，莱拉·哈努姆和她的几个女儿随即叽叽喳喳开了腔，一口气同时把全部新闻都倒给她。

古尔贝雅扎成亲已经满了一个月。她随她老爷的合宅到乡下去了，料想一两个礼拜便回。巴拉卡在欧洲可听说了——想必听说了——那条海上运河来年就要通航，还要大办庆典？——据说法国的国王和王后也要来呢。穆尔贾娜·哈努姆身子一直欠安。所以她没能来迎巴拉卡，只给她捎来最热忱的问候。这一回巴拉卡不住园宅了，改住帕夏的宅邸，同他们住在一处，感赞安拉！菲特娜亲自督着人把几间屋子打扫熏香过了。黑奴里头的扎穆鲁达（Zamurrudah）死了，愿主怜悯她！那只老花斑猫下了崽，好福气的！帕夏的侄女们咬定这事千真万确，虽说谁也没能探出那窝小猫藏身的所在。

巴拉卡被众人又摸又疼地抚爱着，领受这一大篇倾吐，归家的感觉至此圆满。待她走进自己那间金碧辉煌的客厅——正是她当初啜下那口毒药的地方，如今想来恍如一场梦——穆尔贾娜·哈努姆的一个女奴已候在那里，要引她去入浴，还带来一份奇花异果的礼物，和一件荣典锦袍，等她歇过之后穿上，去穆尔贾娜·哈努姆房里用晚饭——众位夫人都被请来同她相会。

===Original===

The ladies, having voided their own news, desired a full account of Paris and her doings. “Inshallah, thou wast happy there!” they all exclaimed. When she replied, “My happiness is here with you,” the answer gave unbounded satisfaction. From their remarks she learnt, to her no small amazement, that Hâfiz Bey was the son of her old friend Amînah Khânum.

“Thou didst not know?” they cried. “How can that be? And Bedr-ul-Budûr﻿—surely thou hast heard of her﻿—the slave whose beauty the Princess was always vaunting? It is very strange!”

The placid gossip and the shaded calm existence were delightful after months of agitation. Barakah fell into the harem habits with enthusiasm, devouring sweetstuff at all hours, enjoying cigarettes and the narghile. The best part of her morning was spent at the bath, where the ladies met for gossip and for healthful exercise; her afternoon in seeing visitors or paying visits. Gulbeyzah came to see her, radiating gladness, extolling not her husband but her fellow-wives.

===Chinese===

夫人们把自己的新闻倾倒完了，便要她把巴黎和她自身的情形来一个全本。“若安拉意欲，你在那边快活了吧！”她们齐声叹道。她答道：“我的快活就在这儿，同你们在一起。”这一答叫她们满足了到极处。从她们的言语里，她得知哈菲兹·贝伊竟是她的老朋友阿米娜·哈努姆的儿子，好不诧异。

“你竟不知道？”她们嚷道。“这怎么说？还有白德尔-布杜尔——你总听说过她吧——公主总是夸耀她美貌的那个女奴？真是奇了！”

几个月的动荡之后，这恬静的闲话、这浓荫下安然的日子，实在可爱。巴拉卡满腔热忱地过起内宅的生活来，无时无刻不嚼着甜食，享用香烟和水烟筒（narghile）。她一早晨最好的时光消磨在浴场——夫人们在那里聚会谈天，做些有益身子的活动；午后则用来会客或出门拜客。古尔贝雅扎来看她，喜气洋洋，夸的不是丈夫，倒是同夫的姐妹们。

===Original===

“We spend such merry days together,” she informed her friend. “Oh, how much better than to be an only wife!”

When Barakah returned the visit, she was received by the four durrahs with one voice of welcome. The four together formed a charming small society, quite independent of the husband’s humours and the outside world. All their possessions they enjoyed in common, even children. Barakah was begged to come and see them often, and to love them all.

She would have been completely happy in those days but for embarrassment arising from a secret which she longed yet feared to tell. She was with child. Suspicion grew to certainty and still she put off the announcement, dreading the outcry of these candid women and the harem ceremonies. It slipped from her by accident, one afternoon, and the fuss they made proved even worse than expectation.

===Chinese===

“我们一块儿过的日子可真快活，”她告诉她的朋友。“哎呀，比做独一个的妻子好多啦！”

巴拉卡回拜时，四位杜拉同声欢迎她。她们四个合成一个惹人喜爱的小社会，全然不系于丈夫的喜怒和外间的世界。她们的一切享用都是共有的，连孩子也不例外。她们恳求巴拉卡常来看她们，并爱她们每一个人。

那些日子她本可以十分幸福，若不是有一桩秘密，既想吐露又怕吐露，害得她好不为难。她有了身孕。疑心渐渐成了定谳，她仍把这消息压着，怕这些口无遮拦的女人大呼小叫，怕内宅的种种仪节。一天下午，她失口说了出来，她们掀起的那阵忙乱，比她预想的还要糟糕。

===Original===

Amînah Khânum brought Bedr-ul-Budûr to see her, saying:

“This girl of mine has news to tell you.”

The old Princess herself proclaimed the news with praise to Allah. A flush suffused the listener from head to foot.

“I too﻿—” she murmured, and then stopped in great confusion. Amînah Khânum pounced on her with eager questions. Bedr-ul-Budûr knelt down before her in an ecstasy.

===Chinese===

阿米娜·哈努姆领着白德尔-布杜尔来看她，说：

“我这丫头有一桩新闻要告诉你。”

老公主便亲自连声感赞着安拉，把那新闻宣布出来。听的人从头顶红到了脚跟。

“我也——”她嗫嚅着，随即大窘，收住了口。阿米娜·哈努姆扑上来急切地追问。白德尔-布杜尔喜不自胜，跪倒在她面前。

===Original===

“Thou, too, art blest? And thou hast kept it secret all this while?” the Princess cried. “O Bedr, go and beg the lady Fitnah to come hither instantly!”

“No, no!” entreated Barakah, distraught with shame.

“Yes, yes!” replied the other, scoffing at her. “Is this the famed false modesty of England? Praise God Most High that thou art fruitful, praise Him loudly!”

===Chinese===

“你也蒙福了？还一直瞒着不声张？”公主喊道。“白德尔，快去求菲特娜夫人即刻到这儿来！”

“别，别！”巴拉卡恳求道，羞得没了主张。

“要，要！”对方答道，把她奚落一番。“这就是英国出了名的假正经？感赞至高的真主使你多产，大声赞美他吧！”

===Original===

The joy of Fitnah Khânum passed all bounds. She sent a messenger at once to Yûsuf, another to the Pasha, with the tidings. The Pasha came at once to pay his compliments to Barakah. Yûsuf came later, having thought it necessary to circulate the happy news among his friends. Ghandûr, who, as the water-carrier of the apartment, sat always in the alley, underneath the lady’s lattice, was heard intoning a loud song of triumph, three parts prayer, of which each verse concluded with: “Twin boys, inshallah!”

Joy-shrieks resounded; the whole household smiled; her friends thronged round her, informed of her good luck as if by miracle, for black-shrouded newsbearers were ever flitting by shadowed walls, along the edge of crowded markets, linking the great harems in one society, and what was done in one was known in all. And Barakah alone saw any call for shame or reticence.

From that day forth she was the idol of her little world, her every want forestalled by warm solicitude. Murjânah Khânum talked to her in a religious strain; Fitnah, more homely, prepared dainties for her; the Pasha’s sister came and told her stories. The very children talked aloud of her condition, and hailed it as a blessing to the house.

===Chinese===

菲特娜·哈努姆的欢喜没了边际。她立刻打发人给优素福送信，又打发一个去帕夏那里报喜讯。帕夏当即亲来向巴拉卡道贺。优素福来得晚些——他觉得有必要把这喜讯在朋友们中间传扬一番。甘杜尔是这处住所的挑水夫，总坐在巷子里、夫人格棂底下，这时只听得他扬起嗓子，唱起一支高声的得胜歌，唱词十之七八是祈祷，每一段的煞尾都是：“双生小子，若安拉意欲！”

欢喜的尖叫声响成一片；合宅上下人人含笑；她的朋友们围拢过来，仿佛得了神启一般尽知她的好运——原来黑衣裹身的报信女人们，永远沿着背阴的墙根、沿着热闹市场的边上穿梭来去，把各大内宅连成一个社会，一家有事，家家知晓。唯独巴拉卡一人，觉得此事有什么可羞、有什么可讳似的。

从那天起，她成了她那个小世界的偶像，每一个心愿都有人怀着热切的体贴先替她想到了。穆尔贾娜·哈努姆同她谈经论道；菲特娜更家常些，亲手给她备办精美吃食；帕夏的姐妹来给她讲故事。连孩子们也大声谈论她的身孕，齐声欢呼这是宅门之福。

===Original===

She had a good excuse for shunning the festivities which took place on the arrival of the Emperor of the French in Cairo; though her husband was employed in the reception, and all the ladies were agog to see the Empress. She wished to be entirely Oriental. Frankish talk disgusted her. Any reminder that the Europeans still existed was annoying; how much more to hear them vaunted by her Eastern friends. Yûsuf himself made fun of her fanaticism. The women humoured her conceit with knowing smiles.

Gulbeyzah and Bedr-ul-Budûr, both in the same condition, were her constant visitors. Amînah Khânum gave advice in her brusque way, and as the Englishwoman’s time drew near, did more for her protection than she knew of in her illness; impressing on Muhammad Pasha through Murjânah the necessity of calling in a Frankish doctor, and herself procuring from the Mufti the religious judgment which stilled the angry outcry of the harem midwives.

The hour of trial came at length﻿—an anguish worse than death, succeeded by a happiness as calm as heaven. From the cries of jubilation filling all the house, from the blessings showered on her within the chamber, she knew that she had borne a son. She saw the blue of evening at the lattice, heard the murmur of the tired city like a voice of waters, and, lulled by vast contentment, fell asleep.

===Chinese===

法兰西皇帝驾临开罗时的那番庆典，她正好有个现成的托辞躲了开去；虽说她丈夫在迎驾的仪节里当差，众位夫人又个个心痒，都想瞻仰皇后。她一心要做十足的东方人。法兰克人的谈吐叫她厌恶。哪怕只是提醒她欧洲人还存在，也惹她厌烦，更何况听她的东方朋友们夸赞他们。优素福本人也拿她的执迷打趣。女人们心照不宣地微笑着，一味顺着她这份痴念。

古尔贝雅扎和白德尔-布杜尔也都在同样的身孕里，时常来看她。阿米娜·哈努姆用她那粗率的样子给她出主意；待到英国女人的产期临近，又在她病中为保全她做了许多她自己并不知道的事：借着穆尔贾娜向穆罕默德·帕夏力陈延请一位法兰克医生的必要，又亲自从穆夫提（Mufti，伊斯兰教法阐释官）那里讨来一纸教法判词，平息了内宅产婆们愤愤的鼓噪。

考验的时辰终于来到——一场比死更甚的剧痛，随后继之以一片天堂般安宁的幸福。满宅欢呼雷动，产房里祝福纷落如雨，她由此知道，自己生了一个儿子。她望见格棂上傍晚的青色，听见疲倦的城市低语，如流水之声；她在巨大的满足中被摇哄着，沉沉睡去。

## XX / 二十

===Original===
During the heat of summer, part of the harem, consisting of the ladies Barakah and Fitnah, with their children and attendants, stayed at a farm belonging to the Pasha, on the banks of the Nile, near Benha. The journey thither was performed on donkeys in a long procession with a eunuch at its head and tail, a eunuch boy leading the donkey of each lady, that she might have freedom to hold up her sunshade and munch nuts and sweetstuff. The slave-girls, some of them, rode two together; they waxed hilarious, exchanging jests with all who passed them on the dyke. Their going raised a goodly cloud of dust. The house to which they went was large and formal, none too clean, though very sparsely furnished. Behind it was a filthy yard hemmed in by hovels, where dwelt the fellahin who worked the farm. Before it was a garden of fruit trees, and beyond that a plantation of young date-palms. There was also a big tree beside a waterwheel, where the ladies took their pleasure in the shade. The land was absolutely flat in all directions, but diversified in hue by divers crops, broken here and there by clumps of trees and squat mud villages.

Here manners were relaxed; for all the peasant women went unveiled, and their example made the slaves less strict than in the city. The lady Fitnah, being of the country, took delight in talking with the villagers, both men and women, and thus, though most correct in her apparel, set the fashion of unbending. Yûsuf, who had now a Government appointment, and the Pasha came to see them when they had the leisure; and Ghandûr also travelled down to see his wife.

To please the lady Fitnah, Barakah gave French and English lessons to the children in the mornings under the great tree, when many of the servants also gathered round and tried to learn. She was begged to be particularly strict with Hamdi, whom the lady Fitnah seemed to think the soul of wickedness, as indeed did everybody else, making his life a burden with perpetual scolding.

===Chinese===
盛夏溽热时节，内宅的一部分人——巴拉卡与菲特娜两位夫人，带着孩子和仆从——住到帕夏名下一座田庄上，就在尼罗河畔、本哈（Benha）附近。去程骑着驴子，排成长长一列，队首队尾各有一名阉人押队，每夫人的驴子前头还有个阉童牵着，好让她腾出手来撑阳伞、嚼坚果和甜食。女奴们有的两人合骑一头，一路兴高采烈，同堤上每个过路人打趣逗乐。她们这一行，扬起好大一片尘土。去住的那座宅子宽大而规整，陈设寥寥无几，却算不得怎么干净。宅后是一个污秽的院子，四面围着破屋棚舍，住着耕种田庄的费拉农人。宅前是一片果园，再往外是一片新栽的椰枣林。水车旁还有一棵大树，夫人们便在树荫下乘凉取乐。四野的地势一望平坦，全无起伏，却因各色庄稼而斑驳多样，间或被一丛丛树木和低矮的泥屋村落点破。

在这里，规矩松了下来；因为农妇们一概不戴面纱，她们做了榜样，奴婢们便不像在城里那么拘谨。菲特娜夫人原是乡下出身，最爱同村里的男女攀谈；这样一来，她虽然衣着依旧循规蹈矩，却开了宽纵风气的先河。如今在政府里有了差事的优素福，同帕夏一道，得空便来探望她们；甘杜尔也赶下来看他的妻子。

为讨菲特娜夫人欢心，巴拉卡每天上午在大树下给孩子们教法文和英文，许多仆人也围拢过来跟着学。众人恳请她对哈姆迪格外严厉些；菲特娜夫人似乎认定这孩子坏到了骨子里，旁人也个个这么想，没完没了的责骂把他压得日子没了活头。

===Original===
This boy, her husband’s younger brother, was attached to Barakah as the only one who never shook him by the neck or cursed him. He told her all his woes, and brought her offerings of curious things he found in his illicit rambles. He was always straying, though with no worse object, he asserted, than the wish to be alone. His lady mother called him “stupid Turk,” vowing that he was all his father’s child, and she herself had neither part nor lot in him; though Hamdi was the true Egyptian adolescent, still but half awake, a slave to every breeze, to every odour, and fascinated by the sight of gleaming objects. He would sit still for hours in contemplation of a sunlit blade of grass; at other times he would walk miles, drawn on invisibly, with great brown eyes which seemed to harbour visions. Barakah found him gentle and obedient. In truth, his only wickedness that she could see consisted in resentment of shrill interruptions. At such times he would battle blindly with assailants, cursing them, and crying out, in his despair:

“Am I not a man full-grown? Do I not sleep in the selamlik? Then let me be, or it shall be the worse for you, by Allah!”

===Chinese===
这孩子是她丈夫的幼弟，单单依恋巴拉卡一人，因为只有她从不揪他的脖子，也从不咒骂他。他向她倾吐种种苦楚，还把私自闲逛时寻来的稀奇物件，当作供献一般带给她。他总是乱跑，不过据他自己分辩，无非是想独自待着，别无更坏的念头。他的母亲大人骂他“蠢突厥人”，赌咒说他全是父亲的种，她自己半点份也没有；其实哈姆迪是个地道的埃及少年，心神尚未全醒，是每一缕风、每一缕气息的奴仆，一见闪光的东西便着迷。他能一连几个钟头坐着不动，凝望一片沐着阳光的草叶；有时又会走上好几里地，被一股无形之力牵引着，棕色的大眼睛里仿佛藏着幻象。巴拉卡觉得他温顺听话。说实在的，在她看来，他唯一的恶处，就是怨恨那些尖声的打搅。每逢这时，他便会不管不顾地同冒犯他的人开战，咒骂他们，绝望地叫喊：

“我不是个长成了的男人吗？我不是睡在塞拉姆利克吗？那就别来惹我，不然叫你们吃不了兜着走，凭安拉起誓！”

===Original===
“A man full-grown, thou sayest?” screamed the lady Fitnah one evening when he came home soaked in mud from head to foot. “Listen, O child of dogs, O malefactor! Knowest thou what I shall do on our return to town? I shall marry thee at once to Na’imah, thy uncle’s child. Thy clothes are in a filthy state, thy tassel gone. Thou hast been sprawling in some ditch, O piggish boy! By the Prophet, I shall do as I have said. Sure, matrimony is the only cure for one like thee. Thou shalt wed Na’imah.”

“Allah forbid!” exclaimed the lad with fervour; whereat the ladies and the servants burst out laughing; for Na’imah, Leylah Khânum’s youngest daughter, had been Hamdi’s chief tormentor there at home, disturbing his still dreams with impish glee, and quick to vanish.

“Is it not cruel thus to hound me?” the unlucky boy asked Barakah. “I do no wrong; they interfere with me. And now my mother threatens to unite me to the most hateful daughter of a dog that ever yelped and bit.”

===Chinese===
“长成了的男人，亏你说得出口？”一天傍晚，他从头顶到脚跟糊满泥浆地回到家里，菲特娜夫人尖声叫道。“听着，你这狗崽子，你这小祸害！你可知道回城之后我要做什么？我要马上把你许给纳伊玛（Na’imah），你叔伯家的闺女。你这一身衣裳脏得不成体统，帽缨也丢了。你准是在哪条沟里打过滚，猪一样的小子！凭先知起誓，我说到做到。像你这样的货色，成亲自然是唯一的药方。你就娶纳伊玛吧。”

“安拉不容！”少年激切地叫道；夫人们和仆人们顿时哄堂大笑；原来莱拉·哈努姆的幼女纳伊玛，在家时正是捉弄哈姆迪的头一号，惯会带着小鬼似的欢快搅扰他静静的梦想，一闹完便溜得飞快。

“这样穷追猛打地欺负我，不也太狠心了吗？”倒霉的孩子问巴拉卡。“我干不出坏事；是他们来搅扰我。如今我母亲又威胁要把我说给那条又吠又咬的狗生出来的最可恨的女儿。”

===Original===
The month of Ramadan came on them in their country life; and the long hours of heat without a bite or sup made everybody irritable except Barakah and the wife of Ghandûr, who were both exempt from fasting﻿—the former as an invalid, the latter as a nursing-mother. The slave-girls lost their usual delight in birds and greenery. A gun fired in the distant market-town announced the moment of release in the first bloom of night; but the party failed to hear it sometimes, and looked out for the lighting of the lamps around a village mosque across the plain. At once arose vast sighs of praise to Allah; cigarettes, prepared in readiness, were seized and lighted; water was handed round and food set out.

It was at that blest hour upon a certain evening of the sacred month that a rapturous surprise befell the party. A little cavalcade was seen approaching on the dyke. It consisted of two donkeys and a baggage mule. A woman sat upon the foremost donkey; on the second rode two children, boy and girl; while the mule was led by a black-bearded, turbaned man of noble presence. The ladies, sitting in the garden, peered, then shouted:

“Tâhir! It is Tâhir! Tâhir, the great singer! O most blessed day! Enter, O son of honour! Deign to favour us!”

===Chinese===
斋月（Ramadan）赶上了他们的乡居生活；漫长的炎热时辰里不进一口饮食，人人都变得烦躁，只有巴拉卡和甘杜尔的妻子例外——一个是有病之身，一个是哺育之母，都免于封斋。女奴们连平日对鸟雀和绿意的兴致也丧失了。远处集镇上一声炮响，通报夜色初绽时开戒的时刻；可他们有时没听见，便守望着平原那头村中清真寺四周亮起的灯火。顿时响起一片赞颂安拉的浩叹；预先备好的香烟抓在手里点上了；清水传了一圈，吃食也摆了开来。

正是在这神圣月份的某个傍晚，那蒙福的时刻，一桩叫众人狂喜的意外落到了他们头上。大堤上望见一小队人马走近。是两头驴子和一匹驮行李的骡子。前头的驴上坐着一位女子；第二头上骑着一双儿女，一男一女；骡子由一个黑须缠头、仪表高贵的男人牵着。坐在园子里的夫人们眯眼张望，随即叫嚷起来：

“塔希尔！是塔希尔！塔希尔，伟大的歌者！哦，至福的日子！请进，尊荣之子！请赏光为我们献艺！”

===Original===
Learning that the master of the house was absent, Tâhir would not enter, but sought a lodging in the hovels of the fellahin, whither a rich meal was sent to him. But after supper he came up into the garden with his lute, followed timidly by all the population of the hamlet; and his wife and children stole into the room where all the women sat with windows open, looking forward to the concert. Once more his little daughter drew to Barakah, and, having kissed her hand, sat down and leaned against her. “I love thee,” she explained, with a soft look; and then with a wide yawn exclaimed: “I am so tired!” Barakah put her arm about her, and the child seemed happy. She did not go to sleep this time, however, but lay still, fondling her protector’s hand, and gazing up at the great stars.

“See, what a man he is!” exclaimed the Galla slave, Fatûmah, her hand upon the shoulder of her mistress, all respect forgotten in intense excitement. “He does not even stay to tune his lute. All that is for the common singers. He is much above it. By Allah, he would sing to a dog’s howl and make it musical.”

One twang of the lute, and then the magic voice arose from out the shadow of the trees. It gave a living spirit to the starlight, a soul to all the nights that ever were or would be. It seemed illumination, yet was all of mystery; it gave the listener a sense of floating disembodied.

===Chinese===
塔希尔得知宅主不在，不肯进宅，在费拉农人的棚屋里寻了个落脚处，一顿丰盛的饭食随即给他送了过去。但晚饭之后，他携着琵琶来到园中，村里老小怯生生地跟在后面；他的妻儿则悄悄溜进女人们坐着的屋子——窗子都敞着，人人盼着这场音乐会。他的小女儿又挨到巴拉卡身边，吻了吻她的手，坐下来倚着她。“我爱你。”她柔柔地望着她，说明缘故；随即打了个大大的呵欠，叫道：“我累极了！”巴拉卡搂住她，孩子像是很快活。不过这一回她没有睡着，只静静躺着，摩挲着她的保护人的手，仰望满天巨星。

“瞧，他是何等人物！”加拉族奴隶法图麦嚷道，一只手搭在主母肩头，激动得把体统忘了个干净。“他连琵琶都犯不上调弦。那一套是留给寻常歌者的。他不知高出多少。安拉在上，他就是冲着一条狗嚎，也能把它唱成音乐。”

琵琶铮然一声，随后那有魔力的嗓音从树影深处升起。它给星光注入活的魂灵，给古往今来一切夜晚赋予灵魂。它仿佛是启示的光明，却又通体是神秘；它令听者生出魂魄离体、飘飘荡荡之感。

===Original===
Once when Tâhir paused to rest, the voice of Hamdi was heard in the garden, begging for leave to hold his lute and play it.

“That boy again! Cut short his life!” cried Fitnah Khânum. “Devoid of manners as of sensibility. Remove him quickly!”

But Tâhir answered pleasantly: “Here, O my son! Take it and play for me. Observe the measure. Strike loudly in the pauses, softly while I sing.” And Fatûmah, quite beside herself, exclaimed:

“Behold the man he is! He can dispense with all things. That which would ruin the performance of another singer is a joy to him.”

===Chinese===
一次塔希尔停下来歇息，园中响起哈姆迪的声音，恳求让他捧一捧琵琶，弹上一弹。

“又是那小子！干脆别叫他活了！”菲特娜·哈努姆喊道。“既无情趣，也无教养。快把他撵走！”

塔希尔却和颜悦色地答道：“来，我的孩子！拿去，替我弹。看好节拍。停歇的空当重弹，我唱的时候轻弹。”法图麦欢喜得忘乎所以，叫道：

“瞧瞧他是怎样的人物！一切他都省得。换了别的歌者要毁掉演出的事，到他这里反倒是一桩乐事。”

===Original===
Hamdi acquitted himself fairly well of the task of accompaniment and won a word of praise from Tâhir, which so moved him that when the singer was departing the next morning early, he stole out to him, and, looking round to ascertain that he was heard of none save Barakah, entreated:

“Take me with thee, O my uncle. Instruct me, let me play for thee forever. This girl, thy daughter, this little sugarplum, shall be my bride. Then we can all live happily together.”

“The honour is too high for us, O my small lord!” the singer answered, with his charming smile. “Thy lot in life is better far, inshallah, than that of us poor players.”

“But they say that thou canst earn a hundred pounds a night.”

“Seldom as much as that, beloved. And my living is at Allah’s pleasure. It is a gift from Him, to whom be praise. Come to me four years hence, and we will think about it.”

===Chinese===
哈姆迪把伴奏的差事当得相当不错，得了塔希尔一句夸奖，激动非凡；次日一早歌者动身离去时，他偷偷溜到他跟前，四下张望，认定除了巴拉卡再无旁人听见，便恳求道：

“带我走吧，我的叔父。教我本事，让我永远为你弹奏。这个姑娘，你的女儿，这颗小糖球儿，就做我的新娘。那样咱们大家就能一起快快活活过日子了。”

“这份荣耀我们高攀不起，我的小老爷！”歌者带着他那迷人的微笑答道。“你的命数好得多，若安拉意欲，总强过我们这些可怜的卖艺人。”

“可人家说，你一夜能挣一百镑哩。”

“很少有那么多，亲爱的。我的生计在安拉的意下。那是他的恩赐，愿赞美归于他。四年后来找我，咱们再商量。”

===Original===
With a dignified salute he started off; the children, on their donkey, waved their hands and screamed farewell. Hamdi was left standing disappointed and a trifle injured.

“O my misfortune!” he exclaimed to Barakah. “I would have given my right hand to go with him. Like that I could escape from persecution and accursed Na’imah, and dwell forever in the sound of music which transports my soul. Allah is greatest!”

And he heaved a mighty sigh.

===Chinese===
他庄重地行了一礼，动身上路；孩子们骑在驴上，挥手尖声作别。哈姆迪被撇在原地站着，满心失望，还带着几分委屈。

“哦，我的苦命！”他对巴拉卡叹道。“只要能跟他走，砍去我的右手也情愿。那样我就能逃脱这些迫害，逃脱那可咒的纳伊玛，永远住在那摄我魂魄的乐声里。安拉至大！”

说罢，他长长地叹了一大口气。

===Original===
When the month of fasting ended, there were mild rejoicings. The fellahin fired guns and let off fireworks. The women smoked too much and over-ate themselves, and felt aggrieved at being far from Cairo, where the means of satisfaction were more varied and abundant.

Then Yûsuf and the Pasha came and stayed a week; delighted, coming fresh to it, with the unoccupied existence over which the others had begun to yawn. At the end of the week they all returned to Cairo, the procession of the ladies keeping half a mile behind their lords. The first view of the citadel on one hand, the pyramids of Gîzah on the other, called forth thankful shouts. The coloured, noisy streets, the odours sweet and foul, the atmosphere of teeming life, excited Barakah. She joined in exclamations of delight.

While she gazed with strange eyes at her gilded salon, superintending the disposal of her baggage, a letter was presented to her by Fatûmah. It had been given to the latter that same minute by Sawwâb the eunuch, who had had it in safe keeping for two months. It was from Mrs. Cameron.

===Chinese===
封斋的月份结束，有过一阵淡淡的欢庆。费拉农人放枪、放焰火。女人们吸烟过了量，吃食过了度，又因远离开罗而心怀不平——在那边，称心的消遣花样更多、也更充裕。

随后优素福和帕夏来了，住了一个星期；旁人已开始对着这无所事事的日子打呵欠，他们新来乍到，却觉其乐陶陶。周末，众人一同返回开罗，女眷的队伍跟在各自老爷后面，隔着半英里地。一头初见城堡，一头望见吉萨（Gîzah）的金字塔，引出阵阵感恩的欢呼。五彩喧闹的街市，或香或臭的气味，蒸腾着挤挤攘攘生命的大气，令巴拉卡兴奋起来。她也随着欢呼赞叹。

她正用异样的眼光打量自己那间鎏金客厅，督着人安放行李，法图麦呈上一封信。信是阉人萨瓦布同一刻刚交给她的，已在他手里安然存了两个月。信是卡梅伦夫人写来的。

===Original===
Barakah, frowning, opened it and read:

> “It grieves me much to learn that you have been seriously ill. I heard of this quite by accident from Doctor Torranelli, whom I chanced to meet at a friend’s house. In some anxiety, I tried to call upon you yesterday, but learnt that you are absent in the country. I trust that the dear baby flourishes. He must be a great comfort and delight to you. Please never forget that I am your sincere friend.”

With an exclamation of annoyance, she tore up the note.

===Chinese===
巴拉卡皱着眉，拆开来读：

> “惊悉你曾患重病，我十分难过。此事我纯属偶然得知，是在一位朋友家中遇见托拉内利（Torranelli）医生，他提起的。我怀着几分焦虑，昨日想去拜访你，却得知你去了乡下。想必那可爱的宝宝茁壮成长。他必是你莫大的安慰与欢喜。请永远不要忘记，我是你真挚的朋友。”

她烦躁地叫了一声，把字条撕碎了。

## XXI / 二十一
===Original===
The idea of seeing Mrs. Cameron again was quite intolerable. She therefore wrote that lady a brief note, an asp for venom, designed to terminate acquaintance and to rankle, and plunged into the harem pleasures with sensations of defiance.

One morning, as she lounged upon her cushioned window-seat, smoking her narghile and listening to the voices wafted with the sunlight through her lattice, Fatûmah came and with a grin announced that Hamdi Bey desired an audience of her Honour. She gave the word, and in came Hamdi, knuckling his two eyes.

“O day of pitch!” he cried. “O vile nefarious day! O my beloved sister, hide me, save me! My father has enforced my mother’s harsh command. I am to be married today to that unholy child of dogs﻿—against my will. I wished to wait a thousand years. Ghandûr is waiting at this minute to conduct me to the bath.”
===Chinese===
再见到卡梅伦夫人，这念头实在叫人无法忍受。于是她给那位夫人写去一封短信——毒如一条蝰蛇，专为断绝这段交情、教那毒口隐隐溃痛；写罢，便带着一股挑战的情绪，一头扎进内宅的种种欢愉。

一天早晨，她正斜倚在铺着垫子的窗座上，抽着水烟筒，听人语声随着阳光从窗棂间飘进来，法图麦进来了，咧着嘴禀报说哈姆迪·贝伊求见夫人。她吩咐传见，哈姆迪走了进来，两只拳头揉着两只眼睛。

“哦，漆黑如沥青的日子！”他叫道。“哦，可恶歹毒的日子！哦，我亲爱的姐姐，把我藏起来，救救我！我父亲执行了我母亲的无情严令。今天我就要娶那个不圣洁的狗崽子——这违背我的心意。我本想再等上一千年。这会儿甘杜尔正在外头等着，要领我去浴场呢。”
===Original===
As if in confirmation of his words, the voice of Ghandûr shouted in the street without: “Make haste, O Hamdi! Lo, the sun is high! The shadow is already on the stone thou fixedst for a limit when I let thee enter.”

“Thou hearest,” snuffled Hamdi, “how they hound me? He has my wedding garments in a bundle﻿—O my hatred! Guests have been bidden﻿—may their fathers perish! Go to my mother (she will hear thee); plead that I may be allowed a few months’ respite. It is Na’imah who, through her mother, hastens on the match. She would destroy my newfound freedom and torment me.”

Barakah could not help laughing, though she uttered words of comfort. Na’imah was a very pretty girl, she pointed out, and not ill-natured, though a great coquette. He would have none of it, but shook his head with ominous frowns.
===Chinese===
像是特地应验他的话似的，甘杜尔的声音随即在外面街上喊了起来：“快点，哈姆迪！瞧，太阳已经老高了！你进来时给自己定作界线的那块石头，影子已经爬上去了！”

“你听见了，”哈姆迪抽着鼻子说，“他们是怎样追逼我的？他把我成亲的衣裳打成了一包——哦，我恨哪！客人都请过了——叫他们的爹都死绝才好！去找我母亲（她会听你的），求她许我缓上几个月。是纳伊玛通过她娘催紧了这门亲事。她要毁掉我新近到手的自由，好折磨我。”

巴拉卡忍不住笑出声来，嘴上还是说着宽慰的话。她说，纳伊玛是个十分标致的姑娘，虽说爱卖弄风情，心肠并不坏。他哪里肯听，只连连摇头，眉头拧出满脸不祥。
===Original===
“I hate her!” he declared. “And knowest thou? I have a mind to drown myself this morning at the bath.”

Then, as Ghandûr’s calls became insistent, he left the room with slow, reluctant steps.

The wedding was a small affair, the parties being children of one house, and their betrothal (which is legal marriage) having taken place in infancy. The bride, enthroned, showed none of the reluctance felt by Hamdi. A bright-eyed and determined little maiden, she was wreathed in smiles; and when Barakah inquired if she were truly happy, replied, “The praise to Allah!” with decision.
===Chinese===
“我恨她！”他宣称。“你可知道？我今天早上有心在浴场里把自己淹死算了。”

随后，甘杜尔越催越紧，他才拖着迟缓而不情愿的脚步离开了房间。

婚礼办得很简省，因为新人原是同一宅门的孩子，订亲（那在教法上即是成婚）早在襁褓中便已行过。新娘高踞上座，没有半点哈姆迪那样的不情愿。她是个眼睛明亮、极有主见的小姑娘，满面堆着笑；巴拉卡问她是否真心快活，她一口答道：“赞美归于安拉！”
===Original===
Next day the house was full of smothered laughter. Hamdi was completely changed. He and his bride were now the fondest pair. The lady Fitnah, who had always held that matrimony was a panacea for the crotchets of young people, male and female, rendered praise where praise was due. For many days, through shame, the bridegroom hid from Barakah, and from everyone else to whom he had proclaimed his dread of marriage.

When she told Gulbeyzah of the case as of a kind of miracle, the Circassian answered:

“I perceive no cause for wonder. The bridegroom had not thought of her before in that relation, had not truly known her﻿—that is all. Love is a blessing that brings gratitude as surely as the Nile makes plants to grow.”
===Chinese===
第二天，宅子里到处是憋着的窃笑。哈姆迪彻底变了样。他和新娘成了顶顶恩爱的一对。菲特娜夫人素来主张婚姻是治少男少女种种怪脾气的万灵药，这回功劳该归谁就归了谁。一连许多天，新郎臊得躲着巴拉卡，也躲着一切他曾向其宣扬过惧怕婚姻的人。

她把这桩事当作一桩奇事讲给古尔贝雅扎听，那切尔克斯女子答道：

“我看不出有什么可怪的。新郎从前不曾往那层关系上想过她，不曾真正认识过她——仅此而已。爱是一种恩赐，必然带来感戴，正如尼罗河必然使草木生长。”
===Original===
Gulbeyzah and Bedr-ul-Budûr﻿—nay, all her friends﻿—viewed love, apart from any individual man, as a material boon. Bred up to it and ripened for it cunningly, they were ready to adore the man who gave it, however unattractive from a European standpoint. This view of love, when realized, explained to Barakah the happiness which every girl of her acquaintance seemed to find in marriage, even where, as in Gulbeyzah’s case, the husband was a greybeard thrice her age. Those who possessed it were content and virtuous. In those who had it not, or were deprived of it, all amorous crime was reckoned pardonable.

Gulbeyzah and Bedr-ul-Budûr explained all this to Barakah in thrilling tones, as if they uttered truths divine.
===Chinese===
古尔贝雅扎和白德尔-布杜尔——不，她所有的朋友——都把爱本身看作一件实实在在的恩惠，与任何一个具体的男人无干。她们自幼为此受调教，又为此被巧妙地催熟，随时乐于崇拜那个把爱赐下来的人，无论以欧洲的眼光看他是多么乏味无吸引力。这套爱的观念一旦领会，便向巴拉卡解释了她相识的每个姑娘何以似乎都能在婚姻里找到幸福——哪怕像古尔贝雅扎那样，丈夫是个年齿三倍于己的老翁。拥有爱的人知足而贞静；没有爱或被夺了爱的人，则一切情罪皆可宽宥。

古尔贝雅扎和白德尔-布杜尔把这番道理用激动的声调讲给巴拉卡听，仿佛口宣的是神启的真理。
===Original===
