“Behold the wisdom of our Faith,” they said, “which grants to every woman this delight in secret. Women can never truly be the friends of men; their soul is different. If thrown with men for long, they feel fatigue. They ask of men one thing﻿—the gift of love. Here we consort with women, true companions, all day long; and in the night the bridegroom comes, and we are blest. Is not this better than the way of Europe, which sets at nought apparent truths﻿—as that most men love more than one of us, whereas most women need but love itself, the hope of children?”

That was one of the occasions when Barakah would have given anything to have an Englishwoman present, and to watch her face. Another came a few days later when she called upon Gulbeyzah. Alighting from her carriage at the palace door, she saw a baby’s coffin being carried out, and thought at once of turning home again. But already smiling eunuchs stood before her bidding welcome, beseeching her to deign to follow them to the haremlik. Gulbeyzah met her with a kiss on either cheek.
===Chinese===
“瞧瞧我们信仰中的智慧，”她们说，“它暗中赐给每个女人这份欢愉。女人永远做不成男人真正的朋友；她们的灵魂不同。与男人长久厮混，她们只觉得疲乏。她们向男人求的只有一样东西——爱的馈赠。在这里，我们同女人、同真正的同伴整日相伴；到了夜里新郎来了，我们便蒙受福泽。这岂不比欧洲的道路强？那路子把明摆着的道理视若无物——比如：大多数男人爱着的不止我们一个，而大多数女人所要的不过是爱本身，加上生儿育女的指望。”

这是巴拉卡情愿付出任何代价、也想有个英国女人在场看看她神色的时刻之一。几天后又碰上一回：她去拜访古尔贝雅扎，在府门前下车时，恰看见一口婴儿的小棺材正被抬出来，她当即就想掉头回家。可是满面堆笑的阉人已经迎上来道欢迎，恳请她赏光随他们进哈雷姆利克去。古尔贝雅扎迎上来，在她两颊上各亲了一吻。
===Original===
“Come, help us to console Nasîbah,” she exclaimed. “Her baby died this night. She is distracted.”

She drew her friend into a chamber where the childless mother lay, face downward, moaning, while the others tried to soothe her.

“It is no matter,” was the burden of their consolations. “It is not as if thou wert left altogether desolate. Are we not one, we four? Thou hast two children left, since ours are thine, and in a day or two Gulbeyzah will present thee with a third, inshallah!”
===Chinese===
“来，帮我们劝劝纳西芭（Nasîbah），”她叫道。“她的婴儿昨夜死了。她都痛得神魂失守了。”

她把朋友领进一间屋子，那位失了孩子的母亲正脸朝下躺着呻吟，其余的人在旁劝慰。

“这算不得什么，”她们劝慰的话翻来覆去总是这一句。“又不是把你撇得孤苦无依。我们四个不是一体的吗？你还有两个孩子呢——我们的就是你的——过一两天，古尔贝雅扎还要送你第三个，若安拉意欲！”
===Original===
“Inshallah!” cried Gulbeyzah. “And it shall be thine entirely. Directly it is born it shall be sent to thee to nurse. I will forget it. And when it is thy turn again, thou wilt repay me. Is not that a good idea?”

Oh that English people, who regard polygamy as something dreadful, could have witnessed that small scene! The wish, escaping Barakah at unawares, begot a heartache, as she realized that all she saw and heard for their instruction was thwarted of its natural vent forevermore.
===Chinese===
“若安拉意欲！”古尔贝雅扎叫道。“而且它整个儿归你。一落生就送到你怀里奶。我会忘掉它。往后再轮到你，你再还我。这主意不好么？”

唉，那些把多妻制看作洪水猛兽的英国人，若能亲眼见到这一幕就好了！这心愿从巴拉卡心头不防溜出，随即生出一阵心痛——她明白，自己为他们长见识而看到听到的一切，从此永远失去了天然的出口。
===Original===
She told herself that she was happy in this life; and so she was upon the surface, where she kept her thoughts, not daring to pry down into the depths. In the early days she had desired more knowledge of the Muslim faith, and a woman learned in religion had been hired to teach her. But the fury of that faith, the scathing nature of its truths, appalled her, awaking recollections of a creed more sentimental, with distressing doubts. She very soon gave up her lessons, closed the eyes of her intelligence, and resolutely sought her pleasure in the passing hour.

Still there were moments when vague fears oppressed her. When, in the third year of her marriage, she brought forth a stillborn child, frightful abysses seemed to yawn around her, and for days she was afflicted with a kind of nightmare of misgiving, derived from recollection of the “zâr” and other horrors.
===Chinese===
她对自己说，这样的日子她是快活的；就表面而论也确实如此——她把心思都留在表面上，不敢往下窥探深处。起初她曾想多了解些穆斯林信仰，宅里还雇了一位精通教门的妇人来做她的教师。可那信仰的烈焰、那些真道的灼人严酷吓住了她，唤起她对一种更缠绵的信条的记忆，伴着令人苦恼的疑虑。没过多久她便放弃了功课，闭上灵智的眼睛，打定主意只在眼前的时辰里寻欢取乐。

然而仍有些时刻，无名的恐惧压上她的心头。婚后第三年，她产下一个死婴，那时可怕的深渊仿佛在她四周张开大口，一连数日，她被一种疑惧的梦魇折磨着——那是从对“扎尔”仪式及种种别的恐怖的回忆里生出来的。
===Original===
The Eastern ladies were so calm and strong compared with her; they flinched at nothing except impropriety. The slaughter of a thousand sheep at Curban Bairam, turning the kitchen court into a shambles, caused them no disgust. It was ordained of God, they told her, and it fed the poor. They had no horror of disease or death or filthy persons, and, though most cleanly, looked on vermin philosophically. The Turks and the Circassians, with their grand ideals, appeared more dreadful than the Africans, whose faith was childlike. Barakah preferred the latter. Her pleasure was in feasts and little outings, in storytellers, dancers, and musicians who beguile the time; her only rapture was in adoration of her small Muhammad.

Her hidden yearnings and beliefs clung round the boy. She dwelt in longing for the days when he should be her friend. He was her hope, the product of both parts of her divided life; giving it sense and sequence, and, in the end perhaps, if Allah willed, consistency. She dreamt of a great future for him, to astonish Europe. But in the meanwhile, being sometimes dull, she felt the need of an intelligent, discreet companion.
===Chinese===
东方的夫人们与她相比是那样镇定、那样坚强；除了失礼之事，没有什么能使她们畏缩。宰牲节（Curban Bairam）上宰一千头羊，把厨房院子变成屠宰场，也不曾引得她们半点憎厌。她们告诉她，这是真主所定的，又能周济穷人。她们对疾病、死亡和腌臜的人毫无恐惧，自己虽然极其爱洁，看待虱虫之属却达观得很。突厥人和切尔克斯人抱着一套宏大的理想，在她看来反倒比非洲人更可怖，因为后者的信仰是孩子一般的。巴拉卡更喜欢后者。她的乐趣在宴饮和小小的出游，在说书的、跳舞的、奏乐的那些消磨光阴的人；她唯一的狂喜，在于疼爱她的小穆罕默德。

她深藏的渴望与信念，都萦系在这个男孩身上。她一心渴盼他长成她朋友的那一天。他是她的希望，是她被分成两半的生活两半共同的结晶，赋予这生活以意义和连贯，或许到头来——若安拉意欲——还能赋予它以一贯。她梦想着他有一个伟大的前程，好教欧洲吃惊。可在那之前，日子有时烦闷，她感到需要一个聪慧而口风紧的伴侣。

## XXII / 二十二
===Original===
On the recurrence of certain anniversaries, at the two Bairams and in the month of Ragab, all Muslim Cairo left the city of the living for the cities of the dead adjoining it upon the east and south. Mothers of sorrow like Murjânah Khânum, whose heart was with her children in the grave, inhabited the mausoleums for a week or more; but the majority performed a one-day visit.

Blue night alive with stars was at her lattice when Barakah was softly roused by her attendants and arrayed in proper garb. She found Leylah Khânum and her daughters waiting for her by the mabeyn screen, where the eunuch had a heap of roses and of henna-flowers to give them, as well as branches of palm and sweet basil. With these they made their way out to the carriage.

The principal streets were thronged with people going in the same direction: men in clean robes, who yawned, still half asleep; women, black-shrouded, bearing palm-branches, with trays of eatables upon their heads; small girls in tinselled gauze of divers colours, and boys in stiff new clothing﻿—all with earnest faces, pressing out towards the cemeteries. Barakah kept peeping through the shutter at the solemn crowd, to which the fitful gleam of swinging lanterns added weirdness. The concourse gave forth a dull clatter, above which was heard the rumble of the carriage wheels upon the stones, the shouts the coachman raised to clear a way. Then suddenly all noise of going ceased, although their wheels still rolled and the besetting throng was even denser than before. They were on sand. The people murmured like a shell. The desert hill rose imminent against the stars. On all sides spread a wilderness of humble graves, each with its family group encamped beside the headstone. Then came a steep incline, up which the horses struggled under whip and cursing; and lo! they were once more in city streets. On every hand rose shadowy buildings, domes, and minarets. A swarm of beggars went from door to door with sacks and trays collecting doles of food.
===Chinese===
每逢某些周年忌日，在两个拜兰节（Bairam）期间和拉贾卜月（Ragab）里，开罗全城的穆斯林便离开生者之城，前往城东城南与之毗连的各座亡者之城。像穆尔贾娜·哈努姆那样心随亡儿葬于坟茔的悲伤母亲，会在陵墓里住上一周或更久；但多数人只作一日之访。

蓝夜里星斗满天，还映在她的窗棂上，巴拉卡已被侍女们轻轻唤醒，穿上了合乎礼数的衣装。她见莱拉·哈努姆带着几个女儿已在马贝因屏旁等候，阉人那里备着一大堆玫瑰和指甲花，还有棕榈枝和甜罗勒，分赠给她们。众人拿着这些，一路出去登车。

一条条大街上挤满朝同一方向去的人：男人们穿着干净的长袍，打着呵欠，睡意还未消；女人们一身黑色罩衫，捧着棕榈枝，头顶顶着盛吃食的托盘；小姑娘们穿着各色闪金的轻纱，男孩们套着浆得硬挺的新衣——个个面孔郑重，向墓园方向挤去。巴拉卡不住地从窗板缝里窥看这肃穆的人群，摇荡的灯笼忽明忽暗地照着，更添几分诡秘。人丛发出一片沉闷的嘈杂，在这嘈杂之上，听得见车轮碾过石路的辘辘声，还有车夫喝令让路的呼喊。忽然间，赶路的一切声响都止息了，虽然车轮还在滚动，四围的人群甚至比先前更密。他们行到沙地上了。人群低语，嗡嗡如贝壳里的回响。沙漠的山丘黑压压地逼临星空。四下里铺开一片简陋坟茔的荒野，每座坟头的墓碑旁都扎着一家人。接着是一道陡坡，马在鞭笞和咒骂声中挣扎着爬上去；瞧啊！他们又回到了城里的街上。四面八方矗起影影绰绰的屋宇、穹顶和宣礼塔。一大群叫花子提着口袋、端着托盘，挨门挨户收集施舍的吃食。
===Original===
Alighting at the gate of a large mosque-like building, Barakah and her companions were conducted through a courtyard to the women’s quarters. Fitnah and Murjânah, who had spent the night there with attendants, made them welcome; after which they paid a visit to the mausoleum proper, or the women’s side of it﻿—for the house of death itself was subdivided by a harem screen. Here, in a gloom made spectral by the hanging lamps, women of repute for sanctity, hired mourners, were reciting the Koran, and through the screen some male professors could be heard performing the same office in strong nasal tones. The visitors bestowed their flowers and bits of palm among the graves, and, having said some prayers, returned to the apartment, where preparations for a feast were being made.

Already the muezzin’s chant announced the dawn. Murjânah Khânum was at her devotions on a corner of the dais. The other ladies, who deemed prayer the man’s affair, helped in the work of setting out the breakfast. While this was going on, a woman and three children rushed in from the twilight court, and with loud blessings began kissing hands.

“It is the wife and children of the guardian of this place, who makes the graves,” Na’imah, her nearest neighbour, informed Barakah. “They come for their accustomed gifts of food and raiment. See, Fitnah Khânum is just going to bestow them in the name of all of us.”
===Chinese===
巴拉卡和同伴们在一座清真寺模样的大建筑物门前下了车，被人引进一方庭院，来到女眷的住处。菲特娜和穆尔贾娜带着仆从已在那里过夜，此刻迎候来客；随后众人去正式拜谒陵墓——或者说陵墓的女眷一侧，因为死亡之居本身也被一道哈雷姆屏隔成两半。在这里，悬吊的油灯把幽暗照得鬼影幢幢，几位以圣洁闻名的妇人——雇来的哭丧人——正诵读《古兰经》；隔着屏子，还听得见几位男诵经师用响亮的鼻音做着同样的功课。来客们把鲜花和棕榈枝分放到座座坟间，祷告了几句，便回到房中，那里正在置办一场宴席。

宣礼员的吟唱已报晓天明。穆尔贾娜·哈努姆在矮台一角做她的礼拜。其余的夫人们认为祷告是男人的事，便帮着摆早餐。正忙间，一个女人带着三个孩子从曙光朦胧的院子里冲进来，高声念着祝福，挨个亲吻众人的手。

“这是本处看坟人的妻子儿女——坟就是他掘的，”挨她坐着的纳伊玛告诉巴拉卡。“他们是照例来领惯常的吃食和衣裳的。瞧，菲特娜·哈努姆这就代表我们大家把东西赏下去。”
===Original===
A minute later the gravedigger’s wife and children were at Barakah, kissing her hand repeatedly and crying, “May it be many a year ere we receive thee here, O queen of charms.”

The Englishwoman shivered at this form of compliment; and then a strange old woman, who had been observing her, sidled across the room and squatted at her feet.

“O Umm ed-Dahak, welcome!” exclaimed Na’imah. “Where hast thou been this long while, that we have not seen thee? There has been no fun at all in life without thee. How is thy health? What new jests dost thou bring us?”

But the old woman had not come, it seemed, to talk to Na’imah; for, replying to these questions in the briefest manner possible, she addressed herself to Barakah in coaxing whispers.
===Chinese===
过了一分钟，掘墓人的妻子和孩子到了巴拉卡跟前，把她的手亲了又亲，口中喊道：“愿许多许多年后才在这里接待你，哦千娇百媚的王后。”

这样的恭维让英国女人打了个寒噤；这时，一个一直在打量她的怪老太婆侧身横挪过屋子，蹲坐在她脚边。

“哦，乌姆·埃德-达哈克（Umm ed-Dahak），欢迎欢迎！”纳伊玛叫道。“这一向你上哪儿去了，叫我们见不着你？没有你，日子里一点儿趣味也没有。你身子可安好？给我们带来什么新鲜笑谈？”

可这老太婆看来并不是来找纳伊玛攀谈的；她把这些问题用最简短的方式敷衍过去，便凑向巴拉卡，用哄人的低语说起话来。
===Original===
“Art thou not happy, O my pearl? I could see from over there that something ailed thee. Is it the thought of death, the air of tombs? The spectacle of graves should rather cheer the living. Give praise to God that thou art still alive; enjoy existence! Allah is merciful! It is certain that He has made provision for our sex hereafter﻿—a finer paradise than that of men, inshallah! Ha, ha! What faces, thinkest thou, the men would wear if they knew that we had heavenly youths for our enjoyment, in our place apart? By Allah, it would spoil their pleasure in the black-eyed maids! I see them sulking even in the home of bliss.﻿ ﻿… The air is chill thus early; the end of night is always a sad hour. A delicate soft flower like thee is dashed by it. Come, let me talk to warm thee. I am called the Mother of Laughter, thou hast heard!﻿ ﻿…
===Chinese===
“你不快活么，哦我的珍珠儿？我打那边就看出你有些不自在。是想到了死，还是坟间的阴气？坟墓的景象原该叫活人宽心才是。感谢真主，你还活着；快享受人生吧！安拉是慈悲的！他必定为我们女人在身后预备了去处——一个比男人的乐园更美的乐园，若安拉意欲！哈，哈！你说说，男人们要是知道我们在自己的一方天地里有天堂美少年供我们受用，会摆出什么脸色？凭安拉起誓，那可要败了他们受用黑眸佳丽的兴致！我瞧得见他们连在极乐家园里都要怄气呢。……这时节这么早，空气还寒；夜的尽头总归是伤心的时辰。像你这样娇嫩柔软的花儿，要被它吹蔫的。来，让我说话给你暖暖。人家管我叫欢笑之母，你听说过吧！……”
===Original===
“Knowest thou what my daughter said in her soul when first her spouse unveiled her? She said (and be the saying far from thee), the while she stood with eyes downcast and bosom rising, falling, ‘May Allah strike me blind this minute if I am half so innocent as thou art, O my knowing lord!’ And she managed him, I can assure thee. Ah, she fooled him perfectly﻿—exclaiming ever at his wisdom, bowing to his lightest word. It is thus we subtle ones beguile the world﻿—the great strong simpleton!﻿—never opposing, lest he knock us down. By Allah, I must ask thy pardon for thus prattling; but ladies condescend to find my talk amusing. I can recount the origin of all that is, being most learned in religious matters. If I chose, I could be howling with those cats in there,”﻿—she nodded towards the hired performers in the tomb﻿—“but they are hypocrites and gloomy. I love merriment. It has long been my desire to meet a foreign lady, to whom I might impart my knowledge of this land. The Franks have great intelligence, and would admire my lore. All the stories of the harem I can teach thee.﻿ ﻿…
===Chinese===
“你可知道我女儿初嫁、丈夫头一回揭她面纱时，心里说的是什么？她当时（愿这话离你远远的）垂着眼站着，胸脯一起一伏，心里说：‘愿安拉这会儿就叫我瞎了眼，若我的清白抵得上你的一半，哦无所不知的夫君！’她把他拿捏得住，这我可以向你担保。啊，她把他哄得十成十——时时惊叹他的智慧，他随口一句话也要躬身领受。我们这些心眼儿细的人就是这样哄骗这世界——那又伟大又强壮的傻瓜！——永不违拗，省得他一拳把我们打翻。凭安拉起誓，我这样絮叨得向你告个罪；不过夫人们赏脸，总还觉得我的话有趣。我能讲述万物的由来，因为论教门学问我是顶有研究的。我要是愿意，本可以跟里头那些猫儿们一块儿嚎去，”——她朝陵里雇来的诵经人点点头——“可他们是些伪君子，灰扑扑的没个活气。我爱快活。我一直盼着能见一位外国太太，好把我对这片土地的见识讲给她听。法兰克人聪明得很，会佩服我的学问。哈雷姆里所有的故事，我都能教给你。……”
===Original===
“Thou knowest the three wives of Ali Bey El Halebi. The red-haired one﻿—the former slave﻿—was killed last night. I had it but an hour ago from a sure source. Her sin, though great, was pardonable, Allah knows. Her husband had neglected her disgracefully: the fact is known. She turned for comfort to a street musician. She lost her wit, it seems, and made confession. I could have saved her, with the help of Allah, had she come to me. The eunuchs held her so﻿—and, click! her neck was severed. Her corpse is floating down the Nile, dismembered, or buried in the garden﻿—Allah knows! Ah! I could keep thee interested for a year together.”
===Chinese===
“你可认识阿里·贝伊·埃尔-哈莱比（Ali Bey El Halebi）的那三房太太。红头发的那个——先前的女奴——昨夜被杀了。我一个钟头前才从可靠的来路听来的。她的罪过虽说重大，却是可赦的，安拉知道。她丈夫冷落她冷落得不成体统：这事人人皆知。她便去一个街头乐师那里寻安慰。她似乎是失了心窍，招认了。她若是来找我，凭安拉相助，我本可以救下她的。阉人们把她这么一按——咔嚓！她的脖子就断了。她的尸首这会儿正肢解着顺尼罗河往下漂呢，要么就埋在园子里——安拉知道！啊！我能叫你听得入迷，足足一整年不带重的。”
===Original===
The old creature’s flattery, more subtle in the tone and manner than the words convey, was irresistible; her twinkling eyes and ever-shifting wrinkles aroused the Englishwoman’s sense of humour, which had long been dormant.

“Praise be to Allah, thou art better!” smiled the crone.

The sun had risen now; the lamps were useless; the city of the dead was blushing like the rose; the chanting of the readers in the tomb had lost its sadness. Barakah was staring at the strange old woman’s face, now plainly visible. Where had she known it? Feature for feature, it resembled one which had been long imprinted in her memory. Umm ed-Dahak grinned when she became aware of this perplexity. With a very roguish look for one so old, she laid her cheek upon her open palm and whispered, “Yûsuf! Come!” It was the same old creature who, luring the future bride of Yûsuf from her chamber in Muhammad Pasha’s house, had been seized and beaten by the eunuchs in the hall, and never seen again until this day.
===Chinese===
这老货的奉承，其声口举止间的微妙远非字面所能传达，叫人无法抗拒；她闪烁的眼睛和不停挪动的皱纹，唤醒了英国女人久已蛰伏的幽默感。

“赞美归于安拉，你好多啦！”老婆子笑道。

太阳此刻已经升起；灯盏全然无用；亡者之城红得像一朵玫瑰；坟中诵经人的吟唱也失了悲伤。巴拉卡盯着这怪老太婆如今清清楚楚显露的脸。她在哪里见过这张脸？一眉一眼，都像一张久已印在她记忆里的面孔。乌姆·埃德-达哈克察觉了她的困惑，咧嘴一笑。以如此高龄而论，她那神情活脱是个促狭鬼；她把脸颊搁在摊开的手掌上，低声道：“优素福！来吧！”正是同一个老家伙，当年把优素福的未婚妻从穆罕默德·帕夏宅中的闺房里诱出来，在厅堂上被阉人捉住痛打，从此再没人见过，直到今天。
===Original===
“Rememberest thou?” she slyly asked. “Allah witness, I was tempted with a bribe. Young men are devils! Never ask me to explain. I cannot bear to be reminded of it, may Our Lord forgive me! We are all weak creatures and succumb occasionally; but Fitnah Khânum will assure thee I am to be trusted.”

With that and a most friendly smile, she edged away, repairing straight to Fitnah Khânum, with whom she held some animated conversation in low tones. The lady, at her instance, shortly came across to Barakah and whispered:

“That old woman seeks thy patronage. I myself have found her useful and obliging. To thee, a foreigner, she could afford much help. Thou needest someone. Umm ed-Dahak is the best I know.”

“Umm ed-Dahak!” cried out Na’imah. “Why, there is no creature in the world to match her for facetiousness. She was the rapture of our life as children. Nobody could be dull or sad with Umm ed-Dahak. She is like a monkey and a clever servant and a mother all in one!”
===Chinese===
“想起来了么？”她狡黠地问。“安拉作证，我是受了贿赂的引诱。年轻男人都是魔鬼！千万别叫我解释。我受不了人提起这事，愿我们的主饶恕我！我们都是软弱的受造之物，偶尔要栽跟头；不过菲特娜·哈努姆会向你保证，我是靠得住的。”

说完，她带着顶顶和气的笑侧身挪开，径直去到菲特娜·哈努姆跟前，同她低声热切地谈了一阵。那位夫人被她说动，不多时便走到巴拉卡这边来，悄声道：

“那老太婆想求你的庇护。我自己就发现她有用、殷勤。对你这样一个外邦人，她帮得上大忙。你需要有个人。乌姆·埃德-达哈克是我知道的当中最好的。”

“乌姆·埃德-达哈克！”纳伊玛扬声叫道。“哎呀，论滑稽逗趣，世上再没有谁比得上她。我们小时候，她就是我们日子里最大的欢喜。有乌姆·埃德-达哈克在，谁也闷不起来、愁不起来。她又像猴子，又像机灵的仆人，又像亲娘，三样合在一个人身上！”
===Original===
This joyful cry was overheard by Leylah Khânum, who frowned upon her daughter and rebuked her sharply. In that place conversation must be held in whispers and only ritual words pronounced aloud. The party breakfasted in solemn silence, to the sound of chanting from the tomb. But the aged Mother of Laughter smiled and nodded﻿—even winked﻿—at Barakah whenever their eyes met, which was not seldom; and the Englishwoman had a new sensation of relief and sympathy. At last she had found somebody who understood her.
===Chinese===
这欢快的叫声被莱拉·哈努姆听见了，她对女儿大皱其眉，狠狠训斥了一顿。在那种地方，交谈必须用耳语，只有礼仪经文才容许出声。一行人伴着坟中传来的吟诵，肃穆无言地用了早餐。可那位年迈的欢笑之母，每逢与巴拉卡目光相接——这可并不稀罕——总对她微笑点头，甚至还挤挤眼；英国女人心头泛起一种新的宽慰与亲近之感。她终于找到了一个能理解她的人。

## XXIII / 二十三
===Original===
Upon the morrow Barakah had quite forgotten the old woman; she was lounging on a sofa, smoking after breakfast, watching the slave-girls dress Muhammad, when Umm ed-Dahak stole in barefoot, making reverence. The crone sank down before her as of right, and kissing her feet, asked how she did and praised her loveliness. Then, looking at the infant, she exclaimed in natural tones, “Mashallah! May Our Lord preserve him in all times and places!” and straightway began making baby noises.

Barakah thought the moment opportune for getting at the secret of that incident which teased her memory. But Umm ed-Dahak, though she answered volubly, made no disclosure. Indeed, as Barakah soon learnt, that seeming reckless chatterer was in the habit of imparting only what she chose to tell.

It was manifest that half her compliments were insincere, nor did she take the slightest trouble to disguise the fact; but in the intervals of soporific fiction and pure blandishment she spoke of things worth knowing in a tone of frank goodwill. She knew the why and how of every custom, the stories giving rise to every proverb, and was so acute a judge of human character that her gossip had the flavour of an intellectual game. Her wiles, it seemed, were worn to show her cleverness, or cynically, to travesty arts which flourish in this transitory world.
===Chinese===
次日，巴拉卡已把那老太婆忘了个干净；她正歪在一张沙发上，早餐过后抽着烟，看女奴们给小穆罕默德穿衣，乌姆·埃德-达哈克赤着脚蹑手蹑脚地溜了进来，行了个礼。老婆子仿佛享有当然的权利，在她面前坐下来，吻了吻她的脚，问她的安，又称颂她的美貌。随后她望着那婴孩，用自然而然的声音叹道：“马沙安拉！愿我们的主无论何时何地都保佑他！”接着便咿咿呀呀地逗起孩子来。

巴拉卡觉得这正是探问那件搅扰她记忆的往事底细的好时机。可是乌姆·埃德-达哈克虽然回答得滔滔不绝，却不吐露半点实情。确实，巴拉卡很快就明白过来，这个看似口没遮拦的饶舌婆有个习惯：只说她自己愿意说的话。

她的恭维话显然有一半言不由衷，而她也丝毫不费心思去掩饰这一点；但在那些催眠的虚构和纯粹的奉承之间，她会用坦率善意的口吻讲些值得一听的事情。她知道每一种风俗的来龙去脉、每一条谚语背后的故事，看人又看得分外透彻，所以她的闲谈带着一种斗智游戏的滋味。她的那些花招，看来是为了显摆自己的聪明，或者出于冷嘲，把这套在浮世上大行其道的伎俩戏谑一番。
===Original===
She became a member of the household, but with privileges. Barakah was the sultan, she the grand vizier, it was agreed. No monarch ever had a more delightful minister. She made the slave-girls more attentive to their mistress, whose comfort she increased in a variety of ways. She knew where to lay her hands upon the leading storytellers and musicians, and was herself the most accomplished female mountebank at that time living. She soon learnt every mood of her protectress, and its antidote. The latest scandal dwelt at her tongue’s tip.

The whole harem knew Umm ed-Dahak as a joker. Slaves from outside were always coining pretexts to enter the apartment, just to look at her; and the more frivolous among the ladies came to hear her stories.

“I am for them a comical performance, not a child of Adam,” she told Barakah. “How different from thy kindness, O my sovereign lady! Thy gracious condescension feeds and clothes me.”
===Chinese===
她成了宅中的一员，却带着特权。大家议定：巴拉卡是苏丹，她是首相。从来没有哪位君主有过比这更叫人愉快的臣子。她叫女奴们侍候主母更加尽心，又想出种种法子增添主母的舒适。她知道上哪儿去找头牌的说书人和乐师，她自己则是当世最出色的滑稽女艺人。她很快摸透了庇护人的每一种心境，以及各色心境的解药。最新的风流丑闻总是挂在她舌尖上。

整个哈雷姆都知道乌姆·埃德-达哈克是个逗趣的角色。外院的女奴们总能编出种种借口进这屋里来，只为看她一眼；夫人小姐们中那些更轻浮的，也来听她讲故事。

“在她们眼里，我是一场滑稽把戏，算不得亚当的儿女，”她告诉巴拉卡。“你的恩慈多么不同啊，哦我的女君！你垂青眷顾，养活了我，给我衣穿。”
===Original===
Therewith she kissed the hand of Barakah, who was affected. By such small means did she confirm her sway.

Her intelligence, her laughing view of life, were stimulating, and prevented Barakah from brooding upon hopeless problems.

Without attempting to fatigue her mind in vain attempts to grasp the universe﻿—as Europeans do, inviting pessimism﻿—this old woman took her portion as it came, with relish and a very searching scrutiny. She likened herself sometimes to a fisher of the Nile, who all his life frequents one reach alone. He knows the currents and the mud-banks, marks the winds, and, without preoccupation with the river’s source or outlet, is cunning in the art of bringing fish to land. The soul of her philosophy was nonresistance; her morality held all means lawful to escape oppression.
===Chinese===
说着她吻了吻巴拉卡的手，巴拉卡心中感动。她就是靠这些小小的手段巩固自己的权势。

她的机敏，她那笑看人生的处世之道，都令人振奋，使巴拉卡不再苦苦纠缠那些无望的难题。

这个老太婆从不绞尽脑汁、妄图一把抓住整个宇宙——像欧洲人那样，结果招来悲观主义——而是来什么份便领受什么份，津津有味，又加以极精明的审视。她有时把自己比作尼罗河上的渔夫，一辈子只出没于同一段河面。他熟悉水流和泥岸，观测风向，不操心河的源头或出口，却精于把鱼弄上岸的手艺。她哲学的精髓是不抵抗；她的道德观认定，为摆脱压迫，一切手段皆属正当。
===Original===
“God is gracious and all-knowing,” she would shrug. “He gives to all His creatures, great and small, the wherewithal to move in their appointed element﻿—to birds wings, fins to fishes, guile to women.”

In her time, she admitted, many sins had soiled her hands; shameful employments had defiled her countenance. They would be pardoned, being but a means to live. She held, against the world’s opinion, that Allah is indulgent to the faults of women and even has a secret fondness for them. Yet, with her guile, she had an admiration for pure virtue, a teardrop for true love, wherever found. And with all her common sense and her acuteness she was superstitious. As the fisher of the Nile, her chosen image, wears an amulet and names the name of power before he casts his net, so Umm ed-Dahak armed herself against malignant influences. Her belief in witchcraft, philtres, and all kinds of charms was quite beyond the reach of argument.

The old woman never asked for any wages. She took what food she wanted, helped herself to cigarettes, and called for a narghile when the fancy seized her. By the Pasha’s order, in accordance with a pious custom observed at that time in good Muslim houses, eatables, such as meat and milk and vegetables which might go bad, were not kept overnight, the remainder of each day’s provision being given in the evening to the poor dependants. Of this dole Umm ed-Dahak claimed her share. If she required a garment or a gift of money, she did not beg for it, but told some tortuous and lengthy story which ended in a present as snakes end in tails. When Barakah saw through the artifice, she was in no way disconcerted. She merely smiled and praised her quick intelligence.
===Chinese===
“真主是仁慈的、全知的，”她会耸耸肩说。“他给他的一切造物，无论大小，都配了在各自天地里走动的本领——给鸟翅膀，给鱼鳍，给女人心机。”

她承认，她这一辈子，许多罪孽玷污过她的双手，若干见不得人的营生糟蹋过她的脸面。这些都会得到赦免，因为不过是谋生的手段罢了。她与世人所见相悖，认定安拉宽宥女人的过失，甚至暗暗地偏爱她们。然而，工于心机的她，对纯粹的德行怀有敬意，对真正的爱情洒得下眼泪，无论它们在哪里出现。而尽管她通达事理、目光锐利，她却迷信。正如她自选的化身——尼罗河渔夫——撒网之前要戴上护符、念诵大能者的名号，乌姆·埃德-达哈克也这样武装自己，抵御邪祟的影响。她对巫术、媚药和种种符咒的信念，任凭什么道理也撼不动。

老太婆从不讨要任何工钱。想吃的东西她自己拿，香烟随手取用，兴头上来还叫人给她端水烟筒。按帕夏的吩咐，遵照当时良善穆斯林人家恪守的敬虔习俗，肉、奶、蔬菜这类可能变质的吃食一律不过夜，每天的剩余到晚间都分给穷苦的依附人等。这份施舍里，乌姆·埃德-达哈克也要认领自己的一份。她若需要一件衣裳或一笔赏钱，从不开口讨，而是讲一个转弯抹角、冗长拖沓的故事，故事的结尾是一份礼物，就像蛇的末梢是一条尾巴。巴拉卡看穿了这套把戏，也丝毫不觉窘迫，只是微微一笑，夸她机灵。
===Original===
“Her need is real, for she is poor,” said Fitnah Khânum, when Barakah remarked on the old woman’s foibles. “But she loves subtlety far more than comfort, and would refuse high monthly wages, to obtain a lesser sum by stealth and coaxing, as occasion offered. She has had much money given to her, to my knowledge; but it is as dust to her. She is like the clever fellow in the story, who, having earned much money by his ingenuity, scrambled it among the crowd; and in the end, when it was finished, sighed, ‘O Allah, would that I had all the gold on earth to go on flinging it and see men fight like dogs for its possession!’ ”

Fitnah, though she scolded the old woman, had a liking for her company and waggish talk. And Umm ed-Dahak, being very diplomatic, paid her court. Indeed, she flattered all the ladies of the house with the assurance that she wished to be the spokesman of their will with Barakah, and went to them for orders every day.

The only person whom she feared was Yûsuf Bey, though she had known him from a child. At the first hint of his approach she fled the house. In vain did Barakah assure her he had no objection to her presence﻿—nay, had said more than once that he would like to see her. The old creature smiled and wriggled, “May our Lord preserve him!” but fled no less. It all came of her desire for surreptitiousness. She would not have felt well in a harem of which the lord approved of her.
===Chinese===
“她确实穷，缺钱是真的，”巴拉卡说起老太婆的这些小毛病，菲特娜·哈努姆道，“可她爱使心眼儿远胜过爱安逸，宁可回绝高额的月薪，也要瞅着机会，连哄带悄悄地弄来数目更小的进项。据我所知，她得过许多钱财；可钱到她手里就像尘土。她就像故事里那个聪明人，凭机灵挣下大钱，却把它撒向人群任人哄抢；末了钱散尽了，才叹道：‘哦安拉，但愿我有全世界的金子，好由着我一直撒下去，看人们像狗一样为争夺它撕咬！’”

菲特娜虽然数落这老太婆，却喜欢有她作伴，喜欢她那插科打诨的谈吐。乌姆·埃德-达哈克极有手腕，也向她献殷勤。的确，她讨好宅中所有夫人，信誓旦旦地说自己愿做她们在巴拉卡跟前的代言人，每天到她们那里去请示。

她唯一怕的人是优素福·贝伊，尽管她打他还是个孩子时就认得他。一有他来的风声，她就逃出宅去。巴拉卡向她保证，他并不嫌弃她在跟前——不，他还不止一次说过想见她呢——也是枉然。老东西笑着扭捏道：“愿我们的主保佑他！”照旧逃之夭夭。这全是她那鬼祟的癖性使然。在一家之主赞许的哈雷姆里，她反倒会浑身不自在。
===Original===
Contentment grew in Barakah from day to day, and as the months wore on she lost the wish to go abroad. The young Muhammad could now run about, although he sometimes tumbled and set up a howl. He had been taught to testify to his religion in a piping voice and screamed at visitors, “There is no God but God. Muhammad is the apostle of God”; for which they blessed him. He had also learned to curse the infidels ferociously. A turbulent and wilful child, his mother and old Umm ed-Dahak thought him perfect. They never tired of watching him torment the slave-girls. “Mashallah!” the Mother of Laughter would croak rapturously. “A blusterer, by the Most High! A boy with all the signs of manhood on him! Inshallah, he will live to bully grownup men!”

Occasionally Barakah paid visits as in duty bound; but she much preferred to stay indoors, to smoke and dream and talk with Umm ed-Dahak. Her husband, by his father’s influence, obtained a post of some importance, necessitating their removal shortly to a proper house, with a selamlik of its own where he could see his courtiers. Barakah looked forward to the change with high indifference, though Umm ed-Dahak strove to waken her enthusiasm, crying:

“Thou wilt now have eunuchs and a carriage of thy very own. Inshallah, Yûsuf Bey will go on rising till thy pomp excels the dignity of mighty queens.”
===Chinese===
巴拉卡心中的满足与日俱增，几个月过去，她连出门的兴致也没了。小穆罕默德如今能满地跑了，虽然有时跌个跟头，便嚎啕大哭。有人教过他作信仰的表白，他便尖着嗓子朝客人们喊：“除真主外，别无神灵。穆罕默德是真主的使者。”客人们因此祝福他。他还学会了凶狠地咒骂异教徒。这孩子蛮横任性，他母亲和老乌姆·埃德-达哈克却觉得他十全十美。她们看他作弄女奴，永远看不厌。“马沙安拉！”欢笑之母会心醉神迷地哑着嗓子喊。“好一个咋咋呼呼的小霸王，凭至高主起誓！好一个浑身带满男子汉印记的娃儿！若安拉意欲，他将来必能长大去欺负成年男人！”

巴拉卡偶尔也照礼数出门拜访；但她更喜欢待在家里，抽烟、做梦、同乌姆·埃德-达哈克闲谈。她丈夫靠父亲的影响力，谋得了一个颇为重要的职位，不久他们就得搬进一所像样的宅子，宅里有自家的塞拉姆利克，他可以在那里接见他的门客。巴拉卡对这一变动抱着高度的淡漠，乌姆·埃德-达哈克却竭力想唤起她的热望，喊道：

“你就要有自己专属的阉人和马车了。若安拉意欲，优素福·贝伊会步步高升，直到你的煊赫气派盖过赫赫王后们的尊荣。”
===Original===
Her life could hardly be more easy, she considered; she was quite content. The Pasha’s ladies would be grieved to lose her, and she would feel quite lost apart from them. She thought they all respected and admired her.

It was therefore a great shock to her when one afternoon Murjânah Khânum sent for her and read her a kind lecture on her way of life.

“My pearl,” she said, “I am the head of this harem and in some sort responsible for all its members. I do not see a slave degenerating without endeavouring to stop the process by a word of warning. How much greater is my duty towards a near relation! My flower, thou art an Englishwoman and we Turks of Europe and of Asia welcomed thee to El Islam as our own sister. We looked to thee for force of character, for the light of education, for refinement. What has happened, on the contrary? Thou shunnest us for boon companions, persons of the country, who, however estimable, are inferior. Amînah Khânum yesterday complained that thou art growing a fellâhah both in speech and conduct. I do not hold with her, I only tell thee what she said﻿—a thing I cannot bear to hear of my dear daughter. My child, I speak in tenderness. Give thought to higher things﻿—our holy Faith, the dignity of life﻿—and spend not all thy time in mere frivolity. Keep that old woman in her place; I say not shun her, since she is amusing. Frequent good houses, study holy books. To spend one’s whole life in the hot room of the bath is not existence.”
===Chinese===
她的日子简直不能再安逸了，她想；她十分知足。帕夏的夫人们若失去她会伤心的，离开了她们她也会茫然若失。她以为她们个个敬重她、爱慕她。

因此，一天下午，穆尔贾娜·哈努姆打发人叫她去，就她的生活方式对她温和地训诫了一番，这让她大受震动。

“我的珍珠儿，”她说，“我是这哈雷姆的主母，对各人多少都担着责任。我见不得一个奴才堕落，总要设法用一句警语拦住这堕势。对自家的近亲，我的责任又该重多少啊！我的花儿，你是个英国女人，我们欧亚两洲的土耳其人迎你入了伊斯兰，把你当作自家的妹妹。我们指望你有坚毅的品性，指望你带来教育的光亮、文雅的教养。可结果怎样呢？你反倒躲避我们，去跟些寻欢作乐的伙伴、本地人厮混；他们再可敬，也终究低人一等。阿米娜·哈努姆昨天抱怨说，你的言谈举止越来越像个费拉农妇（fellâhah，埃及农妇）了。我不赞成她的话，只把她说的转告你——这种话，叫我听见说我亲爱的女儿，我可受不了。我的孩子，我说这话是出于疼爱。你要多想想更高的东西——我们神圣的信仰，生命的尊严——别把光阴全耗在轻浮取乐上。把那老太婆摆在她该在的位置上；我不是叫你躲开她，她到底逗趣。要去正经人家走动，研读圣典。一辈子泡在澡堂的热气房里，那不叫生活。”
===Original===
Barakah was deeply hurt. To have her harmless pleasures so severely criticized was as cruel as to see a flower destroyed by hail. She could not take the lofty standpoint of the Turkish lady. Had she done so, viewing life in all its horror, she would have gone mad. How could she bear to look upon herself, the renegade? She was now glad that she was soon to leave that hateful house.

When she told Umm ed-Dahak of her grief, expecting sympathy, the latter smiled and said:

“The right is with her. We must not neglect the things divine. I will myself instruct thee in them, having some small learning. Inshallah, I will teach thee to endure those thoughts which now appal thee.”

Instruction of that kind was needed two days later, when Barakah was driven to her new abode. As she alighted from her carriage at the door, some men in waiting cut the throat of a live buffalo by way of compliment. Blood spurted in her path across the threshold.
===Chinese===
巴拉卡深深受了伤害。她那些无伤大雅的乐趣遭到如此严厉的指摘，就像眼看一朵花被冰雹砸毁一样残忍。她没法站到那土耳其贵妇的崇高立场上去。倘若她站了上去，把人生的一切可怖尽收眼底，她非发疯不可。她又怎能忍受直视自己——这个叛教的人呢？如今她倒庆幸，不久就要离开那可憎的宅子了。

她把自己的伤心事讲给乌姆·埃德-达哈克听，指望得到些同情，后者却笑着说：

“她说得对。神圣的事我们不可疏忽。我自己多少有点学问，可以亲自教导你。若安拉意欲，我会教会你忍受那些如今叫你胆寒的念头。”

两天之后，这种教导果然派上了用场：巴拉卡被载往她的新居。她在门口下车时，几个侍立的男人宰了一头活水牛，聊表敬意。鲜血喷溅在她跨过门槛的去路上。

## XXIV / 二十四
===Original===
“Blood,” explained Umm ed-Dahak, “is but the juice of living creatures. Had they crushed a fruit before thee, would thy Grace have shrunk or fainted? Those servants sacrificed a thing of value in thy name and scattered blood upon the threshold to bring thee good luck. The flesh of the victim was distributed among the needy, as an almsdeed to the credit of the house of Yûsuf Bey. There are those among the learned who declare such practices to be against religion. Allah knows! Blood is the life of creatures, and a precious offering; and our traditions say that it is wise to shed it upon great occasions. Do but apply thy mind, and thou shalt learn to view such sacrifices with a sort of pleasure. It is true, by Allah! There is a thrill peculiar to the sight of blood.”

To this and many kindred exhortations Barakah replied with shudders. She was downright ill. At last, perceiving her repugnance to be quite invincible, the old woman resigned that branch of her instruction to the Most High, and once more proffered only what she knew would please her. Observing, also, her disgust at the sight of blind, diseased, or crippled persons, numbers of whom frequented the harem in quest of alms, she prevented such from entering her presence.

To gain some credit with Murjânah Khânum, Umm ed-Dahak went and told her, “My sweet lady is too frail. The weakness of the infidels still clings to her. She cannot put her trust in God as we do, but is harassed by the thought of pain and illness. I have tried in vain to win her to a better mind.”
===Chinese===
“血呀，”乌姆·埃德-达哈克解释道，“不过是活物的汁水罢了。倘若他们当着你的面榨碎一只果子，夫人您难道会畏缩、会晕倒不成？那些仆人是以您的名义牺牲了一件值钱的东西，把血洒在门槛上，好给您带来好运。祭牲的肉分给了穷苦人，算一桩善举，记在优素福·贝伊门下的功德上。学者中也有几位宣布，这类习俗是违背教门的。安拉才知道！血是生灵的性命，是贵重的祭献；我们的传统说，逢着大场面，洒上一些才是明智。你只管用心去体会，便能学会带着几分愉悦来看这类献祭了。真的，凭安拉！见血自有一种别处寻不着的战栗。”

对这一番以及许多同类的劝诫，巴拉卡的回答只是阵阵发抖。她简直病了一场。老太婆终于看出她的嫌恶全然无法战胜，便把她训导中的这一门交托给至高主，重新只讲那些她知道必能讨她欢心的话。她还察觉，巴拉卡一见瞎眼的、害病的、残废的人便觉恶心——这类人为着求施舍，成群地出入哈雷姆——便拦住这样的人，不放他们到她跟前。

为在穆尔贾娜·哈努姆跟前讨些好，乌姆·埃德-达哈克跑去对她说：“我那位娇滴滴的主子身子太单薄。异教徒的软弱还黏在她身上。她不能像我们这样把信托付给真主，一想到疼痛疾病便忧烦不休。我想劝她把心境放开阔些，只是白费了力气。”
===Original===
“Leave that to Allah!” was the saint’s reply. “All that I ask of her is to frequent her equals, and not seclude herself in low frivolity.”

“To hear is to obey,” bowed Umm ed-Dahak.

She forthwith set to work to school her mistress in all the courtesies expected of a noble lady. She coached her for her visits, teaching her the names of all the male relations, after whom it was the custom to inquire although she could not know them, together with the private history of each lady of the house.

With such a commentator at her elbow, Barakah found amusement in her social duties. Amînah Khânum was as kind to her as ever, but made no secret of her disapproval of the life she led.
===Chinese===
“那交给安拉吧！”那位圣洁的夫人答道。“我对她别无所求，只求她同与她身份相当的人往来，别把自己关在低贱的轻浮里。”

“听命即顺从。”乌姆·埃德-达哈克躬身应道。

她随即着手调教自家主母，把人们对一位贵妇期望的种种礼数一一教给她。她为她的拜会预先指点，教她记熟男方所有男亲眷的名讳——照习俗她须一一问起这些人，尽管她根本不可能认识他们——又把宅中每位夫人的私史一一讲给她听。

有这样一个评讲的人守在肘边，巴拉卡在应酬的本分里也寻出了乐趣。阿米娜·哈努姆待她一如既往地和善，却对她过的日子不以为然，而且毫不掩饰。
===Original===
“I know,” she said, “that thou must feel bewildered sometimes. Our life here is so different from that of Europe. It is natural for one who has left much behind to seek forgetfulness in little pleasures. But why with vulgar natives of the country? Why not with us, who are more civilized and have a nobler view of El Islam? Thou art not the only European to be found among us. I have asked some others here to meet thee, and rid thee of the sense of loneliness, which must be dreadful.”

She had in truth collected half a dozen other European women who had married Muslims and assumed the veil. But Barakah, instead of being pleased to meet them, seemed annoyed. They came from Italy and Southern Austria. To be ranked with them aroused her English pride. When Amînah Khânum asked why she disdained them, she replied that they were women of the lowest class and doubtful character.

“It is unlawful to say that,” the princess scolded. “Such scorn is not permitted here among us. A woman is invested with her husband’s honour. It is a sin to cast up what she did before her marriage. Thy boast is simply thou wast better guarded. Praise God for that, but do not scorn those others!”

Barakah loved them none the more for this rebuke.
===Chinese===
“我知道，”她说，“你有时必定觉得无所适从。我们这里的生活同欧洲太不一样了。一个抛下了许多旧事的人，在小乐趣里寻求忘却，原是人之常情。可为什么偏同本地的粗俗土人混呢？为什么不与我们往来？我们更文明，对伊斯兰教的见识也更高贵。在我们中间，你并不是唯一的欧洲人。我请了几位别的来同你会面，好替你解解那份想必可怕得很的孤寂。”

她果真召集了六位嫁了穆斯林、戴上面纱的欧洲女人。可巴拉卡同她们相见，非但不喜，反倒像是着了恼。她们来自意大利和奥地利南部。把她们与她相提并论，激起了她英国人的自尊。阿米娜·哈努姆问她为何鄙薄她们，她回答说，她们是些出身最下等、品行可疑的女人。

“这样的话是不合教法的，”公主斥责道。“这样的鄙薄，在我们这里不容许。女人的一身承载着丈夫的荣誉。翻她婚前所为的旧账，是一桩罪过。你夸口的，无非是你从前被人看管得更好。为这个赞美真主吧，可别瞧不起旁人！”

挨了这顿训斥，巴拉卡也没有因此多爱她们几分。
===Original===
In her new dwelling she had three reception-rooms. The gilt salon was kept for very ceremonious visitors. Her intimates were welcomed in a large apartment with cushioned dais and divans round the wall, where she herself was wont to sit with Umm ed-Dahak, though sometimes they would camp upon the housetop under sunshades.

All kinds of suitors came to the selamlik to see Yûsuf; and most of these brought presents, some of which were left at the haremlik entrance to bespeak the intercession of the lady. Ghandûr was made the steward of the house; he and his wife, who still attended on Muhammad, inhabiting a room close by. Barakah was glad to hear his voice again. As a relative by milk, he was allowed sometimes to kiss her hand and raise his chant of honour in her presence.

The winter following her change of residence Barakah was once more brought to bed. The whole household had been praying for another boy; Muhammad had been taught to lisp, “A boy, inshallah!” every time he saw his mother. Umm ed-Dahak had desired her mistress might produce boys only, because, she said, some of the brood were sure to die, and were all boys there was less likelihood of being left with girls alone, like Leylah Khânum. But a girl it proved to be. Muhammad shook his little fist at the intruder, shouting, “Daughter of a dog, who bade thee enter?” There was little joy at her reception in the world, and that little raised to cheer the mother’s spirits.
===Chinese===
新居里她有三间会客的屋子。镀金的客厅留着接待极讲客套的贵客。亲近的女客则在一间大屋里款待，屋里沿墙设着长榻，正中铺着软垫的矮台，她自己惯常同乌姆·埃德-达哈克坐在那里，不过有时她们也干脆在屋顶的遮阳棚下安营闲坐。

形形色色有所请托的人都到塞拉姆利克来见优素福；其中大多带着礼物，有些礼物便留在哈雷姆利克门口，以求这位夫人在男主人跟前代为进言。甘杜尔当上了宅子的管家；他同他妻子——她仍旧侍候着小穆罕默德——住着近旁的一间屋。巴拉卡很高兴又听见他的嗓音。凭着乳汁结下的亲缘，他偶尔获准吻一吻她的手，当着她的面唱起他颂德的歌子。

迁居后的那个冬天，巴拉卡再度临盆。全家上下一直祈求再添一个男孩；小穆罕默德被人教会了，每回见着母亲，就咿咿呀呀地念叨：“要个男孩，若安拉意欲！”乌姆·埃德-达哈克原本巴望自家主母只养男孩，因为她说，一窝儿女里总归有几个保不住，倘若全是男孩，便不大容易落到只剩一群女儿的地步，像莱拉·哈努姆那样。然而生下来偏偏是个女孩。小穆罕默德朝这闯进来的小东西挥着小拳头，嚷道：“狗女儿，谁叫你进来的？”她来到人世，众人没多少欢喜；那仅有的一点喜气，也是为了宽慰母亲的心才提起来的。
===Original===
“It is no matter,” chuckled Umm ed-Dahak, whose optimism triumphed over every obstacle.

“A girl comes not amiss; she has her uses. Since some are bound to die in early childhood, it is as well in every family to have a few who can be spared. And Yûsuf Bey will thank thee for this gift. The fathers always like to have a girl or two.”

“Why should some die? Inshallah, both of mine will be preserved!” wailed Barakah.
===Chinese===
“不打紧，”乌姆·埃德-达哈克咯咯笑道，她的乐观总能战胜一切障碍。

“生个女孩也不碍事；女孩自有女孩的用处。既然有些孩子注定幼年夭折，家家都不妨有几个损失得起的。再说，优素福·贝伊还要为这件礼物谢你呢。做父亲的，向来喜欢有个把女儿。”

“为什么就该死掉一些？若安拉意欲，我的两个都会平安无事！”巴拉卡哀声哭道。
===Original===
“Inshallah! Yet if all the children born were to survive, there soon would not be room to move in our great houses. For example, take the palace of our lord the Pasha, thy good father. Let me see!” She sat in thought and counted on her fingers: “Murjânah Khânum bore him twenty at the least﻿—all dead; Fitnah Khânum more than that﻿—say thirty﻿—of whom six alive. The mother of Ali﻿—she that was a slave﻿—ten at the least, three living. Then there was another concubine﻿ ﻿…”

“Stop, stop! It is not true! It cannot be,” cried Barakah, with a hysteric laugh.

But Umm ed-Dahak answered, “True, wallahi. What dismays thee? A woman’s task is to produce. We leave the rest to Allah.”

And to console her hearer she went on to tell of broods of thirty, even forty, reared successfully; when Barakah’s dismay was turned to laughter.
===Chinese===
“若安拉意欲！可要是生下的孩子个个都活，咱们这些大宅子很快就要挤得没有转身的空儿了。就拿咱们主上帕夏——你的好公公——的宅邸来说吧。让我算算！”她坐着凝神细想，扳着手指头数：“穆尔贾娜·哈努姆至少给他生了二十个——全没了；菲特娜·哈努姆比这还多——就算三十个吧——活着六个。阿里的母亲——她原是个奴隶——至少十个，活着三个。另外还有一房妾……”

“住口，住口！这不是真的！不可能。”巴拉卡喊道，笑声里带着歇斯底里。

乌姆·埃德-达哈克却答道：“真的，凭安拉（wallahi）。有什么好惊惶的？女人的本分就是生养。其余的，我们交给安拉。”

为了宽慰听的人，她接着讲起一窝三十个、甚至四十个全都抚养成人的事；巴拉卡的惊骇这才化作了笑声。
===Original===
In her moments of depression she was haunted by two terrors on her son’s account. One was ophthalmia, a disease so prevalent in Egypt that half the population was composed of blind and one-eyed persons. The other was the plague, of which the women told grim stories with a strange complacency. Many of her friends had been through epidemics of the pestilence and, by their own report, had known no panic. It was a swift and cruel illness, by which they had lost dear ones in despite of careful nursing; it was from Allah; no one’s thinking could avert or cure it. The horror the mere thought of it inspired in Barakah, her futile worry, filled them with a placid wonder.

She had made up her mind that, if the plague drew near, she would carry off her boy to Europe, having no doubt but she could win consent from Yûsuf. But she said nothing of this resolution to the women, knowing they would deem it godless. As a preventive against ophthalmia, she bathed her son’s eyes with cold water twice a day, and gave orders for the flies that settled on them to be brushed away﻿—a thing the slaves would not have thought of doing on their own initiative.

The plague did not come near her; and Muhammad’s eyes continued bright and liquid under long black lashes. An enemy, unfeared as unexpected, struck her joy.
===Chinese===
在她心境低沉的时刻，有两桩恐惧缠着她，都是替儿子担的惊。一桩是眼炎。这病在埃及流行得太厉害，全国人口有一半是瞎子和独眼的人。另一桩是鼠疫，女人们讲起它来尽是凄惨的故事，态度却出奇地安之若素。她的许多朋友都经历过鼠疫的流行，据她们自己说，从不曾惊慌失措。那是一种来势迅猛、极其残酷的病，任凭怎样悉心看护，她们都曾因此失去亲人；它来自安拉，谁的心思也拦不住、治不好。单单想到它便叫巴拉卡心惊胆战，她那份徒劳的忧惧，令她们心中充满一种恬静的诧异。

她已打定主意，鼠疫一旦逼近，就带儿子逃往欧洲，并且深信不疑能从优素福那里求得允准。但这打算她对女人们只字不提，知道她们准会说这是不敬神明的。为预防眼炎，她每天用凉水给儿子洗两回眼睛，还吩咐把落在他眼上的苍蝇随时拂开——这种事，女奴们若无人吩咐是决计想不到去做的。

鼠疫没有临到她跟前；穆罕默德的眼睛在长长的黑睫毛下，依旧清亮水灵。一个她不曾惧怕、也不曾料到的仇敌，击中了她的欢乐。
===Original===
About the period when he was being weaned, Muhammad had a serious illness. An Armenian doctor was called in, who said, “It is the fever.” At that the women wailed and prayed to Allah. The foe was too well known, the scourge of children. There was no need to tell them what to do.

“It carries off a host of infants every year,” said Umm ed-Dahak. “But be not downcast, O beloved. God is great! Many survive, and those who do recover are free from its malignancy forevermore.”

The malady was typhoid fever, or so like it that Barakah could not detect the slightest difference. She had been often told that it did not attack the natives of the land, but only Europeans, who were thought more delicate. Here, then, was the reason. The natives who grew up were all inoculated, having been through the disease in infancy.

Muhammad lived, for which his mother gave wild thanks to Allah, and performed a hundred alms-deeds she had vowed in her suspense. But a year later her small daughter died of the same scourge, and in the after years she lost five children by it.
===Chinese===
大约在给他断奶的那阵子，穆罕默德害了一场大病。请来了一位亚美尼亚医生，说：“是热症。”女人们一听便恸哭起来，向安拉祈祷。这仇敌是人人都太熟悉了——孩子们的灾星。用不着谁来告诉她们该怎么办。

“它每年都带走一大群吃奶的娃娃，”乌姆·埃德-达哈克说，“可别垂头丧气，哦亲爱的。真主是伟大的！许多人熬过来了，凡是熬过来的，终生再不受它的荼毒。”

这病就是伤寒，要不就是同伤寒像得让巴拉卡瞧不出一丝一毫的差别。她常听人说，这病不侵害本地人，只侵害欧洲人，因为欧洲人被认为身子更娇嫩。缘故原来在这里。长大成人的本地人个个都等于种过了疫苗——他们婴孩时都熬过这场病。

穆罕默德活了下来。为这个，他母亲向安拉发了狂似地道谢，把悬心吊胆时许下的一百件善举一一行了个遍。可一年之后，她的小女儿就死于同一场灾殃；此后经年，她又接连被它夺走了五个孩子。

## XXIX / 二十九
===Original===
News from the world of men reached the harem like voices from the street without. From time to time some item, interesting them, was cried in tones of censure or approval; but always in a manner of abstraction. This apathy arose from centuries of strict seclusion, in which, through change of dynasties and strife of factions, the privilege of the harem had been respected. The women felt that politics could not come near them; the government which ruled the men was none of theirs. A realm within the realm, they had their own excitements, their own concerns of life and death and amorous crime. Events the most important failed to move them, while trifling breaches of religion or old custom caused a vast commotion in that nursery of fanaticism.

One day, when Barakah was out driving in her carriage, she was stopped near Abdîn palace by the pressure of excited crowds and heard the sounds of angry tumult. The driver backed the horses and then turned. On reaching home she asked the eunuch of the matter.

He shrugged: “It is the soldiers, O my lady. They are angry at the coming of the Frank commissioners.”
===Chinese===
男人世界的消息传进哈雷姆，就像墙外街上的声响。时或有条新闻叫她们感兴趣，被人以非难或赞许的口吻传说着；却总带着一种心不在焉的神气。这种冷漠，源自数百年严格的深闺幽闭——其间任凭王朝更迭、党争纷起，哈雷姆的特权始终备受尊重。女人们觉得政治挨不着她们的身；统治男人们的那个政府，同她们毫不相干。国中之国，她们自有自己的骚动，自有自己的生死大事与情爱罪案。天大的事件也打动不了她们，倒是些微违犯教门或古礼的小事，会在这狂热的温床里掀起滔天的波澜。

一天，巴拉卡乘车外出，在阿布丁宫（Abdîn）附近被激动的人群堵住去路，听见了怒气冲冲的喧嚷。车夫勒马倒退，随后掉头折返。到家以后，她问那阉人是怎么回事。

他耸耸肩：“是士兵们闹事，我的夫人。他们是冲着法兰克专员们的到来在动怒。”
===Original===
It was then that she presumed to question Yûsuf, and learnt that two commissioners, one French, one English, had come to take control of the finances of the country. The Khedive, that jovial libertine and spendthrift, was now bankrupt. The Europeans, as his creditors, assumed the reins.

“But why the English?” questioned Barakah with irritation, for up to then the French alone had been a power in Egypt.

“Wallahi, just because their men are clever,” was the answer. “They bought up all our Sovereign’s shares in the canal. Their guile is great, but greater Allah’s mercy, for the arrival of these Franks is good for me. Knowing both their languages I am put forward to receive them, and so rise in honour.”

In fact, a few days later he was made a Pasha.
===Chinese===
就是在那时，她才大着胆子去问优素福，这才知道来了两位专员，一位法国人，一位英国人，是来接管这个国家财政的。赫迪夫——那位快活的浪荡子和败家子——如今破产了。欧洲人作为他的债主，接过了缰绳。

“可为什么还有英国人？”巴拉卡愠怒地质问，因为在那以前，埃及称得上势力的唯有法国一家。

“凭安拉，就因为他们的人精明，”对方这样回答。“他们把咱们君王名下的运河股份全数买了去。他们的狡诈固然厉害，安拉的仁慈却更伟大，因为这些法兰克人一来，于我反倒有好处。两国语言我都通晓，便被推举出来接待他们，身价也就随之水涨船高。”

果然，没过几天，他被封为帕夏。
===Original===
But Barakah could not regard the case thus philosophically. The intrusion of the English frightened her. If they should ever come to lord it in the country her degradation would be unendurable. She confided her displeasure to Muhammad, who took an interest in politics as schoolboys will. He bade her have no fear; the Muslims would destroy them presently. The women told her God would intervene. But things went rapidly from bad to worse.

Since a French force under Bonaparte had entered Cairo, before the era of Muhammad Ali, no such fury had possessed the world of women as that which seized them on the deposition of the Khedive Ismaîl. Whatever touched the majesty of El Islam excited them; vile infidels had here contrived the downfall of a Muslim ruler. And there ensued a host of innovations, in which the hand of unbelief was plainly visible.

The slave-trade had been formally abolished under Ismaîl, to please the Franks, but with the customary wink of that facetious monarch. The trade continued gaily with his sly connivance. Now, in his son’s reign, it began to be suppressed in earnest. The slaves themselves were loud in lamentation. When it was known that slavery itself was menaced, the harem chattered like ten thousand angry parrots.
===Chinese===
巴拉卡却不能这样达观地看待这件事。英国人的闯入令她心惊。倘若他们有朝一日当真在这个国家作威作福，她所受的屈辱将叫她无法忍受。她把自己的不快说给穆罕默德听；这孩子对政治的热衷，正像学童们惯有的那样。他叫她不必害怕；穆斯林们很快就会把他们收拾掉。女人们对她说，真主自会出面干预。可时局急转直下，一天坏似一天。

自从当年波拿巴率法军开进开罗——那还在穆罕默德·阿里当政之前——女人的世界就不曾像赫迪夫伊斯梅尔被废黜时这样怒火中烧过。凡是触动伊斯兰威严的事都叫她们激动；卑劣的异教徒这回竟设局搞垮了一位穆斯林君主。随后又来了一连串的新政，其中分明可见不信教者插手的痕迹。

奴隶贸易在伊斯梅尔治下就曾为讨好法兰克人明令废止，只是那位爱打趣的君王照例眨了眨眼。买卖在他的默许下欢欢喜喜照做不误。如今到了他儿子在位，才当真查禁起来。奴隶们自己反倒放声哀叹。待到风闻奴隶制度本身也危在旦夕，整座哈雷姆便像一万只怒气冲冲的鹦鹉一般聒噪起来。
===Original===
“The Lord have mercy on us! It is gross impiety,” screamed Fitnah Khânum. “Does not the august Koran lay down strict rules for the control of slaves? Is it not therefore Allah’s will that they exist?”

“The trade in slaves is holy,” cried Gulbeyzah; “bringing every year a thousand converts out of heathendom. If some are slain, it is no matter, since the death of heathens is uncounted, like the death of beasts. Without the cruel raids, the bloodshed, the survivors had not known salvation. Praise be to Allah, they cannot suppress the trade in us white people, since a father’s right to sell his child resides in nature. Only since the English meddle do we hear such wickedness.”
===Chinese===
“愿真主怜悯我们！这是大大的不敬啊，”菲特娜·哈努姆尖叫道。“尊贵的《古兰经》难道没有定下管束奴隶的严规吗？那么他们存在，岂不正是安拉的意旨？”

“奴隶买卖是神圣的，”古尔贝雅扎喊道，“它每年从异教之地带来上千个皈依的人。纵使有些人死在半路也不要紧，异教徒的死是不上数的，如同牲口的死一般。若没有那些残酷的掳掠、那些流血，活下来的人便无从得救。赞美安拉，他们禁绝不了买卖咱们白人的这门生意，因为父亲卖儿女的权利原是天经地义。打从英国人插手以来，咱们才听得见这样的邪说。”
===Original===
Besides the slave-trade, good old customs were abolished﻿—one ceremony called the trampling, in particular, in which a sheik, renowned for piety, was wont to ride on horseback over strewn believers. Some people thought the world was coming to an end, and looked for the appearance of the final prophet. The times were full of omens, portents, monstrous births. The French and English, in collusion, gave command in Egypt; the monarch was a puppet in their hands. The apathy of men amazed the women looking on. The good Khedive appeared a devil to those hot noncombatants; rebellion a plain duty upon all believers. They prayed for a deliverer to be raised up; and in the absence of the prophet whom they half expected, applauded the exertions of a simple soldier, who ventured to oppose the wicked rulers.
===Chinese===
除了奴隶买卖，一些古老的好风俗也被废除了——尤其是一种称作“践踏礼”（the trampling）的仪式：一位以虔诚著称的谢赫（sheik），惯常策马从横陈于地的信众身上踏过。有些人以为世界末日到了，巴望最后那位先知现身。时世满是凶兆、异象和怪胎。法国人和英国人串通一气，在埃及发号施令；君主成了他们手中的傀儡。男子们的麻木不仁，叫旁观的妇女们骇然。好端端的赫迪夫，在这些激昂的局外看客眼里俨然是个魔鬼；造反则是全体信士一桩义不容辞的本分。她们祈求上天使一位救主兴起；在她们半心巴望的那位先知迟迟不至时，便为一位敢与那班恶主相抗的寻常军人的奔走喝彩。
===Original===
With the exception of some Turks, who sneered from pride of race, the whole harem acclaimed Arâbi from his first appearance as a champion. The women viewed the question very simply. Here, on one hand, was a man who wished to free the land from foreign interference, whose cry of Egypt for the Egyptians, must mean Egypt for the Muslims, since the Copts were nobody; on the other, an infirm, if not a wicked, ruler who was letting all the privilege of El Islam be torn away. In vain their men assured them the Khedive was a good Muslim, and only deferential to the Franks from sheer expediency; that Arâbi’s faction was the work of clever rascals, and boasted not one man of solid parts. They took religious ground and would not listen. They taught their children to admire Arâbi. Muhammad, now a student in the school of war, assisted by his faithful Ali, fought five boys who dared to ridicule the peasant soldier. Though beaten many times the two did not give way, though Ali, for his own part, would have fled thrice over. But Muhammad was indomitable. Bruised and bleeding, he returned with fury to the charge, till his opponents fled in pure religious terror of such dauntless rage. A few weeks later the whole land was cringing before Arâbi’s power. And then excitements followed thick and fast. Muhammad brought his mother all the latest rumours. One day it was:
===Chinese===
除了几个出于种族傲慢而嗤之以鼻的土耳其人，全哈雷姆从阿拉比（Arâbi）一露面就把他当作英雄来拥戴。女人们看这问题简单得很。一边，是一个要把这片土地从外国干涉中解救出来的人，他喊的“埃及人的埃及”，必定就是“穆斯林的埃及”，因为科普特人压根算不得数；另一边，是一个软弱——若不说邪恶——的君主，眼睁睁听任伊斯兰的种种特权被人一一撕去。家中的男人们再三向她们保证，说赫迪夫是个好穆斯林，对法兰克人低声下气不过是权宜之计；又说阿拉比一党是几个精明的无赖捣鼓出来的，拿不出一个有真才实干的人。全是白费唇舌。她们占定宗教的立场，什么也听不进去。她们教孩子们敬慕阿拉比。穆罕默德如今是战争学校的学生，在忠心的阿里帮拳下，同五个敢笑话那农夫军人的男孩干了一仗又一仗。虽说屡屡吃亏，两个人也不肯退让，尽管就阿里本心而论，他早想逃上三回了。可穆罕默德是不屈不挠的。他带着一身青肿和血污，一次次怒气冲冲地扑上去再战，直打得对手们对这股无畏的狂怒生出由衷的宗教恐惧，抱头逃散。几星期后，整个国家都在阿拉比的威势面前俯首帖耳。跟着，种种叫人兴奋的消息接踵而至。穆罕默德把最新的传闻一条条带给他母亲。有一天他说：
===Original===
“Great tidings, O my mother! All the Franks are flying! Ali and I have been to watch them at the railway station. Such a crowd! The faithful, past all patience, have risen up at Tantah and Iskenderîyeh and slain thousands of them.”

A number of the loyal Turks were also flying. Amînah Khânum and Bedr-ul-Budûr came to take leave of Barakah. They were bound for Alexandria, in the train of the Khedive, and thence would take ship for Constantinople if things grew no better. Muhammad, when informed of their departure, rendered praise to Allah.

“They are vanquished,” he remarked. “But would to Allah that we had more Turks on our side. These fellahin, though braggarts, are great cowards. They need the whip to urge them into battle. I, who am half a Turk and half an Englishman, cannot endure the sluggishness of this Nile mud.”
===Chinese===
“天大的喜信，母亲大人！法兰克人全在逃命啦！我和阿里到火车站看他们来着。好大的一群人！信士们在坦塔（Tantah）和伊斯肯德利耶（Iskenderîyeh，即亚历山大港）忍无可忍，奋起暴动，杀掉了他们好几千人。”

一批效忠王室的土耳其人也在逃。阿米娜·哈努姆和白德尔-布杜尔来向巴拉卡辞行。她们要随赫迪夫的行列前往亚历山大港，倘若时局不见好转，再从那里搭船去君士坦丁堡。穆罕默德得悉她们要走，口口声声赞美安拉。

“他们是败定了，”他议论道，“但愿安拉保佑，咱们这边多些土耳其人才好。这些费拉农人虽说会吹，胆子却小得可怜，得拿鞭子抽着才肯上阵。我呢，半个土耳其人半个英国人，实在受不得这尼罗河淤泥的疲沓劲儿。”
===Original===
The boy forgot the portion of his blood which was derived from Fitnah Khânum, his paternal grandmother. It was Nile mud of the thickest, but it did not show in him. All hot and noble counsels moved him to enthusiasm; the lukewarm and the philosophical enraged his soul. Stupidity or insolence in an inferior he could not brook. If his commands were not obeyed at once and with intelligence, he struck hard with the first instrument that came to hand, and called down Allah’s wrath on the offender. The old Pasha was delighted by those outbursts, as showing the commanding spirit of his Turkish race.

“When all these lowborn troubles have passed over, we must procure him some small government,” he said to Yûsuf, who acquiesced with a pathetic smile. He had not that supreme contempt for the Egyptian rebels which kept his aged father calm amid the storm. He held a good position, and he feared to lose it; whereas his father had retired from public life.
===Chinese===
这孩子忘了他血脉里承自祖母菲特娜·哈努姆的那一份。那原是最浓稠的尼罗河淤泥，只是在他身上不显罢了。一切热烈而高贵的议论都叫他热血沸腾；不温不火与达观冷静之论则激得他心头火起。下人的愚钝或无礼，他一刻也容不得。他的吩咐若不立刻被机灵地照办，他便抄起手边头一件家什狠狠打下去，还求安拉降怒于那冒犯的人。老帕夏见了这些发作欢喜得很，说这显出了他土耳其血统的统帅气魄。

“等这些出身微贱的家伙闹出的乱子都过去了，咱们得给他谋个一官半职，”他对优素福说。优素福报以一丝可怜的微笑，默认了。他对埃及叛党没有他老父那一份极度的蔑视——正是那份蔑视，让老人在风暴之中安之若素。他身居好差事，唯恐失掉；他父亲却早已退出宦途。
===Original===
Barakah delighted in her son’s account of the disorders. His excitement and the animation of each glance and gesture provided her with pictures upon which she brooded in the vacancy of summer days. The air which drifted through her lattice was oppressive, the sunlight like a furnace fire without; the voices of the street complained of dust and heat; the ceaseless buzz of flies benumbed the brain; the call for water rang incessantly through all the house, and even Umm ed-Dahak felt too weak to talk. But Barakah was happy, since Muhammad spent much time with her, finding her conversation more congenial to his patriotic mood than that of Yûsuf. In his absence she lay still and smoked, and quenched her thirst at frequent intervals, taking scant notice of her little daughter﻿—the only other of her many children who had managed to survive the second year. Umm ed-Dahak loved the child and schooled her privately, telling her stories of man’s love and woman’s duty, and teaching her to pose and ogle in the proper way. But for the rest she was of no importance; Muhammad’s known affection for her was her only merit.
===Chinese===
巴拉卡津津有味地听儿子讲那些乱子。他的兴奋，他顾盼举手之间的一颦一动，在她眼前展开一幅幅图画，供她在夏日空落落的光阴里细细咀嚼。从花窗飘进来的空气沉滞闷人，窗外的日光像炉膛里的火；街上一片声地抱怨着尘土和酷热；苍蝇无休无止的嗡嗡叫人头脑发木；要水的呼唤在整座宅子里响个不停，连乌姆·埃德-达哈克也懒怠开口了。巴拉卡却是快活的，因为穆罕默德有许多时光守着她，觉得同她说话比同优素福说话更合他爱国的兴致。他不在跟前时，她便静静躺着抽烟，隔一会儿便饮点什么解渴，几乎不大理会她的小女儿——她那一大群孩子里，除了穆罕默德，唯独这一个熬过了两岁。乌姆·埃德-达哈克疼这孩子，私下里调教她，给她讲男人的爱情和女人的本分的故事，教她怎样摆姿势、递眼色才算合乎体统。除此之外，这孩子无足轻重；穆罕默德疼爱她乃是人人皆知的，这便是她唯一的可取之处了。
===Original===
One afternoon Muhammad came in with a mien of wild excitement and, having kissed his mother’s hand, cried out:

“Most dreadful news! O horror! O revenge! The English have destroyed Iskenderîyeh with their cruel guns! The English only, since the French, more honourable, fled from the hateful sight with tears of shame. Simply because the forts were being mended, and work was not relaxed at their command. But, praise to Allah, we have hurt them also. Quite half their fleet has been destroyed by our brave fire. After this, we give no quarter﻿—no, by Allah! It is holy war. Muhammad Tewfik is proclaimed a scoundrel. Our Arâbi is Dictator. The army is to be augmented fourfold by forced levies. I met a boy, no older than myself, who goes to fight. I go this minute to implore my father to let me likewise join the army in the field.”
===Chinese===
一天下午，穆罕默德带着狂热的兴奋神色走进来，吻过母亲的手，大声嚷道：

“最可怕的消息！哦，可恨！哦，报仇啊！英国人用他们狠毒的炮火毁了伊斯肯德利耶！只英国人一家干的，法国人还要些脸面，含着羞愧的眼泪躲开了那可憎的场面。就因为炮台正在修缮，他们下了命令，工事也没有停。可是，赞美安拉，咱们也伤了他们：他们整整一半的舰队，叫咱们英勇的炮火给报销了。从此以后，咱们绝不留情——不，凭安拉！这是圣战。穆罕默德·陶菲克被宣布为恶棍。咱们的阿拉比做了独裁者。军队要用强征扩充四倍。我碰上一个年纪不比我大的男孩，就要上阵去了。我这会儿就去求父亲，让他准我也一样投军出征。”
===Original===
“Thy age is but fifteen. O Lord, he must not go!” cried out his mother in an agony of apprehension.

“I am a man full-grown, proficient in all exercises that belong to war. As young as I are going. Think, it is against the English, O my mother﻿—thy vile enemies!”

Embracing her without a thought for her despair, he left her in great haste to find his father.
===Chinese===
“你才十五岁。天哪，万万不能让他去！”他母亲忧惧万分地喊道。

“我是十足的成年人了，凡属战争的操练样样在行。同我一般年纪的都在去呢。想想吧，这是打英国人，哦母亲——你那些狠毒的仇敌！”

他搂了她一下，全不顾她的绝望，急匆匆丢下她，找他父亲去了。

## XXV / 二十五
===Original===
Her boy was her delight in life. No other woman was allowed to scold him. When Yûsuf slapped him in the cause of order, which happened often, for the child was naughty, she made it up to him with sugarplums and fond caresses. In his father’s absence Muhammad was the lord of the harem; all vied to please him. His foster-mother and the servants told him fairy stories in which good children killed all kinds of monsters. One, which he never tired of hearing, ended thus:
“Then little Hâfiz took a sword and reaped the head of the atrocious ghoul; and beat to death the hag who had ill-used him, and with the help of all the neighbours, who acclaimed his goodness, burnt all his wicked little cousins in a cheerful fire.”
He knew that tale by heart and went about repeating it. He had a lot of toys, but none which gave him so much pleasure as a little cane. With this he beat the slave-girls, uttering terrific curses. The victims, for his satisfaction, made believe to cry, and assured him they were seriously injured. His mother and old Umm ed-Dahak praised his manly spirit.
===Chinese===
儿子是她此生的欢愉。别的女人一概不许责骂他。优素福为了立规矩掴他耳光——这是常有的事，因为这孩子实在淘气——过后她总拿糖果蜜饯和百般爱抚来补偿他。父亲不在跟前时，穆罕默德便是哈雷姆里的主子；人人争着讨他欢心。乳母和仆人们给他讲神仙故事，故事里听话的好孩子杀尽各式各样的妖魔。有一则他百听不厌，结尾是这样的：
“于是小哈菲兹拔出宝剑，割下了那万恶食尸鬼的脑袋；把虐待他的老妖婆活活打死；又在齐声称赞他心肠好的众邻居帮衬之下，把他那些坏心眼的小堂兄弟一股脑儿扔进欢腾的火堆，烧了个干净。”
这故事他背得烂熟，走到哪儿念到哪儿。他有一大堆玩具，可哪一样也不及一根小手杖叫他欢喜。他拿着它抽打女奴们，口中发出骇人的咒骂。挨打的人为讨他快意，装作啼哭，还一口咬定自己伤得不轻。他母亲和老乌姆·埃德-达哈克齐声夸他有丈夫气概。
===Original===
Fitnah Khânum sometimes shook her head and spoke of necessary discipline. Barakah only smiled; as she did also, when young Na’imah, puffed up with pride of her new motherhood, exclaimed: “By Allah, I will bring up my son otherwise.” But when the prim and dainty Turkish ladies looked fastidious, glancing around her room where toys lay scattered, she felt angry. The salons of those ladies were maintained in spotless cleanliness; their children, though untidy to avert ill-fortune, were as courtly as small chamberlains towards their elders.
“It is strange! Thou art an Englishwoman, yet thou likest these things!” Amînah Khânum exclaimed once, remarking her affection for a certain sweetstuff, common in the markets but unknown in decent houses﻿—a taste she had acquired through Umm ed-Dahak. “Thou art too much with the women of the country. Be more discerning in the choice of friends.”
But Barakah was happy as she was; or, if not altogether happy, chose to seem so from a blend of pride and indolence. Against the condescension of the Turkish ladies she armed her dignity with the reflection that she was born above all Eastern women. Yet she dared not let remembrance dwell on England for fear of terrible misgivings she had sworn to banish. Her boy, she thought, should be her vindication. He was visibly superior to other children of the land.
===Chinese===
菲特娜·哈努姆有时摇摇头，说管教是免不了的。巴拉卡只付诸一笑；年轻的纳伊玛初为人母、正自得意，嚷道“凭安拉，我养儿子可不这么养”，她听了也同样一笑置之。可等到那些一板一眼、娇贵齐整的土耳其夫人们面露挑剔，朝她满屋散乱的玩具四下打量时，她心里就起了火。那些夫人的客厅永远保持得一尘不染；她们的孩子虽说故意穿得邋遢以避邪祟，在长辈跟前却彬彬有礼，一个个像小小的宫廷侍从。
“真是稀奇！你是个英国女人，偏偏好这一口！”阿米娜·哈努姆有一回嚷道——她见巴拉卡偏爱某种甜食，那东西市面上随处可见，体面人家却从不沾唇——这口味是她打乌姆·埃德-达哈克那里染上的。“你同本地女人混得太多了。择友要更有眼力些才是。”
但巴拉卡安于眼下的日子；或者说，即便并不全然快活，出于骄傲与怠惰参半，她也宁肯显得快活。面对土耳其夫人们的屈尊俯就，她以一念武装起自己的尊严：她生来就高出天下所有东方女人。然而她不敢让思绪在英国流连，唯恐那些她发誓要驱散的可怖疑惧卷土重来。她的儿子，她想，将是替她昭雪的凭证。他分明比这地方所有孩子都出众。
===Original===
To him, clasped tightly to her breast, she poured out all her secret and tormenting thoughts.
The English had ill-treated her most shamefully. Her son must hate the English for her sake. And yet he must remember he was half an Englishman, a being of a different order from the children round him. And when he prayed he must ask Allah to increase his strength and wisdom, so that he might prove a match for any Englishman he might encounter in the course of life. The child, with bright eyes, drank in all she said, but God alone knew what his mind could make of it; for Barakah’s opinions were a tangle as of angry serpents, their utterance as incoherent as the cries of battle. She heard him once hurl “Englishwoman!” at a slave who had enraged him. The girl laughed back: “Thy mother is an Englishwoman,” when he replied: “A noble race and warlike﻿—the Muslimîn among them, like my mother. But thou art a low Christian of that race, a filthy harlot!”
===Chinese===
她把他紧紧搂在怀里，向他倾吐自己全部隐秘而煎熬的心事。
英国人待她，寡廉鲜耻到了极点。她的儿子必须替她恨英国人。可他又必须记住自己是半个英国人，同周围的孩子不是一个等次的人。祈祷的时候，他必须求安拉增长自己的力量和智慧，好叫他此生无论撞见哪个英国人，都能与之匹敌。孩子睁着亮晶晶的眼睛，把她的话尽数喝了进去；可他的小心灵究竟领会出什么，唯有真主知道；因为巴拉卡的种种见解纠缠成一团，好似一窝怒蛇，说出来前言不搭后语，如同战场上的呐喊。有一回，她听见他冲一个惹恼了他的女奴骂出“英国女人！”那姑娘笑着回嘴：“你母亲就是英国女人。”他答道：“那是高贵的种族，尚武的种族——他们中间的穆斯林，就像我母亲。可你是那族里下等的基督徒，一个肮脏的娼妇！”
===Original===
Outside her own house and her husband’s family Barakah’s chief friends were Gulbeyzah and Bedr-ul-Budûr. With them she laid aside the pride which had become her usual armour in society. Yet Gulbeyzah said one day when Barakah was calling on her: “How thou art changed! Rememberest thou the days when we talked French together? Then thou wast as timid and demure as mice are; and so good and wise! Now thou art a high and mighty Arab lady. I am half afraid!”
“Thou too art greatly changed, O wicked joker!” cried Barakah, impounding the Circassian’s hand. “Rememberest thou the little window in the passage?”
“Hush!” said Gulbeyzah, with uplifted finger. “By Allah, thou art owner of a shameless memory. But come with me!”
She led her friend away from the reception-room, upstairs, and showed her such another little window as that they both remembered, looking out on distant roofs. “I come and dream here sometimes as of old﻿—I, the mother of two children!” sighed Gulbeyzah. “There is a roof well fitted for a hopeless lover, but no one ever comes. Now thou knowest that I have not changed my foolish nature, although in motherhood I have acquired a soul.”
===Chinese===
除了自家宅门和夫家亲族，巴拉卡最要好的朋友便是古尔贝雅扎和白德尔-布杜尔。同她们在一起，她能卸下那副在交际场上惯常披挂的骄傲铠甲。可有一天巴拉卡上门看她，古尔贝雅扎却说：“你变得多厉害呀！还记得咱们一起讲法语的日子吗？那时候你胆小娴静得像只老鼠，又那样善良聪明！如今你成了一位高高在上的阿拉伯贵妇。我都有些怕你了！”
“你也变得厉害呀，坏心眼的促狭鬼！”巴拉卡喊道，一把捉住那切尔克斯姑娘的手。“记得过道里那扇小窗吗？”
“嘘！”古尔贝雅扎竖起一根指头说。“凭安拉，你这记性真不知羞耻。跟我来吧！”
她领着朋友出了客厅，上了楼，指给她看一扇同两人记忆里一模一样的小窗，窗外望得见远处的重重屋顶。“我有时还到这儿来做梦，像从前一样——我，两个孩子的母亲了！”古尔贝雅扎叹道。“那边有一片屋顶，正配一个无望的恋人，可从来没有人来。这下你知道了，虽做了母亲、得了灵魂，我这傻性子可一点儿没改。”
===Original===
That the Turkish ladies rather wondered at her preference for Gulbeyzah and Bedr-ul-Budûr, two former slaves, made Barakah the more enamoured of their friendship. Muhammad was allowed to visit them, and play games with their children, a transcendent favour; and it was with a horror as of treason and of base ingratitude that she heard them, too, declare that he was sadly spoilt.
It was at the wedding-feast which Tâhir, the great singer, gave his daughter. The ladies of the grand harems flocked thither eagerly, for it was known that Tâhir would perform. The two Circassians found out Barakah amid the throng, and went and sat with her in a deserted corner. Muhammad had that day been playing with the children of Gulbeyzah’s house.
“He is a little tyrant!” said his hostess, laughing. “A young savage. He attacked my little girl as if to kill her, because she tried to get back her own doll. I had to shake him. I told him that his mother would be very angry at his conduct. He cursed my religion and then spat at me. By our lady Zeynab, thou shouldst beat him sometimes, O my soul!”
“His spirit is too high and needs restraining. Everyone says so,” said Bedr-ul-Budûr.
“You must have thwarted him. He is not used to it. He has the noblest, the most generous nature,” answered Barakah.
“By Allah, it is difficult not to thwart a boy who claims the eyes from out one’s face as his to play with! He must be denied, and when denied he grows infuriated,” said Gulbeyzah mildly.
===Chinese===
土耳其夫人们对她偏爱古尔贝雅扎和白德尔-布杜尔这两个昔日女奴颇为诧异，这反倒叫巴拉卡愈发迷恋她们的情谊。穆罕默德获准上她们家去，同她们的孩子玩耍——这是破格的恩典；可后来连她们也说他被惯得不像话，她听着，心里一阵骇然，如同受了背叛、受了卑劣的忘恩负义一般。
那是在伟大的歌者塔希尔为女儿摆下的喜筵上。各大哈雷姆的夫人们争先恐后地涌去，因为人人都知道塔希尔要献唱。两位切尔克斯姑娘在人堆里寻见巴拉卡，便过去同她坐在一个冷清的角落里。那天穆罕默德一直在同古尔贝雅扎家的孩子们玩耍。
“他是个小暴君！”他做客那家的女主人笑道。“一个小野人。我小姑娘不过想把娃娃拿回来，他就扑上去，简直像要她的命。我不得不摇撼他。我告诉他，他母亲见了他这行径定要生气的。他咒骂我的教门，还朝我啐唾沫。凭咱们的宰娜白夫人（Zeynab），你有时真该揍揍他，我的亲人！”
“他性子太傲，该收一收了。人人都这么说。”白德尔-布杜尔说。
“准是你违拗了他。他不惯这个。他的天性最高贵、最宽厚。”巴拉卡答道。
“凭安拉，一个孩子若连你脸上的眼珠子都讨了去当玩物，想不违拗他也难哪！总得驳回他，一驳回他就暴跳如雷。”古尔贝雅扎温和地说。
===Original===
Barakah was on the point of making a fierce answer, when the glorious voice of Tâhir rose, compelling silence. She had heard a hundred singers, male and female, since she came to Cairo; but Tâhir’s voice alone had power to move her. The others mouthed and shrieked to individual passions; but Tâhir took the soul and soared with it, producing exaltation and a sense of peace. He sang from the pure heart of El Islam, and shed its fervent calm on all who heard him. When the song died she had forgotten anger.
That wedding-feast became forever memorable by reason of a shocking tragedy at its conclusion. Barakah and her friends were led by Umm ed-Dahak, who was a relative of Tâhir’s wife, to view the nuptial chamber. It was full of flowering plants; the bed, with silken coverings, was quite embowered. In addition to the odour of so many blossoms the air was thick with perfumes burnt and sprinkled. The room, they were informed, had been arranged, the flowers provided, by rich admirers of the singer’s talent.
“By Allah, pretty! But I should not like to sleep there!” had been Gulbeyzah’s comment, little guessing what would happen. For next morning it was known to high and low, through all the city, that the bridegroom and the bride had died of suffocation. When people went to rouse them in the morning they found corpses. The news was brought to Barakah by Umm ed-Dahak, who had herself been present at the sad discovery. She told the story with an artist’s relish.
===Chinese===
巴拉卡正要恶狠狠回敬一句，塔希尔那辉煌的嗓音扬起来了，满堂顿时肃静。自到开罗以来，她听过的歌手男女合计不下一百个；可唯有塔希尔的歌声有打动她的力量。别的歌手做张做势，为一己的激情尖声怪叫；塔希尔却把人的灵魂摄住，携着它扶摇直上，叫人心中生出狂喜，又生出安宁。他唱的是伊斯兰教纯净的心，把那炽热的恬静洒在每一个听的人身上。歌声止歇时，她已把怒气忘了个干净。
那场喜筵最终因一桩骇人的惨剧而永志难忘。乌姆·埃德-达哈克是塔希尔妻子的亲戚，由她引领，巴拉卡和朋友们去参观了喜房。满屋子都是开花的植物；喜床覆着丝绸被褥，几乎叫花枝整个掩没了。除了那许多花朵的芬芳，空气里还浓重地弥漫着熏烧和喷洒的香露。她们得知，这屋子的布置、这些花的供奉，都出自那位歌者才华的豪富仰慕者之手。
“凭安拉，真美！可我才不愿睡在那儿呢！”这是古尔贝雅扎的评语，她哪里想得到后来会发生什么。因为第二天一早，全城上下、无论尊卑都知道了：新郎和新娘已窒息而死。早晨人们去唤醒他们时，找到的是两具尸首。消息是乌姆·埃德-达哈克带给巴拉卡的，那凄惨的发现她本人也在场。她讲起这段故事来津津有味，俨然一位艺术家。
===Original===
“What did Tâhir do? The poor demented father? What did he? He took his lute and struck the chords and sang a song more mournful than was ever heard on earth till now. Many present had to leave the room in grievous pains. And then, with the last note﻿—*C‑r‑r‑a‑c‑k!*﻿—he broke the lute, and swore the binding oath that he would never sing again. Inshallah he will change his mind,” said Umm ed-Dahak, in her ordinary tone. “The world would lack a soul without his singing. His oath has spread despair through all the town.”
For months the news of Tâhir was demanded eagerly. After his daughter’s death he went to Tantah for a while. Returning to the capital, prepared to keep his vow, he took a shop and furnished it with goods, intending to become a merchant. He thought to work out bargains over cups of coffee, by way of pastime only, for he was a wealthy man. But the people, his admirers, would not have it. They thronged his shop directly it was opened, and bought up all his goods in a few hours, paying the price first asked without a protest. He stocked his shop again; the same thing happened, till, finding himself debarred from occupation, he cursed the day when he was born; and in the end repaired to the Grand Qadi, and asked for liberation from his vow. The reverend judge released him with a grin and “Praise to Allah!” It was what his Honour and the whole of Egypt had been wanting. Enormous crowds assembled to hear Tâhir call to noonday prayer at the great mosque El Azhar﻿—the first occasion of his singing since his daughter’s death.
“The praise to Allah, we possess him once again,” said Umm ed-Dahak, when reporting his defeat. “It has cost us trouble to regain him, Allah knows. He did wrong to swear that oath; which was as impious as swearing to cut off his hand or foot, the work of God; and so the Qadi told him in his judgment yesterday. He brought the grief upon himself by doting on the girl above her merits, calling her his soul of music, neglecting the son who is still with him﻿—a fine lad. By the Prophet, it was courting sorrow to make all that fuss about a daughter. Now had it been his son, his source of honour﻿—”
Barakah interrupted with a prayer to Allah to avert the omen of her stabbing fear. She clutched Muhammad to her bosom; but he, intent on playthings, kicked and struggled, even swore at her. And at that moment Fitnah Khânum was announced.
===Chinese===
“塔希尔做了什么？那可怜的失心疯的父亲？他做了什么？他取过琵琶，拨响琴弦，唱了一支歌，比人间历来听过的任何歌都更哀恸。在场许多人痛楚难当，只得退出屋去。随后，随着末一个音——*咔——嚓！*——他折断了琵琶，立下不可违逆的重誓，从此永不再唱。若安拉意欲，他会回心转意的。”乌姆·埃德-达哈克用她平常的语气说。“没有了他的歌，世界就缺了灵魂。他的誓言把绝望撒遍了全城。”
一连数月，人们急切地打听着塔希尔的消息。女儿死后，他去坦塔（Tantah）住了些时日。回到都城，他打定主意守誓，盘下一间铺子，置办了货物，打算改行经商。他原想就着一杯杯咖啡把买卖谈成，不过当个消遣，因为他本是个富有的人。可是老百姓——他的仰慕者们——不答应。铺子一开张，他们便挤上门来，几个钟头里把货物抢购一空，要价多少给多少，没有一句还价。他再进一批货；又是同样的光景。到末了，他见自己竟被断绝了营生，咒骂起自己出世的那一天；最后只好去见大卡迪，请求解除誓言的约束。那位可敬的法官咧嘴一笑，说了句“赞美安拉！”，便把他放了。这正是他老人家和整个埃及早就盼着的事。塔希尔在爱资哈尔（El Azhar）大清真寺唱晌礼唤礼的时候，人山人海聚拢来听——那是自他女儿死后，他头一遭开嗓歌唱。
“赞美归于安拉，咱们又拥有他了。”乌姆·埃德-达哈克来报他败阵的消息时说。“为了把他弄回来，咱们费了多少事，安拉知道。他起那个誓是错了；那誓之亵渎，就如同赌咒要砍掉自己的手或脚——真主的造物——一样；昨天卡迪在判词里也是这样训他的。这场伤心是他自招的：他溺爱那闺女，爱过了她的分量，管她叫他的音乐之魂，却怠慢了仍在他身边的儿子——一个多好的少年。凭先知起誓，为一个女儿闹得这般惊天动地，分明是自讨苦吃。倘若是他的儿子，他荣源的所在——”
巴拉卡赶忙插进一句祈祷，求安拉禳除凶兆，消解她那剜心般的恐惧。她把穆罕默德紧紧搂在怀里；可他一心惦着玩具，又踢又挣，连咒骂也出了口。正在这时，有人通报菲特娜·哈努姆到了。

## XXVI / 二十六
===Original===
When Fitnah Khânum entered, the small boy was stamping about on the dais, hurling frightful imprecations at his mother, who was on her knees endeavouring to soothe him. His fez was off, and he had trampled on it in his rage; he tore his clothing. Umm ed-Dahak, crouching by the wall with her narghile, made clucking noises to attract the child; while the wife of Ghandûr, standing, smiled upon the scene, awaiting the command to bear him off. The floor was littered with his broken playthings. The light that filtered in through the rich lattice was blue with all the dust that he had raised.
“Look, here comes thy grandmother, a great lady. Hush, O Muhammad! Be a good boy. I will give thee such nice sweeties.”
“Mayest thou be ravished and then cut in pieces!” shrieked Muhammad, knuckling both his eyes. Therewith he spurned his mother with his foot.
The visitor remained a moment petrified. It was the first time she had seen her grandson at his worst. Then, boxing both ears of the wife of Ghandûr, who stood grinning near her, she rushed upon the wicked boy, and slapped him hard, regardless of his kicks and blows, his horrid language.
“Learn to respect thy mother, little malefactor,” she admonished him, enforcing every word with punishment. “Thou art no better than a heathen, than a wild beast. Thou wilt merit fire hereafter!”
===Chinese===
菲特娜·哈努姆进屋时，那小男孩正在矮台上乱蹦乱跺，朝他母亲发出种种骇人的咒骂，母亲跪在地上，一味哄他。他的塔布什帽甩落在地，盛怒之下已被他踩了个稀烂；他还撕扯自己的衣裳。乌姆·埃德-达哈克揣着水烟筒蹲在墙边，咂着嘴咯咯作声逗引孩子；甘杜尔的妻子站在一旁，笑望这情景，只等一声吩咐便把他抱走。满地都是他砸坏的玩具。透过华丽格窗滤进来的光线，映着他扬起的漫天尘土，一片蓝蒙蒙的。
“瞧，你祖母来了，一位尊贵的夫人。嘘，穆罕默德，乖乖的。我给你顶好顶好的糖吃。”
“叫人把你先奸了，再剁成碎块！”穆罕默德嚎叫着，用拳头揉着两只眼睛，说罢抬脚把母亲踹开。
来客一时呆立当场。这是她头一回见到孙儿最不堪的样子。随后她左右开弓，掴了站在近旁咧嘴笑着的甘杜尔妻子两记耳光，便朝那恶童扑过去，狠狠打他的耳光，全然不管他又踢又打、满口秽语。
“学着敬重你的母亲，小恶棍。”她训诫道，字字都辅以责打。“你不比异教徒强，也不比野兽强。你来世是要下火狱的！”
===Original===
But Barakah sprang on her like a tigress. “He is my child! Let him alone!” she panted.
“He is thy child, truly, but a Muslim first. To curse and kick his mother is a dreadful crime.”
“Let him alone, I say! By Allah, no one shall chastise my son but me, his mother!”
The ladies, both alike indignant, screamed against each other; Umm ed-Dahak, ever ready to applaud a truth, however adverse, begged her mistress to hear wisdom from the mouth of Fitnah Khânum; the wife of Ghandûr was in tears, and all the slave-girls, assembling in the hope to see a fight, shrieked prayers to Allah and implored the ladies to be calm. Muhammad, in disgust at being quite forgotten, set up a dismal howl, which no one heeded.
At length, perceiving the futility of further argument, the visitor retired, by no means vanquished.
“The child must be removed if thou wilt not control him,” were her parting words, unheard of anyone amid the din.
===Chinese===
巴拉卡却像母虎一般扑到她身上。“他是我的孩子！放开他！”她喘着气喊。
“他确是你的孩子，可他首先是个穆斯林。咒骂母亲、踢打母亲，是天大的罪过。”
“放开他，我说！凭安拉，除了我——他的母亲，谁也不许责罚我的儿子！”
两位夫人都动了气，尖着嗓子对骂起来；乌姆·埃德-达哈克素来乐于为真理喝彩，哪怕真理逆耳，这时便央求自家主母听听菲特娜·哈努姆口中的道理；甘杜尔的妻子哭了起来；众女奴聚拢来，巴望着看一场厮打，一面尖声向安拉祈祷，一面恳求夫人们息怒。穆罕默德见众人把自己忘了个干净，只觉扫兴，扯开嗓子嚎了起来，却没人理会。
到末了，来客见再争下去也是无益，便退了出来，心里可绝没有认输。
“你若管不住他，就得把这孩子送走。”这是她的临别之言，可是在一片喧闹中谁也没有听见。
===Original===
In the greatest agitation and distress of mind, Fitnah Khânum went back to her carriage and was driven home. She sought immediate audience of Murjânah Khânum. She had a warm affection for the wife of Yûsuf, and something like a passion for her little grandson. The need to take stern measures with them filled her eyes with tears; but her religion nerved her to perform a duty. A scene like that she had just witnessed must never be allowed to be repeated in a Muslim house.
Murjânah’s look grew worried as she heard the story.
“I have spoken to the dear one once, and fain would never speak to her again in chiding tones,” she murmured. “I pity her extremely, for she is alone among us and, I think, afraid. Consider what might have become of one of us if set down all alone amid the life of Europe! But it devolves on us to intervene since Yûsuf, as thou sayest, will not act against her.”
===Chinese===
菲特娜·哈努姆心乱如麻、悲愤交加，回到车上，一路驶回家中。她即刻求见穆尔贾娜·哈努姆。她对优素福之妻怀着一片慈爱，对小孙儿更近乎一种痴爱。想到必须对他们采取严厉手段，她双眼噙满泪水；可她的宗教给了她勇气去尽一份本分。方才亲眼所见的那幕景象，断不能容它在穆斯林宅中重演。
穆尔贾娜听着这番叙述，神色渐渐忧虑起来。
“那可爱的人儿，我说过她一回，实在不愿再拿责备的口气同她说话。”她低声道。“我太怜惜她了，她孤身处在咱们中间，而且，我瞧，心里是害怕的。想想看，咱们哪一个若被孤零零撂在欧洲的生活里，会落得什么下场！可既然优素福——如你所说——不肯对她采取措施，这干预之责便落到咱们肩上了。”
===Original===
As a result of Fitnah’s allegations, Murjânah Khânum called a council of the matrons of the family, including in the number her ex-slave, Gulbeyzah, who, as Barakah’s best friend, might plead her cause. But Gulbeyzah, when the case was laid before her, shrugged and cried:
“By Allah, it is true, she will destroy the child! How often have I tried to warn her! But she is haughty in her weakness, and impatient of advice. She loves the fawning voice of her own servants. She has greatly changed. Yûsuf Bey, however, is for discipline. She has more than once complained to me of his severity towards the boy.”
“What good is that when she consoles Muhammad afterwards, and talks about his cruel father? I have heard her,” put in Na’imah, who was a member of the conclave, though a child in years. She spoke with great excitement. All the ladies smiled. Murjânah Khânum touched her cheek affectionately, and called her the most excellent of little mothers. Murjânah added:
“The whole trouble, as I see it, is her want of faith. She has lost the comfort of her own religion, without acquiring ours in more than name. Is such a woman, full of cowardice and self-indulgence, fit to rear a Muslim? Unless she change her whole behaviour, which appears unlikely, for her strength is gone, will it be wise to leave the child with her?”
===Chinese===
因菲特娜的指控，穆尔贾娜·哈努姆召集族中主妇们开了一个议事会，连她昔日的女奴古尔贝雅扎也在与会之列——她是巴拉卡最要好的朋友，正好为巴拉卡申辩。可是案情一经摆到古尔贝雅扎面前，她耸耸肩，嚷道：
“凭安拉，这是真的，她会毁了这孩子！我多少回想要警醒她！可她软弱里偏带着傲气，听不得半句劝。她爱听自家仆人谄媚的声音。她变得太厉害了。倒是优素福·贝伊主张严管。她还不止一次向我抱怨他对那孩子太严厉。”
“那顶什么用？她过后照样哄穆罕默德，照样念叨他那狠心的父亲。我亲耳听见过的。”纳伊玛插嘴道——她虽还是个孩子年纪，却是议事会中的一员。她说得激动万分。众夫人都笑了。穆尔贾娜·哈努姆怜爱地摸摸她的脸颊，称她是最最出色的小母亲。穆尔贾娜又接着说：
“依我看，病根全在她没有信仰。她已失去自己宗教的慰藉，我们的宗教她也只得了个名分，未曾真得。这样一个满心怯懦、一味放纵的女人，配教养一个穆斯林吗？除非她改了全部的行止——看样子是不大可能了，她的心力已然耗尽——把这孩子留在她身边，可算明智吗？”
===Original===
“No!” came from all sides.
“Let his grandmother take charge of him,” said Leylah Khânum.
“God forbid!” cried Fitnah, “lest his mother hate me. Let him be given to the wisest, most benign of women, to our dear Murjânah.”
This motion won applause from all the ladies on the divan. They smiled to one another with rouged lips and kohled eyes. The room was beautifully cool and sweet, the cigarettes were of the best, the coffee excellent, and everyone enjoyed the sense of doing serious business.
===Chinese===
“不！”四下里齐声喊道。
“让他的祖母来照管他吧。”莱拉·哈努姆说。
“真主不容！”菲特娜嚷道，“免得他母亲恨我。把他交给最智慧、最仁慈的女人，交给咱们亲爱的穆尔贾娜吧。”
这项动议博得长榻上众夫人的一片喝彩。她们涂着口红的唇、描着眼黛的眸，相视而笑。屋里凉爽宜人、香风习习，香烟是上等的，咖啡也极佳，人人都享受着办一桩正经大事的意趣。
===Original===
Murjânah showed no fear of the responsibility. Assured of Fitnah Khânum’s gracious help, she said the task of civilizing the small boy would not displease her; but first the menfolk had to be consulted, and due warning must be given to the luckless mother. The ladies Fitnah, Leylah, and Murjânah were deputed to convey the verdict of the council to the Pasha and to Yûsuf Bey, who were invited to Murjânah’s rooms that evening.
Yûsuf displayed some irritation when he heard the charges.
“But my wife is a Frank!” he cried. “Allah knows it is but natural her ways should be different from ours.”
Murjânah took no notice of the interruption, but proceeded to relate the scene described by Fitnah. She mentioned also facts which he could not gainsay, as that Muhammad never kissed his father’s hand, that he sat down in his father’s presence without asking leave, and that he did not wait upon his parents as behoved a child. Yûsuf was silenced, though he looked annoyed. The Pasha wore his blandest diplomatic visage.
===Chinese===
穆尔贾娜对肩上的责任并无惧色。既有菲特娜·哈努姆慨然相助，她说，教化那小男孩的差事她并不嫌弃；只是先得同男人们商议，还得给那不幸的母亲一句正式的知会。菲特娜、莱拉、穆尔贾娜三位夫人受命向帕夏和优素福·贝伊转达议事会的裁决，当晚便请二人到穆尔贾娜屋里来。
优素福听了这些指控，面露几分愠色。
“可我的妻子是个法兰克人！”他喊道。“安拉知道，她的做派与我们不同，本是天经地义。”
穆尔贾娜不理会这阵插话，只管细细讲述菲特娜描绘的那幕情景。她还举出几件他无从辩驳的事实：穆罕默德从不吻父亲的手，当着父亲的面不经许可便坐下，也不像为人子者本分该做的那样侍奉父母。优素福哑口无言了，虽然满脸不情愿。帕夏则摆出他最温文的外交面孔。
===Original===
“With thy permission, O my lord,” said the great lady, “we have thought upon this matter and discussed it fully. If it be allowed for us to proffer a suggestion, it is that thou, who didst endow the bride of Yûsuf and stand in some sort for her people at the wedding, shouldst of thy gracious favour go and reason with her.”
The Pasha, fingering his beads, observed that God is merciful.
“Of thy kindness condescend to view the case,” she urged. “The boy is brought up utterly devoid of reverence. What is his fate when he goes out to face the world﻿—unmannerly, rebellious, a mad dog, a savage, detestable alike to great and small. Of what use will he be to El Islam? Oh, God forbid that he should grow like that﻿—a scourge to his two parents, and the scorn of others. It is to save him and his mother from the consequences of her folly that we beg thee of thy mercy to remonstrate with her, and if she will not hearken, to confide the education of the child to us. The word is spoken. May our Lord preserve thee ever!”
“May Allah help us all!” replied the Pasha gently.
===Chinese===
