“老爷容禀，”大夫人说，“我们已把这事思量过了，也议得十分透彻。倘若容许我们进上一言，那便是：当初优素福的新娘妆奁由您置办，婚礼上您又多少算作她的娘家人；如今务请老爷开恩，亲自去同她讲讲道理。”
帕夏捻着念珠，说真主是慈悲的。
“还求老爷发善心，屈尊体察这桩案子。”她催促道。“这孩子教养得全无敬畏之心。等他出到世人面前去闯荡——无礼、悖逆，一条疯狗，一个野人，尊卑老幼人人厌恶——那将是什么下场？他对伊斯兰教还有什么用处？啊，真主不容他长成那样——成为双亲的祸害、旁人的笑柄。正是为了救他和他的母亲，使她不因愚行自食其果，我们才求老爷发慈悲，去劝诫她；她若不听，便将孩子的教养托付给我们。话已出口。愿我们的主永远护佑老爷！”
“愿安拉佑助我们大家！”帕夏温和地答道。
===Original===
When he and Yûsuf had departed from the ladies and were returning through dim corridors to the selamlik, he inquired:
“What sayest thou?”
“They much exaggerate,” said Yûsuf warmly. “I keep an eye upon the boy. In course of time I shall correct his conduct.”
“Do it now!”
“What meanest thou? Why dost thou smile, my father?”
“I smile because I have observed that when the women take that tone﻿—‘of thy great kindness deign to listen,’ and the rest, there is no safe course for man but to obey. The boy is five years old and it is time he learnt behaviour. It is thy business, O my son; remonstrate with her.”
===Chinese===
父子二人辞别了夫人们，穿过昏暗的走廊往塞拉姆利克去，途中帕夏问道：
“你怎么说？”
“她们言过其实了。”优素福急切地说。“我盯着那孩子呢。日子久了，我自会纠正他的行止。”
“现在就纠正！”
“您这是什么意思？父亲，您为何发笑？”
“我笑，是因为我瞧出了一条规律：每逢女人们用起那种腔调——‘承您天大的慈悲，肯屈尊一听’，以及诸如此类——男人便只有顺从这一条安稳路可走。孩子五岁了，该学规矩了。这是你的事，我的儿子；去劝诫她吧。”
===Original===
“Nay, for they charged thee with the office.”
“It is thine of right.”
“Very gladly I resign it to thee. Thy words have more weight. And how can I turn round upon her suddenly? She will think me mad.”
“By Allah, I implore thee to perform thy duty.”
“By the Prophet, I beseech, adjure thee to befriend me now, as thou hast done from childhood. I will tell her to expect a visit from thee in the morning.”
“Well, God is greatest!” The good Pasha heaved a sigh, proclaiming his acceptance of the part allotted.
===Chinese===
“不，这差事是她们派给您的。”
“论本分，这是你的。”
“我巴不得让给您呢。您的话更有分量。可我怎能突然翻脸对她发作？她会当我疯了。”
“凭安拉，我恳求你尽你的本分。”
“凭先知起誓，我求告您、央告您，如今再帮儿子一把，就像儿子从小到大您一直帮衬的那样。我会告诉她，让她明早恭候您大驾。”
“唉，真主至大！”好心的帕夏长叹一声，算是认下了派给他的这个角色。
===Original===
Accordingly, next morning, arrayed in his official black frock-coat and newest fez, he waited upon Barakah, who received him with delight, evidently unsuspecting the real purpose of his visit. He thought that Yûsuf might at least have warned her. However, with a shrug, he opened business in his usual courtly and confiding manner, speaking in French, since servants are born eavesdroppers.
“Madame my daughter,” he began, “from the moment when you did my house the honour of espousing my dear son, I have been your servant and admirer; that is known. Yûsuf himself has not more tender veneration for your many virtues and accomplishments, so rare among us.” He went on to recite the panegyric of her general conduct as a wife and mother, paid tribute to her beauty and her piety, and then said, “But there is one small point on which I have to scold you. In your great goodness, your untiring kindness, you forget to claim the service due to you. Your slaves, as I have heard, grow fat and lazy, and though devoted to you﻿—as what soul would not be?﻿—do not keep your house so scrupulously clean and nicely ordered as the dwelling-place of such a treasure ought to be. I beg you to make hard your heart from time to time, to think a little less for others and more often for yourself. Even your own son should be brought up to reverence you, as one to whom he owes incalculable debts of gratitude. He should kiss your hand whenever he approaches, and bow and ask your blessing when he takes his leave. It is our custom for small children and, I think, a good one. How is the little one this morning? Am I not to be allowed to see him for one moment?”
Barakah clapped her hands and, when a slave appeared, gave order for Muhammad to be brought. He came in presently, escorted by his foster-mother, who stood and watched his progress to the dais with loving smiles. He was in docile mood, and Barakah detained him, giving the wife of Ghandûr leave to go.
===Chinese===
于是第二天早晨，他穿上那件官员的黑色礼服大衣，戴上崭新的塔布什帽，前去拜望巴拉卡。巴拉卡欢天喜地地迎他进门，显然没有疑心此行的真正来意。他心里想，优素福总该至少给她递个信儿。然而他耸了耸肩，便以他惯常那种谦和而推心置腹的态度谈起正事来。他说的是法语，因为仆人天生都是偷听的好手。
“我的女儿夫人，”他开口道，“打从您赏光下嫁我亲爱的儿子、光耀寒舍的那一刻起，我便一直是您的仆人和仰慕者，这是人人皆知的。就连优素福本人，对您那许多美德与才艺——在我们中间何等罕见的美德与才艺——也不怀抱着更温存的敬意。”接下来他朗朗诵起一篇颂词，赞她为人妻、为人母的种种品行，又称颂她的美貌与虔诚，然后说道：“不过有一桩小事，我须得说说您。您心肠太好，仁慈不知疲倦，却忘了索要别人应尽的服侍。我听说，您的奴仆们养得又肥又懒，虽说对您忠心耿耿——哪个魂儿能不忠呢？——却没能把您的宅子收拾得纤尘不染、井井有条，配不上这样一件珍宝的居所。我求您时时把心肠硬上一硬，少为别人打算一点，多为自己打算几回。就连您自己的儿子，也该教养得敬重您，把他看作欠下还不清恩债的人。他每次走近您，都该吻您的手；告退的时候，该躬身请求您的祝福。这是我们这儿对小孩子的规矩，依我看，是条好规矩。小家伙今早可好？能不能容我见他一小会儿？”
巴拉卡拍拍手，待女奴进来，便吩咐把穆罕默德带来。不多时他来了，由乳母领着；乳母站在一旁，含着慈爱的笑，望他一路走到矮台跟前。他这会儿乖顺，巴拉卡把他留在身边，打发甘杜尔的妻子退下了。
===Original===
“What fault is there to find in his behaviour?” she inquired in French, with arch defiance of the Pasha.
“None in the world,” he made reply, with vast politeness, “except that he has not kissed hands, nor waited your permission to sit down with us.”
“Absurd!” laughed Barakah.
“Absurd, in verity, like many of our customs. Only, my cherished daughter, he is one of us and must observe them. If you refuse to teach him the behaviour which we consider fitting for young children, I announce with deep regret that we must take him from you.”
===Chinese===
“他的举止有什么可挑剔的？”她用法语问，冲着帕夏娇俏地摆出一副叫阵的神气。
“天底下也挑不出毛病，”他极有礼貌地答道，“只是他未曾吻手，也未等您许可，便同我们坐下了。”
“可笑！”巴拉卡笑道。
“的确可笑，我们的许多规矩都是如此。只是，我珍爱的女儿，他是我们中间的一员，必须遵守这些规矩。您若不肯教他我们认为小孩子该有的规矩，那我只好深怀歉疚地宣布：我们必须把他从您身边带走。”
===Original===
Barakah gasped. She looked for signs of jesting; but the Pasha’s visage, though urbane, was serious.
“It has been told me,” he continued very gravely, “that this boy, when angry, kicks and curses his own mother. That is, for us others, a most dreadful crime, apart from the regard in which I hold you personally. My grandson must not be brought up to shame our house; the authority of the family must be exerted to avert dishonour. In fact, dear madame, if you will not punish him, he must be given for a while to someone who will do so.”
“But it is unheard of!” cried the mother wildly. “How can you think of such abominable cruelty? He is my child. My right to him exists in nature.”
“And is inalienable,” said the Pasha, with a splendid bow. “No one else can ever bear him, but someone else will have to educate him, since madame refuses.”
===Chinese===
巴拉卡倒抽了一口气。她在他脸上搜寻玩笑的痕迹；可帕夏的面容虽然温文，却是郑重的。
“有人告诉我，”他极其郑重地接着说，“这孩子发起怒来，对亲生母亲又踢又咒。撇开我对您个人的敬重不论，这对我们其余的人来说，是最最可怕的罪愆。我的孙儿万不可教养得叫我们的门第蒙羞；家族的权威必须行使起来，以杜绝这桩耻辱。事实上，亲爱的夫人，您若不肯责罚他，他就得暂时交给肯责罚他的人。”
“可这是闻所未闻的事！”做母亲的发狂般喊道。“你们怎么想得出这般伤天害理的酷行？他是我的孩子。我对他的权利，是天赋的、与生俱来的。”
“而且不可剥夺。”帕夏说着，风度翩翩地鞠了一躬。“再没有第二个人能生养他，可夫人既然不肯教，便只好由别人来教养他了。”
===Original===
“I am an Englishwoman. I shall complain to my Consul.”
“Believe me, dear madame, he will not listen. Your son is a Turkish subject; we inhabit Egypt; and in a case of this sort we allow no interference. The English are a race distinguished for intelligence and force of character; I beg you to display those qualities on this occasion.”
He left her in hysterics, clinging fiercely to her boy.
===Chinese===
“我是英国女人。我要向我的领事控告。”
“相信我，亲爱的夫人，他不会听的。令郎是土耳其臣民；我们身居埃及；这一类事，我们不容任何人干预。英国人以智慧与性格的刚毅著称于世；我恳请您在这一场合把贵民族的那些品质显露出来。”
他丢下她走了；她歇斯底里地发作着，死死搂住她的儿子。

## XXVII / 二十七
===Original===
No sooner was the Pasha gone than Umm ed-Dahak crept back softly to her mistress and cooed of consolation in her ear. Muhammad, who had started howling out of sympathy, she told to go and play with Ghandûr’s son.

“By Allah, it is all my fault, not thine,” she whispered. “I ought to have foreseen this grief and warned thee. Vex not thy soul at all! It is no matter! Praise be to Allah, we can change our policy. Tomorrow thou wilt beat thy son a little, and all the world will praise thy management.”

But the mother’s tears were flowing less from sense of guilt than for the helplessness, the lack of energy, which she discovered in herself at such a crisis. The call to make an effort paralysed her; she hung on Umm ed-Dahak like a frightened child, agreeing with loud sobs to the old woman’s statement that on the morrow they would make a new beginning.

That afternoon the little boy had been invited to Gulbeyzah’s house. His mother being too unwell to bear him company, he started off on foot in the custody of Ghandûr. Barakah adjured him to be very good and mind his manners, on which he kissed her with a most angelic smile.
===Chinese===
帕夏前脚刚走，乌姆·埃德-达哈克便蹑手蹑脚地溜回主母身边，凑在她耳边咕咕哝哝地劝慰。穆罕默德先前跟着嚎了一场，她便打发他去同甘杜尔的儿子玩儿。

“凭安拉，这全是我的错，不是你的。”她低声道。“我本该料到这场伤心事，早些提醒你。千万别再折磨自己了！没有关系的！感赞安拉，咱们还能换一套章法。明天你把儿子稍稍打上几下，天下人都要称赞你会管教呢。”

可是这位母亲的眼泪，与其说是出于负疚之感，不如说是为了她在这样的危急关头发现自己那般无依无靠、那般提不起劲来。要她振作行事的呼声反倒使她瘫软了；她像个吓坏了的孩子似的偎着乌姆·埃德-达哈克，一边大声抽泣，一边连声应和老婆子的话——明天，她们要重新来过。

那天下午，小家伙受邀到古尔贝雅扎家去玩。母亲身子不适，不能陪他同往，他便由甘杜尔领着，步行出发了。巴拉卡千叮万嘱，要他乖乖的、守着规矩，他听了，带着一脸天使般的微笑吻了吻她。
===Original===
“See how obedient and how good he is!” she wailed, her anguish breaking out afresh when he was gone. “How can they say he is not well brought up?”

“Without a doubt they have been misinformed,” cooed Umm ed-Dahak. “They have mistaken some exceptional disorder for his general conduct Mashallah! With but a touch of discipline, a very little teaching of good manners, thou wilt make him glorious, a pattern to all other children of this age.”

But Muhammad, who had set forth as an angel, returned a little devil, in a sullen rage. He would not speak a word, refused all nourishment, and sat aloof with frowning brows and gnashing teeth. Ghandûr, who brought him home, had sent in word that he had been a naughty boy and needed punishment. So Ghandûr also was his mother’s enemy.

Muhammad struck at all the women who came near him. He swore by the Most High to ravish every one of them, to tear their eyes out and cut off their hands and feet. The servants laughed at his ferocious impotence, which made things worse. When his mother came and knelt beside him, he at first repelled her; but after half an hour’s incessant coaxing she elicited his cause of grief.
===Chinese===
“瞧他多听话、多乖！”他走后，她的悲苦又发作起来，哭着说。“他们怎么能说他没教养好呢？”

“他们准是听了误传。”乌姆·埃德-达哈克柔声咕哝道。“他们把偶然一遭的失态，当成他平日的行止了。马沙安拉！只消稍稍管教一下，再教上一点点好规矩，你就能把他调教得光彩照人，做这一辈孩子个个的表率。”

可是穆罕默德出门时是个天使，回来却成了个小魔鬼，憋着一腔闷怒。他一句话也不肯说，什么吃食都不要，远远坐在一边，眉头拧着，咬牙切齿。送他回家的甘杜尔捎话进来，说他今天淘了气、该受罚。这么着，甘杜尔也成了他母亲的仇人。

穆罕默德见哪个女人走近就打哪个。他凭至高主起誓，要把她们个个都奸了，挖掉她们的眼睛，剁掉她们的手脚。仆人们笑话他那副凶狠而无能的样子，这更是火上浇油。母亲过来跪在他身边，起初被他一把推开；可是足足哄了半个钟头，她才哄出他伤心的缘由来。
===Original===
He had been pretending in his play to kill Gulbeyzah’s little girl﻿—“not really hurting her,” he blubbered, “though she shrieked like a dying fowl”﻿—when all at once, without the slightest provocation, a big boy assailed him, flung him down and knelt upon him, pinning his two hands. While he was in that position the ladies of the harem had come in and reviled him, praising his cruel persecutor as a hero. They had then conveyed him, kicking, to Ghandûr, who, like the beast he was, believed their lies.

“It is no matter, O beloved! Dry thy tears! Never﻿—never shalt thou visit that unfriendly house again,” his mother whispered.

Muhammad hiccuped on a sob, “Wallahi!” and fell again to gnashing of his teeth and moaning.

“See!” murmured Umm ed-Dahak. “See his dauntless spirit! By Allah, it is true, he must be tamed a little.”

That night he cried himself to sleep, and in the morning was snappish and morose, with furtive eyes. About the fourth hour of the day his mother missed him, and having sought through all the house in vain, conceived grave fears. She sent a eunuch to the Pasha’s palace, while Ghandûr cried the tidings through the quarter. Distraught with grief, she ran from room to room in the hottest hours of the day, always expecting to find Muhammad hiding somewhere. At last she sank down on a couch, exhausted.
===Chinese===
原来他玩耍时装作杀死古尔贝雅扎的小女儿——“并没有真弄疼她，”他哭哭啼啼地辩白，“可她尖叫得像只垂死的鸡”——冷不防一个半大男孩无端向他扑来，把他掀翻在地，跪压在他身上，按住他两只手。正当他趴在那儿动弹不得的时候，内宅的夫人们进来了，把他骂了个狗血喷头，倒把那个残忍欺侮他的家伙夸成英雄。随后她们又连推带拽地把他送到甘杜尔跟前，而甘杜尔那条畜生，竟信了她们的一派谎话。

“不要紧的，心肝儿！擦干眼泪！那家不友善的门户，你再——永远也不去了。”他母亲低声说。

穆罕默德打着哭嗝，迸出一句“凭安拉！”便又咬起牙来，呜呜咽咽个不住。

“瞧！”乌姆·埃德-达哈克咕哝道。“瞧他这股子天不怕地不怕的劲头！凭安拉，说真的，是得稍稍收收他的性子。”

当夜他哭着哭着睡着了，早晨醒来烦躁阴沉，眼珠子躲躲闪闪。白昼约莫第四个钟点，母亲发觉他不见了，满宅子找了个遍也不见踪影，顿时生出深重的恐惧。她打发一名阉人去帕夏府上，甘杜尔则满街区奔走呼号，把消息传遍四邻。她悲痛欲绝，在一天里最酷热的时辰，一间屋一间屋地奔跑，总盼着能发现穆罕默德躲在哪个角落里。末了她颓然跌坐在一张长榻上，力气耗尽了。
===Original===
The third hour after noon, as she was lying thus, Gulbeyzah and her durrahs were announced. They entered with much tragic exclamation. Then the truth was known. Muhammad had repaired that morning to their house and joined the children’s games, appearing friendly. But he was only waiting for his chance of vengeance; for, luring Gulbeyzah’s little girl apart, he stabbed her with a dagger he had got﻿—the Lord knew how!﻿—and cried to her big brother, “Thy account, O tyrant!”

His victim﻿—praise to Allah﻿—was not killed; nor even, by His mercy, maimed for life; but the ensuing uproar in the house may be imagined. The murderous child had been imprisoned in a room apart; the lord of the harem, when summoned, had sent at once for Yûsuf Bey, who was even now examining the culprit. Directly the responsibility had been lifted off them, they (the ladies) had flown straight to Barakah to assure her of their unimpaired affection. But﻿—merciful Allah!﻿—what was the world coming to? They sought refuge in Allah from such revengeful fury in so small a child.

“You must have used him very cruelly,” the mother cried. “He is by nature the most generous of children, not a criminal!”
===Chinese===
午后第三个钟点，她正这般躺着，有人来报，古尔贝雅扎和她的几位杜拉到了。她们进得门来，一路发出凄凄惨惨的惊呼。真相这才大白。那天早上，穆罕默德自己跑到她们家去了，装出一副友好的样子加入孩子们的游戏。其实他只是在等候复仇的时机；果然，他把古尔贝雅扎的小女儿诱到一边，用一把匕首——主才知道那匕首是打哪儿弄来的！——捅了她一刀，还冲她的大哥哥喊道：“暴君，跟你算账！”

他害的那孩子——感赞安拉——没有丧命；托主的慈悲，也没有落下终身残废；可是随后那宅子里掀起的一场大乱，可想而知。那行凶的孩子已被关进一间僻静的屋子；内宅之主闻讯唤回，当即派人去请优素福·贝伊，此刻他正在审问那小罪犯。责任一经从她们肩上卸下，她们（诸位夫人）便径直飞奔到巴拉卡这里，向她保证她们的情谊分毫未损。可是——慈悲的安拉啊！——这世道究竟要变成什么样？这么小的孩子竟怀着这般复仇的狂怒，她们惟求安拉庇佑。

“你们准是待他太狠毒了。”做母亲的嚷道。“他天生是天下孩子中最慷慨的一个，才不是什么罪犯！”
===Original===
At that, all four began to talk at once. Barakah talked against them, and the slave-girls and dependants, looking on, raised cries. The argument was at its height when Yûsuf was announced. The din ceased instantly. The four Circassians raised their mouth-veils in alarm and slipped away; the servants, silenced, went into another room.

Yûsuf entered, stern of countenance, dragging by the arm the peccant boy, whose mouth hung open, while his eyes stared wildly, fixed in the imbecility of abject fear.

Barakah fell down at her husband’s feet and screamed for mercy. He was obdurate.

“Let be, O woman!” he commanded. “My child, as trained by thee, is now a malefactor. He robs and kills; he breaks the law of hospitality. He stole a weapon from Ghandûr, his foster-father, and with it stabbed a little girl, whose guest he was. Henceforth I take him from thee, and give him to my mothers to be educated. Seek not to counteract their efforts, or by the Kaaba I will beat thee soundly as I now beat him.”

With that, he marched his son into an inner room, whence presently there issued sounds of blows and bitter wailing. Barakah ground her face upon the floor and stopped her ears.
===Chinese===
这一句惹得四个人齐声开了腔。巴拉卡抢白她们，一旁看热闹的女奴和仆从也跟着起哄叫嚷。正吵到最激烈的当口，有人通报优素福到了。喧哗顿时停歇。四位切尔克斯女人慌忙掩上面纱，悄悄溜了出去；噤了声的仆人们也退进另一间屋去。

优素福走了进来，面色铁青，手里拽着那犯了过失的孩子的胳膊。孩子嘴巴大张，两眼直瞪，定定地吓成了呆相。

巴拉卡扑倒在丈夫脚边，尖声哀告求饶。他却铁石心肠。

“放开，女人！”他喝令道。“我的孩子，经你一手调教，如今成了个作恶的。他又偷又杀；还践踏了宾主之道的法度。他从乳父甘杜尔那里偷了凶器，用它捅了他做客那一家的一个小姑娘。从今往后，我把他从你身边领走，交给我的母亲们去教养。你休想暗中掣她们的后腿，不然凭克尔白（Kaaba）起誓，我就像现在打他这样，结结实实打你一顿。”

说罢，他把儿子押进一间里屋；不多时，里面便传出一阵棍棒声和凄惨的哭嚎。巴拉卡把脸死死抵在地板上，双手捂住了耳朵。
===Original===
Muhammad, by his father’s orders, was shut off from her that night; and the next morning, before Yûsuf went to business, the Pasha’s harem carriage came to fetch the child. The eunuch brought a letter from Murjânah Khânum, inviting Barakah to come and give her counsel. But Barakah’s sole answer was an angry cry.

For several days she would not budge from her own rooms, refused to see the Pasha’s ladies when they called, and persisted, notwithstanding every argument, in posing as the victim of most foul injustice. And Umm ed-Dahak coaxed and soothed her all that while. At length, one day, Murjânah Khânum entered, unannounced; and Barakah, in act to rise and make indignant protest, was silenced by the sight of her own child.

“Go, O Muhammad, do what I have told thee,” said the old lady, with her hand on the boy’s shoulder. Whereat Muhammad went up gravely and bowed over his mother’s hand to kiss it, but she caught him in her arms, preventing him. He called out to Murjânah Khânum that it was not fair, and struggled to get free. She put him down, when he went on with his polite performance, kissed her hand and pressed his forehead to it, inquired after her health and asked her blessing; and then in the most courtly Arabic asked what he had done that one of his parents, who were dearer to him than all living creatures, should punish him by five days of avoidance.

“The harem of my grandfather, Muhammad Pasha Sâlih, depute me to request that thou wilt honour us this day and every day with thy most gracious presence, O my mother.”
===Chinese===
当夜，穆罕默德奉父亲之命与她隔开，不许相见；第二天早晨，优素福出门办事之前，帕夏家的深闺马车来接孩子了。阉人带来穆尔贾娜·哈努姆的一封信，邀巴拉卡过去，要给她些开导。可巴拉卡的回答只有一声怒喊。

一连好几天，她守在自家屋里不肯挪窝，帕夏家的夫人们来探望，她也一概拒之门外，任凭众人怎么说，只一口咬定自己是奇冤极枉的受害者。这期间，全靠乌姆·埃德-达哈克哄着劝着。终于有一天，穆尔贾娜·哈努姆不经通报走了进来；巴拉卡正待起身、发出愤怒的抗议，一看见自己的孩子，便哑然了。

“去吧，穆罕默德，照我教你的做。”老夫人说着，手搭在男孩肩上。于是穆罕默德郑重其事地走上前去，俯身要吻母亲的手，她却一把将他搂进怀里，不让他行礼。他朝穆尔贾娜·哈努姆喊这不公平，挣扎着要脱身。她把他放下，他便继续行完他那套礼貌的功课：吻了母亲的手，把额头贴在上面，问她的安康，请她的祝福；然后又用最文雅的阿拉伯语问道：他自己做了什么错事，竟叫他那比世上一切生灵都亲的双亲之一，用五天避而不理来责罚他。

“我的母亲，祖父穆罕默德·帕夏·萨利赫的内宅差孩儿前来恳请：今日以及往后每一天，都望母亲大人赏光，屈尊降临。”
===Original===
Before the termination of this speech and ceremony, Barakah was lying on her face in tears. She had thought, through the long hours of deprivation, that they were teaching her own child to disregard, if not to hate her. The relief was great. Murjânah sat beside her and caressed her, while Muhammad, standing reverently, looked concerned.

They took her with them in the carriage to the Pasha’s house, where, instead of reprobation, she met boundless sympathy. The ladies Fitnah and Murjânah told her all that had been done for the small boy, with evident anxiety for her approval. Muhammad showed her all the harem pets. He bade a slave-girl bring his own white doves. She brought three in her bosom. At his call, they flew to him and settled on his head and shoulders. There dwelt a parrot in the house of Na’imah, a monkey in the house of Fitnah Khânum, which she had to visit; as well as roving cats, and little birds in cages, and several street-dogs who came round for food. He also showed with pride his plot of garden, consisting of a box of scented herbs. And all the while that she was in the house, he waited on her like a page, kissing her hand whenever he could get a chance, and telling her the joy he felt in seeing her. When, left alone with him, she strove to whisper consolation, he shook his head decidedly, and told her: “O my mother, I have learnt to know that I was very wicked. Thou wast ever much too gentle and too kind with me. Allah knows how much I love thee﻿—my grandmothers have taught me that﻿—but it is well that I should be removed from thee a while and brought to reverence. It is not right that one so delicate as thou art should have a rough, ill-mannered boy to vex her.”

He loved her more than ever, it appeared, but thought her not much wiser than himself.
===Chinese===
这番话和这一套礼数还没行完，巴拉卡早已伏在地上泪流满面。在骨肉分离的漫长时日里，她一直以为他们是在教她自己的孩子轻贱她——若不是恨她。这一下如释重负。穆尔贾娜坐在她身旁抚慰她，穆罕默德毕恭毕敬地立着，满脸关切。

她们带她同车前往帕夏府上。在那里迎接她的不是责难，而是无尽的同情。菲特娜和穆尔贾娜两位夫人把为小家伙做的一切一一讲给她听，眼见得急切盼望她的首肯。穆罕默德领她看了内宅里所有的宠儿。他吩咐一个女奴去取他自己的白鸽；女奴怀里揣着三只带来，他一呼唤，鸽子便飞向他，落在他的头上肩上。纳伊玛家里住着一只鹦鹉，菲特娜·哈努姆家里养着一只猴子，都是她非去看不可的；此外还有四处游荡的猫、笼中的小鸟，和几只围着讨食的野狗。他还得意地领她看他的那片园圃——一箱香草。她在宅中的整个时辰，他都像个小侍从似的侍候她，一得机会便吻她的手，对她说见到她心里多么欢喜。待到只剩他们母子二人，她凑近了要低声安慰他，他却毅然摇头，对她说：“我的母亲啊，我已经明白自己从前太坏了。您对我一向太温柔、太宽厚了。安拉知道我多么爱您——这是我的祖母们教给我的——可我确实应该离开您一些时日，学着敬畏。像您这样娇贵的人，身边不该有个粗野无礼的男孩来惹您烦心。”

看来，他爱她胜过以往，只是觉得她并不比自己高明多少。
===Original===
Her fear of the stern rules of El Islam was tamed by reverence.

“By Allah, they are like the string and we the beads,” said Umm ed-Dahak, holding up a rosary to point her meaning. “Thirty-three beads of no intrinsic worth. If scattered, useless and soon lost. If strung together, a comely instrument of praise to God.”

Barakah watched Muhammad with humility; not jealous of the change which had been wrought by others, but choosing to regard it as a miracle direct from Heaven. His pride, once wayward, now was focused on his coming manhood. He told her all his thoughts, which seemed to her most wise. He waited on her hand and foot when in her presence. Yet in this deference there was a touch of condescension which was absent from the honour which he paid to Yûsuf. His father was his sovereign, she his tender care. Such wisdom in so small a child appeared miraculous. She worshipped his perfections while he bowed before her.
===Chinese===
她对伊斯兰教严规的畏惧，也因这一份敬畏而驯服了。

“凭安拉，他们好比那根线，咱们就是珠子。”乌姆·埃德-达哈克举起一串念珠，点明她的意思。“三十三颗珠子，论本身毫无价值。散了，便一无用处，转眼就丢。串在一起，便是一件赞美真主的体面法器。”

巴拉卡怀着谦卑望着穆罕默德；对旁人一手促成的这番转变，她并不嫉妒，宁愿把它看作直接来自上天的奇迹。他那曾经任性的骄傲，如今都凝注在即将到来的成人身上了。他把心里的话都讲给她听，那些话在她听来满含智慧。他在她跟前时，侍候得体贴入微。然而这份敬重里带着一丝居高临下的怜惜，那是他敬奉优素福时所没有的。父亲是他的君主，她则是他温存照看的人。这么小的孩子便有这般见识，简直是奇迹。她崇拜他的种种完美，而他向她躬身行礼。

## XXVIII / 二十八
===Original===
At seven years old Muhammad went to school. It was customary for the scions of great houses to be taught at home by private tutors, but the family council had decreed that so exceptional a child must feel the yoke of public discipline and mix with other boys as soon as possible. The school, just founded by the widow of a former ruler, was reckoned modern, for the simple reason that the scholars learnt geography and history, and handled other books as well as the august Koran.

Ghandûr led off Muhammad every morning, and brought him home at evening through the perils of the streets. Barakah’s thoughts were with him all day long; she liked to guess at his employment at a given moment; while Umm ed-Dahak painted flattering pictures of his skill in learning, the astonishment of all his masters at his brilliant genius.

When she was driven out to pay her calls, Barakah arranged beforehand with the eunuch that the carriage should pull up before the school. Then through the shutter she would watch the iron screen which filled each window-arch and listen to the drone of children’s voices.

The school was an octagonal kiosk of marble, touching the wall of a world-famous mosque. Its salient bulk half throttled an important thoroughfare, forming a narrow strait where traffic battled, and on each side a little bay or backwater where the carriage could draw up without obstruction. There, underneath the windows with their arabesques of iron screen-work, sat street sorcerers with trays of sand before them, venders of sugarcane and slabs of bread and divers nuts; and holy beggars slumbered in the shade. Barakah knew exactly where Muhammad had his seat and, waiting upon that side, watched a certain opening in the ironwork, from which there presently emerged a little hand. It fluttered for a moment and was then withdrawn. She waited for a second signal and a third before she gave the order to drive on.
===Chinese===
穆罕默德七岁那年上了学。世家大族的子弟，照例是延师在家受教的，可家庭会议却断定：如此出类拔萃的孩子，必须尽早套上公众管教的轭，同别的男孩混在一处。这所学堂是一位先代君王的遗孀新近创办的，被看作新派，理由极其简单——学生们除了至尊的《古兰经》，还学地理、历史，还捧读别样的书。

每天早晨，甘杜尔领着穆罕默德上学去，傍晚再穿过街市的种种凶险把他领回家来。巴拉卡的心思整天跟着他；她喜欢猜他此时此刻正在做什么；乌姆·埃德-达哈克则描出一幅幅动听的图景：说他学业何等聪敏，诸位先生又如何为他璀璨的天才惊倒。

每逢坐车出门拜客，巴拉卡总事先同阉人讲妥，叫马车在学堂前面停一停。于是她便隔着遮窗的板叶，凝望那填满一道道窗拱的铁栅栏，细听孩子们嗡嗡不歇的诵书声。

学堂是一座八面的大理石凉亭，紧贴着一座举世闻名的大清真寺的院墙。它凸出的躯体把一条要道扼得半死，挤成一段狭窄的海峡，车马人流在那里争挤厮缠；两侧各有一湾小小的静水，马车可以不受阻碍地靠拢。就在那花叶纹样的铁栅窗下，坐着街头的术士，面前摆着一盘盘细沙；还有卖甘蔗、大片面包和各色坚果的小贩；圣洁的乞丐们在荫凉里酣睡。巴拉卡确切知道穆罕默德的座位在哪一边，便守定那一侧，盯住铁栅上一个小小的孔洞；不多时，一只小手从洞里探出来，扑动了一下，又缩了回去。她等到它发出第二回、第三回信号，才吩咐驱车前行。
===Original===
At school Muhammad’s aim was to excel by all means. The counsels of Murjânah Khânum, who used religious and inspiring words, had fired his brain. He had but one ambition now﻿—to please his father. He would prove the best of Muslims, the most zealous, the most learned, and then his father would forget his former wickedness. In pursuance of this end he chafed at every obstacle and was infuriated by stupidity or sloth in others. He beat his foster-brother more than once through mere impatience, and in the end put zeal into that vacant but receptive youth. And Barakah, whose worship of her paragon extended to the son of Ghandûr as his shadow, became the confidante of all their thoughts and projects.

The report which the headmaster made to Yûsuf Bey after Muhammad’s first few weeks at school was satisfactory.

“The boy, thy son,” remarked the reverend man, “is highly gifted and extremely diligent. Inshallah, he will live to be a light to El Islam, a glory to this land of Masr, and a worthy slave of the Most High. We have only one small fault to find with his behaviour, which is that, in his eagerness, he answers questions we address to other boys, and is inclined to argue with the teacher as if instruction were for him alone.”

His mother was delighted with this verdict, whose one restriction seemed to her the highest praise. She began to cherish visions of his future greatness, and with the aid of Umm ed-Dahak built grand castles in the air.
===Chinese===
在学堂里，穆罕默德的目标是无论如何都要出人头地。穆尔贾娜·哈努姆那些带着宗教意味、鼓舞人心的话，早把他一腔脑子点着了。他如今只剩一个抱负——讨父亲的欢心。他要证明自己是最出色的穆斯林，最虔诚热忱，最有学问，到那时，父亲便会忘掉他从前的顽劣。为着这个目标，他遇着任何障碍都焦躁难耐，见别人愚蠢或怠惰便怒不可遏。他不止一次纯粹出于不耐烦而动手打他的乳兄弟，到头来竟把一股热忱灌进了那个头脑空空、却肯受教的少年心里。巴拉卡对自己心中完人的崇拜，连带着推及甘杜尔的儿子——他是那完人的影子——于是成了他们全部心思与谋划的知心人。

穆罕默德入学头几个星期之后，校长向优素福·贝伊所作的汇报，令人满意。

“这孩子，令郎，”那位可敬的先生说，“天分极高，又极其勤勉。若安拉意欲（Inshallah），他将来必是伊斯兰的一盏明灯，埃及（Masr）大地的荣光，至高主当之无愧的奴仆。他的品行，我们只有一处小疵可挑：他性子太切，我们问别的孩子的问题，他也抢着作答，还爱同先生争辩，仿佛这课业只为他一个人开设似的。”

他母亲听了这番考语，满心欢喜；那里面仅有的一个小小保留，在她听来竟像是至高的褒奖。她开始萦怀起儿子日后显贵的种种幻景，又在乌姆·埃德-达哈克的帮衬下，筑起一座座堂皇的空中楼阁。
===Original===
“Inshallah, he will rise to rule in Egypt; he will be the right hand of the Khedive, the chief vizier, the leader of the armies; the sword and shield of El Islam, the scourge of Allah on the heathen and all infidels.”

Thus Umm ed-Dahak, seated on the floor beside her mistress; who, reclining on the dais at ease with her narghile, removed the amber mouthpiece from her lips to sigh, “Inshallah!”

In order to be worthy of her son’s magnificence, Barakah had evolved a fine romantic history out of her own past. The transmutation of that dross to gold took place so naturally that she was not aware of lying when she told her crony that she was of royal birth. Gentility being something inconceivable by Umm ed-Dahak, who knew of no inherited prestige save that of an Emir, she was obliged, in order to convey the status of a governess, to compare it with the lot of fallen princes. From thence to the invention of a principality was but a step. The remonstrance of the Consul and of Mrs. Cameron against her marriage became the rage of a fanatical and angry nation. The noise of her conversion had disturbed all Europe, and nearly brought on a religious war. Let Umm ed-Dahak ask the Pasha, if she doubted!
===Chinese===
“若安拉意欲，他将崛起而统治埃及；他要做赫迪夫的左右手，做首席维齐尔，做大军的统帅；做伊斯兰的剑与盾，做安拉降在异教徒和一切不信者头上的鞭子。”

这话是乌姆·埃德-达哈克坐在主母身旁的地板上说的；主母斜倚在矮台上，悠然吸着水烟，把琥珀烟嘴从唇边移开，叹了一声：“若安拉意欲！”

为了配得上儿子的煌煌气象，巴拉卡从自己的过去里演化出了一段美妙的罗曼史。那些渣滓点化成金的过程如此自然，以致当她告诉老姐妹自己出身王室时，浑然不觉自己在撒谎。贵裔身份这种东西，是乌姆·埃德-达哈克构想不来的——她所知道的世袭尊贵，只有埃米尔（Emir）那一种——于是巴拉卡要说明家庭女教师的身份，只好拿落难王孙的际遇来比照。由此再凭空造出一个公国，也不过一步之遥。领事和卡梅伦夫人对她那桩婚事的劝阻，变成了一个狂热而愤怒的民族的一场雷霆之怒。她改宗的风波搅动了整个欧洲，几乎酿成一场宗教战争。乌姆·埃德-达哈克若是犯疑，只管去问帕夏！
===Original===
But Umm ed-Dahak was not of the kind who doubt. For her, romantic fiction was more worth than fact. She accepted this, as she accepted every tale, artistically, and even added likely details unperceived of Barakah.

The servants came to know the weakness of their mistress and addressed her as “Emirah” with all kinds of ceremony. The disease was catching; they themselves became infected. With the blacks illusion took the form of demoniacal possession. Each one began to brag of “him who dwells in me,” his power and jurisdiction over other demons. Barakah overheard them talking of their inmates, discussing pedigrees and finding out relationships which had existence only in the world of jinn. She once complained of their insanity to Fitnah Khânum, and asked what could be done to put a stop to it.

“I know one cure for devils as for every other illness of unmarried girls, and that is matrimony,” was the answer. “Among us here it is a sovereign remedy; among the Franks it seems less efficacious.”
===Chinese===
可是乌姆·埃德-达哈克不是那等爱犯疑的人。在她看来，罗曼蒂克的虚构比事实更贵重。她像对待每一个故事那样，以一种艺术鉴赏的态度把它全盘受了，甚至还添上些合情合理的细节，叫巴拉卡毫无察觉。

仆人们渐渐摸着了主母这桩软处，便满口礼数地称她“埃米拉”（Emirah，公主之谓）。这病是会过人的；他们自己也染上了。在黑奴们身上，幻觉走的是邪魔附体一路。人人各自夸耀起“住在我身体里的那一位”，夸他的神通，夸他辖制其他群魔的权柄。巴拉卡不止一次听见他们谈论自家体内的住客，替它们排起世系、攀起亲眷——那些亲眷只在恶灵（jinn）的世界里存在。她曾向菲特娜·哈努姆抱怨他们疯癫，问有什么法子才能煞住。

“魔鬼这病，同闺中姑娘的一切别的病症一样，我倒知道一剂药，那就是嫁人。”对方这样回答。“在咱们这儿，它是万应灵丹；在法兰克人那边，效力似乎就要差些。”
===Original===
“Among the Franks such foolish fancies are unknown,” laughed Barakah, when Fitnah Khânum sniffed, but said no more.

“The poor one is herself possessed,” she told Murjânah afterwards. “The servants say a princess of the jinn inhabits her; and she complains because they also harbour inmates. She ought to see a proper exorcist.”

The ladies all agreed to pity her. But Barakah, unconscious of their criticism, pursued her path of dreams with Umm ed-Dahak.
===Chinese===
“法兰克人那里，可没有这些痴心妄想。”巴拉卡笑道。菲特娜·哈努姆嗤了嗤鼻，却没有再说下去。

“可怜的人，她自己就附了魔。”她事后对穆尔贾娜说。“仆人们讲，有位恶灵的公主住在她身子里；她还抱怨呢，怪旁人家里也收留房客。她真该去请个正派的驱魔人才是。”

夫人们众口一词，都答应怜悯她。巴拉卡却不曾觉察她们的讥评，依旧同乌姆·埃德-达哈克一道，走她的做梦之路。
===Original===
“May fire consume the infidels who thus dethroned thee, who robbed thee of thy land and honours!” cried the latter. “O day of milk, when thou didst fly for succour to the Muslimîn! They will avenge thy wrongs, inshallah, in the time to come. Thy son shall win his birthright back with fire and sword.﻿ ﻿… Mashallah! Do I not behold his state? I see him on a throne, with courtiers prone before him﻿—Muhammad Yûsuf Pasha, styled ‘the Great’﻿—nay, what say I?﻿—the Emir, the King Muhammad in virtue of his mother’s dignity!” cried Umm ed-Dahak with dilated eyes. “By Allah, the most splendid scene I ever witnessed! He is Grand Vizier!”

But the downfall of the Khedive’s favourite, occurring at this epoch, dashed the ardour of the seers, and caused them in alarm to change their vision. The man, whose pomp had served them for a measure of Muhammad’s greatness, disappeared from life. The story ran that, having grown too great, he had been trapped by order of his loving master, accommodated with a weighted sack, and dropped into the Nile. The tidings caused a flutter in the world of women like that of seafarers beholding shipwreck. For the favourite’s death involved the ruin of a great harem, boasting its troupes of dancers and of trained musicians, lavish of entertainment and of gay repute. Its members, far too many to be all beloved, had, some of them, found vent in wild amours which furnished thrilling stories to more lucky women. Now all the slaves were scattered among other houses; the ladies, owning private property, returned to their relations pending further marriage. The great man’s children were reduced to mediocrity; his honours and emoluments divided up among a score of courtiers; his name became a byword for pride’s fall.
===Chinese===
“愿烈火烧了那些异教徒，把你掀下宝座、夺了你土地和尊荣的异教徒！”老婆子喊道。“啊，牛奶般的好日子，那时你飞奔到穆斯林们跟前求庇护！他们终要替你昭雪冤仇的，若安拉意欲，来日方长。你的儿子要以火与剑赢回他的生来权利。……马沙安拉！我不是亲眼望见他的气派了么？我望见他高踞宝座，朝臣们匍匐在他脚前——穆罕默德·优素福·帕夏，尊号‘伟人’——不，我说到哪里去了？——是埃米尔，是穆罕默德国王，凭着他母亲的尊贵而称王！”乌姆·埃德-达哈克睁大眼睛喊道。“凭安拉，这是我平生所见最气派的场面！他是大维齐尔！”

偏偏在这个当口，赫迪夫的宠臣倒了台，浇灭了两位女先知的火热心肠，叫她们惊慌之下改换了幻象。那人煊赫的排场，原是她们用来度量穆罕默德前程的一把尺，如今他人已从人间消失。传言说，他权势太大，被那位仁爱的主子下旨诱捕，赏了他一条坠重的麻袋，沉入了尼罗河。这消息在女人的世界里激起一阵颤动，好比航海人望见海难。因为那宠臣一死，牵连着一座大内宅的败落——那宅子以成班的舞娘和受过调教的乐师自夸，宴饮豪奢，艳名在外。宅中人口太多，断不能个个得宠，其中一些便在放纵的情事里寻出路，倒给运气更好的女人们供了多少惊心动魄的谈资。如今奴隶们已四散分拨到各家宅里；几位夫人自有私财，回娘家静候再嫁。大人物的孩子沦为庸常之辈；他的头衔与俸禄被二十来位朝臣瓜分；他的名字成了骄者必跌的一句口头禅。
===Original===
“Wallahi, our beloved must not follow in his steps too closely. Allah forbid!” said Umm ed-Dahak solemnly. And forthwith she began to make another forecast, with frequent “Inshallahs” and “Mashallahs,” to rob it of all taint of boastfulness. “He goes up gently, rousing no suspicion in the ruler, winning the people’s voice, as did Muhammad Ali. Then, when the times are ripe, he asks the Sovereign and his courtiers to a banquet and cuts all their throats. Then he ascends the throne and does good deeds, till all men praise the Maker for his rare benevolence. And thou, his mother, wilt reside in splendid state, and when the great ones of the English come with gifts for thee, thou wilt spit upon them and repel them with thy little foot. Inshallah!”

Barakah would be a widow in those days, by Allah’s mercy. A queen, she would of course have many lovers. Did she desire a man﻿—one word, and he was hers as quick as lightning! And Umm ed-Dahak would be ever at her call to spread the net for goodly youths and guard her secret.

“But I shall be too old by then!” laughed Barakah.

“Please Allah, no!” cried the old woman, a trifle vexed at being brought to earth. “Thou wilt be still quite youthful. See thee now: what beauty, what a youthful figure! By Allah, almost wicked in a mother! Thou dost not grow old.”
===Chinese===
“凭安拉，咱们心爱的这一位，可不能跟得太紧。安拉不容！”乌姆·埃德-达哈克郑重地说。随即她又开始另作一番预言，“若安拉意欲”“马沙安拉”念个不住，好把这预言身上一切自夸的气味都洗掉。“他稳稳当当地往上走，不惹君主的疑心，赢取万民的称许，就像穆罕默德·阿里那样。待到时机成熟，他请君主和满朝文武赴一场御宴，把他们的喉咙统统割断。然后他登上宝座，广行善事，直到万民同声称颂造物主，赞他世所罕有的仁德。而你这做母亲的，将起居于煊赫的荣华之中；英国的达官贵人们带着礼物来叩门，你要啐他们一脸，抬起你的小脚把他们踢开。若安拉意欲！”

到了那些日子，托安拉的慈悲，巴拉卡该已守寡。既是王后，自然会有许多的情郎。她看上了哪个男人——只消一句话，那人便如闪电般归她所有！乌姆·埃德-达哈克也随唤随到，替她张网罗致俊俏的青年，替她守住秘密。

“可到那时候，我就太老啦！”巴拉卡笑道。

“托安拉的福，才不会呢！”老婆子嚷道，被这么一下子拽回地上，未免有几分恼。“你到那时还年轻得很呢。瞧瞧你现在：怎样的容貌，怎样一副年轻的身段！凭安拉，做母亲的人这样，几乎算是罪过！你根本不见老。”
===Original===
In fact, her shape, though something fat, was not ungainly, like that of younger women leading the same life. She took no care of it, conforming to the harem custom for women who bear children to let beauty go. “The time and purpose of the bloom is past, the fruit succeeds, more noble,” they assured her. She saw the rarest beauties, like Bedr-ul-Budûr, already changing into fat old women. Compared with them she felt still young and comely. But when, her carriage rolling on the Gîzah road, she saw real Frankish women, riding, driving, she felt a raddled and unwieldy hag. There was one Englishwoman in particular who often passed her, driving a light dogcart with a Nubian groom behind﻿—straight as a lance and trim of waist, with rosy cheeks and bright eyes under grizzled hair. A creature of free air and open sunlight, the shuttered, perfumed shade could not produce her like. A jealousy near hatred stirred in Barakah.

One evening Yûsuf, thinking to amuse her, had sent her with his sisters and Muhammad to the new opera-house which the Khedive had built to please the European visitors, and also to provide His Highness with relays of mistresses. There, in a harem box behind a screen, she smoked cigarettes and listened to what seemed mere senseless screeching to one who had admired the voice of Tâhir. The opera was *Don Giovanni*. Never had she witnessed a performance so stilted, artificial, and absurd. She quite agreed with the remarks of her companions, who, after their first wonder at the building and the lighted stage, yawned openly and called it simple madness. Yet the entertainment was no bad one to the taste of Europe, as she knew from the applause of people in the unscreened boxes, where barefaced, brilliant women sat and stared about them. The mere existence of those women there in Cairo, transgressing every native rule of conduct, was an insult. The freshness even of the old ones made her conscious of decay. When the girls after the second act proposed to go, she agreed gladly. Muhammad screamed to stay, and had to be transported bodily by Barakah, while one of his young aunts held her hand upon his mouth. A very small boy at the time, he had supposed the scene was laid in hell, and all the hideous screams of the performers denoted pangs of tortured infidels.
===Chinese===
其实她的身段虽略显丰腴，倒还不算笨拙，不像过着同样生活的更年轻的女人那样。她并不加意保养，依着内宅的规矩，生了养了孩子的女人尽可任凭美貌自去。“花期连同花开的用意都已过去，接下去是果实，更为高贵。”她们这样宽慰她。她眼见那些绝色的美人，比如白德尔-布杜尔，已经在变成一个个肥胖的老太婆。同她们一比，她自觉仍旧年轻、仍旧标致。可是每当她的马车滚行在吉萨大道上，望见真正的法兰克女人——骑马的、驾车的——她便觉得自己是个脂粉斑驳、臃肿笨拙的老丑婆。其中有一个英国女人尤其常从她身旁掠过，驾着一辆轻便的双轮马车，车后跟着个努比亚马夫——腰身笔直如枪，收束利落，灰白的头发底下双颊红润、两眼明亮。那是个自由空气和朗朗日光养出的造物，这闭着窗板、熏着香气的幽暗处所，生不出她这样的人物。一种近乎憎恨的妒意，在巴拉卡心里翻涌起来。

一天晚上，优素福想给她解闷，打发她随着自己的姐妹们和穆罕默德，去看新落成的歌剧院——那是赫迪夫为取悦欧洲游客而建的，顺带也替殿下本人备下一班接一班的情妇。在那里，她坐在屏风后面的女眷包厢里，抽着香烟，听着那在她这双耳朵——欣赏过塔希尔歌喉的耳朵——听来纯属无谓嘶叫的东西。那出歌剧是《唐·乔万尼》。她从未见过这样矫揉造作、虚虚假假、荒唐透顶的演出。她完全赞同同伴们的议论：她们对那座殿堂和灯烛通明的舞台惊叹过一阵之后，便公然打起呵欠，说这简直是发疯。然而这娱乐按欧洲的口味倒也不坏——这是她从无遮拦的包厢里众人的喝彩声中得知的；那些包厢里坐着不知羞掩、光彩照人的女人，四下里顾盼。这些女人单是身在开罗、践踏着本地一切的行为规矩，便已是一种侮辱。连她们中年长些的那份新鲜气，也叫她意识到自己的衰败。第二幕过后，姑娘们提议回去，她欣然赞同。穆罕默德尖叫着不肯走，只得由巴拉卡整个人抱了出去，他的一位年轻姑母一路用手捂着他的嘴。他那时还是个很小的孩子，只当戏里的场景设在地狱，演员们那一声声凄厉的嚎叫，都是受刑的异教徒在哀号。
===Original===
Muhammad, for his mother’s sake, abhorred the English; and yet he loved his mother, who was of that race. He reconciled these warring passions by supposing the existence of a race of Muslims in the British Isles.

One day, when he was ten years old, he came home with a face of indignation, demanding, “O my mother, is it not quite true that the English nation is as strong and warlike as the French, and nowise subject to the lord of Paris?”

“True, O my son.”
===Chinese===
穆罕默德看在母亲份上，憎恶英国人；可他又爱他的母亲，而母亲正是那一族的人。他把这两股交战的情感调和开来的法子，是假定英伦诸岛上住着一支穆斯林的种族。

他十岁那年，有一天回到家来，满脸义愤地质问道：“我的母亲啊，英国民族同法兰西民族一样强大、一样尚武，决计不受巴黎之主的辖制——这话难道不是千真万确的么？”

“千真万确，我的儿子。”
===Original===
“By Allah, that is what I said. We were arguing, a dozen of us, after school. They all opposed me, stating that the French were much the greater and more civilized. I, sure of my contention, asked a master who stood by. He foolishly asserted that the French were stronger. I informed him of his error in all courtesy, when, to my horror, he began abusing me, detained me in the school an hour against my will, and himself remained to gloat on my imprisonment.

“Nor is that all. No sooner was I free than I went to the house of the principal and made complaint of the injustice. He said﻿—the malefactor!﻿—thus escaping from the question, that it was a sin for true believers to quarrel for the sake of infidels. I told him there were Muslimîn among the English, as witness my own mother, who is one of them. He had the rudeness to declare thou art a convert. It was all that I could do to keep from plucking at his beard. I shall ask my father to remove me straightway from a school where lying insults and oppression thus prevail.”

“The principal spoke truth. I am a convert,” murmured Barakah, hanging her head through fear of her son’s shame.
===Chinese===
“凭安拉，我正是这么说的。放学之后，我们十来个人争论了一场。他们都反对我，断言法兰西人强大得多、开化得多。我笃定自己有理，便请教了站在近旁的一位先生。他昏聩地断言法兰西人更强。我客客气气地向他指出他的错处，哪知——真吓死我了——他竟破口骂起我来，违背我的意愿，把我强留在学堂里关了一个钟头，自己还守在一旁，幸灾乐祸地看我受这一场监禁。

“这还不算完。我一脱开身，便到校长宅上，告了这场不公道的状。他说——那作恶的！——就这样把话头滑了过去，说信士们为了异教徒的缘故起争执，是一桩罪过。我告诉他，英国人里面也有穆斯林，我自己的母亲便是明证，她就是其中之一。他竟无礼到宣称你是个皈依的人。我好容易才忍住，没有伸手去揪他的胡子。我要请父亲即刻把我从这所学堂里领出去——这里谎言、侮辱与欺压横行到这般田地。”

“校长说的是实话。我是皈依了的人。”巴拉卡喃喃道，因怕儿子羞惭而垂下了头。
===Original===
“Merciful Allah!” cried Muhammad, greatly shocked.

But in a moment he recovered from the blow. Kissing her hand, he murmured fondly:

“Be not downcast, O beloved, it is not thy fault. My comrades sneer at converts; but no matter. I shall still maintain that thou wert born in the right way. Thou art still my dearest mother, loved and honoured.”

The lover-like, protecting air became him rarely.
===Chinese===
“慈悲的安拉啊！”穆罕默德叫出声来，大吃一惊。

可一转眼，他便从这一击底下缓了过来。他吻着她的手，深情地低语道：

“别难过，心爱的，这不是你的过错。同学们讥笑皈依的人；可是不要紧。我仍旧要一口咬定，你生来就走在这正道上了。你永远是我最亲爱的母亲，又受疼爱，又受敬重。”

那副情人似的、护佑人的神气，在他身上难得地相称。

## XXX / 三十
===Original===
Yûsuf Pasha was upon the point of going out when his son was shown into his presence in his private room. He smiled upon the stripling’s prayer to be allowed to fight, but said:

“No, no, my son. Thou art too young as yet. Wait till the war is ended and then join the fray.”

With that he patted the boy’s cheek, bestowed his blessing on him, and went out, little guessing that he left despair behind him. A carriage waited for him at the door. An armed slave scrambled up beside the driver. It was the hour of sunset. Two months since the ways would have been merry at that hour. But now the passengers were few and fully armed; they looked suspicious and, where groups were formed, the talk seemed guarded. A curse had fallen on the happy city. The sunset blushed on her high roofs, the crescent flashed on all her spires and domes, and in the gullies which were streets lay depths of shade; yet no one felt the rapture of the evening.
===Chinese===
优素福·帕夏正要出门，他的儿子却被引进私室，来到他面前。少年恳求准许他上前线打仗，他微微一笑，却说道：

“不行，不行，我的儿子。你还太年轻。且等到战事结束，再去加入厮杀不迟。”

说完他拍了拍孩子的面颊，赐福于他，便出门去了，全没想到自己身后留下的是一场绝望。一辆马车候在门前，一名持械的奴隶爬上车夫旁边的座位。这正是日落的时辰。两个月前，一到这个钟点，条条大路上本是一片欢愉。如今行人寥寥，个个全副武装；他们神色多疑，偶有人聚作一团，谈话也似乎字字留神。一道诅咒落在了这座快乐的城市上。落日在她高高的屋顶上染出红晕，新月在所有的尖塔和穹顶上闪光，一道道充作街道的深巷里积着浓浓的暗影；然而谁也感受不到黄昏的欢悦了。
===Original===
Yûsuf, lolling in the carriage, gnawed his black moustache and cursed the revolutionaries from his heart. He had attained the wisdom which comes easily to middle age, hated disturbance and distrusted novelty. The nervous passion which had marked his youth still dwelt in him; but he reserved its transports for the calls of private life; having another wife besides the Englishwoman, and two concubines, whom he kept in the provincial centre whither public business often called him. Politics had been for him a well-ruled game, on which a man would be a fool to waste vitality. As a functionary, he had lounged on sofas, telling beads, dictating orders to his secretaries, at ease except when called before superiors; until this military rising scared his soul. Its swiftness and success seemed downright fiendish.

One day a painstaking, obedient native officer had been selected by the Khedive Ismaîl to organize a riot hostile to the Frank commissioners. He seemed so trusty and discreet that Ismaîl forbore to execute him for the trifling service. Within two years he was the idol of the native soldiers, the spokesman of their grievances against the foreign Turks; in five, he was the incubus and dread of Egypt, first Minister of War and now Dictator. That first employment recommended him to schemers as one who did not fear to lead rebellion. Straightforward and excitable, extremely zealous in whatever charge he undertook, he was thrust forward by the clever ones to posts of hazard. His prompters, Asiatics, saw the bounds of his intelligence and thought to keep him in their hands, a priceless instrument. But they had not allowed for the inflation of the African, who, being once exalted, swelled and swelled until his greatness overawed its very founders.

An honest man and a good Muslim, Ahmad Arâbi lacked the cleverness of the conspirator; nor was he one. The sordid plots which guided his career were spun behind him; while he pressed onward with clear brow and conquering smile﻿—a doomed man, in the view of calm spectators.
===Chinese===
优素福半躺半倚在马车里，咬着他的黑髭，从心底里咒骂革命党。他已经达到了中年轻易便能拥有的那份明智，憎恶骚乱，猜疑新奇。当年标志着他青年时代的暴烈性子依然留在他身上；只是那激情的发作，他专门留给私生活的需要——除那英国女人之外，他还另有一房妻室，还有两房姬妾，都安置在外省的省治——公务常常把他召到那里去。政治于他向来是一场规则井然的博弈，谁肯为它耗损元气，谁便是傻子。做官的时候，他向来歪在沙发里捻着念珠，向秘书们口授命令，除了被召到上官面前，无不安闲自在；直到这场兵变吓着了他的魂灵。它的迅疾与得手，简直像魔鬼在背后驱使。

当年，赫迪夫伊斯梅尔曾经拣选了一个勤勉恭顺的土著军官，叫他去组织一场敌视法兰克专员们的暴乱。这人是那样可靠、那样口风紧，以至为着那样一桩微末的差使，伊斯梅尔竟没有把他处死。不出两年，他已是土著士兵的偶像，是替他们向外国土耳其人鸣不平的代言人；不出五年，他已是埃及的梦魇和恐怖，先做了陆军大臣，如今做了独裁者。那头一件差使，向弄权的谋士们举荐了他：这是一个不怕领头造反的人。他率直而易怒，凡承担的职分无不尽心竭力，于是被聪明人推到一个个凶险的位子上去。他背后的提线人，都是些亚洲人，看准了他智力的边界，一心要把他攥在掌中，当一件无价的工具。可他们没有算到这非洲人的自我膨胀：他一旦得势，便一阵接一阵地胀大起来，直到他那份伟大镇住了亲手造就它的诸位始作俑者。

艾哈迈德·阿拉比是个诚实的人、善良的穆斯林，却没有阴谋家的机巧；他本来也不是阴谋家。引导他一生行止的那些龌龊计谋，全是在他背后纺出来的；他自己只管昂然向前，额头明朗，笑容必胜——在冷静的旁观者看来，这是个劫数已定的人。
===Original===
Yûsuf had known Arâbi for some years and liked him personally; but the Khedive Muhammad Tewfik was his friend from childhood. Entreated by the agitators to take office with them, he had referred the question to the good Khedive, who begged him to accept the post thus offered, that he (Muhammad Tewfik Pasha, Lord of Egypt) might have one friend among his so-called servants. Tied by his duties, he had not fled to Alexandria with the Sovereign; but remained behind in an absurd position, a member of the rebel government which he abhorred. He was now upon his way to meet some other Turks thus stranded, to decide on some safe line of future conduct.

The rendezvous was at his father’s house, where, in the great reception-room, he found a score of men assembled. All had the faces of conspirators except his father, a very old man now, who bade them welcome as to some court function.

“Where is my son Hamdi?” asked the patriarch upon the dais, peering round upon the red-capped and black-coated throng.

“He is not with us. He has joined the fellahin. He dared not tell thee,” answered Yûsuf sadly.

“Well, well,” remarked Muhammad Pasha, with benignity. “Boys will be foolish! In Allah’s name I bid you welcome, O my friends. It is well known that I myself despise these upstarts and have told their leader my opinion to his face. Less old, I should have spent my life and fortune for the young Khedive, whose ancestor, the great Muhammad Ali, raised my house to honour; as it is, I pray to God to grant him victory. But his dependence on the English likes me not; and God forbid that I should influence your counsels. You have, each one, his life and fortune to protect, his duty to decide towards El Islam.”
===Chinese===
优素福认识阿拉比已有几年，私交上也喜欢他；可赫迪夫穆罕默德·陶菲克却是他自幼的朋友。煽动者们恳求他入伙共事，他把这个问题请示了那位好心的赫迪夫；赫迪夫求他接受人家这样送上门的职位，好让他——穆罕默德·陶菲克·帕夏，埃及之主——在自己那班所谓臣仆中间能有一个朋友。他被职责拴住，没能随君主逃往亚历山大港；只落得留在背后，处在一个荒唐的境地：做了他所憎恶的叛党政府的一员。他此刻正要去会见另一些同样搁浅的土耳其人，商定日后行止的安全路线。

聚会的地点在他父亲宅中。他在大客厅里见到二十来个人已经到齐。人人都是一副阴谋家的面孔，只有他父亲例外——老人如今已是十分年迈了——他招呼众人，仿佛迎迓前来赴一场宫廷大典的宾客。

“我的儿子哈姆迪在哪里？”高台上，老太爷问道，眯着眼环视那一群戴红帽、穿黑外套的人。

“他没有同我们在一起。他投到费拉农人那边去了。他不敢禀告您。”优素福黯然答道。

“也罢，也罢。”穆罕默德·帕夏宽厚地说。“男孩子总要胡闹一场！我奉安拉之名欢迎你们，我的朋友们。谁都知道，我自己就瞧不起这些暴发户，还当面对他们的头领说过我的看法。若不是我年事已高，我本该为年轻的赫迪夫舍却性命和家财——他的先祖、伟大的穆罕默德·阿里，曾把我这一门抬举到尊荣的地位；如今呢，我只求真主赐他胜利。只是他倚仗英国人，我不喜欢；真主也断不容我去左右你们的计议。你们每一位，都有性命家财要保全，都有一份对伊斯兰教当决的义务。”
===Original===
He stopped, and an uneasy silence reigned for quite a minute. It was broken by a man exclaiming, “They have set up a tribunal in each town with power to ruin or to kill a man on mere suspicion. Hear the wording of a document which I received this day.”

With that, he took a paper from his breast and read aloud its contents﻿—a call in truculent, inflated language upon the patriot Mahmûd the son of Hâfiz to show his fervour by a contribution to the war fund; failing which, he would be prosecuted as a foe to Egypt﻿—“for the public safety.”

“Aha!” laughed the old Pasha in his thin, cracked voice. “A French model, by my beard! For men who would eschew all foreign influence! That is the hand of Tulbah, not Arâbi. The mountebanks! The silly children﻿—apish imitators!”
===Chinese===
他说完了，足足有一分钟，一片不安的寂静笼罩着众人。后来有一个人喊起来，打破了沉默：“他们在每座城里都设了法庭，有权仅凭猜疑就毁掉一个人、杀掉一个人。听听我今天收到的一纸文书的措辞。”

说着，他从怀里掏出一纸文书，高声宣读其中的内容——用凶横夸张的言语，号召爱国志士哈菲兹之子马哈茂德捐一笔军费，以表白热忱；倘若不从，他将以埃及的仇敌之身被起诉——说是“为了公共安全”。

“啊哈！”老帕夏用他那细弱沙哑的嗓音笑道。“照我的胡子起誓，这是法兰西的样板！亏他们还是立志摒弃一切外国影响的人！这是图勒巴（Tulbah）的手笔，不是阿拉比的。一帮卖艺的江湖骗子！一群傻孩子——学人样的小猴子！”
===Original===
“By your Excellency’s leave the matter is extremely serious﻿—for me at least,” groaned out the owner of the notice.

“Thou wilt make the contribution?” inquired Yûsuf.

“Better flee,” remarked another.

And then they all began to talk together in low whispers with frightened glances round the room, for spies were everywhere. Flight was now hopeless, everyone agreed; nothing remained but to feign ardour in Arâbi’s cause, give up communication with the loyalists at Alexandria, and pray for the usurper’s overthrow.

“They cannot last, I tell you,” chuckled the old Pasha. “These fellahin are quite unfit for government. The young Khedive has been too kind. He has not whipped them. My son and I were present when his father warned him to execute these men, his creatures, who had tasted power. A sad mistake, by Allah! For, Allah knows, we do not want the English in this land. My lifework, that of all the old diplomatists, has been to stave off European interference, by compliments, by guile, by small concessions. O Allah, let me die before the evil day! The Lord preserve us from the domination of the infidels!”
===Chinese===
“恕我直言，阁下，这事非同小可——至少对我是如此。”收到文书的那位呻吟道。

“你打算认捐么？”优素福问。

“不如逃走。”另一人说道。

接着他们全体交头接耳地低语起来，惊惶的眼光扫视屋子的四角，因为密探无处不在。人人都承认，逃走如今已无望；剩下的只有装出对阿拉比事业的热忱，断绝同亚历山大港效忠派的往来，再祈求上天叫僭主倾覆。

“他们长不了的，我告诉你们。”老帕夏咯咯地笑道。“这些费拉农人压根儿不配执政。年轻的赫迪夫太仁慈了。他没有鞭打他们。他父亲警告他把这些尝过权柄滋味的人、他亲手豢养的人处死的时候，我父子二人就在场。凭安拉说，这是一个痛心的失策！因为，安拉知道，我们并不想让英国人进这个国家。我毕生的事业，也是所有老外交家的事业，就是靠恭维、靠权谋、靠小小的让步，把欧洲的干涉挡在外面。安拉啊，让我死在灾祸临头之前吧！愿主保佑我们，不受异教徒的统治！”
===Original===
The old man dropped his hands and hung his head.

“Better the English than this present anarchy,” another murmured. “Already the whole land is overrun by gangs of brigands. The streets here in the capital grow dangerous. There is no order kept except among the soldiers. All trade, all enterprise is at a standstill, and every public undertaking goes to ruin. Already all the people hate Arâbi.”

“The Lord deliver us,” said Yûsuf, “from him and from the English both. A dreadful quandary!”

When he went forth to his carriage, still in waiting, he told his slave to have his pistols ready, and himself examined the revolver which he carried. He wrapped a shawl about his face to pass unrecognized and, thus protected and disguised, drove through the darkling streets, where every wayfarer betrayed the like anxiety. Only the street-dogs went about their work as usual, prowling along the walls in search of offal.
===Chinese===
老人垂下双手，低下了头。

“宁可要英国人，也不要眼下这场无政府状态。”另一人喃喃道。“全国已经遍地是成帮结伙的强盗。都城的街上越来越危险。除了军队里，哪里都维持不了秩序。一切商业、一切营生都陷于停顿，每一项公共事业都在败落。百姓已经人人恨阿拉比了。”

“愿主拯救我们，”优素福说，“救我们脱离他，也脱离英国人。好一个可怕的进退两难！”

他出门走向仍在等候的马车，吩咐奴隶把他的手枪备好，又亲自查验了随身携带的左轮枪。他用一条披巾裹住脸，好让人认不出来；这样防护着、乔装着，驱车穿过渐暗的街道——那里每一个行人都流露出同样的焦虑。只有街头的狗照旧做它们的营生，沿着墙根逡巡，寻觅腐臭的残羹。
===Original===
At his own door a man accosted him. It was one of his paid spies. He led the way across the hall into his private room.

“What news?” he questioned.

“May Allah turn it to thy good!” the spy replied, with his profoundest reverence. “I have it from a member of the new Committee that your Highness is marked down as a suspected notable. They say it may mean destitution, even death.”

“I thank thee,” murmured Yûsuf and dismissed the man. Directly he was gone he called Ghandûr and said:
===Chinese===
到了自家门口，一个男人上前搭话。那是他雇下的密探之一。他引路穿过门厅，进了私室。

“有什么消息？”他问。

“愿安拉把这消息化作您的吉兆！”密探以最深切的敬意答道。“我从新委员会的一名成员那里得到消息：殿下已被登记在册，算作可疑的显贵。他们说，这可能意味着倾家荡产，甚至掉脑袋。”

“谢谢你。”优素福低声道，打发那人走了。他前脚刚走，优素福便唤来甘杜尔，说：
===Original===
“Didst thou not tell me, O beloved, that thou hadst some relative a member of the new Committee for the Public Safety?”

“Yes, O my lord! The person is my father’s brother, a small merchant.”

“Where is their place of meeting?”

“I can show it thee.”

“Do they meet every day?”

“I think so, but will ascertain.”
===Chinese===
“亲爱的，你不是告诉过我，你有位亲戚在新设的公共安全委员会里么？”

“是的，我的老爷！那人是我父亲的兄弟，做个小买卖的。”

“他们在哪里聚会？”

“我能带您去。”

“他们天天都聚么？”

“我想是天天聚，容我去打听确实。”
===Original===
“Good. I shall wait upon them in the morning. At daybreak take ten pounds out of the treasury and carry it to thy relation to bespeak his favour.”

“Has aught untoward happened?”

“Untoward? Listen!” Yûsuf told the story.

“Merciful Allah! How can such things be?” exclaimed Ghandûr. “We are the greatest in the land, they﻿—filthy upstarts. How much does my good lord propose to give?”

“A thousand pounds were not too much to save my life.”
===Chinese===
“很好。明早我去拜会他们。天一亮，你从库房里取出十磅，送给你那位亲戚，预先买下他的好感。”

“出了什么不顺心的事么？”

“不顺心？你听着！”优素福把事情原原本本讲了。

“慈悲的安拉！这种事怎么会发生？”甘杜尔叫道。“咱们是全国第一等的人家，他们——一群肮脏的暴发户。我的好老爷打算送多少？”

“为救我的命，一千磅也不算多。”
===Original===
“Deign but to hear my counsel! Give a hundred and ask leave for thy son to join the army. He is prostrated by thy late refusal. His going will prove more than any gift of money that thy heart is with the cause﻿—which, Allah knows, may be the right one, since our lord has chosen to put trust in infidels. His mother even wishes it, to heal his chagrin. She sent for me and asked me to entreat your Excellency. We have good friends within the army who will see that he is kept from fighting. My son shall go along with him, to be his servant.”

Ghandûr, the simple creature, was in tears.

“By Allah, I will think about it,” murmured Yûsuf.

Five minutes later he repaired to his son’s room, revived the lad, and passing thence to the haremlik, told Barakah that her request was granted. She was half stunned, for she had counted on his obduracy.

Not noticing her dazed condition, for his mind ran still on puzzles of diplomacy, he added:
===Chinese===
“求您屈尊听听我的主意！送上一百磅，再求您开恩，准令郎从军。您前番回绝，他已委顿不堪了。他这一去，比任何金钱的馈赠更能证明您的心同这项事业在一起——这项事业，安拉知道，兴许还是正义的一方，既然咱们的君主大人竟把信任交给了异教徒。他母亲也巴望如此，好消解他的郁愤。她打发人唤我，托我恳求阁下。军队里有咱们的好朋友，自会照应着不让他真上火线。我儿子也随他同去，充当他的仆从。”

甘杜尔这憨直的人，已经泪眼汪汪。

“凭安拉，我会考虑的。”优素福喃喃道。

五分钟后，他去了儿子的房间，让那少年重新提起了精神；随后从那里走进哈雷姆利克，告诉巴拉卡，说她求的事准了。她简直半晌发懵，因为她原本拿准了他会执拗到底。

他没有留意她那副神志恍惚的模样，因为他的心思还转在外交的难题上，只顾接着说：
===Original===
“Thou, who art English, O my sweet one, inform me of that nation! Are they harsh as conquerors? What is their custom with regard to vengeance? Do they burn and ravish, or merely punish those who have borne arms against them? It is important I should know beforehand if they win the day.”

Barakah stared at him vaguely for a moment; then bursting into tears, exclaimed:

“Cut short thy life! O most unfeeling father! O appalling prospect! I would sooner die a thousand deaths than see them conquer.”

“Merciful Allah, are they so fanatical?” gasped Yûsuf, with a face of great dismay. “I meant not to alarm thee, O beloved. I was thinking only of myself, how to behave in case things happened so, which God forbid!”

But Barakah thought only of their son.
===Chinese===
“你既是英国人，我的甜人儿，给我讲讲你那个民族吧！他们做了征服者，手段狠不狠？他们报仇有什么规矩？他们是烧杀奸淫呢，还是只惩办那些拿枪打过他们的人？万一他们得了胜，我最好事先心中有数。”

巴拉卡怔怔地盯了他片刻；随即泪如泉涌，喊道：

“叫你短命！天下最没有心肝的父亲！啊，这可怕的前景！我宁可死一千回，也不愿眼看他们得胜。”

“慈悲的安拉，他们竟这样狂热么？”优素福倒抽一口气，满脸惊惶。“我无意吓你，亲爱的。我方才心里只有我自己——万一事情真到了那步田地，我该拿什么举止应对——但愿真主不容它发生！”

巴拉卡心里想的，却只有他们的儿子。

## XXXI / 三十一
===Original===
“A splendid victory at Kafr ed-Dowâr! A thousand infidels dispatched to Hell, and not a single blessed martyr gone to Paradise!” cried Umm ed-Dahak, entering her lady’s presence on a summer evening. “Ghandûr has got the news-sheet, and craves leave to read it to thee.”
The lady ordered him to be admitted instantly. Muhammad and his servant Ali were at Kafr ed-Dowâr. Drawing her head-veil so as to leave one eye visible, she listened to the short triumphant notice, which began and ended with “the praise to Allah!”
“The praise to Allah truly!” she suspired. “Not one was killed.”

===Chinese===
一个夏日傍晚，乌姆·埃德-达哈克走进女主人的房里，喊道：“卡夫尔·达瓦尔（Kafr ed-Dowâr）大捷！一千名异教徒被打发下了地狱，蒙福的殉道者却一个也没有升上天堂！”“甘杜尔拿到了新闻纸，恳求念给您听。”
女主人吩咐立刻放他进来。穆罕默德和他的仆人阿里正在卡夫尔·达瓦尔。她拉起头纱，只留一只眼睛露在外面，听那份简短的捷报——开头结尾都是“赞美安拉”。
“真是赞美安拉！”她轻叹道。“一个人也没有阵亡。”

===Original===
Ghandûr assured her then, as he had done a score of times, that Muhammad, with the blessing of the Highest, ran no danger. By arrangement with the leaders he was kept at work in the trenched camp, away from fighting. But her anxiety was not allayed, her boy was venturesome and, burning as he was to fight, might break through rules.
Every evening in Arâbi’s journal there was news of some fresh triumph, either at Kafr ed-Dowâr, by Alexandria, or on the banks of the Canal, where the main force of the English was now operating. She heard it said on all hands that the war would soon be over. Yet, though everyone abounded in exultant phrases, no single soul appeared exceptionally cheerful; and she herself did not disguise her sorrow. The absence of Muhammad was a constant pain. She gave attention to her little daughter fitfully.
The weather was intensely hot, the town a desert full of dismal noises. So many men had been compelled to join the army, so many beasts of burden had been pressed for transport purposes, that trade was paralysed and traffic almost ceased. When she drove out, the aspect of the streets dismayed her; it was as if the city had been ravaged by a pestilence. The European, Syrian, Armenian quarters were utterly deserted, all the houses closed; and elsewhere there was very little movement. In other summers the harem had gone into the country, and Barakah would gladly have drawn nearer to the seat of war; but her husband vetoed the proposal instantly, the country districts were unsafe and overrun by brigands. Yûsuf was irritable in those days. He had his bed in the selamlik and seldom could spare time to visit Barakah.

===Chinese===
甘杜尔随即向她担保——这样的担保他已作过二十来遍——说托至高主的福，穆罕默德不会有任何危险。经与各位首领讲妥，他被留在掘壕营地里做杂务，远离战阵。可她的焦虑并未因此平息：她的孩子胆子大，又烧着一腔求战的热望，保不住会越出规矩。
每天晚上，阿拉比的报纸上总有新的捷报：不是在卡夫尔·达瓦尔，便是在亚历山大港一带，要不然就在运河两岸——英军主力此刻正在那里作战。她听人人都说，战事不久便可结束。然而，尽管个个满口欢腾的言辞，却没有一个人显得格外快活；她自己也不掩饰悲伤。穆罕默德不在跟前，是无时不在的痛。她对小女儿的照料也是有一搭没一搭。
天气酷热难当，城池成了一片荒漠，满是凄凉的声响。太多男子被强征入伍，太多驮兽被抓去运送军需，商业陷于瘫痪，交通几乎断绝。她乘车出门时，街上的景象使她心惊：这座城仿佛遭了一场瘟疫的蹂躏。欧人区、叙利亚人区、亚美尼亚人区空无一人，家家门户紧闭；别处也几乎不见动静。往年夏天，内眷们总要到乡间去住，巴拉卡本很乐意挪得离战地近些；可她丈夫一口否决了这个提议——乡间不安全，遍地是强盗。那些日子优素福烦躁易怒。他把床铺安在塞拉姆利克，难得匀出工夫来看巴拉卡。

===Original===
“I believe he has another woman somewhere,” she told Umm ed-Dahak in a hopeless tone.
“It is his right, by Allah,” answered the old woman; “and no slight to thee, if thou wouldst view it fairly, and throw aside the silly fiction of the Franks. It is the nature of a man to have more wives than one, and a woman should no more resent his doing so﻿—always provided he does not defraud her﻿—than blame a cat for having several kittens at a birth. Ibrahîm, the father of the faithful, Mûsa﻿—all the prophets till the crown of them (God bless and save him) married more than one. Polygamy was in the customs of the Jews and Christians until they fell away from El Islam. Nay, a remembrance of it still exists among the Franks. For do not their religious women dwell together in one house, obedient to a rule like ours, attired like us, and call themselves﻿—I ask pardon of the Lord﻿—Harem Allah (the wives of God)? Rank blasphemy, by Allah! Yet it shows that the old rule is not entirely lost.”
Barakah was too disconsolate to be contentious. Let Muhammad but return to her in safety and she would not care though Yûsuf took a thousand wives; but in his absence everything seemed grievous.

===Chinese===
“我看他在外头什么地方养着另一个女人。”她用绝望的声调对乌姆·埃德-达哈克说。
“凭安拉说，这是他的权利，”老妇人答道；“你若公道看待，抛开法兰克人那套愚蠢的虚构，便知这也算不得对你的轻慢。男人天性就不止娶一房妻室；只要他不亏待你，女人对此便不该怀怨——正如不该怪一只猫一胎生下好几只小猫。众信士之父易卜拉欣（Ibrahîm）、穆萨（Mûsa）——直到众先知的冠冕（愿真主赐福保佑他）——哪一位娶的都不止一人。多妻原是犹太人和基督徒的风俗，直到他们背离了伊斯兰教才丢弃。不，法兰克人中间至今还留着这规矩的一点记忆呢。他们那些出家修道的女人，岂不正是一同住在一所宅子里，遵着一套同我们相仿的规矩，穿着也同我们一样，还自称——我求主恕罪——‘安拉的后宫’（Harem Allah，真主的众妻）吗？凭安拉说，真是滔天的渎神！可这恰恰说明，古老的规矩并未完全失传。”
巴拉卡愁闷至极，无心争辩。只要穆罕默德平安回到她身边，哪怕优素福娶上一千房妻妾她也不在意；可在他不在这段日子，样样事情都显得凄苦。

===Original===
A real sorrow overhung the house of Yûsuf; for the old Pasha was fast sinking to the grave. Hamdi, the hot disciple of Arâbi, the poet of rebellion, author of the famous calls to patriotism which were printed every week in the official journal, was bowed down by grief. He thought his siding with the malcontents had killed his father.
“But what was I to do?” he asked of Barakah, to whom, as an old friend, he took his troubles. “Their cries had fired my spirit. I could not keep silent. Na’imah tells me not to worry, yet I feel most guilty.”
Yûsuf, too, was downcast and repentant.
“We have been like fools,” he sighed, “wasting in vanity the precious hours we might have spent with him﻿—as if we thought that he would live forever. Now the end draws near, we can but beat our breasts and curse our folly.”

===Chinese===
一场真正的哀伤笼罩着优素福的家；老帕夏正迅速沉向坟墓。哈姆迪——阿拉比的火热信徒、叛乱的诗人、每周刊印在官报上的那些著名爱国号召的作者——被忧伤压弯了腰。他认为，是自己站到怨党一边，害死了父亲。
“可我又能怎么样呢？”他向巴拉卡问——把她当作老朋友，他有烦恼都来向她倾诉。“他们的呐喊点燃了我的心。我没法保持沉默。纳伊玛劝我别烦心，可我总觉得自己罪过最深。”
优素福也一样的沮丧悔恨。
“我们一直像傻子一样，”他叹道，“把本可以陪他度过的宝贵光阴，虚掷在浮华事务上——仿佛以为他会活到永远。如今末日临近，我们只有捶着胸膛，咒骂自己的愚蠢。”

===Original===
When Barakah went to the old palace to inquire, she was struck by the despairing looks of all the servants. A eunuch with a very woeful smile conducted her to Fitnah Khânum, who exclaimed at sight of her:
“The praise to Allah, thou art come! Our lord has asked for thee. Murjânah was just going to dispatch a messenger. Come! Come at once! There is no time to lose. He has refused to take a potion which I had prepared. He will not let a charm be hung upon him. He resigns his life to Allah. It is the end.”
Murjânah Khânum sat beside the bed, holding the old man’s hand. About the walls crouched many black-robed women, waiting in silence, like a flock of vultures.
“Here is the wife of Yûsuf,” said Murjânah, giving place to Barakah.

===Chinese===
巴拉卡到老公馆去问病，众仆人绝望的神色令她心惊。一个面带惨笑的阉人引她去见菲特娜·哈努姆，后者一见她便喊道：
“赞美安拉，你来了！咱们的主上问起了你。穆尔贾娜正要打发人去传信呢。来！快来！一刻也耽误不得。他不肯喝我备下的药饮，也不许人把护符挂上身。他把自己的性命交托给安拉。这就是尽头了。”
穆尔贾娜·哈努姆坐在床边，握着老人的手。四壁墙根蹲着许多黑衣女人，静默地等候，像一群兀鹫。
“优素福的妻子来了。”穆尔贾娜说着，把位置让给巴拉卡。

===Original===
The Pasha spoke in French. His voice was faint.
“Madame,” he said, “I am about to die, and I am glad to be allowed to say adieu to you. Very often have I thought of you and of your life among us. I feel a very grave responsibility. I trust that you have been, upon the whole, content?”
Barakah declared herself quite happy, and he said, “Thank God!”
“But you will not leave us yet; you will recover,” she exclaimed.
“No, no, my cherished daughter. My last hour has sounded. I have lived to see my lifework all undone. The Christians always sought a war with El Islam. We kept a calm face under insults, even made concessions, as one gives a rabid dog a stick to worry.” For a moment the worn face resumed its light of humour. “But now the war has come.﻿ ﻿… Those rash fanatics!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
帕夏说法语。他的声音十分微弱。
“夫人，”他说，“我就要死了，临终还容我向你道一声别，我很欣慰。我时常想到你，想到你在我们中间过的日子。我感到责任重大。总的来说，我相信你过得还称心吧？”
巴拉卡称自己十分幸福，他便说：“感谢真主！”
“可您还不会撇下我们走；您会好起来的。”她喊道。
“不，不，我珍爱的女儿。我最后的一个钟头已经敲响。我活到今日，亲眼看见毕生的事业尽付东流。基督徒们一向谋求同伊斯兰教开战。我们在侮辱之下保持镇静的脸色，甚至步步退让，就像人递给疯狗一根棍子去啃咬。”一时之间，那张憔悴的脸上又泛起幽默的光。“可如今战争到底来了。……那些鲁莽的狂热之徒！……”

===Original===
There rose a murmur in the room.
“The Grand Mufti comes,” announced Murjânah Khânum.
“Forgive me, dear madame. It is an old and cherished friend,” the dying man suspired, with a faint smile. “Adieu! Adieu!”
And Barakah, with all the women save Murjânah Khânum, hurried out into the passage. At the door a tall and stately man brushed past her. His head was so erect beneath the massive turban, his long robe fell so straight from well-squared shoulders, that it astonished her to see his beard as white as snow. He passed into the room. The door was shut.
A minute later, Murjânah Khânum uttered a loud cry; the Mufti came forth sobbing, with head bowed; the black-cowled women scurried shrieking to the death-room, where they instantly began the dance of death. They leapt and pirouetted, waving arms above their heads, with frenzied cries. Barakah was gazing horror-stricken at the sight, when someone took her hand and whispered, “Come away!”

===Chinese===
屋里起了一阵低语。
“大穆夫提来了。”穆尔贾娜·哈努姆通报道。
“原谅我，亲爱的夫人。来的是一位交情深厚的老友。”垂死的人带着一丝微笑轻叹道。“别了！别了！”
巴拉卡便同除穆尔贾娜·哈努姆之外的所有女人一齐匆匆退到走廊里。门口，一位身材高大、气度庄严的男人与她擦身而过。那颗头在硕大的缠头巾下挺得那样直，长袍从端正的双肩垂得那样笔挺，以至她看见他胡须白得像雪时，不由得暗暗吃惊。他走进房去。门关上了。
一分钟后，穆尔贾娜·哈努姆放声哭喊起来；穆夫提低头啜泣着走了出来；戴黑兜帽的女人们尖叫着奔向停灵的屋子，立刻在那里跳起死亡之舞。她们腾跃旋转，双臂在头顶挥舞，发出狂乱的号叫。巴拉卡正惊骇地注视着这幅景象，忽有人拉住她的手，低声说：“离开这儿吧！”

===Original===
It was Murjânah.
“I cannot bear these customs,” she confessed. “The women of the country keep them in defiance of religion. It is useless to protest; one has to suffer. I am very tired, my dear; for I have not slept for many nights. Indeed, my weariness and grief are such that I can hardly look for rest save in the grave.”
Barakah took coffee in Murjânah’s room, and tried to comfort her. She too was sad. But her despair was turned to joy when that same day Muhammad rushed into her arms. He had been called by telegram. She held him back from her and gazed at him until he blushed and hung his head. The uniform, the high-crowned fez, the sword, the snowy gloves, embellished him. When she had gazed her fill, she made him tell her of the camp, his friends, his duties; and, started on that theme, he talked for hours.

===Chinese===
是穆尔贾娜。
“我受不住这些习俗。”她坦言道。“这地方的乡下女人硬是顶着教规保留它们。抗议也没有用，只好忍受。我累极了，亲爱的；我已经许多夜没有合眼了。说真的，我的疲惫和悲伤这样深重，除了坟墓，我简直不指望别处得着安息。”
巴拉卡在穆尔贾娜房里喝了咖啡，尽力安慰她。她自己心里也悲伤。可就在当天，穆罕默德扑进她怀中，她的绝望顿时化成了欢喜。他是被电报召回来的。她把他从怀里推开一点，上下端详他，直到他红着脸垂下头去。军装、高顶的塔布什帽、佩剑、雪白的手套，把他装点得英武出众。她看够了，才叫他讲营地、讲朋友、讲职守；话头一起，他一谈就是几个钟头。

===Original===
“If only I could be transferred to the Canal!” he sighed. “That is the real centre of the war. The fighting where I am is empty show, and I am kept from taking part in it. Day after day, I have to teach recruits, dull fellahin, who know not right from left. Instruction seems to make them stupider. I beat and beat them, till my arm aches. By my sword and valour, I could often kill them! Think, O my mother!﻿—El Islam is menaced, armed infidels have set foot in our land, and these men, Muslims, will not learn their exercises!”
His mother laughed at his impetuosity. She told his grandfather’s last words to her, and how he feared the English would take hold of Egypt.
“There is no fear of that, inshallah!” cried Muhammad. “Our faithful host will sweep them off like fleas. I wish I had been there to reassure the dear one. May Our Lord have mercy on him!”

===Chinese===
“要是能把我调到运河去就好了！”他叹道。“那才是战争真正的中心。我驻的地方，打仗全是虚架子，还不许我上阵。日复一日，我只得教练新兵——一群愚钝的费拉农人，连左右都分不清。操练似乎越教越把他们教蠢。我打了又打，打到胳膊发酸。凭我的剑和胆气起誓，我常常恨不得杀了他们！想想吧，我的母亲啊！——伊斯兰教正遭威胁，武装的异教徒已踏上我们的国土，可这些人，这些穆斯林，竟不肯学好操练！”
他母亲笑他性子急躁。她把老公临终对她说的话讲给他听，讲他如何担心英国人会把埃及攥到手里。
“那个用不着担心，若安拉意欲！”穆罕默德喊道。“咱们忠勇的大军会把他们像跳蚤一样扫出去。真希望我当时在场，好宽慰那位亲爱的老人。愿主怜悯他！”

===Original===
The funeral of Muhammad Pasha Sâlih was among the greatest ever known, although the town was empty. The harassed population flocked to pay respect to one who had denounced Arâbi﻿—a demonstration which could not be punished since sons of the dead man﻿—nay, half his family﻿—acclaimed the tyrant. In the front of the procession were led sheep and bullocks to be slaughtered at the tomb, their meat distributed among the needy in the name of the deceased. Then came hired chanters of the Koran, then half the male inhabitants of Cairo, walking, flanked by two thin lines of soldiers, then the male relations, then a choir of boys shrieking an ode in honour of the Prophet. Immediately behind these moved the lidless coffin, carried on men’s shoulders, with its coloured pall, and then the females of the family in shuttered carriages. A crowd of black-cowled women of the city, whose wailing sounded birdlike in the open air, brought up the rear.
The train, a mile long, wound out in the blinding sunlight over the sandhill to the city of the dead, from which at its approach the kites and crows went up, affrighted. There ensued a period of forced inaction, which to Barakah in the haremlik at the mausoleum seemed interminable. The ceaseless chanting in the tomb, the wailing of the crowd outside, attacked her nerves. Muhammad was to leave again that evening, and every minute she was parted from him seemed an hour. He was kept upon the men’s side of the tomb; nor would she see him till they reached the house again; she had first to drive home in the stuffy carriage with Na’imah and two of the late Pasha’s daughters. It was maddening.

===Chinese===
穆罕默德·帕夏·萨利赫的葬礼，虽在这样一座空荡荡的城里举办，也算得前所未见的盛大之一。饱受磨难的百姓蜂拥而来，向这位曾斥责过阿拉比的人致敬——这场哀荣是无法问罪的，因为死者的儿子们——不，他的半数家人——都在称颂那位僭主。行列前头牵着羊和牛，要在墓前宰杀，肉以死者的名义分给穷人。随后是雇来的《古兰经》诵经师，再后是半城开罗的男丁步行随行，两侧夹着两列稀疏的士兵；再后是男亲属们，然后是一队尖声唱着颂扬先知赞歌的男童。紧随其后行进的，是那口无盖的棺材，由人扛在肩上，覆着彩色棺衣；再后是家中的女眷，坐在遮得严严实实的马车里。城中一群戴黑兜帽的女人殿在后头，她们的号哭在露天里听来宛如鸟啼。
送葬的长队足有一英里，在炫目的日光里蜿蜒出城，越过沙丘，向死者之城行进；队伍临近时，鸢鸟和乌鸦受惊飞起。接着便是一段被迫的枯守，对身在陵墓哈雷姆利克里的巴拉卡来说，这段时间仿佛没有尽头。墓内诵经不歇，墓外人群号哭不绝，一齐折磨着她的神经。穆罕默德当晚又要走了，同他分开的每一分钟，在她都像一个钟头。他被留在坟墓男客的一边；不到回府她见不着他；她得先同纳伊玛和已故帕夏的两个女儿一道，坐那辆闷人的马车回家。这简直要叫人发疯。

===Original===
In fact, she saw him only for a moment, ere he ran to catch his train. She wept a little at the disappointment, but his visit had relieved her of a weight of sorrow. She had only to dispatch a telegram and he would come again. Moreover, she was now quite certain he was not in danger.
When told by Yûsuf that her drives must cease, because the horses had been taken for the army, she did not complain, but hired a donkey when she had to pay a call; nor could the prospect of a famine frighten her. Her mind had rest. Each evening brought the news of an Egyptian victory. The English would be driven out. Her son was safe. Once more she joked and dreamt with Umm ed-Dahak.

===Chinese===
其实，她只来得及见他一面，他便跑去赶火车了。这失望叫她落了几点泪，可他这一趟来，已卸下她心头一大块悲伤。她只消再发一封电报，他就会回来。何况她如今已十分确信，他不会有危险。
优素福告诉她，马车不能再用了，因为马匹已被征去充军；她并无怨言，出门拜客时便雇一头毛驴，饥荒的前景也吓不着她。她的心境安宁了。每天晚上都传来埃及人得胜的消息。英国人终会被赶出去。她的儿子平安无事。她又一次同乌姆·埃德-达哈克说笑做梦了。

## XXXII / 三十二
===Original===
At Kafr ed-Dowâr Muhammad was kept drilling conscripts, relieving older officers who were required for actual fighting. Almost every day he heard the boom of cannon, the stirring noises of attack and skirmish; and often in his leisure moments he would perch in some high nook and watch the flashes, the white puffs of smoke, dispersed upon the green of level fields between the seacoast sandhills and the lake﻿—a pretty sight. Beyond the plain of water skimmed by white-winged birds the town of Alexandria basked in sunlit haze. Upon the land-plain doves were wheeling round deserted villages, kites and vultures hovered high in air. Franks from the seaport rode out in the rearward of the English troops, and from the vantage-point of dykes and hillocks watched the operations through their field-glasses. The assaults, as he had told his mother, were not serious; mere “fantaziyeh” the old soldiers called them. The aim of the assailants was to keep a portion of Arâbi’s troops from joining the main army on the banks of the Canal, where war was being waged in bitter earnest. Muhammad fretted at his dull employment. The atmosphere of strife, the bugle-calls, the march of men, no longer satisfied him as at first. He wished to fight, and begged the general-in-chief, who favoured him, being a close friend of his uncle Hamdi, to move him to some post of danger. The great one laughed and patted him upon the back.
“We cannot spare thee yet from the recruits,” he said. “That work is useful, and it must be done. Think, thou hast given us a thousand soldiers, none like them for rigidity and speed of motion.”

===Chinese===
在卡夫尔·达瓦尔，穆罕默德奉命操练新兵，替下须去实战的老军官。几乎每天他都听得见炮声隆隆，听得见进攻和小接仗那些撩动人心的声响；闲暇时分，他常蹲坐在什么高高的角落里，望着火光闪动、白烟一团团散开在滨海沙丘与湖泊之间平畴的绿色上——一幅好看的景象。隔着白翼飞鸟掠过的茫茫水面，亚历山大港浴在阳光的薄霭里。陆地上，鸽群绕着废弃的村庄盘旋，鸢鸟和兀鹫高高悬在空中。从海港来的法兰克人骑马跟在英军后部，占着堤岸和土丘的便利地势，用望远镜观战。那些进攻，正如他告诉过母亲的，并不当真；老兵们管它们叫“方塔齐耶”（fantaziyeh，做戏）。进攻一方的目的，是拖住阿拉比的一部分队伍，不让他们去运河两岸同主力会合——那里正打着殊死的真仗。穆罕默德为这枯燥的差事烦躁。争战的气氛、军号声、队伍的行进，已不像起初那样令他满足了。他想去打仗，便恳求总司令把自己调到某个危险的岗位——总司令是他叔叔哈姆迪的密友，对他颇为眷顾。那位大人物笑着拍了拍他的背。
“我们还不能把你从新兵那儿抽走，”他说。“那差事有用处，总得有人来做。想想吧，你已经给了我们一千名士兵，论举手投足的刻板和动作的迅速，没有一队比得上他们。”

===Original===
Muhammad hated the recruits, who still were driven in by hundreds every day﻿—men past their prime, and boys dragged from the wretched villages, and active rogues caught hiding in some ditch or patch of cane. The land had been already drained; the dregs were called for. And they were stupid, dazed, those fellahin; a flock of sheep has more intelligence! Muhammad, for whom soldiering was a religion and every detail of the drill had sanctity, was driven frantic by their apathy, their foolish stare. Dancing with fury, he reviled their mothers and beat them with his cane about the ears.
“By the Prophet, they are pigs!” he told the son of Ghandûr, who served him in his tent and hung upon his every word. “Here is El Islam in danger; they are Muslims; yet they yawn and gape if asked to hold a gun. Ah! if I had a hundred Turks instead of them!”

===Chinese===
穆罕默德恨透了这些新兵——如今每天仍有成百的人被驱赶进来：过了盛年的汉子，从破败村庄里拖出来的男孩，在什么沟渠或蔗丛里躲藏时被逮个正着的机灵流氓。这片土地早已被榨干；如今是连渣滓也要征来了。而这些费拉农人又蠢又呆，一群羊也比他们多些智性！在穆罕默德，当兵是一门宗教，操练的每个细节都有神圣的意味，他们的麻木、他们的傻瞪，简直把他逼得发狂。他怒得跳脚，咒骂他们的母亲，用手杖抽打他们的耳侧。
“凭先知起誓，他们是猪！”他对甘杜尔的儿子说——那少年在他帐中当差，他说的每一个字都奉如纶音。“伊斯兰教正临着危险；他们是穆斯林；可叫他们拿一拿枪，他们竟又打哈欠又发怔。啊！我若能拿一百个土耳其人换掉他们该多好！”

===Original===
The son of Ghandûr, who to please Muhammad would himself have put his head into a cannon’s mouth, was horrified at the behaviour of the conscripts. That they could fail to see the light of inspiration on Muhammad’s brow was proof sufficient of their utter baseness. For the same reason he despised the generals. Muhammad was more gifted for command than they, and yet they kept him ever at this menial task. Had Muhammad﻿—or his foster-brother even﻿—owned the leadership, Iskenderîyeh would long since have fallen, and all the English have been pushed into the sea. He dared to proffer this opinion to his lord one evening. But Muhammad in his wisdom answered:
“No, we cannot take the town, for this good reason, that a portion of their fleet, unseen from here, commands it, and would pour in shells to our destruction.”
Ali received this information with head bowed and thanks to God. He prayed the Maker of the World to put some mind in the recruits in order that they too might profit by such high instruction.

===Chinese===
甘杜尔的儿子，为了讨穆罕默德欢心，自己把脑袋伸进炮口也情愿；新兵们的行径叫他骇然。他们竟看不出穆罕默德眉宇间的灵光，仅此一端，便足证他们卑劣至极。出于同一个缘故，他也看不起那些将军。穆罕默德比他们更富统帅之才，他们却总派他干这份下贱差事。倘若由穆罕默德——哪怕干脆由他这位奶兄弟——执掌了指挥权，亚历山大城（Iskenderîyeh）早就陷落，英国人也早被统统推下海去了。一天晚上，他斗胆把这层意思进言给主人。穆罕默德却以其睿智答道：
“不，我们拿不下那座城，理由很现成：他们舰队的一部分就在这里望不见的地方控制着它，会把炮弹倾泻进来，置我们于死地。”
阿里垂着头领受了这番道理，向真主致谢。他祈求造物主往新兵们脑子里放进一点心智，好叫他们也能沾益于这般高明的教诲。

===Original===
It was usual at that time for officers to handle soldiers roughly on parade, caning them upon the head and shoulders, kicking them, and heaping on them every species of abuse. Muhammad might be called indulgent as commanders went; but he was overmuch in earnest. His outbreaks lacked the touch of humour which endears. Old soldiers might have borne them with a laugh for the sake of his enthusiasm, which was very evident.
But these were men who had been driven from their homes like cattle, at the goad’s point. For days they had been herded up in pens in a provincial town, and there harangued by holy men and maddened by religious shouting till they lost what little wits remained to them and hardly knew a true believer from an infidel. Arâbi had proclaimed the golden age; yet here they were imprisoned, hounded, driven, and now subjected to the cuffs and insults of a shameless boy. Huddling together, they looked on with lowered brows, too scared to understand what the young Turk was shouting. Arâbi had proclaimed the Turks abolished. Where was reason? They gave forth inarticulate harsh cries like frightened beasts.
Each squad Muhammad handled seemed more stupid than the last﻿—so stupid that one early morning an inspecting general advised him, laughing, to give drill a rest, and take them to the trenches; they were used to digging.

===Chinese===
在那个年头，军官们在操场上粗暴对待士兵是常事：用手杖打头打肩，用脚踢，百般辱骂，无所不至。照带兵将官的通例而论，穆罕默德还称得上宽厚；只是他过于较真。他的发作缺少那一笔讨人喜欢的幽默。老兵们念着他那份显而易见的热忱，原可以一笑置之地忍受。
可这些人，是像牲口一样被刺棒的尖端赶出家门的。一连数日，他们被圈在外省一座城的栏圈里，听神职人员们训话，被宗教的呼号搅得发狂，直到仅存的一点神智也丧失净尽，几乎分不清信士与异教徒。阿拉比宣告过黄金时代；可他们如今被监禁、被追迫、被驱使，还要受一个恬不知耻的少年的拳脚和侮辱。他们挤作一团，低眉蹙额地看着，吓昏了头，听不懂那年轻的土耳其人在嚷些什么。阿拉比宣告过废除土耳其人。道理何在？他们发出受惊牲口般含混粗厉的叫声。
穆罕默德接手的每一队新兵都比前一队更蠢——蠢到有一日清晨，一位前来视察的将军笑着劝他：操练且歇一歇，带他们去挖壕吧；挖土他们是惯会的。

===Original===
Muhammad felt the order as a whip-cut; he was furious. The general despised his work as an instructor, whereas God knew what trouble he had taken. It was all their fault. In the trenches he allowed them to do nothing right, but shrieked out contradictory orders emphasized by slashes of his cane. Slowly it dawned on them that he was quite alone; the place was hidden by high banks from supervision.
The daily pageant of attack was then in progress. The crackle of a volley came from no great distance. Muhammad implored Allah to direct the bullets so as to kill them all, for they were worse than infidels. He did not notice the changed manner of their breathing, nor the new fire which smouldered in their eyes.
At a blow across the face, accompanied by frightful insults, a burly fellow seized Muhammad’s wrists and deftly tripped him. The boy lay on his back bereft of speech. His captor knelt upon his belly, while the others crowded round like cattle interested. He could feel their breath.

===Chinese===
这道命令在穆罕默德感觉如同挨了一鞭；他怒不可遏。将军竟轻视他做教官的功夫，而真主才知道他下过多少苦心。这全是他们的错。在壕沟里，他不许他们做对一件事，只是尖声喊出前后矛盾的口令，还用手杖一顿抽打来加重语气。他们渐渐省悟到，这里只有他孤身一人；那地方被高高的土堤遮蔽，无人督察。
每日例行的进攻排戏正在上演。一排齐射的脆响从不远处传来。穆罕默德祈求安拉指引子弹，把他们统统打死，因为他们比异教徒还要坏。他没有留意他们呼吸变了样子，也没有留意他们眼里阴燃起来的新的火。
一记抽在脸上的鞭打，伴着骇人的辱骂——一个壮实的汉子攥住穆罕默德的双腕，手脚利落地一绊，把他放倒在地。少年仰面躺着，说不出话来。擒他的人跪压在他肚子上，其余的人像一群凑热闹的牲口围拢过来。他感觉得到他们的鼻息。

===Original===
“Hear, O my little son! Swear by the Sayyid Ahmad to be civil. It were best for thee.”
Muhammad, with his pride undaunted, answered: “Sinful hog! I swear to have thee thrashed with the nailed whip and then decapitated. O Muslimîn! Do you not know that this is mutiny, an awful crime?”
“Then we must finish him,” remarked his captor, with a sigh. “With his own sword! Here! Quickly, while I stop his screeching.”

===Chinese===
“听着，我的小少爷！凭赛义德·艾哈迈德（Sayyid Ahmad）起誓，放规矩些。这样于你最好。”
穆罕默德傲气不折，答道：“犯罪的猪！我发誓要叫人用带钉的皮鞭抽你，然后砍你的头。穆斯林们啊！你们不知道这是哗变，是滔天大罪吗？”
“那我们就只好结果了他，”擒他的人叹道。“就用他自己的剑！来！快，趁我堵住他的尖叫。”

===Original===
The speaker pressed his hand down on Muhammad’s mouth, while another drew the sword and plunged it several times into the prostrate form. They watched until the last convulsions ceased; then piously observed: “Our Lord have mercy on him! There is no power nor might save in Allah, the High, the Tremendous!”
“By Allah, he could bite!” observed his first assailant, shaking blood from his right hand. The palm was bitten through. He stopped to bandage it; and then they made a litter with their spades and so conveyed the body back to camp with wailing.
“The darling of our souls is dead,” they chanted. “Slain by the infidels, whom we repulsed. Our brother, Abdul Câder, too, is wounded in the hand.”

===Chinese===
说话的人把手掌往下压紧穆罕默德的嘴，另一人抽出剑来，朝那横陈的身躯连捅数下。他们盯着看，直到最后的抽搐止息；这才虔敬地说道：“愿我们的主怜悯他！除崇高威严的安拉外，别无权能与力量！”
“凭安拉说，他真会咬！”最先动手的那人说道，甩去右手上的血。掌心被咬穿了。他停下来包扎；随后他们用铁锹拼成一副担架，一路哭嚎着把尸身抬回营地。
“我们心尖上的宝贝死了，”他们齐声哭唱。“被异教徒杀死了——那些异教徒已被我们击退。我们的兄弟阿卜杜勒·卡迪尔（Abdul Câder）也伤了手。”

===Original===
The lie was quite transparent, yet it passed unquestioned. The high commanders shrugged and let it go. There were a hundred men concerned, with Allah knew how many sympathizers. They dreaded a stampede of all the conscripts in the camp.
When Ali, mad with grief, demanded justice, he was told to hold his tongue. The general was profoundly grieved; he shed some tears, and swore that every honour should be paid to the remains. A telegram was sent to Yûsuf Pasha announcing that his son had died a martyr, and that the blessed body was upon its way to Cairo. Within an hour of death it had been dressed for burial. It was carried in a fine procession to the railway, where a special train﻿—a locomotive and an open truck﻿—was waiting. The corpse was laid down in the truck, and covered with some tent-cloth; and Ali sat beside it, while the train sped hooting on past empty villages, where only a few children played upon the dust-heaps, a few women stood in doorways with hands shading eyes, past palm-groves and the fields of cotton and of sugarcane until the citadel rose up before him in the evening sky.

===Chinese===
这谎话一望便知，却无人质疑。高级军官们耸耸肩，随它去了。牵涉其中的有一百来人，抱同情的还有多少，唯有安拉知道。他们生怕全营的新兵一齐惊溃。
阿里悲愤欲狂，要求伸冤，却被告知闭嘴。将军深感哀恸；他洒了几点泪，发誓要给遗骸以一切的哀荣。一封电报发往优素福·帕夏，告知他的儿子已列殉道之位，蒙福的遗体正在运往开罗途中。死后不到一个钟头，遗体已穿好安葬的衣服。它被一列体面的行列抬送到铁路跟前，一列专车——一台机车加一节敞篷货车——正候在那里。尸身安放在货车里，盖上几块帐篷布；阿里坐在旁边；列车鸣着汽笛一路疾驰，掠过一座座空村——村里只有几个孩子在土堆上玩耍，几个女人站在门口以手遮眼张望——掠过棕榈林和棉花田、甘蔗田，直到暮色四合的天空里，城堡的轮廓在他面前耸起。

## XXXIII / 三十三
===Original===
The news was broken gently to the stricken mother. Yûsuf, overcoming his own grief, came in at noon and sat an hour with her, leading her up by little steps to view the glory that their son had died a martyr for the Faith. When the announcement came at length, the fortitude he had assumed gave way. He wept profusely. But Barakah was tearless. She sat rigid, with pale eyes staring vaguely in a face of stone.
