She asked that Ali, as soon as he arrived, might be sent in to her; and that was all. Umm ed-Dahak came and mumbled on her hand, moaning endearments which she did not hear. Then Ali was announced. At the same instant dreadful wailing filled the house. She drew her head-veil round her face (the movement had become instinctive) when he fell before her, pouring forth his awful story, concluding with the words: “The funeral sets forth this minute, O my lady. His body will not keep with all those wounds.”
And then her anguish passed the bounds of suffering; she moved and looked and spoke, but felt no more.
Her women, half demented, danced around her. They tore their flesh with fingernails, defiled their faces, and raised an endless chant, reviewing all the charms and virtues of the dear one, his mother’s love, the blackness of the world, each verse concluding with a shriek of “O calamity!” It was the triumph-song of death.

===Chinese===
噩耗被小心翼翼地透露给那位深受打击的母亲。优素福强抑着自己的悲痛，中午进来陪她坐了一个钟头，一点一点引着她去领会那份光荣——他们的儿子已为信仰殉道而死。待到这消息终于和盘托出，他强撑起来的坚毅垮了下来。他泪如泉涌。巴拉卡却无泪。她直挺挺地坐着，苍白的双眼在一张石头般的脸上茫然直视。她只要求阿里一到就领来见她；再没有别的话了。乌姆·埃德-达哈克进来，伏在她手上喃喃低语，呜咽着唤出种种爱称，她一概没有听见。随后阿里被通报进来。就在同一瞬，骇人的恸哭响彻了整座宅子。她把头纱往脸上一拢（这动作早已成了本能）——阿里扑倒在她面前，倒出那段骇人听闻的经过，末了说道：“夫人啊，灵柩这就要出发了。他浑身是伤，尸身搁不住。”
就在这时，她的悲恸越出了受苦的界限；她还能动、能看、能说，却再也感觉不到什么了。
她的女仆们半疯半癫，绕着她舞蹈。她们用指甲抓挠皮肉，污损面容，扬起一支没完没了的挽歌，历数心肝宝贝的种种妩媚、种种贤德，他母亲的爱，这世界的昏黑，每一节都以一声“啊，灾祸啊！”的尖嚎收尾。那是死亡的凯歌。

===Original===
Robbed of the corpse, the funeral over, they thronged her chamber, keeping up the ghastly round, the death-chant, in the hope to give her tears. Her petrifaction filled them with dismay. To women who accept with rapture all life’s chances, whose custom is to celebrate each blow that strikes them and magnify it as a witness to the power of God, her stony apathy appeared uncanny. They increased their efforts, while Umm ed-Dahak poured into her ear a song of memory designed to loose the frozen fountain of despair.
“She was the fairest daughter of the seed of Adam. See her now! Her feet, her fingertips dropped perfume. She had the grace of flowers, the voice of turtles. Now behold her! In a moment blind and deaf and dumb and paralyzed. And why? Alas, O thou who askest! it is because the sunshine of her life is fled. We saw her follow his dead body to the grave. As the cow pursues the calf that has been reft from her, so did she follow blindly with a noise of lowing. She has not even strength to beat her face. Her breath is painful, husky like the voice of doves; its sound is all the sobbing of the childless mother. Say, O beloved, what is in thy mind? Dost thou remember his tarbush, his yellow slippers, the loveliness of all that touched his body, which was perfumed amber? There was a little mole upon his breast well known to thee. O Allah, waken memory, or grief will slay her!”

===Chinese===
亡人的尸身被夺了去，葬礼已毕，她们仍挤满她的屋子，继续着那套阴惨的轮舞、那支死亡挽歌，指望引出她的眼泪。她这般僵若顽石，叫她们满心惶恐。对这些把命运的一切遭际都欣然领受、惯于把打在身上的每一记打击当作庆典来办、夸大成真主权能之见证的女人而言，她这石头一般的冷漠显得邪气逼人。她们愈发卖力，乌姆·埃德-达哈克则对着她的耳朵倾注一支追忆之歌，只求凿开那冻结的绝望之泉。
“她是亚当后裔中最美丽的女儿。瞧她如今！她的双脚、她的指尖，都滴落着香露。她有百花的娉婷、斑鸠的清音。如今看她！顷刻之间，又瞎、又聋、又哑、又瘫了。这是为什么？唉，你这动问的人啊！只因她生命里的阳光遁去了。我们眼看她跟着他的遗体走到了坟前。像母牛追觅被夺走的牛犊，她也盲目地跟随，一路发出哞哞的哀鸣。她连抓破自己脸颊的力气都没有了。她的呼吸艰难，嘶哑如鸽子的声音；那声响，全是无子之母的呜咽。说呀，心爱的人，你心里在想些什么？你可记得他的塔布什帽、他的黄拖鞋、那贴过他身子的一切物事的可爱？那身子是熏了龙涎香的。他胸前有一颗小痣，你再熟识不过。安拉啊，唤醒她的记忆吧，不然悲痛就要杀了她！”

===Original===
Barakah saw and heard as in a trance. She thought herself in Hell, bound fast and gagged while devils taunted her. She was tortured by the memory of English winter evenings, of walking back from church in the long train of orphans, the patter of their feet resounding sadly. That dreariness appeared a state of bliss compared with this luxurious life enclosed in heat. She longed for a cold wind, with rain in it. Remembrance of a garden under sunset came to her; she saw once more a cool verandah with long windows open on an English drawing-room, and heard the earnest voice of Mrs. Cameron entreating her to stay and save her soul. This was God’s punishment. Her life from then till now had been all frowardness and self-indulgence. While basking in it she had been aware that it was baneful. A thousand awful faces rose to sneer, “We warned you!” The glimpses she had had of horrid depths, the scenes of bloodshed and the tales of cruelty, seemed now emphatic warnings of this end. She had sunk downward till she had no faith nor virtue more than beasts have. Her all was in her son, whom God had killed. Crushed, maimed, defrauded, she was flung upon the earth, the scorn of men and angels and the sport of fiends.
As by degrees her sense returned to her, she looked about her with strange eyes and tried to think. But every effort was a sword that pierced her heart. One morning, peering dully through her lattice, she saw a gay pavilion in the yard, and leading to it rows of masts with lanterns hung between. They were erected for the meytam, or reception for the dead. She had seen them often when she visited great houses; but now her mind attached no meaning to them. It was two hours later, in the middle of the function, that her sense returned. A mighty gust of grief, a cry of “O calamity!” swept through the crowd of black-clad women in her great reception-room. It roused her mind. She saw, and was alarmed. What was she doing? What was all this crowd of people? Were they human?

===Chinese===
巴拉卡如在迷离恍惚中看着、听着。她只当自己身在地狱，被牢牢捆住、塞住了口，任魔鬼们嘲弄。英国冬日黄昏的记忆折磨着她：从教堂出来，随着孤儿们长长的队列走回去，他们细碎的脚步声凄凉地回响。比起这困在溽热中的奢华生活，那样一种阴郁凄凉竟显得是一种极乐之境。她渴望一阵冷风，风里带着雨。一座夕阳下的花园浮上她的记忆；她又一次望见一道清凉的游廊，长窗开向一间英式客厅，又听见卡梅伦夫人恳切的声音，劝她留下来，拯救自己的灵魂。这是真主的惩罚。从那时直到如今，她这一生全是执拗悖逆、恣意纵乐。沉溺其间的时候，她原也知道它是有害的。一千张狰狞的面孔涌起来讥笑：“我们警告过你！”她曾瞥见的那些可怖的深渊、那些流血的场面与残酷的传闻，此刻看来都是指向这个下场的恳切警兆。她一路沉沦，直到信念与德行都不比畜生更多。她的一切都在儿子身上，而儿子被真主杀死了。被碾碎、被伤残、被欺诈一空，她被摔在地上，任世人与众天使耻笑，任恶魔戏弄。
她的神志渐渐回来，她用陌生的眼光环顾四周，试着思索。可每一次努力都是一柄刺穿她心的剑。一天早晨，她隔着窗棂呆呆外望，看见院子里搭起一座花花绿绿的凉棚，一排排木杆通到棚前，杆间挂着灯笼。那是为“迈伊塔姆”（meytam）——接待亡者的仪式——竖起来的。她从前去高门大宅做客时常常见到；可此刻她的心思给它们安不上任何意义。两个钟头之后，仪式进行到中段，她的神志才算回来。一阵强猛的悲恸、一声“啊，灾祸啊！”，扫过她那大客厅里满堂黑衣的女人。这唤醒了她的心智。她看见了，心里惊惶。她在做什么？这一大群人是怎么回事？他们还是人吗？

===Original===
The great saloon was full of women. The ladies sat up on the dais with flourished handkerchiefs, beating their breasts, their faces, at each burst of woe. Dependants crouched upon the ground and rocked incessantly, with foaming lips. Some faces wore a hideous fixed grin; some mouthed continually. The hired performers stood and chanted with obscene contortions, or squatted on a mat and wailed in chorus. The words “O my calamity,” recurring in a sort of running chant without coherence, shook the assembly like a tempest-blast. And all the while dainties were being handed round by weeping servants, and accepted by the mourners as fresh cause for grief.
An ague of intense repugnance seized on Barakah. She felt that she must fly from this inferno, must keep the hope of flight before her resolutely, or her soul was lost. It was as if a hostile hand compressed her throat. She struggled, was determined to get free. Towards that end she battled with instinctive cunning.

===Chinese===
大客厅里挤满了女人。太太们高坐在台上，挥舞着手帕，每一阵悲声爆发，便捶打自己的胸口和脸颊。仆妇们蹲在地上，摇个不停，嘴唇上挂着白沫。有的脸上凝着狰狞的僵笑；有的嘴不停地张合。雇来的哭丧女人站着，扭出不堪入目的姿态唱着挽歌，或是蹲在席上齐声嚎哭。“我这灾祸啊”几个字，以一种漫无条贯的流水腔反复出现，像一阵风暴撼动着满堂的人。而这全部过程中，垂泪的仆人们还在轮番奉上精美点心，吊客们接过来，又当作新的悲伤缘由。
一阵强烈憎恶引起的寒战攫住了巴拉卡。她感到必须逃出这活地狱，必须决然把出逃的希望悬在眼前，否则她的灵魂就完了。仿佛有一只敌意的手掐紧了她的喉咙。她挣扎着，决意脱身。为达此目的，她凭着本能的狡黠作战。

===Original===
After the meytam, when she seemed exhausted, her brain, enamoured of this hope, was planning madly.
“Take heart, O moon of moons,” the servants told her. “Inshallah thou shalt bring forth sons instead of him.”
She strove to smile.

===Chinese===
迈伊塔姆过后，她显出一副精疲力竭的样子，脑子里却迷恋着那个希望，发狂似的设计筹划。
“宽心吧，月亮中的月亮，”仆人们对她说。“若安拉意欲，你还会生下儿子来代替他。”
她强颜作了一笑。

===Original===
Her resolution was to leave her husband and her little daughter, the comfortable house, the easy life, to stray alone and homeless, back to Christian lands. There she would enter some religious order, and spend the residue of life in prayer for Muslims.
Everyone was kind. The tender sympathy of Yûsuf, though himself hard stricken, might well have won her heart had she possessed one. Her heart was dead and buried in the grave. The ladies and her servants tried at first to cheer her; but when they found their efforts useless, let her be. Only Umm ed-Dahak remained with her constantly. Discreet as ever, she kept silence for long hours, watching her mistress with a doleful mow. They thought her too depressed to take a step unaided, had not the least suspicion of her wish to flee. It was, besides, a time of national anxiety, when everyone who could went out to seek the news, and those imprisoned listened to the noises of the street.

===Chinese===
她的决心是：抛下丈夫和小女儿，抛下舒适的宅子、安逸的日子，孤身一人、无家可归地流落回基督教的国度去。到了那里，她要进一家修会，把余生用来为穆斯林们祈祷。
人人都待她很好。优素福自己也遭受了沉重打击，他那份温柔的怜惜，本可以轻易赢得她的心——倘若她还保有一颗心的话。她的心已死，葬进了坟墓。太太们和她的仆人们起初还想逗她开怀；待发现一切徒劳，也就由她去了。只有乌姆·埃德-达哈克始终陪着她。她一如既往地知趣谨饬，一连好几个钟头默不作声，带着一脸愁苦的怪相望着主母。众人都以为她哀伤得没人扶着便迈不出一步，对她想出逃的心思没有丝毫疑心。再说，那正是举国惶惶的时节，能走动的人都出去打听消息，被困在宅中的人便听街上的声息。

===Original===
One day, in the full heat of noon, when men are sleepy, she sent out the old woman on an errand; and went and kissed her child, Afîfah, who was fast asleep. Then, having made sure that the slave-girls were not moving, she returned to her own room and donned a common habbarah, which she had sometimes worn when she went out with Umm ed-Dahak. From the store of money Yûsuf had entrusted to her she took sufficient to defray her fare to France, and hung it in a bag around her neck.
Thus furnished, she stole out through the selamlik hall. No eunuch challenged; the doorkeeper was snoring on his couch within the entry. Beside him lay the best part of a watermelon.

===Chinese===
一天，正午溽热最盛、人人昏昏欲睡的时分，她打发老妇人出门办事；自己去亲了亲睡得正沉的小女儿阿菲法（Afîfah）。随后，确认女奴们没有动静，她回到自己房里，穿上一件寻常的哈巴拉罩袍——随乌姆·埃德-达哈克出门时她偶尔穿过。她从优素福交托她保管的积蓄里，取出足以支付去法国旅费的钱，装在袋子里挂在颈间。
如此装备停当，她悄悄溜出去，穿过塞拉姆利克大厅。没有阉人喝问；守门人躺在门厅里侧的躺椅上打着鼾。他身边搁着大半个西瓜。

## XXXIV / 三十四

===Original===
Barakah had not made many steps outside the house before she was completely lost. Although for sixteen years her home had been in Cairo, she had never walked in the streets before. Which was the way? She could not tell, but went on bravely, hoping for some guide. At last she met a donkey-driver with a pleasant face. In answer to her timid hail, he smiled delighted and praised his Maker for the honour of her patronage. “To the railway station,” she enjoined at mounting, and he answered “Ready!”

Away they went, arousing echoes in the stony alleys, the driver shouting as he ran beside the ambling beast. Barakah felt exhilarated by the change of motion, the little spice of danger when they dashed round corners, or charged some group of wayfarers with warning cries. The first stage of her flight would soon be over; and once on board the train, she thought, escape was sure.

The streets were empty even for that hour. Scavenger dogs slept undisturbed in every spot of shade. The persons they encountered seemed to have no business, but stood about in groups conversing glumly. On the wide, dusty square before the railway station groups were many. A little crowd beset the station doors. These were all closed, to Barakah’s amazement. The building looked deserted.

===Chinese===
巴拉卡出了宅门没走几步，便完全迷了路。十六年来她虽以开罗为家，却从未在街上走过路。该走哪条道？她说不上来，只管壮着胆子往前走，巴望能遇上个引路的。终于，她碰到一个面相和善的赶驴人。她怯生生的一声招呼，他便喜笑颜开，赞美造化他的主，感激她赏光惠顾。上驴的时候她吩咐道：“去火车站。”他答一声：“这就走！”

他们上路了，蹄声在石头巷子里激起回响，赶驴人跑在缓步而行的牲口旁，一路吆喝。这一番换了的行进令巴拉卡精神一爽——疾冲过街角、或高喊着让路冲向一堆行人时那一点点险趣，更叫她心头轻快。出逃的第一程眼看就要完结；她想，只要登上火车，逃脱便已成定局。

即便以那个时辰而论，街道也空得出奇。食腐的野狗在每一处荫凉里酣睡，无人惊扰。他们遇见的人似乎都无事可办，只成群站在一处，闷闷地交谈。火车站前那片尘土漫漫的宽阔广场上，人堆更多。一小群人围在车站门口。令巴拉卡惊愕的是，门全都关着。那座建筑看上去已被弃置。

===Original===
“Ask when the next train starts for the seacoast,” she ordered her attendant, who addressed a shout to persons standing near.

“The seacoast? Allah knows! It may be never!” The reply was shrugged. “A great fight has taken place. The end has come. The English fell upon the camp at daybreak﻿—yesterday or this morning, Allah knows! The rebel army was dispersed like chaff. The leader﻿—the arch-traitor﻿—escaped hither on an engine, and is in the town now somewhere, herding with his kind. It is clearly seen how foully he deceived us, seducing us from our allegiance with the promise of success.”

“Praise be to Allah that his reign is ended,” said another. “If the English were but true believers, one would bless them.”

“Nay, the tidings are not certain,” cried a third with anguish.

“As certain as the sun is hot upon my reins this minute. I have it from a man who saw Arâbi. The rascal’s face was yellow as a corpse.”

===Chinese===
“去问问下一班开往海边的火车几点动身，”她吩咐随行的童子，他便冲站在近旁的人们喊了一嗓子。

“海边？安拉才知道！兴许永远也没有了！”对方耸耸肩，这样回答。“打了一场大仗。一切都完了。英国人破晓时分袭击了营地——昨天早上还是今早，安拉才知道！叛军像糠秕一样给驱散了。那位领袖——那个头号叛贼——坐着一台机车逃到这儿，如今就躲在城里什么地方，与其同类厮混一处。他多么卑劣地欺骗了我们，拿必胜的许诺诱我们背弃了臣节，如今看得明明白白。”

“赞美安拉，他的统治总算到头了，”另一人说。“英国人若是信士，就该为他们祝福才是。”

“不，这消息还不确凿，”第三个人痛苦地喊道。

“就像此刻日头灼在我腰上一样确凿。我是从一个亲眼见过阿拉比的人那儿听来的。那流氓的脸黄得像死尸。”

===Original===
Barakah’s mind received no more than the initial statement. The way that she had meant to take was closed against her.

“Whither, my lady?” asked the donkey-boy, with willing smile.

“Far, far away﻿—towards the seacoast. Anywhere!”

“Ready!” he laughed. “It is for thee to order. By Allah, we will go to Gebel Qaf if thou desire it.”

===Chinese===
这话巴拉卡只听进了头一句。她原本要走的路，已对她封死了。

“往哪儿去，夫人？”赶驴的童子一脸殷勤的笑，问道。

“远远地——往海边去。哪儿都行！”

“这就走！”他笑道。“全凭您吩咐。凭安拉，只要您愿意，咱们就是上卡夫山也成。”

===Original===
He smote his donkey, and they jogged along once more, out through new suburbs to the open fields. The sun was an armed foe, the dust a persecutor; her habbarah and face-veil made a sheath of fire. The donkey-boy kept looking at her with compassion, smiling encouragement whenever he could meet her gaze. He thought her mad, and so indulged her fancy, assuring her that it would not take long to reach the sea. But when she murmured of the heat and wished to rest, he showed immense relief.

“That is the best,” he cried. “Wait till I find some pleasant shade for thee. See, yonder is a tree. There thou shalt rest till the great heat is past, and then, at thy command, we can resume the journey.”

Dismounting under leaves, she sank upon the ground and wept despairingly. The tears, which bitter grief had failed to wring from her, flowed freely for her impotence. Escape was hopeless. Her project now appeared the last absurdity. The change of clothes, the change of manners, now presented difficulties which she felt that she would never have the strength to overcome. The donkey-boy’s consoling words, his friendly grin, were teasing. She sent him to fetch water from a village near at hand. He came back with a pitcher and two slabs of bread; which so revived her spirit that she once more saw beyond the moment and conceived a plan.

She would wait till nightfall and then seek the city of the dead, to die on her son’s grave, if Allah willed it. At least she would spend all the night in prayer imploring Allah’s mercy for him in the name of Christ.

===Chinese===
他一掌拍在驴身上，他们又颠簸着上路，穿过新辟的城郊，到了开阔的田野。太阳是个持械的仇敌，尘土是个逼迫者；她的哈巴拉罩袍和面纱成了一具火的鞘壳。赶驴的童子不住怜悯地看她，每当能遇上她的目光，便笑着一副鼓励的神气。他只当她疯了，索性由着她的痴念，一口担保说到海边去用不了多少时候。可等她低声抱怨暑热、想要歇息时，他却显出大大的如释重负。

“这就再好不过了，”他喊道。“且等我给您寻个舒坦的荫凉。瞧，那边有棵树。您就在那儿歇着，等这大热过去，然后凭您一句话，咱们再接着赶路。”

她在树荫下下了驴，瘫坐在地上，绝望地哭了起来。苦悲没能从她眼里逼出的泪水，此刻为她自己的束手无策畅流不止。逃脱是没有指望的了。她的盘算如今显得荒谬到了极处。换装束、改举止，这些此刻都成了重重难关，她觉得自己再也没有气力去克服。赶驴童子的宽慰话、他那友好的咧嘴笑，都成了揶揄。她打发他去近旁一座村子取水。他带着一罐水、两块面饼回来了；这让她精神一振，目光又能越过眼前，想出了一个主意。

她要等到夜幕降临，再去寻那亡者之城，若安拉意欲，就死在儿子的坟上。至少，她要整夜祈祷，以基督之名恳求安拉怜悯他。

===Original===
She had sat a long while, cross-legged, gazing straight before her, her hands locked in her lap, when a soft voice disturbed her. The donkey-boy was plucking at her sleeve.

“The heat is spent,” he told her. “Best be moving! It is back into the city﻿—not so?﻿—thy command? Much better than to journey to the sea, like this, without provision. Say, which way?”

Barakah pointed a direction listlessly. She had no wish to enter Cairo before dark, so chose a long way round, among the fields.

Soon the sunset reddened all the plain, stretching their shadows far before them on the dyke. The citadel upon its height was hotly flushed one minute, the next ash-grey and lifeless like a skull. It lived in her imagination as a monstrous spider which held her with its web and drew her in.

===Chinese===
她盘腿坐了许久，直直望着前方，双手交扣在膝上，一个轻柔的声音惊动了她。是那赶驴的童子在拉她的衣袖。

“暑气退了，”他告诉她。“最好动身了！是回城里去——是不是？——您的吩咐？总比这样不带盘缠干粮往海边赶强得多。说吧，走哪条路？”

巴拉卡有气无力地指了个方向。她不愿在天黑之前进开罗，便拣了一条远路，打田野间绕行。

不多时，落日染红了整个平原，把他们的影子顺着堤道远远地投在前头。高冈上的城堡一时红得发烫，转眼又灰白如死，像一颗骷髅。在她想象里，它活似一只巨蛛，用蛛网把她缠住，往里拖拽。

===Original===
The donkey-boy beside her prattled ceaselessly.

“O lady, I will not forsake thee﻿—no, by the Prophet, never, till thy mind is healed. Do I know the cemetery El Afîfi? Wallahi! I can guide thee thither. Not a bad idea; for Allah comforts those who visit the deceased. By the Sayyid Ahmad, thou art as my mother. May God cut short my life if I desert thee in thy present state.”

The lad’s support was of some comfort to her.

===Chinese===
身旁的赶驴童子絮絮叨叨，说个不停。

“夫人啊，我不会丢下您的——不，凭先知起誓，绝不，除非您的心神复原。那埃尔-阿菲菲墓园（El Afîfi），我认得吗？凭安拉！我能领您去。这主意不坏；因为安拉会安慰那些探望亡人的人。凭赛义德·艾哈迈德起誓，您就像我的亲娘。您眼下这般光景，我若丢下您走，愿真主短了我的寿数。”

这少年的扶持，给了她些许安慰。

===Original===
In the first blue of night, when daylight lingers in the memory, they were following a sandy road towards the city, when a noise as of the sea arose behind them. The donkey-boy was first to hear it. He stood still and listened, holding up his hand. It seemed approaching on the road behind them. He looked puzzled; then suddenly let fall his hands, and made a bound.

“It is the army! Come, O my lady! We must hide ourselves. Hold fast!” He made the donkey gallop for a hundred yards, then led it down into a patch of cane. Peeping out between the stems they saw vague forms in clouds of dust approaching on the dyke above. The roar became the jangle of accoutrements, the roll of heavy carriages upon the road and murmuring voices.

Innumerable ranks of horsemen passed, dust-stained and weary, with faces resolutely strained towards Cairo. Barakah saw them as the figures of a dream. Their silhouette against the sky appeared familiar. The words with which they cheered their tired horses rang on memory.

“It is the English,” whispered her companion hoarsely.

“The English! Allah, help me!” murmured Barakah. Until that moment she had lost remembrance of the war.

===Chinese===
夜色初蓝、白昼还萦回在记忆里的时分，他们正顺着一条沙路往城里去，身后忽然涌起一阵如海的声响。赶驴的童子头一个听见。他站定细听，举起手来。那声音似乎正从他们身后的路上逼近。他一脸困惑；随后猛地垂下手，纵身一跃。

“是大军！快来，夫人！咱们得躲一躲。坐稳了！”他赶着驴疾驰了百来码，随后把它牵下路，引进一片蔗田。他们从蔗秆间偷眼张望，只见上方堤道上尘雾滚滚，幢幢人影正逼近过来。那轰鸣渐渐分辨出装具的铿锵、沉重的车轮碾过路面的隆隆声，还有嘈嘈的人语。

数不清的骑兵队列过去了，风尘满面，疲惫不堪，一张张脸决然地朝向开罗。巴拉卡看他们，如同看梦中的形影。他们衬在天际的剪影透着熟悉。他们激励疲马的吆喝声，在她记忆里铮铮回响。

“是英国人，”她的同伴哑着嗓子低声道。

“英国人！安拉啊，救救我！”巴拉卡喃喃道。直到那一刻，她才想起还有战争这回事。

## XXXIX / 三十九

===Original===
“You asked for a private interview. It is a little unusual, I believe, in this country; but I granted your request upon the understanding that you have important secrets to communicate, as stated in your letter. Let me see﻿—ah, here it is!”

The English official﻿—a broad-shouldered, fresh-complexioned man inclined to baldness﻿—having studied her appearance through a monocle, let fall that weapon and, disturbing papers on his desk, produced the letter she had written to him, which looked somehow pitiful.

“I am an English lady. My name is Mary Smith. I did a very wicked thing. I turned Muhammadan, and married a Turkish gentleman, a Pasha, here in Cairo. I want to leave him and return to Christianity. I am an English lady, by name Mary Smith; not what they call me. I am prepared to take my oath that this is true, and Mrs. Cameron can tell you﻿—I must get away!”

===Chinese===
“你要求单独会面。我相信，这在这个国家多少有些不寻常；但我应允了你的请求，依据的是你在信中的申明——你有重要的机密要相告。让我瞧瞧——啊，在这儿！”

那位英国官员——一个宽肩膀、面色红润、头顶见稀的男人——先用单片眼镜端详了一番她的模样，随后放下那件武器，翻乱桌上的公文，找出她写给他的那封信；那信瞧着竟有几分可怜。

我是个英国女人。我的名字是玛丽·史密斯。我做了一件十分罪恶的事。我皈依了伊斯兰，在开罗这里嫁给了一位土耳其绅士、一位帕夏。我要离开他，回到基督教里来。我是英国女人，名叫玛丽·史密斯；不是他们叫我的那个名字。我情愿起誓证明这是真的，卡梅伦夫人可以对您说——我必须逃出去！

===Original===
“What is all this, and who is Mrs. Cameron? In what way does your private history concern me? I beg you to pass on to the important statement which you have to make.”

“I ask your help to get away from the harem.”

At that the Englishman resumed his eyeglass and surveyed her with a slight gape of amazement.

The scene of conversation was a large room, sparsely furnished with a desk, a table and a few plain chairs. The light from the high window shone on Barakah who, to prove that she was really English, had removed her face-veil. The critic’s wondering stare first made her conscious of the discrepancy with her request of highly raddled cheeks and lips, and kohled eyes﻿—the touches Umm ed-Dahak had declared so beautiful. She was not a European any longer. Her very words resounded with a foreign accent. From the moment of her entering the presence of this hateful man, she had been persuaded of the folly of her errand, out of heart with it. Her speech, when uttered, carried no conviction.

===Chinese===
“这都是些什么话？卡梅伦夫人又是谁？你的私事与我有什么相干？我请你还是言归正传，说说你有必要申明的重要事项吧。”

“我请求您帮我离开这内宅。”

听了这话，那英国人重新举单片眼镜，微微张着嘴，惊讶地打量她。

交谈的场所是一间大屋，陈设寥寥：一张书桌、一张桌子和几把素净的椅子。高窗透下的光正照在巴拉卡身上——为了证明自己真是英国人，她已摘去了面纱。这位品评者惊异的凝视，才使她头一回意识到，这一身妆扮与自己的请求多么不称：双颊和嘴唇涂得俗艳，眼睛描着眼墨——正是乌姆·埃德-达哈克夸为极美的那些妆点。她已经不再是个欧洲人了。她一开口，字字都带着外国腔。从踏进这个可憎男人面前的那一刻起，她便已明白此行的荒唐，心灰意冷了。话出了口，也全无说服力。

===Original===
“Indeed, indeed, I am an Englishwoman,” she persisted, with a kind of whimper. “I want to get away from here and lead a Christian life.”

But while she spoke the words her hands were busy readjusting the white muslin mouth-veil as a step towards going.

The great official shrugged his shoulders “Is that all you have to say?”

“Perhaps﻿—I mean, I know that I did wrong to come here.” She was quivering from head to foot with shame. The act of sitting on a chair embarrassed her. She was completely out of touch with English ways.

===Chinese===
“真的，真的，我是个英国女人，”她近乎呜咽地坚持道。“我要离开这儿，过基督徒的生活。”

可话音未落，她的双手已忙着重新掩好那方白色细纱的口纱，权作离去的第一步。

这位大官员耸了耸肩。“你要说的就这些吗？”

“也许——我是说，我知道自己上这儿来是错了。”她羞得从头到脚直打颤。坐椅子这个动作也叫她局促不安。她与英国人的规矩已全然隔膜了。

===Original===
“Well, I don’t quite see what I can do for you,” said her appraiser, in a tone of bland reproach. “You see we are here as guardians of the laws and customs of the country. We could hardly, therefore, interfere in a case such as yours﻿—a harem quarrel. As for the religious controversy, I can tell you we avoid it like hot coals. Our one desire is to uphold the institutions of the country. Really, my dear lady, I think the only thing for you to do is to go straight home and make the best of it.”

At that she rose. He passed before her to the door and held it open. She thought of offering her hand, but his grand bow forbade it; and she went out in profound humiliation.

“Well, art thou happy?” chuckled Umm ed-Dahak, still believing that she was the servant of a criminal intrigue. She prattled merrily till they regained the carriage and were driving homeward, when she noticed that her lady trembled and looked sad.

“Alas!” she cried. “My dove, my poor one, is it so? Woe, woe for womankind! There comes a time to all of us when love escapes.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，我实在看不出能为你做点什么，”她那位品评者用一种温和的责备口吻说道。“你要明白，我们在这里是本国法律和风俗的守护者。因此，像你这样的案子——一场内宅口角——我们几乎无从干预。至于宗教上的争执，我可以告诉你，我们避之犹如烫手的火炭。我们唯一的愿望，就是维护这个国家的种种制度。说真的，亲爱的女士，我看你唯一可走的路，就是径直回家去，将就着把日子过好。”

听了这话，她站起身来。他抢在她前头走到门边，替她拉开门。她本想伸手相握，却被他那堂皇的一躬止住了；她便在深深的屈辱中走了出去。

“怎么样，您称心了吗？”乌姆·埃德-达哈克窃笑着问，心里还当自家主母是在为一桩犯罪的密谋跑腿。她一路絮絮地说笑，直到两人重新登上马车、往家赶去，才发觉她的夫人浑身发抖、面带愁容。

“哎呀！”她叫道。“我的鸽子，我可怜的人儿，当真如此吗？悲哉，悲哉，我们女人家！我们谁都有爱情悄然溜走的那一天。”

===Original===
But Barakah surveyed a wider disillusion.

Until just now she had been strong in the conceit that she was different from Eastern women, recognizably of higher race. From her dreams with Umm ed-Dahak, built on memories of Mrs. Cameron’s entreaties and the Consul’s arguments, she had derived the notion that she was of value to the English, who would fain reclaim her. Now that mirage, born of the sleepy harem atmosphere, was swept away; and she was nothing. With English people, she would always long for Orientals; with Orientals, feel a yearning for the life of Europe.

And in religion, likewise, she was nothing. A Christian by conviction after years of scoffing, she was doomed to play the part of a Muhammadan, to lose her soul. And she was glad to be returning to the life so lately dreaded, the vision of herself in English eyes had so appalled her. Well, she was nothing, and her soul of small account. The harem was her natural home; the teaching of the wise and kindly Prophet her protection. She now beheld the vanity of all her struggles, the vulgarity of much concern about the future. God was merciful! In self-annihilation there was peace. Thus through her striving after Christianity she reached at last the living heart of El Islam.

===Chinese===
巴拉卡眼里映出的，却是一场更广大的幻灭。

直到方才，她还固守着一股自矜：觉得自己与东方女子不同，一望便知属于更高的种族。在与乌姆·埃德-达哈克共做的那些梦里——那梦建立在卡梅伦夫人的恳求和领事的说辞之上——她得出了这样的念头：她对英国人自有价值，他们会乐意把她收回去。如今，那生于昏昏欲睡的内宅氛围中的海市蜃楼被一扫而空；她什么也不是了。与英国人相处，她会永远渴念东方人；与东方人相处，又会向往欧洲的生活。

在宗教上，她同样什么也不是。嘲弄了多年之后诚心归信了基督，她却注定要扮演一个穆斯林，注定要失去自己的灵魂。而她竟庆幸就要回到不久前还惧怕的生活里去——她自己在英国人眼中的模样，已把她吓成这样。罢了，她什么也不是，她的灵魂也无足轻重。内宅是她天生的家；那位睿智仁厚的先知的教诲，是她的庇护。她如今看清了自己一切挣扎的虚妄，看清了为未来操心劳神的庸人自扰。真主是慈悲的！在自我的泯灭中有安宁。就这样，经由她对基督教的苦苦追求，她终于抵达了伊斯兰教那活的心灵。

## XXXV / 三十五

===Original===
The streets by night were full of people, in striking contrast with their emptiness at noon that day. The mosques were all alight inside, and from the glimpse which Barakah obtained through open doorways appeared crowded.
She saw men making towards them through the press, embracing precious bundles, with the look of fugitives.
“Their fear is of the English,” said the donkey-boy. “Who knows what they will do by way of punishment?”

===Chinese===
夜里的街道满是行人，与当天正午的空荡恰成鲜明的对照。清真寺里全都亮着灯火，巴拉卡从敞开的寺门望进去，只觉里面人满为患。
她看见男人们怀里搂着贵重的包袱，一副逃难者的神色，从人丛里挤着往清真寺去。
“他们怕的是英国人，”赶驴的童子说。“谁知道他们会拿出什么责罚来？”

===Original===
But the look on all the faces when a ray of light revealed them, the note of the vast murmur lapping the whole city, was rather of relief and comfort than anxiety. To hide away their treasures was a mere precaution which only madmen would neglect in presence of a conquering host; but men were thankful for the coming of the English, which meant an end to anarchy and wild suspense.
“Wallahi, they are warriors,” one orator was declaiming at a street corner. “The fight was far away at daybreak, and now behold them here among us in the citadel. Wallahi, they are mighty! They smite hard﻿—one blow and all is said. Wallahi, they are not of those who loiter. They appeared among us like a vision of the rising night; they demanded the keys of our strong places as of right divine. The people in the street stood still and gaped on them, rubbing their eyes to ascertain that they were not asleep. May Allah make them merciful. The praise to Allah!”

===Chinese===
然而，一线灯光照出那些面孔时，那神情，连同漫过全城的浩浩低语的声调，与其说是惶惶不安，毋宁说是宽慰与安宁。把财宝藏起来不过是未雨绸缪——征服大军压境之际，唯有疯子才会疏于此道；而人们是感激英国人到来的，因为那意味着无政府的乱局和提心吊胆的悬望都到了头。
“凭安拉，他们是勇士，”一位演说家正在街角上高声宣讲。“破晓时分，战事还远在天边，可瞧吧，这会儿他们已经到了咱们中间，进了城堡。凭安拉，他们何等强大！出手极狠——一击便大局已定。凭安拉，他们绝非迟缓流连之辈。他们像夜色初起时的一场幻象，突临咱们中间；他们索取咱们要塞的钥匙，仿佛凭着天赋神权。街上的人都呆立着望他们，直揉眼睛，要确证自己并没有睡着。愿安拉使他们仁慈。一切赞颂归于安拉！”

===Original===
The donkey-boy, who had been looking at the lady’s eyes at frequent intervals as if in expectation of a change of purpose, asked at length:
“Whither shall I conduct thee, O my mistress? Is it not thy wish to return to the house?”
“I have no house,” was her reply. “Did I not tell thee? To the El Afîfi cemetery!”
“Not by night! Hear reason, O my lady!” he besought her. “Tell me where thou dwellest, that I may conduct thee thither!”
“I go to the cemetery, as I told thee. It is necessary. If thou art weary of my service, I will pay thee and go out alone.”

===Chinese===
赶驴的童子时时去瞧夫人的眼睛，仿佛巴望她回心转意，末了终于问道：
“我该送您去哪儿，我的夫人？您不是想回宅子里去吗？”
“我没有宅子了，”她答道。“我没有告诉过你吗？去埃尔-阿菲菲墓园！”
“夜里可去不得！讲点儿道理吧，夫人！”他央告她。“告诉我您住在哪儿，我好送您回府！”
“我说了，我要去墓园。非去不可。你要是厌烦了伺候我，我付你钱，一个人去。”

===Original===
Barakah’s tone grew plaintive, almost tearful. The resolution in her words was mere bravado. She knew that she was utterly dependent on this friendly youth, whose company alone kept up her courage. From the moment of her turning back she had felt stupid, useless, relying on this boy to bring her to the cemetery, where she hoped to die. It seemed a certainty that if she prayed her utmost, full as her heart was, the vexed soul must leave the body, and the prayer by sheer brute force become acceptable. At thought of being baulked of her self-sacrifice, the boy’s help failing, she began to whimper.
“Nay, dearest lady, weep not!” he entreated. “By Allah, thou shalt neither walk nor go alone. I will conduct thee thither; but it may be necessary that we wait till morning, since the way is lonely and the haunt of jinn. See here, before us is my mother’s house. Deign to go in and rest awhile, and take refreshment, while I feed the donkey. I will make inquiries. If it is possible to go tonight, I swear to take thee. If not, thou canst rest here until the dawn.”

===Chinese===
巴拉卡的语气变得哀婉，几乎带出哭腔。她话里的决绝不过是虚张声势。她知道自己全然仰仗这个好心的少年，全靠有他作伴，她才撑得住勇气。自从掉头回城那一刻起，她就觉得自己又痴钝又无用，全赖这少年领她去那墓园——她指望死在那里。她仿佛认定：只要她倾尽全力地祈祷，把满怀心事尽数倾倒出来，那颗愁苦的灵魂必会脱离躯壳，那场祷告也必凭着一股蛮力蒙得悦纳。一想到这场自我牺牲会被断送、少年的帮扶又要落空，她便呜咽起来。
“别哭了，最亲爱的夫人，别哭！”他央求道。“凭安拉，您决不能独自步行，也决不能独自前往。我一定送您到那儿去；只是说不定得等天亮，因为那一路荒僻，又是精灵（jinn）出没的地方。瞧，我娘的房子就在眼前。请您赏光进去歇一会儿，用些点心，我好去喂驴。我去打听打听。今夜若能去，我发誓带您去。若不能，您就在这儿歇到天亮。”

===Original===
They had stopped before a doorway in a narrow alley. He went a little way into the gloom and whispered:
“O my mother!”
“Is it thou, Selîm?” came back the answer.
“O my mother, come at once! I have a lady, a great lady in disguise. She has run mad through grief in these bad times, and wants to go out to the cemetery. Receive her in thy house a minute, feed her, talk to calm her; while I discover if the way is safe.”
“The cemetery! Go not thither. Best come in and sleep.”

===Chinese===
他们在一条窄巷的一扇门前停了下来。他朝幽暗里走了几步，低声道：
“母亲啊！”
“是你吗，塞利姆（Selîm）？”里面应声道。
“母亲啊，快过来！我这儿有位夫人，一位乔装的贵妇人。赶上这乱世，她悲伤得疯了心，一心要上墓园去。请把她让进屋里待一会儿，给她吃的，陪她说说话，让她定定神；我去探探路上安不安全。”
“墓园！可别去那儿。还是进来睡觉的好。”

===Original===
“The lady is distraught with grief. I reverence her like a parent. She is absent from the world; she does not hear us. I think that she is going to the tombs to pray. It were a good deed to conduct her thither.”
“True, wallahi! May Allah heal her soul, the poor one! These be dreadful times!”
A woman came out to the doorway, holding up an earthen lamp.
“Deign to enter, O my sweet,” she called seductively.
Selîm assisted his employer to dismount.

===Chinese===
“这位夫人悲伤得神魂错乱了。我敬她如同敬奉父母。她魂不守舍，听不见咱们说话。我想她是要到坟上去祈祷。送她去那儿是一桩善功。”
“说得是，凭安拉！愿安拉治愈她的灵魂，可怜的人！这世道真是可怕！”
一个妇人举着一盏陶灯，来到门口。
“请进来吧，我的心肝儿，”她柔声诱哄着唤道。
塞利姆扶他的雇主下驴。

===Original===
“Go in and rest,” he whispered. “My mother and my sister are alone in there. Thou canst unveil. The dwellings of the poor are all haremlik. In a little while I shall return and call thee from without. I go but to make sure the ways are safe.”
The room in which she found herself was small and stuffy. It was lighted only by the little lamp the woman carried. Barakah was glad to loose her veil awhile. She refused the food, but drank the water, which the women offered, and listened to their cordial blessings with a sense of dreaming. Her prayer was that the boy might not decide to wait till morning. Desire to reach the tomb at once absorbed her life. Deprived of it, she would have had no further being. Her prayer now took the Christian form, and now the Muslim; the two religions growing tangled in her tired mind. At length the boy’s voice sounded:
“Deign to come, O lady. The ways are thronged, they tell me, as in Ragab. Tonight is not as other nights, it is well seen.”

===Chinese===
“进去歇着吧，”他低声说。“我娘和我妹妹独自在里头。您可以揭去面纱。穷人的住家，处处都是哈雷姆利克（haremlik，女眷内室）。过一会儿我就回来，在外面唤您。我去去就来，只为探明路上安稳。”
她置身的那间屋子又小又闷，只有那妇人手里提着的小灯照亮。巴拉卡乐得把面纱松开片刻。她谢绝了食物，却喝了妇人们奉上的水，听着她们热诚的祝福，恍然如置身梦中。她一心祈求的，是那少年别决定等到天亮。即刻到达坟茔的渴望吞没了她的性命；失了它，她便再无存在的凭依。她的祈祷一时取基督教的形式，一时又取穆斯林的形式；两种宗教在她困乏的头脑里纠缠不清。终于，少年的声音响了起来：
“请出来吧，夫人。人家告诉我，路上人头攒动，跟拉贾卜月（Ragab）里一样。今夜不是寻常的夜晚，这是明摆着的。”

===Original===
With praise to Allah she went out once more. But with its object now assured, her mind grew dull. It was as if suspense alone had held it wakeful. It lost the comprehension of its purpose, regained it with an effort, and then let it go.
They passed beneath an ancient gateway. The city was behind them. Still there was no solitude. Groups of people crossed the sand in all directions. It was a moonless night. The many lanterns moving in the darkness seemed reflections of the stars which shone like gems of many facets in the silky sky. Barakah saw them both alike as golden insects swarming in the cup of a great purple flower. At moments, her head swimming, she mistook the earth for sky, and had the sense of moving upside down.

===Chinese===
她赞颂着安拉，再次走了出去。然而目的既已拿稳，她的头脑便迟钝下来。仿佛先前唯有那悬念撑着它清醒似的。它把此行的用意丢了，费一把劲才又拾起，随即又撒手放掉。
他们从一道古老的门楼下穿过。城市落在了身后。四下依然不见荒僻。成群的人四面八方地穿行在沙地上。这是一个无月的夜。黑暗中移动的点点灯笼，仿佛是繁星的倒影；星星在绸缎般的夜空里闪烁，像一颗颗多面的宝石。巴拉卡看着这两者，都像是金色的虫豸，糜集在一朵巨大紫花的杯盏里。时而，她头晕目眩，错把大地当作天空，只觉得自己是倒悬着行走。

===Original===
“There is the cemetery,” said her guide. His whisper seemed to her a long way off. Nor did she see the city of the dead till they were in its streets, which loomed mysterious. The very stars looked sinister above the frowning domes, from which a blacker darkness seemed to emanate. The many crescents looked like horns against the sky. Bats flitted past her; from the distance came a jackal’s howl. What had she come to do there? She could not remember. “To pray,” she told herself, but that meant nothing. She strove with all her might to recollect. Then in a flash remembrance came to her; it bore her on, excited, to the mausoleum. She dismounted, and then, upon the threshold, she forgot once more. She entered, shuddering, too dazed to question why the gate was left ajar, and turned instinctively towards the women’s quarters. A step or two and she stood still in deadly terror, hardly venturing to breathe. There was a light upon the men’s side; beasts were tethered in the court; she heard a sound of digging and men’s voices. Her thought was, “They expect me, and have dug a grave.” As soon as fear would let her, she fled back to where the guide was waiting.

===Chinese===
“墓园到了，”向导说。他的低语声在她听来远得像隔了千山万水。直到走进亡者之城的街巷，她才望见那座城，黑影幢幢，透着诡秘。连星星在那一张张阴沉颦蹙的穹顶之上都显得不祥，仿佛有更浓黑的暗影正从穹顶里弥散出来。那许多新月衬着天，宛如一只只兽角。蝙蝠从她身边掠过；远处传来一声豺嚎。她到这儿来是要做什么？她想不起来了。“来祈祷，”她对自己说，可这话毫无意义。她竭尽全力去追想。忽然，记忆一闪而回；它载着她，一路亢奋地直奔那座陵墓。她下了驴，然而一到门槛上，她又忘了。她打着寒颤走进去，昏昏沉沉，竟不去问一问大门为何虚掩，便本能地朝女眷的居室转去。走出一两步，她便僵立在死一般的恐怖中，几乎不敢喘气。男宾那侧亮着灯；院子里拴着牲口；她听见掘土声和男人们的说话声。她想：“他们在等我，坟已经掘好了。”等恐惧稍一松手，她便逃回向导等候的地方。

===Original===
“There are people. We must fly! Make haste!” she whispered.
He helped her to remount, and they retraced their steps. The solemn thoughts which had possessed her mind gave place to rattle of dry bones and impish laughter. A merry dance was going on within her brain, as mad as could be, though her senses were quite clear﻿—clearer than ever they had been before, she knew exultantly. She rode out from the place of tombs across the sandhills towards the city.
“Hist!” said her companion suddenly, and stopped the donkey, hanging on to its tail to prevent braying. “There are men without a lantern﻿—robbers! I hear voices.”
She strained her ears in the direction pointed.

===Chinese===
“那边有人。咱们得快走！赶紧！”她低声道。
他扶她重新上驴，他们循原路折返。先前盘踞她心头的庄重念头，让位给了枯骨的咔咔声与小鬼的嬉笑。她的脑子里正开着一席欢舞，疯癫得无以复加，尽管她的神智清明得很——她狂喜地知道，比以往任何时候都清明。她骑驴出了那片坟场，越过沙丘，往城里去。
“嘘！”同伴忽然出声，一把拽住驴，揪着驴尾巴不让它嘶叫。“那边有人没打灯笼——是强盗！我听见说话声了。”
她朝他指的方向侧耳细听。

===Original===
“Am I not acknowledged sheik of all the thieves?” some unseen man amid the darkness was exclaiming angrily. “Was it not I alone who had the wisdom to foresee that every man would seek to hide his wealth this night? It is light work for you; they fly like conies at a shout, leaving their treasure, and the light for you to count it. Why then grumble that I sit here and receive the gold? Someone must hold it for fair distribution. Say, have I ever wronged a man among you of one small piaster? See, yonder comes another lantern. Go, do your work, and say no more to me.”

===Chinese===
“我不是众家贼人公认的谢赫（sheik）吗？”黑暗中某个看不见的人正怒气冲冲地嚷道。“难道不是只有我一人有那份见识，料定今夜人人都要藏匿财物？这活计对你们多么轻省：一声吆喝，他们便像蹄兔似的逃窜，丢下财宝，还把灯留下给你们点数。那你们还咕哝什么，怨我坐在这儿收金子？总得有人把它收着，好公平分派。倒说说看，我几时短过你们当中谁一个小小的皮阿斯特？瞧，那边又来了一盏灯笼。去，干你们的活，别再跟我多话。”

===Original===
“Stay, O my lady! For the love of Allah,” moaned Selîm. “They are robbers, murderers, the worst of ruffians.”
But Barakah had urged the donkey forward; the laughter in her brain deriding fear. She headed straight towards the voices, waving her left arm and shouting madly. She heard a shriek of “The afrîtah! Help, O Allah!” and saw men running as if fiends pursued them. Her next sensation was a dive into the sand. The ass had stumbled. Selîm assisted her to rise, and murmured reassuring words which made her cry.
Remembrance of her little daughter overcame her. She had prayed to Christ to guard her child before she recollected that the prayer was useless. There was no mercy for disciples of the Arab prophet. She reeled and would have fallen had not Selîm caught her. As it was, she sank upon the ground, refusing to remount or take another step.

===Chinese===
“别过去，夫人！看在安拉的分上，”塞利姆哀声说。“他们是强盗、凶手，最下等的恶棍。”
巴拉卡却已催驴向前；她脑子里的笑声正嘲弄着恐惧。她径直朝那些人声赶去，挥着左臂，狂喊乱叫。她听得一声尖叫——“阿弗里塔女妖（afrîtah）！救命啊，安拉！”——又看见男人们奔逃，仿佛恶魔追在脚跟。她的下一个知觉，是一头栽进沙里。驴失了蹄。塞利姆扶她起身，低声宽慰，那些话却叫她哭了。
对小女儿的思念把她压倒了。她方才还在祈求基督保佑她的孩子，随即想起这祈祷全无用处。阿拉伯先知的门徒是得不到怜悯的。她一个踉跄，若非塞利姆扶住，便要跌倒。她索性瘫坐在地上，再不肯上驴，也不肯挪动一步。

===Original===
The boy, resigned, sat down beside her, holding his donkey by the halter-rope. They were upon the trodden plain below the citadel. Lying upon her back, she saw a blackness rising till it took the shape of bastions, walls, towers, surmounted by a dome and needle-pointed minarets. Gazing at this and at the stars she fell asleep.
When she awoke it was still night. The donkey-boy was snoring on the ground hard by. A chill and a strange silence hung about her. The stars above were throbbing violently as if about to burst in showers of light. Her grief returned upon her like an ague. “O Lord, have mercy on me!” she exclaimed, and groaned aloud.
“What ails thee, O my sister?” said a voice so sweet, so unexpected in its nearness, that it stopped her heart.

===Chinese===
少年无可奈何，只得在她身旁坐下，一手攥着拴驴的缰绳。他们身在城堡下那片被人踏平的旷野上。她仰面躺着，看见一片黑影渐渐隆起，显出碉垒、城墙、塔楼的轮廓，顶上托着一座穹顶和几根针尖似的宣礼塔。她望着这黑影，又望着星星，睡着了。
她醒来时仍是深夜。赶驴的童子就在近旁的地上打鼾。一股寒意和一种异样的沉寂笼罩着她。头顶的星星剧烈地悸动，仿佛就要迸裂成光的骤雨。悲哀像疟疾一般重新袭上她身。“主啊，怜悯我吧！”她喊出声来，大声呻吟。
“你怎么了，我的姐妹？”一个声音说道——那声音这般甜柔，又这般近得出乎意料，惊得她心都停跳了。

## XXXVI / 三十六

===Original===
From the shadow of a mass of houses close at hand emerged the figure of a man in flowing robes, and glided towards her. For the moment she supposed it was an angel. Again the sweet voice thrilled her, asking:

“What ails thee, O my sister? Art thou wounded? May Allah heal and comfort thee in thy distress!”

She knew him then and felt a sudden craving.

“O Tâhir, sing to me!” she moaned. “Thy voice is healing. Canst thou still sing when thy delight is dead?”

===Chinese===
从近处一大片房舍的暗影里，转出一个长袍飘拂的男人身影，朝她翩然滑近。一时之间，她还当他是位天使。那甜美的嗓音又一次震颤了她的心，只听得它问道：

“你怎么了，我的姐妹？你受伤了吗？愿安拉治愈你、抚慰你的患难！”

她这才认出他来，心里蓦地生出一阵渴念。

“塔希尔啊，给我唱一支吧！”她呜咽道。“你的嗓音能治病。你的欢喜已经死了，你还能唱吗？”

===Original===
“Who art thou, lady?” He peered hard at her.

“I am the English wife of Yûsuf Pasha.”

“True; it is true,” he murmured, recollecting. “I heard that she had fled the house distraught with grief.﻿ ﻿… Hearken, O my lady, I am waiting here for the muezzin of the Sultan Hasan mosque, to ask his leave to call the Dawn instead of him. Victorious infidels are on the height above us; and no man can predict the future of this land. It is a black day for the Faith, may Allah help us! Our souls are humbled, weeping tears of blood. I lay upon my bed, but could not sleep for thinking on this grief. My heart and brain were full of singing, sad and noble. I felt the need to sing to God alone. And I vowed within my soul that none but Tâhir should call to prayer this dawn at yonder mosque within the shadow of the citadel which holds our shame. Now till my vow is paid I cannot guide thee. I beg thee enter the muezzin’s house and rest till my return.﻿ ﻿… Ah, here he comes.”

===Chinese===
“您是谁，夫人？”他定睛打量她。

“我是优素福·帕夏的英国妻子。”

“不错；正是，”他回过神来，喃喃道。“听说她悲恸得神智错乱，从宅子里逃了出来。……听我说，夫人，我在这儿等候苏丹哈桑清真寺（Sultan Hasan）的宣礼员，想求他俯允，让我替他唤晨礼。打了胜仗的异教徒就驻在咱们头顶的高地上；这片国土的前途，无人能够逆料。今天是信仰的黑日子，愿安拉佑助咱们！咱们的灵魂受了大辱，泣着血泪。我躺在床上，想着这桩悲恸，怎么也睡不着。我的心和头脑里满是歌声，悲哀而高贵。我觉着非得独自向真主歌唱不可。于是我在灵魂里立誓：今日拂晓，在近旁那座清真寺——就在那收藏着咱们耻辱的城堡的阴影之下——唤礼的，除了塔希尔决不能是旁人。誓愿未偿之前，我无法为您引路。求您进宣礼员家里歇着，等我回来。……啊，他来了。”

===Original===
The thud as of a wooden bolt withdrawn, the creak of a door opening reached their ears. The singer ran in the direction of the sound. She heard him coaxing the muezzin, who replied upon a yawn:

“With honour and with reverence, O Tâhir! It is thine to order.”

They had both drawn near to Barakah, entreating her to go indoors and rest, when the donkey-boy, aroused at last, rushed on them with stick raised.

“Where is my lady?” he cried out dementedly. “For the love of Allah, harm her not; her mind is troubled!”

They had some ado to reassure the lad, who was but half awake. Tâhir renewed his prayer to Barakah to enter the muezzin’s house without delay. She cried to be allowed to wait and hear his singing.

===Chinese===
一声木闩抽开的钝响、一扇门开启的吱呀声，传进他们耳中。歌者朝那声响的方向奔去。她听见他在温言央求那宣礼员，宣礼员打着呵欠答道：

“敬领，恭领，塔希尔啊！悉凭您吩咐。”

两人都已走近巴拉卡身旁，正劝她进屋歇息，不想赶驴的童子终于惊醒，高举棍子朝他们扑来。

“我的夫人在哪儿？”他发狂似的喊道。“看在安拉的分上，别伤害她；她的心神迷乱了！”

他们好一番口舌，才安顿下这半睡半醒的孩子。塔希尔再次恳求巴拉卡，请她即刻进宣礼员的家里去。她哭喊着求让自己留下，好听他歌唱。

===Original===
“Well, stay with her, O Mustafa! Bring cushions out! And thou, O best of donkey-drivers, seek the house of Yûsuf Pasha, inquire for one Ghandûr, and bring him hither!”

The boy bestrode his ass and disappeared into the darkness; the singer strode off, eager to perform his vow. The muezzin fetched some cushions from his house, and led the lady through the gloom until the minaret of Sultan Hasan loomed before them, and Barakah could distinguish its projecting gallery. Then he spread the cushions as a couch, himself subsiding on the ground behind her.

Barakah waited for what seemed long hours, so great was her impatience, like the sharpest hunger. Then, suddenly, when she had almost ceased to hope, a high, sweet note, sustained most wonderfully, filled her ear. It caused a parting of the lips, a melting rapture. It broke in a cascade of melody. Then came the long sweet note again, not held this time, but uttered often with a sobbed insistence. And then the song soared up to heights of praise, or hovered over depths of sorrow; she was lost in it. Uprising from the fount of hope in sadness, it soared to certainty of endless joy. The sound was no made music, but a soul poured forth in glorious melody, as spontaneous and unerring as the song of birds. The greatest singer in the world stood there unseen in the suspended gallery, and sang his heart out to the praise of the Creator, watching the dawn’s first gleam above the eastern hill.

===Chinese===
“好吧，穆斯塔法（Mustafa），你陪着她！搬几块坐垫出来！你呢，赶驴人里的头号好手，去优素福·帕夏的宅子，寻一个叫甘杜尔的人，把他带到这儿来！”

童子跨上毛驴，消失在黑暗里；歌者大步离去，一心要还他的誓愿。宣礼员从家里搬出几块坐垫，领着夫人穿过幽暗，直到苏丹哈桑的宣礼塔赫然耸现在眼前，巴拉卡才辨认出那挑出的塔廊。他便把坐垫铺成一张卧榻，自己退坐在她身后的地上。

巴拉卡等了仿佛好几个时辰，她的焦灼太深，就像最锋利的饥饿。忽然，就在她几乎不再指望的时候，一个高亢甜美的音符，以最不可思议的绵延，灌满了她的耳中。它叫人的双唇不由自主地张开，叫人涌起一阵消融的狂喜。随即它迸散成一道旋律的瀑布。接着又响起那悠长甜美的音，这一回不再绵延不去，而是一声声频频吐出，带着呜咽似的执拗。随后歌声扶摇直上，攀向颂赞的高天，又盘旋在忧伤的深渊之上；她迷失在其中了。它从悲哀中的希望之泉里升起，飞升到无尽喜乐的确信。那声音不是造作出来的音乐，而是一个灵魂倾泻而出的辉煌旋律，像鸟儿的歌唱一样自然天成、毫厘不爽。全世界最伟大的歌者就站在那悬空的塔廊里，无人得见，把整颗心唱出来颂赞造物主，望着东方山丘上空黎明的第一抹微光。

===Original===
On Barakah the song fell like a voice from heaven. She beheld great light. Her grief, her terrors, became natural shadows. There was one God for Christian and for Muslim. Beyond the striving and the hatred waited peace and love.

The professional muezzin on the ground behind her was rocking with enjoyment, gasping, sobbing: “Enough, O Tâhir! Of thy kindness, stop! Wouldst kill me quite? I faint, expire! It is too much of rapture! See me die! Praise be to Allah for the faith of El Islam. Praise to the benign Creator who has vouchsafed a voice to creatures for His glory!”

Another whispered: “That is no man’s song, but the song of Israfîl. Surely the last day is dawning. Praise to Allah!” And yet another murmured: “Praise to Him who sleepeth not nor dieth, the Merciful, the Compassionate, the Light of Lights, the Living King!”

===Chinese===
这歌声落在巴拉卡身上，宛如天上来音。她看见了大光。她的悲哀、她的恐惧，都成了自然的阴影。基督徒与穆斯林同有一位真主。在争斗与仇恨的那一头，等待着的原是安宁与慈爱。

她身后的地上，那以宣礼为业的宣礼员正听得前仰后合，喘着气，抽泣着：“够了，塔希尔啊！行行好，停一停吧！你是要人死吗？我要晕了，要断气了！这狂喜太叫人受不住了！看我都要死了！感赞安拉，为着伊斯兰教的信仰。赞颂那仁厚的造物主，他赐给受造之物一副歌喉，以彰显他的荣耀！”

另一个人低语道：“这不是凡人的歌，是伊斯拉菲勒（Israfîl）的歌。末日想必正在破晓。赞颂安拉！”又有一个人喃喃道：“赞颂那不眠亦不死的他，那普慈的、特慈的、众光之光、永生的君王！”

===Original===
Selîm the donkey-boy had come up with Ghandûr. They spoke no word to Barakah until the last note died. By then the pallor of the dawn shone on them faintly, showing the look of sadness which succeeds enchantment. Ghandûr then came and kissed the hand of Barakah, begging her of her kindness to return with him.

He and Selîm together lifted her on to the donkey.

As they left the square the English bugles sounded on the height above.

===Chinese===
赶驴的童子塞利姆已领着甘杜尔来了。直到最后一个音符消歇，他们才向巴拉卡开口。那时黎明的苍白微光淡淡照在他们身上，照出那紧随销魂而来的怅惘神色。甘杜尔这才走上前，吻了巴拉卡的手，恳请她发发善心，随他回去。

他和塞利姆一起把她搀上驴背。

他们离开广场时，英国人的军号在头顶的高地上吹响了。

## XXXVII / 三十七

===Original===
Quickly the daylight spread and filled the streets; while overhead successive darts of light pierced the incumbent darkness and dispelled it, till the sun’s first ray reddened the minarets and plunged the streets in azure shade. Men came out from their doorways as from tombs, and went about their business listlessly. Among the lower classes it was quite expected that the English would take vengeance on the town that day. The people did not care; they were in Allah’s hands, and gave Him thanks because the war was ended.

For Barakah the city wore its usual air; the only wretched figure was her own. She was being led back to a life which had become intolerable. After her tragic flight of yesterday, how ignominious was this meek return! Ghandûr, beside her, talked of the extreme anxiety in which her flight had plunged the Pasha’s family.

===Chinese===
天光迅速漫开来，灌满了各条街道；头顶上，一束接一束的光箭刺透沉沉压顶的黑暗，将它驱散，直到太阳的第一缕光线染红了宣礼塔，把街道浸入湛蓝的荫翳里。人们从各自的门口走出来，像从坟墓里走出来一样，没精打采地各办各的事。下层百姓中间，人人都料定英国人当天要向这座城泄愤报复。人们并不在意；他们全在安拉的手里，还感赞他，因为战争已经结束了。

在巴拉卡眼里，这座城仍是寻常那副面目；唯一愁惨的身影是她自己。她正被领回去，去过那种已变得无法忍受的生活。昨日那场惨痛的出走之后，这样温顺地归来，何其丢脸！甘杜尔走在她身旁，絮絮说着她的出逃使帕夏一家陷入了何等极度的焦灼。

===Original===
“O my lady, how hadst thou the heart to cause us such despair? Think of it! One like thee, alone and in the streets at such a time, when all authority is in abeyance, and the English host may come at any moment with the lust of conquerors! A hundred men were searching for thee through the night. My lord the Pasha thought that grief might lead thee to the place of tombs, and he himself went thither with the slaves enjoined to hide our valuables. Praise be to Allah, thou art found at last! Take comfort, O my lady! Often and often have I grieved for thee, alone among us! And when our great calamity befell﻿—alas, that son of mine should bear such evil tidings!﻿—I prayed to Allah to reveal to thee His boundless mercy. For it has no limits. For all who suffer in this world He will redress the balance. Even the unhorned cattle, O my lady! It is written.”

===Chinese===
“夫人啊，您怎么忍心叫咱们这样绝望？您想想看！像您这样身份的人，孤身一人，又赶上那样的时节上街，官府的权威尽皆失效，英国大军随时可能带着征服者的贪欲开进来！上百号人找您找了整整一夜。老爷帕夏想着，悲伤也许会把您引到亡人墓地那儿去，便亲自带着奴仆们去了，吩咐他们把咱们的贵重物件藏起来。感赞安拉，您总算找到了！宽宽心吧，我的夫人！我常常为您心疼，您孤孤单单地处在咱们中间！咱们的大难临头的时候——唉，偏偏是我那儿子去报的凶信！——我祈求安拉，愿他把那无边的慈悲显示给您。因为那慈悲没有边际。这世上一切受苦的人，他都要还他们一个公道。连没有角的牲畜也一样，夫人啊！经上写着呢。”

===Original===
Barakah heard these consolations as a dreary murmur.

“I am taking thee to the late Pasha’s house, to the great lady,” he informed her. “My lord considers it will be less sad for thee.”

The great lady meant no other than Murjânah Khânum. Recalling the authority Murjânah wielded, Barakah imagined she was being led to punishment.

Two eunuchs came forth, bowing, crying, “Praise to Allah!” They helped her to dismount, and both supported her. A minute later she had passed the harem screen. Her brief excursion in the world was ended. She was once more caged.

===Chinese===
巴拉卡听着这些宽慰的话，只当是一片沉闷的絮语。

“我这就带您到先帕夏的宅子里去，到大夫人那儿，”他告诉她。“老爷想着，这样您会少一些伤心。”

这大夫人不是别人，正是穆尔贾娜·哈努姆。想起穆尔贾娜素来执掌的威权，巴拉卡只道自己是被押去受罚了。

两名阉人迎上前来，躬着身，喊道：“感赞安拉！”他们扶她下驴，两人一左一右搀住她。不消一分钟，她已过了内宅的帷屏。她在这世上短短的出走就此告终。她再度被关进了笼子。

===Original===
Imagining her crime to be as great as that of Christian nun in breaking convent, and knowing that Murjânah Khânum could be ruthless, she expected torture; instead of which she was caressed and put to bed.

She had her lodging in Murjânah’s rooms, was dosed by Fitnah, comforted by Leylah Khânum. The younger ladies came as visitors and talked to cheer her. Old Umm ed-Dahak, not to be excluded, crouched by her bed and crooned as to an infant.

“Why are you all so kind to me?” she asked one day. “I tried to flee, I tell you, to escape to Europe﻿—yet you pet me!”

===Chinese===
她想着自己的罪过，重得就像基督教修女私逃出修道院一般，又深知穆尔贾娜·哈努姆下得了狠手，便等着受刑；等来的却是百般爱抚，被人服侍着上了床。

她宿在穆尔贾娜的房间里，由菲特娜给她喂药，莱拉·哈努姆（Leylah Khânum）从旁宽慰。年轻些的太太们像客人似的来串门，陪她说话解闷。老乌姆·埃德-达哈克也不甘被撇下，蹲在她床边，像哄婴孩一样哼着调儿。

“你们为什么都对我这样好？”有一天她问。“我告诉你们，我原本是想逃走，逃到欧洲去——可你们还这样宠着我！”

===Original===
“All things are pardoned to great grief,” replied Murjânah. “It was not thy fault, O poor one! Would to Allah I could show thee what I see more clearly than I see thee in this room﻿—the power of God, His mercy all around us. Fain would I hear thee give Him praise for thy misfortune. He sees and knows; we fancy; it is weak to strive. Think, O my fawn, my lily, thou hast still one child; thou hadst thy boy for thy delight for fifteen years. More fortunate than I who lost all mine in infancy! What peace can come to woman in thy case who does not offer up her will to God? The men have promise of a certain paradise; we have no certitude of what awaits us. Yet are we not dejected, for we know God’s mercy, and leave the future gladly in His hands. We women are not bargainers, we serve for love; and the mercy of the Highest cannot fail. Thou hast been brought up otherwise to prouder thoughts. Humble thy soul if thou wouldst find relief.”

===Chinese===
“巨大的悲伤能赦免一切，”穆尔贾娜答道。“那不是你的过错，可怜的人！但愿安拉容我把那景象指给你看——我看得比在这屋里看你还清楚——真主的权能，和他四面环绕着我们的慈悲。我巴不得听你为自己的不幸称颂他。他看见，他知道；我们不过妄自揣想；挣扎便是软弱。想想吧，我的小鹿，我的百合，你还有一个孩子；你那儿子给过你十五年的欢喜。比我福气多了，我的孩子全在襁褓里就夭折了！似你这般处境的女人，若不把自己的意志献给真主，还能得着什么安宁？男人们有明许给他们的乐园；我们却无从确知等待自己的是什么。但我们并不消沉，因为我们认得真主的慈悲，甘愿把未来托在他手里。我们女人不是讨价还价的人，我们是出于爱而侍奉；至高者的慈悲必不落空。你受的教养是另一路，心思养得更傲。你若要寻得宽解，就谦卑你的灵魂吧。”

===Original===
“I proud?” cried Barakah. “Thou, the proudest woman I have ever known, canst call me so? I am not as thou art﻿—strong and dauntless, cruel in thy resignation. I am feeble and afraid.”

“May Allah strengthen thee and drive out fear!”

Barakah had lost the vision which had come from Tâhir’s singing﻿—a vision which ignored divergences of race and custom. Without her son the harem life was senseless; she held the Muslim faith in secret dread; and longed for sentimental Christian people. Yûsuf, her husband, proved the soul of kindness, yet she had almost hated him in her revolt from all his race.

===Chinese===
“我高傲？”巴拉卡喊道。“你，我所认得的最最高傲的女人，倒能这样说我？我不像你那样——坚强、无畏，连顺命都顺得冷酷。我又软弱，又害怕。”

“愿安拉使你坚强，把恐惧驱走！”

巴拉卡已经失去了塔希尔的歌声带给她的那个异象——一个无视种族与习俗分歧的异象。没有了儿子，深闺生活于她全无意义；她心底暗暗畏惧穆斯林的信仰；一心渴念那些多愁善感的基督徒众人。她的丈夫优素福事实证明仁厚到了骨子里，可她在对他整个族类的反叛之中，几乎恨过他。

===Original===
One day he told the ladies in her presence:

“The English are not bad. They take wise measures for the land’s redemption. They have asked me to take office, and I have a mind to do so.”

It was the first time she had heard the English mentioned since her reimprisonment. In fact, the Turkish pride had suffered cruelly from this intrusion of a European power, the more so that the natives of the land acclaimed it. Though the English arms restored their party in the State, the Turks in Egypt gnawed their lips and could not speak of them.

===Chinese===
有一天，他当着她的面对太太们说：

“英国人不坏。他们为救赎这片土地采取着明智的措施。他们请我出任官职，我有意应承下来。”

这是她再度被禁闭以来头一回听人提起英国人。事实上，欧洲强权的这一闯入，狠狠挫伤了土耳其人的自尊，更兼这片土地的本土百姓对它齐声欢呼。虽然英国人的武力让他们这一党在国内恢复了权位，埃及的土耳其人仍旧咬着嘴唇，绝口不愿谈论他们。

===Original===
A new way of escape appeared to Barakah. She could obtain an audience of the English rulers and announce her longing to return to Christianity. She pined for the ideals of Christian lands, the independent life of women, and their varied interests. Here she had lost her value, having lost her son. She would soon be an old woman, a mere worn-out animal.

Directly she conceived this plan, she grew more cheerful, and even felt some kindness for the harem walls. While making her endeavour to find out from Yûsuf the names of Englishmen of influence, their character and reputation, she wanted to make certain he would be consoled.

“Light of my eyes,” she whispered, nestling to him, “I have quite outgrown my foolish prejudice. I beg thee now to wed another wife. The son I bore to thee is dead, and I grow old.”

===Chinese===
一条新的出路在巴拉卡眼前出现了。她可以设法求见英国的统治者们，宣布自己渴望回归基督教。她渴慕基督教国土的那些理想——女人独立的生活，还有她们林林总总的志趣。在这里她已失了价值，因为儿子没了。她很快就要成为老妇，一头用残了的牲口罢了。

这计划一在心头成形，她便开朗了些，甚至对这深闺的四壁也生出几分好感。她一面设法从优素福口中探听有势力的英国人的姓名、品性与声望，一面又想确定他会得到宽慰。

“我眼里的光啊，”她偎着他，低声说，“我早把那点愚蠢的偏见抛干净了。如今我求你再娶一房吧。我给你生的儿子死了，我也见老了。”

===Original===
“Wallahi, thou art still delicious,” he replied gallantly; but all the same he thanked her, seeming much relieved.

Perceiving that the anguish of her grief was past, the ladies let her go to her own house.

“Remember my advice,” said old Murjânah in farewell. “Behold, my eyes grow dim, my days are numbered. I speak not frivolously like the young. Give up thy will. That is to islam truly. May Allah grant thee resignation, which is strength.”

===Chinese===
“凭安拉，你如今还是这样叫人心醉，”他殷勤地答道；但他仍旧谢了她，显得如释重负。

太太们看出她悲恸的苦楚已经熬了过去，便让她回自己的宅子去了。

“记住我的话，”老穆尔贾娜临别时说。“看哪，我的眼睛昏花了，我的日子有数了。我不像年轻人那样轻嘴薄舌。舍弃你自己的意志，那才是真正的皈依伊斯兰。愿安拉赐你顺命，顺命即是力量。”

## XXXVIII / 三十八

===Original===
She had not been in her own house a day, before she said to Umm ed-Dahak:

“Wilt thou do me a great service?”

“Wallahi, that will I! Even﻿—saving thy presence﻿—one most sinful!”

“And canst thou keep a secret from the seed of Adam?”

“Not only that, but from the walls and air.”

“I want a letter carried to a great one of the English.”

“I seek refuge in Allah!” gasped the old woman, grinning widely. “Knowest thou it is a crime unheard of that thou askest of me? Fie upon thee! Wallahi, if I did my duty I should leave thee straightway!”

But far from flying from her mistress she came nearer. Her wrinkles ran to smiles; her old eyes twinkled.

“Come, let us reason!” she remarked, as she sat down, and, fingering her lady’s hand, began the argument.

“If thou desirest recreation of a shameful kind, let me discover some devout believer. Thus the sin is less. Or better still, approach thy husband, tell him thou art weary, implore him of his mercy to release thee, with a portion of thy dowry. No man would refuse the offer after years of marriage. Then I could find thee a good Muslim, for diversion. But a Frank﻿—an unbeliever! Ask me not! It is too horrible!”

===Chinese===
她回到自己宅里不到一天，就对乌姆·埃德-达哈克说：

“你肯替我办一件大事吗？”

“凭安拉，我肯的！哪怕是——恕我冒昧——顶顶有罪的营生我也干！”

“这秘密你还能瞒得过亚当的子孙吗？”

“不止呢，连墙壁和空气也一并瞒过。”

“我要送一封信，给英国人里头的一位大人物。”

“我求庇于安拉！”老太婆倒抽一口气，咧开大嘴笑道。“你可知道你求我办的这档子事，是闻所未闻的罪过？呸，不害臊！凭安拉，我若尽本分，这会儿就该撇下你一走了之！”

可她非但没有从主母身边逃开，反倒凑得更近了。满脸的皱纹都堆成了笑，一双老眼忽闪忽闪地放光。

“来，咱们讲讲道理！”她说着坐下来，一面抚弄着主母的手，一面开始陈说利害。

“你若想寻那种见不得人的快活，就让我给你物色个虔诚的信徒，这样罪过还轻些。再不然，更好的法子：去求你丈夫，告诉他你倦怠了，求他发个慈悲休了你，从你的嫁妆里分你一份。做了这么多年的夫妻，没有哪个男人会回绝这样的提议。往后我再给你寻个好穆斯林，给你消遣解闷。可要是法兰克人——一个异教徒！休要开口求我！那太可怕了！”

===Original===
“By Allah, my desire is not the thing thou thinkest!” Barakah made answer gaily. “This Englishman is one I knew in childhood. I would speak with him. The matter is no other than my lord’s advancement, though if he knew I meddled he would kill me!”

“Swear to that! But swear to that!” cried Umm ed-Dahak, much excited, “and I can do thy errand without sin. But if thy mind is for a Frank, I could unearth thee Muslims of that race; though most of them are idiotic from hashish.”

“My errand is to this one only!”

“Good, I go.”

===Chinese===
“以安拉起誓，我想要的可不是你想的那回事！”巴拉卡快活地答道。“这个英国人是我小时候认得的。我不过要同他说话。事情无非是替我家老爷谋个高升，只是他若知道我插手其间，会杀了我的！”

“这话你可要起誓！千万起誓！”乌姆·埃德-达哈克大为兴奋，喊道，“这样我替你跑这趟腿就不担罪名了。不过你心里若真惦记法兰克人，我也能给你挖出几个那一路的穆斯林来；只是他们多半抽哈希什（hashish）抽得痴痴呆呆。”

“我的差事只找这一个！”

“好，我这就去。”

===Original===
The lady clapped her hands and called for writing things. The letter taxed her mind for hours; the fitting phrase, the correct tone, eluding one who for so many years had penned no word of English. At last it was completed. She implored the great official, of his mercy, his great kindness, to receive an English lady, long immured in the harem, where she had suffered greatly. She wished to make a most important statement (this she underlined) and begged him to secure the utmost secrecy. She would not write her name for fear the letter should be intercepted, but would reveal it to him with the other matter when they met. The document, enveloped and sealed down, was put into the hands of Umm ed-Dahak. After two hours, she brought back the answer, “Tomorrow at the fourth hour,” given her by word of mouth. She had not seen the Englishman himself.

“Wallahi, we will make thee beautiful,” she chuckled.

===Chinese===
巴拉卡拍拍手，唤人取来笔墨。这封信绞尽了她几个钟头的脑汁；对一个多年不曾写过一个英文词的人来说，贴切的措辞、得体的语气，总是捉摸不住。信终于写成了。她恳求那位大员大发慈悲、广施仁心，接见一位久困内宅、在那里头受尽苦楚的英国女士。她有一件极其紧要的事要当面陈明（这一句她划了底线），并恳求他务必做到至为周密的保密。她不肯写出自己的名字，惟恐信件半路被截，只允诺见面时连同那桩要事一并向他吐露。信装进信封、封了口，交到乌姆·埃德-达哈克手里。两个钟头后，她带回了口头的答复：“明天四时。”她没有见到那位英国人本人。

“凭安拉，咱们要把您打扮得漂漂亮亮的，”她咯咯地笑道。

===Original===
Then Barakah reviewed her prison with affection. She went from room to room, observing for remembrance. In one, the slave-girls crouched round an old hag who told a story. The light which fell like powder from the lattice singled out their teeth and eyeballs, and woke a blue sheen in the copper vessels round the wall. In another, the child Afîfah stood up on the seat beside the lattice, feeding pigeons; the wife of Ghandûr, standing by, supported her. A little wicket in the tracery was open.

“H’m-h’m-h’m-h’m!” Afîfah gave the pigeon-call, and held out crumbs. A fluttering cloud of white and iridescent down, pink, shell-like claws, and avid beaks and eyes, beset the lattice from without, its shadow watering the child’s delighted face.

Barakah retired without disturbing them. She had a hankering to take the little girl with her. But no, Afîfah was a child of El Islam. Like all the rest, she would condemn and curse her mother.

===Chinese===
随后，巴拉卡怀着眷恋巡视了一遍自己的牢狱。她一间屋一间屋地走，一一看在眼里、记在心头。一间屋里，女奴们团团围蹲着一个讲古的老婆子。从花格窗里筛落下来的光像粉末一般，单单照出她们的牙齿和眼白，又唤醒了四壁铜器上幽幽的蓝光。另一间屋里，小阿菲法站在花格窗旁的座位上喂鸽子；甘杜尔的妻子立在旁边扶着她。花格窗棂间开着一扇小活门。

“咕——咕——咕——咕！”阿菲法发出唤鸽的叫声，把面包屑递了出去。一团扑腾的白羽，带着闪虹彩的软绒、粉红而贝壳似的爪子、贪馋的喙和眼睛，从外面团团围住了花格窗；那团影子在孩子欢喜的小脸上如水般荡漾。

巴拉卡没有惊动她们，悄悄退了出来。她心里痒痒的，想把小女儿一并带走。可是不成的，阿菲法是伊斯兰的孩子。同其余所有人一样，她将来也会斥责自己的母亲、咒骂自己的母亲。

===Original===
Then visitors arrived﻿—Gulbeyzah and Bedr-ul-Budûr﻿—and Barakah waxed sentimental in her talk with them, recalling all the pleasant hours which they had spent together. Both were now grown obese and double-chinned. Nothing remained of the resplendent beauty which had marked their girlhood save the eyes, which made them still attractive when they wore the face-veil. She pitied them, with anguish for herself; and kissed them fondly when they rose to go.

Then Yûsuf came to spend an hour with her. She thanked him with sincere emotion for his never-failing kindness to her during all those years.

“It is nothing but thy due,” he answered, greatly touched. “Thou art alone among us, and my cherished wife.”

===Chinese===
接着来了客人——古尔贝雅扎和白德尔-布杜尔——巴拉卡同她们叙谈时大发感伤，回想起她们一同度过的种种好时光。两人如今都发福了，长出了双下巴。少女时代那光彩照人的美貌，除了一双眼睛，一丝也没剩下；全凭这双眼睛，她们蒙上面纱依旧动人。她怜惜她们，也为自己心疼得发苦；她们起身告辞时，她恋恋地吻了她们。

随后优素福来陪她消磨了一个钟头。她怀着真挚的情感，感谢他这些年来对她从不间断的仁爱。

“那不过是分内应得的，”他答道，深受感动。“你在我们中间孤身一人，是我珍爱的妻子。”

===Original===
That night the very howling of the street-dogs sounded sweet; the starlight at her lattice seemed a humble friend. Her heart bled for the parting which was very near. For not a doubt existed in her mind but that the English, once informed of her desire for Christianity, would snatch her from the Muslims with a mighty hand. The power was theirs; they governed Egypt; and she knew from her remembrance that they were fanatical. They would welcome her conversion, and defend her.

===Chinese===
那一夜，连街头野狗的嚎叫听来也是甜的；花格窗前的星光，仿佛一位谦卑的朋友。离别近在眼前，她的心为它淌血。因为她心里没有半点怀疑：英国人一旦听说她向往基督教，定会伸出一只大能的手，把她从穆斯林中间夺走。权柄在他们手里；埃及归他们治理；她还凭着自己的记忆知道，他们原是些宗教狂热之徒。他们会欢迎她皈依，并且护住她。

===Original===
In the morning Umm ed-Dahak bubbled over with excitement. She accompanied her lady to the bath, and bade the bath attendant take all measures to enhance her beauty. She assured her mistress in an eager whisper:

“Trust Umm ed-Dahak, I have managed everything.”

She had given orders in her lady’s name that the harem carriage and a eunuch should be ready at a certain hour. She and Barakah were driven to a shop of good repute, famed for its stock of Frankish boots and gloves, of which the harem ladies were enamoured as showing off their pretty hands and feet.

“Our business here may take some time﻿—an hour, perhaps,” she told the eunuch, who took position sentry-wise beside the entrance. The shop possessed two doors. Making a trifling purchase, they went out unnoticed, and found themselves within a stone’s throw of the public office which the English ruler had appointed for the interview.

===Chinese===
早晨，乌姆·埃德-达哈克满心兴奋，几乎要溢出来。她陪着主母上澡堂，吩咐澡堂侍女使出浑身解数增添她的美貌。她凑近主母，急切地低声担保：

“放心把事交给乌姆·埃德-达哈克，我一切都安排妥当了。”

她已假传主母之命，吩咐在某个钟点备好内宅的马车和一名阉人。她同巴拉卡坐车来到一家声誉极佳的铺子，铺子以囤存的法兰克靴子和手套闻名，内宅的夫人们酷爱这些东西，好炫耀她们漂亮的手脚。

“咱们在这儿办的事兴许要耽搁些时候——大概一个钟头，”她告诉那阉人；阉人便像哨兵似的守住门口。这铺子有两扇门。她们买了一样小东西，趁人不备出了去，一抬头，离英国长官指定作会面之用的官署只隔一投石之地。

===Original===
The street in blazing sunlight was flowing with a many-coloured crowd, which kept up such a jabber that Barakah could not think clearly. The scene she had rehearsed appeared ridiculous. Seized with panic, she was anxious to turn back; but Umm ed-Dahak at her elbow whispered courage. In a minute she had entered a great doorway leading to a wide stone hall, where soldiers lounged. One of them came forward at a beck from Umm ed-Dahak. Then the old woman went and squatted on the doorstep, and Barakah, half dead with terror, was led on alone.

===Chinese===
烈日灼灼的街上，流动着五彩斑斓的人群，嘁嘁喳喳嚷个不停，闹得巴拉卡没法凝神细想。她预先排演过的那一幕场面，此刻显得荒唐可笑。她一阵惊慌，只想回头；可是乌姆·埃德-达哈克在肘边低声替她壮胆。转眼间，她已走进一道大门，门里是一个宽阔的石厅，士兵们在厅里懒懒散散地倚着。乌姆·埃德-达哈克一招手，其中一个士兵迎上前来。老太婆便走到一边，在门槛上蹲下身来；巴拉卡吓得半死，独自一人被领了进去。
